ROUTES: leg.state.nv.us works; legis.iowa.gov/docs/iac/agency/481.pdf IS AVAILABLE at 19.7 MB (Iowa is reachable, just large) while the per-chapter path returns a 10 KB viewer wrapper; Oregon OARD and Kansas KAR indexes respond and were not walked; adminrules.utah.gov is a JavaScript SPA returning 84 characters on every path INCLUDING ITS SEARCH ENDPOINT. AND A SECOND SOFT-200 IN TWO ROUNDS: eregulations.ct.gov returned 200 with the body "eRegulations - Unable to Acccess Resource" (the misspelling is the portal's own) -- the body check added after Alabama's 404page caught it immediately. Two forms, two rounds, one check. METHOD: a three-pattern regex scan over 1.6M characters blew the 120s limit and finished in the background; extracting the chapter's own contents list first and jumping to named sections is what produced everything useful. 32 states touched. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-13-iowa-platform-registration.md (NEW), pathways-model doc edited (Fourth) -- IOWA REGISTERS THE SOFTWARE PLATFORM ITSELF, WHICH IS THE FIRST RULE IN THIS SURVEY THAT COULD CONSTRAIN SAIRNSENIOR RATHER THAN ITS CUSTOMERS. Iowa Code ch. 135Q / 481 IAC ch. 55 "Health Care Employment Agencies and HEALTH CARE TECHNOLOGY PLATFORMS" (ARC 6711C eff. 2023-01-04, amended ARC 9022C eff. 2025-04-23): 55.2(1) requires a health care employment agency OR HEALTH CARE TECHNOLOGY PLATFORM operating in the state to REGISTER ANNUALLY with the department and pay a fee. Two parallel tracks -- employment agency/"agency worker" and technology platform/"INDEPENDENT NURSING SERVICES PROFESSIONAL" -- and "health care entity" expressly includes ANY HOME HEALTH AGENCY plus assisted living (231C) and adult day services (231D). 55.3(1) MOVES AXES A, B AND D ONTO THE PLATFORM: it must complete the criminal, dependent-adult-abuse and child-abuse record checks, the physical examination and TB screening/testing, and confirmation that the professional completed ALL education, training and continuing education requirements and is in good standing -- everything "that would otherwise be the responsibility of the health care entity". 55.3(2) requires IMMEDIATE notification to the facility on an abuse allegation so the person can be separated -- an inbound report with an immediate outbound obligation, not a ticket queue. 55.5(2)(a) requires reporting COMPLETED WORK ASSIGNMENTS of CNAs to the direct care worker registry sufficient to maintain active status (42 CFR 483.35(d)(6)), so completed shifts are a REGULATORY FILING and a worker's registry status depends on the scheduling system's own data. 55.5(3) requires QUARTERLY REPORTING of every Medicare/Medicaid entity contracted with AND THE AVERAGE AMOUNT CHARGED, broken down by provider type and worker category -- price disclosure to the state. 55.6 gives anyone a confidential complaint route with a 45-working-day investigation trigger and a preponderance standard. THE TRIGGER IS UNVERIFIED AND THAT IS THE POINT: ch. 55 incorporates the definitions at Iowa Code 135Q.1 BY REFERENCE and I have NOT read 135Q.1, so exactly which software is in scope is unestablished -- a tool scheduling an agency's own W-2 employees is plausibly outside, a marketplace matching independent professionals to facilities is plausibly inside, and neither reading is established. ROUTED TO sairn-decision-gate rather than answered in a research file, because it is a question about what SAIRNsenior may SELL in Iowa. Flagged at the top of the pathways-model doc too. NEVADA'S TWO FLAGGED SECTIONS NOW READ, and one upgrades an earlier label: 449.3978 is the FIRST ENUMERATED TASK DENY-LIST in the survey -- attendants must not insert or irrigate a catheter, irrigate any body cavity, apply dressings involving prescription medication or aseptic technique, give injections or administer medication (both except as authorised by 449.39775), perform physical assessments, use specialized feeding techniques, perform digital rectal exams, TRIM OR CUT TOENAILS, massage, provide specialized range-of-motion services, or do medical case management. Colorado forbids a FEATURE to a registration category; Nevada forbids named TASKS to a role, so a care-plan or task-catalogue builder needs a per-state per-role deny-list, and toenail trimming is exactly the ordinary-seeming task a product would offer without thinking. 449.3979's disclosure must be SIGNED by the client or representative and filed in the client record, and must state in easily understandable terms that IT IS NOT WITHIN THE SCOPE OF THE AGENCY'S LICENCE to manage medical conditions that become unstable or unpredictable, plus attendant qualifications and training requirements, charges, billing methods and price-increase notification policy, termination criteria, contact procedures for ALL hours services are provided and the on-call policy, and client rights and grievance procedure -- same artefact family as Arizona 36-144 but at ACCEPTANCE and SIGNED rather than annual and unsigned. 449.3982 requires documented, dated, signed supervisory home visits OR TELEPHONE CALLS evaluating, among other things, WHETHER THE ATTENDANT HAS RECEIVED SUFFICIENT TRAINING FOR THE SERVICES THAT ATTENDANT PROVIDES TO THAT CLIENT -- the Louisiana per-client family again, arriving through a supervision rule. AND 449.39519 IS UPGRADED FROM INFERENCE TO VERIFIED: round 12 labelled the client-as-trainer finding as resting on a section heading; the body confirms it and enumerates nine content areas incl. bed-bath and tub-bath technique, catheter and pressure-sore care, assistive technology, nutrition, and maintaining health records with confidentiality. ALSO IOWA 481-50.9: background duties attach PRIOR TO EMPLOYMENT for facilities including home health agencies, reach CNA TRAINING STUDENTS, and "crime" excludes ch. 321 simple misdemeanours. ROUTES: the 19.7 MB / 2,463-page Iowa PDF is searchable page-by-page in about four minutes and is a BETTER route than the per-chapter viewer that returns a 10 KB wrapper -- round 12 recorded Iowa as "large" as if that were a problem and it was not. OREGON IS NOW A SPLIT STATE: ORS reachable at oregonlegislature.gov (ors443.html fetched, 141 KB, unread) while secure.sos.state.or.us/oard is BOT-WALLED, returning "Please enable JavaScript... Your support ID is:" on every page -- same shape as Arizona and Oklahoma. Kansas's KAR index page is site chrome with no document links. METHOD: the Iowa platform chapter turned up because the sweep included "home care" generally rather than because anything suggested a state regulates software vendors -- a survey looking only for aide training would never have found it. 33 states touched. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-iowa-135Q-scope-determination.md (NEW), round-13 and pathways-model docs updated (Fourth) -- IOWA SCOPE QUESTION SETTLED AGAINST THE STATUTE: SAIRNSENIOR AS BUILT IS OUTSIDE ch. 135Q, AND THE TRAP IS NOT THE ONE I EXPECTED. Read Iowa Code 2025 ch. 135Q from the Legislature's own PDF. 135Q.1(6) defines "health care technology platform" as an entity maintaining an INTERNET- OR APPLICATION-BASED MARKETPLACE through which an INDEPENDENT NURSING SERVICES PROFESSIONAL BIDS ON OPEN SHIFTS POSTED BY A HEALTH CARE ENTITY -- three CUMULATIVE elements. SAIRNsenior has none of them: no marketplace (single-tenant agency-facing app, panels dashboard/ai/clients/scheduling/billing/caregivers/compliance/reports/security/settings), no bidding (ZERO occurrences of "open shift", "bid", "bidding", "marketplace", "independent contractor" or "1099" anywhere in sairnsenior.html), and no posted open shifts (roster-and-assign -- 65 "assign" hits, a roster, and the employee list read through the shared employees resource). 135Q.1(7) defines the professional BY THE BIDDING BEHAVIOUR and only "through a health care technology platform", so an assigned employee is not one. 135Q.1(3) does not reach a software vendor at all -- an employment agency is one that CONTRACTS TO PROVIDE WORKERS -- and (b) excludes a health care entity staffing itself while (c) excludes platforms. THE TRAP: "nursing services" at 135Q.1(10) IS NOT LIMITED TO NURSING -- it expressly includes services by home health aides and by NONCERTIFIED OR NONLICENSED STAFF PROVIDING PERSONAL CARE under 231C.2. So the intuitive defence "we only do non-medical personal care, this is a nursing statute" FAILS ON THE STATUTE'S OWN WORDS, and a personal-care marketplace would be squarely in scope. Written into the determination so whoever evaluates a future marketplace feature cannot reach for it. WHAT INSIDE WOULD COST, documented so the trade-off is visible BEFORE the feature decision rather than after: 135Q.3(1)(a) annual registration with DIAL and a $500 annual fee; (1)(b) failure to register PROHIBITS CONTRACTING WITH ANY HEALTH CARE ENTITY IN IOWA -- a market ban, not a fine; (1)(c) the platform becomes an authorized agency for the single contact repository and MUST RERUN BACKGROUND CHECKS AFTER TWO CONSECUTIVE YEARS OF INACTIVITY, a dormancy-triggered job driven by scheduling data; (2) the platform must VERIFY each professional supplies documentation meeting ALL APPLICABLE STATE REQUIREMENTS AND QUALIFICATIONS, meets minimum licensing/certification, and carries professional liability insurance of $1M per occurrence / $3M aggregate; (3)(a)(1) no noncompete restricting their employment opportunities; plus ch. 55's immediate abuse-allegation notification, documentation on demand, and quarterly reporting of contracted Medicare/Medicaid entities AND AVERAGE AMOUNTS CHARGED. 135Q.3(2)(a) IS THE ONE THAT WOULD BITE HARDEST -- it makes the PLATFORM responsible for verifying each worker meets all applicable state requirements, which across the 33 states surveyed is the entire five-axis model plus the behaviour constraints, per worker, as a condition of operating. FRAMED AS A DOCUMENTED READING, NOT LEGAL ADVICE, with counsel confirmation recommended before anything customer-facing rests on it, per sairn-decision-gate's rule about claims made outside the team. AND RECORDED AS A STANDING CONDITION RATHER THAN A ONE-TIME ANSWER: it is about the product as built on 2026-08-30 and must be re-run against 135Q.1(6) before any feature ships that lets a non-employee choose shifts. NOT CHECKED AND SAID SO: 135Q.2, 135Q.4, 135Q.5, the rest of 135Q.3(3), Iowa Code 231C.2's personal-care definition, and -- the important one -- WHETHER ANY OTHER STATE HAS AN EQUIVALENT PLATFORM-REGISTRATION STATUTE. This one was found only because a keyword sweep happened to include "home care"; no other state has been checked for it and the absence of a finding is not evidence of absence. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-14-oregon.md (NEW), Iowa determination updated (Fourth) -- IOWA WAS NOT ISOLATED: FOUR STATES REGULATE THE MATCHING BUSINESS UNDER FOUR NAMES WITH NO SHARED KEYWORD, AND OREGON WRITES A MOBILE APP INTO STATUTE. Round 13 cautioned that no other state had been checked for a platform-registration category and that absence of a finding was not evidence of absence. That caution was warranted and is now answered: Iowa (health care technology platform, REGISTRATION, $500/yr), Colorado (home care placement agency, REGISTRATION), OREGON (caregiver registry, LICENCE -- ORS 443.100), and Nevada (referral agency AND employment agency to provide nonmedical services, both licensed, sitting in a NAC 449 fee schedule I had already read past). ORS 443.100 has TWO TRIGGERS and the second is unusual: a person may not conduct a caregiver registry OR "REPRESENT TO THE PUBLIC THAT THE PERSON IS A CAREGIVER REGISTRY" without a licence -- a marketing claim alone can trip it, which Iowa's definition cannot. 443.105 lets OHA regulate the MINIMUM QUALIFICATIONS of individuals whose services are offered through a registry and require CRIMINAL BACKGROUND CHECKS on individuals placed on a roster -- the same shape as Iowa 135Q.3(2), two states independently putting axes A and B on the intermediary. VOCABULARY POINT HANDLED CAREFULLY RATHER THAN ALARMINGLY: 443.105(5) uses "roster" and SAIRNsenior's UI uses "roster" 13 times, but that is ordinary English, not a trigger -- the statutory hook is offering individuals' services TO THE PUBLIC as a registry. The Oregon "represent to the public" limb does mean the MARKETING COPY matters in a way Iowa's does not, so describing the product to Oregon prospects as a "caregiver registry" would be a poor choice of words with a statutory edge on it. OREGON ORS 443.190/443.195 (2025 c.535) IS THE MOST CONCRETE PRODUCT REQUIREMENT IN THE WHOLE SURVEY. 443.190(3) SPECIFIES THE MINIMUM CONTENTS OF A CLIENT INTAKE QUESTIONNAIRE IN PRIMARY LEGISLATION: presence of PETS and whether they can be secured away from the care area IF THE STAFF MEMBER REQUESTS, suspected PEST INFESTATIONS, and the client's willingness to SECURELY STORE ANY WEAPONS before any visit. Nothing else in this survey descends to the field level. And it is DIRECTIONAL -- (2)(b) requires the information be provided to EACH STAFF MEMBER WHO WILL PROVIDE THE SERVICES, making it a property of the ASSIGNMENT rather than the client record, the same per-assignment shape as Louisiana's per-client competency but arriving from worker-safety law. (2)(c) adds that for hospital-discharge referrals any CLIENT HISTORY OF VIOLENCE learned through continuity of care must be passed on. 443.195 then requires NIOSH/OSHA-consistent hazard training, QUARTERLY SAFETY ASSESSMENTS with assigned staff, identifying information to VERIFY THE CLIENT'S IDENTITY BEFORE AN INITIAL VISIT, "mechanisms by which home health care staff can perform safety checks, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED TO THE USE OF A MOBILE APPLICATION" to access that safety information, and policies allowing staff to DO DATA ENTRY AND CHART UPDATES OUTSIDE THE CLIENT'S HOME and to BE ACCOMPANIED BY AN ESCORT where there are safety concerns. A state naming the software as the compliance mechanism, and the charting-away-from-home point cuts directly against a point-of-care-only documentation design. THE KEYWORD SWEEP WOULD HAVE MISSED OREGON: technology platform 0, marketplace 0, staffing agency 0, employment agency 0, open shift 0, independent contractor 0, referral agency 0 across the whole chapter -- and the category was still there under "caregiver registry". What found it was READING THE CHAPTER'S OWN SECTION INDEX, the fifth state where the index was the whole answer. Sweep for the FUNCTION (registry, referral, placement, staffing, marketplace, platform, temporary, per diem, bids, open shift), not a term. ALSO WORTH KEEPING: ORS 443.190/443.195 carry an editor's note that they were enacted into law but NOT added to chapter 443 or any series therein -- a search scoped to the chapter's series would skip them entirely. NOT DONE: the OAR rules implementing 443.105 are unreachable (OARD bot-walled), ORS 443.090/443.095 and the in-home care agency licensing sections unread, ORS 654.412 unread, and THE IOWA-STYLE SCOPE DETERMINATION HAS NOT BEEN RUN FOR OREGON, COLORADO OR NEVADA -- on the face of ORS 443.100 the answer looks the same but that is an impression, not a reading, and it is labelled as such. 34 states touched. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-intermediary-scope-or-co-nv.md (NEW), round-14 and pathways-model docs corrected (Fourth) -- ALL THREE INTERMEDIARY DETERMINATIONS RUN PROPERLY, AND READING THE DEFINITIONS CAUGHT ONE OF MY OWN CLAIMS. Round 14 said the answer for OR/CO/NV "looks the same on the face of it" and labelled that an impression. Read properly the conclusion holds for all three, and one round-14 claim was WRONG. OREGON ORS 443.014(1) defines a caregiver registry as a person that PREQUALIFIES, establishes and maintains a roster OF QUALIFIED PRIVATE CONTRACTOR CAREGIVERS, PROVIDED TO A CLIENT OR THE CLIENT'S REPRESENTATIVE, FOR CONSIDERATION IN THE HIRING of an individual -- four cumulative elements, and SAIRNsenior fails 2, 3 and 4 independently (its roster is the agency's own EMPLOYEES, never exposed to clients, and clients do not hire). OUTSIDE. But ORS 443.100's second limb is a real constraint on WORDS rather than architecture: representing to the public that you ARE a caregiver registry is itself unlawful without a licence, so Oregon marketing copy must not describe the product that way or frame the roster as something clients browse and hire from. And if it were inside, ORS 443.019 requires an OHA IN-PERSON SITE INSPECTION before licensure and at least every three years. COLORADO 6 CCR 1011-1 ch.26 s2.12 defines a home care placement agency as an organisation that FOR A FEE PROVIDES ONLY REFERRALS of providers to consumers and does NOT provide services directly or by contract. SAIRNsenior refers nobody and takes no referral fee -- OUTSIDE. THE WORD "ONLY" MATERIALLY NARROWS THE ROUND-6 BUILD CONSTRAINT: an organisation that both refers AND provides services is NOT a placement agency, it is a licensed HCA under Parts 5-7, so the Part 3 prohibition on directing/controlling/SCHEDULING/training binds PURE-REFERRAL BUSINESSES ONLY. The account-level capability gate must key on pure-referral status, not on "has a Colorado registration", and the product needs to know which of the two a Colorado customer is. NEVADA NAC 449.0033 defines an employment agency to provide nonmedical services as an employment agency THAT CONTRACTS WITH PERSONS to provide nonmedical personal care to elderly or disabled persons (NRS 449.01517), licensed as a "facility" via NAC 449.0034. The operative element is CONTRACTING WITH THE CAREGIVERS; SAIRNsenior contracts only with agency customers for software -- OUTSIDE. MY OWN CORRECTION: round 14's table listed Nevada as contributing TWO intermediary categories. NAC 449.0061 defines "referral agency" as a business providing REFERRALS TO RESIDENTIAL FACILITIES FOR GROUPS under NRS 449.0305 -- referring CLIENTS INTO ASSISTED LIVING, not matching caregivers to homes. It is not an analogue at all. I took it from a category name in NAC 449's FEE SCHEDULE and matched it to the pattern I was already looking for without reading the definition -- the same class of mistake as the Missouri false negative and the Tennessee wrong-guess citation, in a third disguise. A CATEGORY NAME IN A FEE LIST IS NOT A DEFINITION. Revised count: THREE states have a caregiver-matching intermediary category (IA, CO, OR) plus Nevada's employment-agency variant, which is adjacent but hinges on contracting with workers. TWO MORE OREGON CATEGORIES FOUND IN THE CHAPTER INDEX AND NOT READ: ORS 443.360 "Agency with choice services; license required" and ORS 443.370-443.376 "LONG TERM CARE REFERRAL -- registration; duties of referral agents; violation as unlawful practice". Oregon may have three intermediary categories, not one. ALSO FLAGGED: the four determinations are about the product AS BUILT ON 2026-08-30 and the triggers DIFFER -- Iowa's is bidding, Oregon's is a roster provided to clients for hiring, Colorado's is fee-for-referral, Nevada's is contracting with workers -- so a future marketplace feature must be re-tested against all four SEPARATELY because THEY WOULD NOT ALL FLIP TOGETHER. OREGON INTAKE FIELD LIST PROMOTED per Michael's instruction to the top of the pathways-model doc as a field specification rather than compliance boilerplate: pets AND whether they can be secured away from the care area IF THE CAREGIVER REQUESTS (two fields, not one), suspected pest infestations, and the client's willingness to SECURELY STORE WEAPONS BEFORE ANY VISIT (a client commitment captured pre-first-visit) -- plus the directional duty to push it to each ASSIGNED staff member, the hospital-discharge violence-history duty, the "household individual" scope, and 443.195's named MOBILE APPLICATION, quarterly safety assessments, pre-first-visit identity verification and the requirement to permit CHARTING OUTSIDE THE CLIENT'S HOME. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-15-oregon-count-and-mw-ne-routes.md (NEW), round-14 doc updated (Fourth) -- OREGON HAS ONE CAREGIVER-MATCHING CATEGORY, NOT THREE, AND THAT IS THE NEVADA OVER-COUNT CAUGHT IN THE ROUND IT WAS RAISED. Round 14 speculated that ORS 443.360 and 443.370-376 might be two more intermediary categories. READ: they are not, and the platform count stays at THREE states (IA, CO, OR). ORS 443.370-376 "long term care referral" is FACILITY PLACEMENT -- every operative phrase is about facilities ("refer a client to a facility", "the length of any contract the referral agent has with a FACILITY regarding placement information", "referrals only to FACILITIES with which the agent has an existing contract") -- the same business as Nevada's referral agency, not caregiver matching. ORS 443.360 "agency with choice services" is a STATE-PROCURED PROGRAMME: OHA and DHS "shall contract with UP TO TWO AGENCIES" by 2026-01-01, with staged expansion to Oregon Project Independence clients and, via the Home Care Commission, to private-pay home care worker clients after a stakeholder data analysis. Not an open licensing category anyone can enter. Like 443.190/443.195 it is law that was NEVER CODIFIED into chapter 443's series. RECORDED THE FACILITY-REFERRAL REGIME ANYWAY because it would bind any "help me find a facility" feature: 443.373 requires DHS registration, a $750 FEE renewed every two years, an identified responsible individual, AT LEAST $1M GENERAL LIABILITY INSURANCE, and BACKGROUND CHECKS ON REFERRAL AGENTS WITH DIRECT CLIENT CONTACT, with civil penalties. 443.376 requires a PRE-REFERRAL DISCLOSURE that may be oral ONLY IF AUDIO-RECORDED WITH CONSENT and followed in writing, covering the referral description INCLUDING THE LENGTH OF ANY CONTRACT THE AGENT HOLDS WITH A FACILITY, contact details, THE AGENT'S PRIVACY POLICY, whether it refers only to contracted facilities, and WHETHER THE FACILITY PAYS THE REFERRAL FEE. THE TRANSFERABLE PART IS THE PER-INSTANCE CONSENT RULE: an agent may not share or sell a client's placement information to a facility or marketing affiliate "without obtaining affirmative consent from the client FOR EACH INSTANCE of sharing or selling" -- a data-sharing constraint unlike anything else in this survey, and it would apply to any lead-routing or partner-referral feature. Also no referrals to a facility the agent or an IMMEDIATE FAMILY MEMBER owns, no contact after a written stop request, and a website must LINK TO THE STATE COMPLAINTS LISTING. MOUNTAIN WEST / NEW ENGLAND ROUTE LEDGER, bodies checked not status codes, NOTHING SUBSTANTIVE READ: Montana rules.mt.gov 200 with THIRTEEN CHARACTERS of body (SPA shell); IDAHO adminrules.idaho.gov 404 and the page itself says it is "experiencing a temporary technical issue affecting document search and listings" -- A STATE-SIDE OUTAGE, NOT A BLOCK, so it is a retry rather than a workaround; Wyoming rules.wyo.gov chrome plus a maintenance notice; New Mexico srca.nm.gov 4.4 KB of navigation; New Hampshire gencourt 404 on a guessed path; MAINE legislature.maine.gov returned a REAL statute page but for "Appointment of Receivers" -- WRONG CITATION, RIGHT HOST; Vermont legislature.vermont.gov loads the Human Services title; Rhode Island rules.sos.ri.gov 404 on a guessed organisation id. TWO WORKING HOSTS FOUND (Maine, Vermont) and one confirmed transient failure (Idaho). THE HONEST POINT: every failure there is a GUESSED PATH, which is exactly the habit five earlier states already taught me to drop -- I applied index-first to choosing the HOST but not to choosing the PATH WITHIN IT. Next step written down so it is not re-derived: fetch each host's statute/rule TABLE OF CONTENTS and read section titles rather than constructing a citation. METHOD RULE NOW EARNED: A CATEGORY IS NOT COUNTED UNTIL ITS DEFINITION HAS BEEN READ -- two over-counts in two rounds, Nevada's hardened into a published table before the definition was read, Oregon's was checked immediately. And a facility-referral category and a caregiver-matching category look IDENTICAL FROM THE INDEX; only the definition separates them. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-16-maine-vermont.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- VERMONT IS A CLOSED MARKET, THE ONLY ONE IN THE SURVEY, AND TOC-FIRST WORKED ON THE FIRST ATTEMPT IN BOTH STATES. Round 15's correction was the whole difference: Maine's chapter list named "Chapter 419: HOME HEALTH SERVICES" and Vermont's named "Chapter 063: Home Care Programs", and neither would have come from a keyword sweep or a guessed citation. VERMONT 33 V.S.A. 6304: designation "shall provide each designated agency A FRANCHISE to provide home health services within one or more geographic service areas within which it shall have THE OBLIGATION AND RESPONSIBILITY of providing home health services FOR FOUR YEARS", and "NO HOME HEALTH AGENCY SHALL RENDER HOME HEALTH SERVICES TO PATIENTS RESIDING IN A DESIGNATED SERVICE AREA WITHOUT BEING DESIGNATED" -- with initial areas written into the statute BY AGENCY NAME AND COUNTY, anchored to existing certificate-of-need holders under 18 V.S.A. ch. 221. Everywhere else licensure is a bar an applicant clears; in Vermont a designation is EXCLUSIVE TO A TERRITORY and carries an obligation to serve. Two consequences: "which agency serves this address" is a DETERMINATE STATE-ASSIGNED ANSWER in Vermont rather than a competitive one, and the addressable customer list is FINITE AND ENUMERABLE -- a commercial fact, not a compliance one. 6307 leaves a narrow gate for the Commissioner to contract with non-designated agencies for specialised services not available from the designated ones. VERMONT 6309 (added 2023 No. 115, eff. 2024-07-01) IS A CLIENT-LEVEL SAFETY FLAG THAT SURVIVES DISCHARGE AND GATES DISPATCH: where an individual was previously discharged TO PROTECT STAFF SAFETY and the behaviour cannot reasonably be mitigated, the agency may deny a subsequent admission OR DECLINE TO SEND AN EMPLOYEE TO A VISIT IF IT HAS REASON TO BELIEVE THAT INDIVIDUAL IS PRESENT IN THE HOME, need not enter the home to assess, and MUST give notice of any denial WITH THE REASON and how to complain under 6308. Note precisely: the block is not on the CLIENT, it is on sending a caregiver to a home where a particular individual is believed present -- which may be a HOUSEHOLD MEMBER, pairing exactly with Oregon's "household individual" definition and its duty to push risk info to the assigned caregiver. TWO STATES, TWO LEGISLATURES, 2024 AND 2025: caregiver safety is an emerging statutory dimension and both instances land on the ASSIGNMENT, not the client record, so dispatch logic needs a reason-coded refusal path with a notice obligation attached. VERMONT 6321 attendant care is the THIRD self-direction model after Nevada's client-as-managing-employer and Oregon's agency-with-choice: recipients "shall have the opportunity to HIRE, TRAIN, AND TERMINATE the employment of attendants, ESTABLISH WORK SCHEDULES, manage the services, and oversee PAYMENTS and RECORDKEEPING" -- so the SCHEDULING AUTHORITY IS THE CLIENT'S, which an agency-side scheduling design inverts. Three named variants: group-directed, personal services, participant-directed. And 6308 requires grievance procedures open to recipients, family members AND EMPLOYEES plus a state external process with a dedicated toll-free line -- employees as complainants is unusual here. MAINE 22 M.R.S. 2149-A: written policies prohibiting abuse, neglect or MISAPPROPRIATION OF A CLIENT'S PROPERTY, plus a comprehensive background check for "direct access personnel" as defined at 1717(1)(A-2), subject to the employment restrictions at 1812-G, when HIRING, EMPLOYING OR PLACING such personnel including CNAs and direct care workers. AXIS B BY DOUBLE CROSS-REFERENCE with neither target read, and the word "PLACING" reaches an agency that assigns without employing. Maine's chapter section list carries NO training-hour requirement; whether 2145 standards or department rules supply one is not established. RECORDED SO IT IS NOT RE-FETCHED: 22 M.R.S. ch. 1625 "Personal Care Assistance Services for Severely Physically Disabled Adults" is ENTIRELY REPEALED, all three sections. That is the second Maine chapter that looked relevant from its NAME and was not usable -- a TOC tells you a chapter exists, not that it is live, which is the fee-schedule lesson one level up. ROUTES OPEN AND RECORDED: legislature.maine.gov (TOC at /statutes/22/title22ch0sec0.html, sections at /statutes/22/title22sec<N>.html) and legislature.vermont.gov (TOC at /statutes/title/33, and /statutes/fullchapter/33/<n> RETURNS THE WHOLE CHAPTER IN ONE REQUEST -- 29K chars -- which is the efficient form). STILL OPEN: New Mexico (titles index loads, not walked), New Hampshire (gencourt index returns TWO CHARACTERS), Montana (SPA shell), Wyoming (chrome + maintenance notice), Idaho (self-declared state-side outage, retry), Rhode Island (404 on a guessed org id). 36 states touched. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-3-pathways-model.md (CONSOLIDATION INSERTED) (Fourth) -- FIVE MORE STATES RESOLVED OR EXHAUSTED, IDAHO RETRIED ONCE AND LOGGED AS A STATE PROPERTY, AND THE WHOLE SURVEY CONSOLIDATED INTO ONE SECTION AT THE TOP OF THE MODEL DOC. TOC-FIRST AGAIN, AND IT SURFACED TWO TOMBSTONES: Montana Title 50 CHAPTER 7 "HOME HEALTH AGENCIES" IS REPEALED IN ITS ENTIRETY (all three parts -- general provisions, certificates of need, licences), and New Hampshire RSA CHAPTER 126-F "ADULT IN-HOME CARE" IS REPEALED. With Maine ch.1625 (also wholly repealed) that is THREE repealed chapters found from TOC entries -- a TOC tells you a chapter EXISTS, not that it is LIVE, which is the fee-schedule lesson one level up. SUCCESSORS IDENTIFIED RATHER THAN LEFT AS DEAD ENDS: Montana now defines "home health agency" at MCA 50-5-101(21), inside Title 50 ch. 5 HOSPITALS AND RELATED FACILITIES (Part 2 Licensing) -- "a public agency or private organization ... engaged in providing home health services to individuals in the places where they live", requiring the services of A LICENSED REGISTERED NURSE AND AT LEAST ONE OTHER THERAPEUTIC SERVICE. Flagged as INFERENCE not verified: on that definition a Montana non-medical personal care agency is not a home health agency and appears unlicensed, but Montana's other titles were not searched. NH's successor (RSA 151) was not reached -- the guessed filename 404s and the TOC-X listing did not expose it. RHODE ISLAND DEFEATED BY A SOFT-200: webserver.rilin.state.ri.us returns the SAME ALL-TITLES LIST (1,676 chars) for every Statutes path tried including TITLE23/INDEX.HTM and TITLE23/23-17/INDEX.HTM -- a third soft-200 form after Alabama's "404page" body and Connecticut's "Unable to Acccess Resource". NEW MEXICO: srca.nm.gov/parts/title07/index.html returns 403 Forbidden (directory listing blocked); the NMAC titles page loads but the per-title path pattern is unknown. WYOMING: statutes title 35 IS available as a 2.9 MB PDF from wyoleg.gov (fetched, NOT read); rules.wyo.gov remains a chrome+maintenance shell. IDAHO RETRIED ONCE PER INSTRUCTION and the page still carries its own banner -- "We're experiencing a temporary technical issue affecting document search and listings" -- so it is LOGGED AS A STATE-SIDE PROPERTY and no further attempts will be spent on it. CONSOLIDATION PASS DONE per Michael's instruction: a single "CONSOLIDATED FINDINGS" section now sits at the TOP of the pathways doc, above the boxed warnings, indexing the whole survey with a citation on every claim so nothing needs a re-read of sixteen round documents. It covers: Layer 0 registration category (six states where it decides which axes apply, plus the FIVE distinct answers to the unlicensed-segment question); Layer 1 the five axes, with axis A's EIGHT written forms, the fact that only 6 of 38 states name an hour figure, axis B's three shapes and its expiry/curability cases, and axis C's three polarities plus its dated-and-contestable status; Layer 2 the eight BEHAVIOUR CONSTRAINTS in one table with polarity (Colorado and Nevada forbid, Louisiana/Oregon/Oklahoma/Kentucky compel, Vermont permits-with-notice); Layer 3 the intermediary category with all three triggers side by side and the standing note that they would NOT all flip together for a future marketplace; Layer 4 the cross-cutting patterns -- federal coupling, dated rules inside apparent constants, delegation as a shape, the three self-direction models, the emerging caregiver-safety dimension (VT 2024 + OR 2025, both landing on the ASSIGNMENT), the four-state dementia overlay, wage rules inside licensure rules, market structure (Vermont's exclusive franchises, Oklahoma's referral regulation), and scope triggers that are not service type (Arkansas's client-age test, CMS's congregate/PACE exclusions); and a COVERAGE STATEMENT that names 38 states touched, says plainly that is NOT coverage, and lists every state with no route found. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-18-wy-nm-ri.md (NEW), pathways-model coverage line updated (Fourth) -- RHODE ISLAND AND NEW MEXICO OPENED BY THE SOUTH CAROLINA METHOD, WYOMING READ, AND RI HAS THE BROADEST LICENSURE TRIGGER IN THE SURVEY. RI was logged in round 17 as defeated by a soft-200 (webserver.rilin.state.ri.us returns the same 1,676-char all-titles list for every Statutes path). That host is still useless; the REGULATIONS live on a different host under a different numbering scheme -- exactly the SC shape. rules.sos.ri.gov/regulations/part/216-40-10-<nn> returns full text, and the part is 216-RICR-40-10-17 "Licensing Home Nursing Care Providers and Home Care Providers", 81,660 chars. FOUND BY ENUMERATING PART NUMBERS 01-20 AND TREATING EVERY 7,418-BYTE RESPONSE AS THE NOT-FOUND SHELL -- the four real parts were obvious by size before any body was read. RI's definition is the widest trigger seen: a "home care provider" is any person that "PROVIDES, ARRANGES TO PROVIDE, OFFERS TO PROVIDE, or in any other way provides for the delivery" of direct health care services in the home requiring RN supervision, excluding ongoing direct nursing by an RN/LPN, and it EXPRESSLY INCLUDES HOMEMAKER SERVICES. Every other state puts matching in a SEPARATE category with its own definition; RI appears to sweep ARRANGING into the ordinary home care licence. SAIRNSENIOR'S POSITION RECORDED AS NOT FULLY DETERMINED, unlike the other four: the verb list attaches to providing for the delivery of CARE, and SAIRNsenior licenses software rather than arranging care, so on that reading it is outside -- but this is THE NARROWEST MARGIN OF THE FIVE DETERMINATIONS, it rests on ONE DEFINITION rather than a reading of the part, and s17 itself points at Part 10 "Licensing Nursing Service Agencies" WHICH HAS NOT BEEN READ AT ALL and may be RI's intermediary analogue. That is now the priority item. RI also requires a BCI check under R.I. Gen. Laws 23-17-34 with policies mandating "TIMELY JUDGMENT REGARDING CONTINUED EMPLOYMENT of an employee upon whom disqualifying information has been found" -- a duty to DECIDE, not merely to check -- plus health screening and in-service education under 17.5.3, documented proof that CONTRACTED personnel meet the same licensure/screening/background requirements, and ACCREDITATION BY A NATIONALLY RECOGNIZED ACCREDITING BODY WITHIN TWO YEARS OF INITIAL LICENSURE, which no other state in this survey mandates. WYOMING: W.S. 35-2-901(a)(x) puts HOME HEALTH AGENCY inside the licensed "health care facility" list (alongside assisted living, adult day care, adult foster care home, ALTERNATIVE ELDERCARE HOME, boarding home, hospice and others), and (a)(xi) defines it as "an agency primarily engaged in ARRANGING AND DIRECTLY PROVIDING nursing or other health care services to persons at their residence" -- "arranging" is in Wyoming's definition too, but CONJUNCTIVE where Rhode Island's list is DISJUNCTIVE, so one word produces very different scopes. Also W.S. 35-2-1201 SENIOR HEALTH CARE DISTRICTS: a special district may be established to deliver care to a person AT LEAST 60, a disabled adult 18+, or a person with medical/behavioural needs, THROUGH HOME CARE SERVICES among others -- a public-financing structure, not a licensure category, and the SECOND age-scoped trigger after Arkansas's 50+ test at a different threshold. A Wyoming customer may be a DISTRICT rather than a private agency. NEW MEXICO: route solved -- srca.nm.gov/nmac-home/nmac-titles/title-7-health/chapter-28-home-health-services/ resolves, and the earlier /parts/title07/index.html 403 was DIRECTORY ACCESS FORBIDDEN, a server config rather than a block on content. AND THE CHAPTER IS AN EMPTY PLACEHOLDER: "Chapter 28 -- 7.28.1 NMAC GENERAL PROVISIONS [RESERVED]", inside a run of reserved chapters 28-37. So NM's operative rules are elsewhere, most likely under health-facility licensing (NMSA 24-1-5), NOT LOCATED. Recorded as ROUTE SOLVED, CONTENT NOT FOUND -- a different state from "no route". FOUR KINDS OF DEAD ENTRY NOW SEEN: repealed (MT, NH, ME), renumbered (SC), reserved-and-empty (NM), and never-existed (MO, TN wrong-guess citations). Only the last is a bad citation; the other three are facts about the law worth recording. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-rhode-island-determination.md (NEW), round-18 and pathways-model docs updated (Fourth) -- RHODE ISLAND CLOSED, AND IT FORECLOSES THE IOWA MARKETPLACE MODEL BY STATUTE RATHER THAN BY LICENSING. Read 216-RICR-40-10-10 "Licensing Nursing Service Agencies" and its statutory definition. R.I. Gen. Laws 23-17.7.1-2(d): a nursing service agency is anyone "doing business within the state that SUPPLIES, ON A TEMPORARY BASIS, registered nurses, licensed practical nurses, or NURSING ASSISTANTS to a hospital, nursing home, or other facility", excluding hospitals, home nursing care providers, home care providers and hospices licensed in the state -- and 23-17.7.1-2(c) defines "nursing assistant" to include a HOME HEALTH AIDE, so the category is not nurses-only. THE HEADLINE CLAUSE: "For all purposes a nursing service agency shall be considered an EMPLOYER and those persons that it supplies on a temporary basis shall be considered EMPLOYEES AND NOT INDEPENDENT CONTRACTORS, and the nursing service agency shall be subject to all state and federal laws which govern employer-employee relations." Iowa's platform category EXISTS BECAUSE independent contractors bid on open shifts, with 135Q.1(7) conditioning the status on the worker choosing in their SOLE DISCRETION where, when and how often to work. Rhode Island says the opposite for all purposes. So the standing marketplace condition is now stronger in one state than "a trigger that might flip": in RI the workers would be STATUTORILY DEEMED EMPLOYEES of whoever supplies them, with all employer obligations attaching. That is a business-model question and belongs in the same decision-gate pass as the Iowa scope question. DETERMINATION: SAIRNsenior is OUTSIDE both RI categories -- outside Part 10 on the operative verb (it supplies software, not personnel), and outside Part 17 on firmer ground than round 18 had, because READING THE NEIGHBOUR RESOLVED THE AMBIGUITY: Part 17's "provides, arranges to provide, offers to provide" looked alarmingly broad in isolation, and Part 10 shows the scheme's actual axis is FURNISHING PERSONNEL OR CARE, with 23-17.7.1-2(d) expressly excluding licensed home care providers. A vendor whose CUSTOMER arranges the care is not an entity that arranges care. Five determinations, five states, all outside. LIMIT STATED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED: this rests on two definitions plus one general-requirements section, not on 81,660 + 28,769 characters read in full. SECOND HOLDING-OUT LIMB FOUND: 10.3(A)(1) bars an agency from holding itself out or using the term "nursing service agency" OR OTHER SIMILAR TERM in advertising, publicity or any other form of communication unless licensed -- after Oregon ORS 443.100, so two states now put a statutory edge on MARKETING COPY. Also 10.3(A)(2) requires a LICENSED CENTRAL OFFICE WITHIN THE STATE (compare Texas's ban on a virtual office); 10.3(A)(3) requires ACCREDITATION WITHIN 24 MONTHS of initial licensure, maintained as a condition -- and since Part 17 carries the same duty, accreditation is a RHODE ISLAND POLICY rather than a one-off, and RI remains the only state in the survey to mandate third-party accreditation; 10.4 licences per CENTRAL OFFICE with branches subject to the Part but not the fee, plus a NOTARISED list of direct and indirect owners with percentages; and 10.3(B) exempts agencies under 23-17.7.1-13/-14/-15 BUT KEEPS THEM SUBJECT TO THE SAME NURSING-ASSISTANT TRAINING REQUIREMENTS -- an exemption from licensure that is not an exemption from training, the same per-axis exemption shape as Oklahoma's DHS contractors. FLAGGED AS UNCHECKED, with the caution that already proved warranted once: whether ANY OTHER STATE has a comparable statutory reclassification of supplied workers has NOT been checked; this was found only because Part 10 was read for a different reason, and absence of a finding elsewhere is not evidence of absence. METHOD: when a definition looks too broad, READ THE CATEGORY NEXT TO IT before concluding. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-DECISION-GATE-INPUT-marketplace-model.md (NEW), 2026-08-30-sairnsenior-reclassification-sweep.md (NEW), RI determination widened (Fourth) -- BOTH BUSINESS-MODEL FINDINGS NOW ROUTE TO ONE DECISION-GATE PACKET, AND THE SWEEP FOUND THREE STATES ASSIGNING EMPLOYMENT STATUS BY LAW, NOT ONE. Built the decision-gate input packet per Michael's instruction so Iowa and Rhode Island land TOGETHER rather than as separate compliance items -- they are one business-model question and they answer it in OPPOSITE directions: Iowa CREATES a licensed category for a contractor marketplace and tells you what it costs (annual registration, $500, market ban for non-registration, 135Q.3(2)(a) making the platform verify each worker meets ALL applicable state requirements which across 38 states is this entire survey per worker, $1M/$3M insurance verification, dormancy-triggered background re-checks, quarterly price disclosure), while Rhode Island DENIES THE MODEL'S PREMISE by deeming supplied workers employees and not independent contractors for all purposes. A plan that satisfies Iowa can still be structurally impossible in Rhode Island, and a fifty-state product cannot fix that by registering. The packet also carries the five separate triggers (IA bidding, OR roster-given-to-clients-for-hiring, CO fee-for-referral-only, NV contracting-with-caregivers, RI supplying-personnel) with the warning that they do NOT move together, the two LIVE marketing-copy constraints that bind today (Oregon ORS 443.100 and RI 10.3(A)(1) holding-out limbs), and a recommended framing: treat it as a fifty-state business-model decision, decide the state footprint first, and if the answer is no WRITE IT DOWN WITH ITS TRIGGER so the standing conditions have something to point at. THEN RAN THE SWEEP: ten patterns over every corpus gathered tonight -- 60+ files including the 19.7 MB Iowa PDF, the 2.9 MB Wyoming PDF, both Louisiana .docx volumes and the 787-page South Carolina chapter. 17 hits, 14 real, 3 discarded (Iowa's agricultural-labour joint-employer test under 29 CFR 516.33 and Wyoming's county joint-community-board provision -- both unrelated chapters). THREE STATES, THREE SHAPES. (1) RHODE ISLAND IS BROADER THAN I RECORDED: the identical clause sits in 216-RICR-40-10-17 -- the HOME CARE regulation -- applying to "a home nursing care provider OR HOME CARE PROVIDER", so it is not confined to temporary-staffing businesses and an ordinary RI home care agency also cannot treat supplied caregivers as contractors. The determination doc has been corrected in place rather than left as first written. (2) NEVADA: an intermediary service organization SHALL SERVE AS THE EMPLOYER OF RECORD for each personal assistant, with personnel-file duties -- in the same self-directed branch where the CLIENT is managing employer and trainer, so employer-of-record and who-directs-the-work are SPLIT. (3) OREGON ORS 443.360(1)(g): the agency "functions as the COMMON LAW EMPLOYER of direct support workers RECRUITED BY THE INDIVIDUAL", and "the individual directs the direct support workers and IS CONSIDERED A CO-EMPLOYER with the agency" -- a three-party employment structure in statute where neither party alone is the employer. THREE CONSEQUENCES WRITTEN INTO THE MODEL: employment_type is NOT A FREE FIELD because the state supplies the answer and can contradict the contract; "employer" and "who directs the work" are SEPARABLE and two states separate them explicitly; and a contractor marketplace has no lawful shape at all in Rhode Island. LIMIT STATED PLAINLY: the sweep covers TEXT ALREADY ON DISK -- the chapters read tonight, not full state codes and not the ~12 untouched states -- so a state with no hit may still have such a clause in a chapter never fetched, the same caution that proved warranted when Iowa's platform category turned out to have three siblings. METHOD WORTH KEEPING: Rhode Island's Part 17 clause was in a file ALREADY ON DISK, in a part ALREADY READ, and was missed because I was reading for SCOPE and not for EMPLOYMENT STATUS -- once a pattern has two confirmed instances, sweep the corpus you already have before fetching anything new, because it is nearly free and it found two states the reading had walked past. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-19-nebraska.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- NEBRASKA REQUIRES FIVE REGISTRY CHECKS IN ONE SUBSECTION, AND THE RECLASSIFICATION CHECK NOW RUNS PER STATE AS IT IS READ. Nebraska has a DEDICATED In-Home Personal Services Act for the non-medical segment (Neb. Rev. Stat. 71-6501 to 71-6504) plus home health aide requirements at 71-6601 to 71-6608.02 -- a SIXTH answer to the unlicensed-segment question, since it neither leaves it alone (LA), registers it (FL), makes it disclose (AZ), sets an hour floor (AR), nor licenses it as a facility (NV/OH/CO): it sets STATUTORY QUALIFICATIONS FOR THE WORKER and DUTIES ON THE AGENCY directly in the code. 71-6502 requires a worker to be 18+, of good moral character, not convicted of a crime carrying MORE THAN ONE YEAR's imprisonment AND RATIONALLY RELATED TO FITNESS for the work -- a THIRD axis-B shape, neither an enumerated list nor pure agency discretion but a statutory two-part test -- and to have NO ADVERSE FINDINGS ON FIVE REGISTRIES: Adult Protective Services Central Registry, the 28-718 child abuse registry, the MEDICATION AIDE Registry, the NURSE AIDE Registry, and the STATE PATROL SEX OFFENDER registry. Five separate lookups against five authorities, each needing its own date and outcome -- a registry_checked boolean is hopeless. THE LANGUAGE REQUIREMENT IS CLIENT-MATCHED: able to speak and understand English OR THE LANGUAGE OF THE PERSON FOR WHOM HE OR SHE IS PROVIDING SERVICES, so language is a PER-ASSIGNMENT qualification rather than a worker attribute (and 71-6603 goes further for home health aides -- the language must be understood by the PATIENT AND THE SUPERVISING STAFF MEMBER). Axis A is delegated in one clause -- "training sufficient to provide the requisite level of in-home personal services offered", no hours, no topics, no approved-programme list -- so Nebraska joins South Carolina in delegating axis A while specifying B and C precisely. 71-6503 requires the agency to EMPLOY OR CONTRACT WITH ONLY persons meeting 71-6502, to run a criminal history check AND A DRIVING RECORD CHECK with the DMV OR ANY OTHER STATE THAT ISSUED AN OPERATOR'S LICENCE, WHEN DRIVING IS A SERVICE PROVIDED, and to keep documentation for inspection at its place of business -- a background check CONDITIONED ON THE SERVICE, the same per-assignment shape as Nevada's transport insurance but on a check rather than a document, and it reaches out-of-state licences that a single-state DMV lookup would miss. RECLASSIFICATION CHECK RUN AT READ TIME PER MICHAEL'S INSTRUCTION AND NEBRASKA IS CLEAN: ten patterns against 71-6502, 71-6503, 71-6603 and the full chapter 71 index, ZERO hits, and its "employ or contract with" language confirms contractors are contemplated -- the direct contrast with Rhode Island, where the same activity makes the agency the employer for all purposes. That check cost one command and is now standing practice rather than a retrospective sweep. 71-6603 gives home health aides SIX qualification routes including TWO GRANDFATHER CLAUSES ANCHORED TO 1991-09-06 (home health aide I and II), still operative -- the same dated-rule shape as Ohio's pre/post-2008 split and Louisiana's 2018 administrator test. ROUTE LEDGER FOR THE SIX STILL CLOSED, AND THEY ARE NOT ALIKE: Delaware, Hawaii and West Virginia all returned 200 and the failure is MY PARSER OR MY PATH, not the publisher (DE's chapter list parsed poorly, HI is an IIS directory listing my href extractor did not read, WV returned whole-code navigation instead of article 5D); NORTH DAKOTA returned 300 MULTIPLE CHOICES with the server itself saying the document name could not be found -- a wrong filename, flagged by the server; ALASKA returned "This page is no longer used please use ..." -- a SELF-DECLARED MOVE, the South Carolina shape, so follow the pointer; and SOUTH DAKOTA is a genuine SPA at 288 characters. Only the SPA is a property of the publisher. NOT READ AND FLAGGED: 71-6608.01 IS WHERE ANY NEBRASKA HOUR FIGURE WOULD LIVE, plus 71-6501, 71-6504 (applicability), 71-6608.02, 71-6039, 71-417, and Title 175 NAC -- and whether Nebraska LICENSES in-home personal services agencies at all is NOT ESTABLISHED, since 71-6502/6503 impose duties without, in the text read, a licensure requirement. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-20-nd-and-nebraska-closed.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- NORTH DAKOTA OPENED, NEBRASKA'S TWO FLAGS BOTH CLOSED, AND PER-STATE DIAGNOSIS PAID OFF ON THE FIRST TRY. NEBRASKA 71-6608.01 SUPPLIES THE HOUR FIGURE I FLAGGED: a home health aide training course must total AT LEAST 75 HOURS of classroom plus supervised practical, "with at least sixteen hours devoted to supervised practical training AFTER the individual being trained has completed at least sixteen hours of classroom training". Seventh hour-denominated state, and THE FIRST WITH AN EXPLICIT SEQUENCING RULE -- Arkansas NESTS (40 containing 16 containing 4), Nebraska ORDERS, and a model holding {total:75, practical:16} still cannot express "the practical hours must come after sixteen classroom hours". Topics include reading and recording temperature/pulse/respiration and changes in body functioning that must be reported to the supervisor. THE LICENSURE QUESTION ANSWERS ITSELF IN 71-6504: the whole scheme is 71-6501 to 71-6504, FOUR SECTIONS, AND NONE IS A LICENSURE REQUIREMENT -- Nebraska imposes statutory worker qualifications and agency duties WITHOUT LICENSING THE AGENCY, on the text read. Stated as a reading of those four sections; whether the term appears among licensed facility types under 71-401 et seq. was NOT separately verified, which is a different claim and is flagged as such. 71-6501(6) defines the agency as one that "PROVIDES OR OFFERS TO PROVIDE" in-home personal services for compensation "by employees of the agency OR BY PERSONS WITH WHOM THE AGENCY HAS CONTRACTED" -- so Nebraska uses the same "offers" language that made Rhode Island's trigger the widest in the survey BUT THE CONSEQUENCE IS COMPLETELY DIFFERENT: in RI it pulls you into a licence, in Nebraska into worker-qualification duties with no licence attached. Contractors are expressly contemplated in the definition itself, and the reclassification check is clean again. AND 71-6504'S CARVE-OUT IS A PAYER/PROGRAMME CARVE-OUT: 71-6501 to 71-6503 do not apply to designated care aides under 38-2219 or to persons providing PERSONAL ASSISTANT SERVICES, RESPITE CARE OR HABILITATION SERVICES, OR AGED AND DISABLED SERVICES -- the Medicaid-waiver family is entirely outside, so a Nebraska agency's obligations differ BY PROGRAMME within one workforce, the same per-client-by-payer shape as Arkansas's DHS exemption. NORTH DAKOTA OPENED AND MY DIAGNOSIS WAS HALF RIGHT: the 300 Multiple Choices was CONTENT NEGOTIATION, not merely a wrong filename -- the same chapter exists as .pdf and t23c17-5.pdf returned instantly. AND THE CHAPTER WAS THE WRONG ONE ANYWAY: 23-17.5 is "HEALTH CARE PROVIDER COOPERATIVE AGREEMENTS [Repealed by S.L. 2013]", the FIFTH repealed chapter in this survey. The right one came from the title index as it has in every state that worked: NDCC ch. 23-17.3 HOME HEALTH AGENCY LICENSURE, defining a home health agency as one providing home health services "to individuals and families WHERE THEY ARE PRESENTLY RESIDING", with a clinical record that must cover services provided directly AND THROUGH ARRANGEMENTS WITH ANOTHER AGENCY. Reclassification check clean. TWO LESSONS COMPOUNDED: a 300 can be content negotiation rather than a dead end, AND OPENING THE ROUTE IS NOT FINDING THE CHAPTER -- the route worked on the first retry and still delivered a repealed chapter about the wrong subject, so the title index was still necessary, five states in a row now. REMAINING FIVE RE-DIAGNOSED RATHER THAN BATCHED: Delaware's parser is FIXED (143 chapters extracted) but NO home-health or home-care chapter is among them -- nearest are ch.10 Hospitals, ch.11 Long-Term Care Facilities and Services, ch.30A Nurse Assistant training -- so route works, chapter unidentified; Hawaii's parser is FIXED (the IIS listing uses uppercase <A HREF= and yields per-section HRS_0321-NNNN.htm files) but the relevant sections are unidentified; West Virginia's path is STILL WRONG, both /16-5D/ and /16-5D-1/ returning whole-code navigation; Alaska's self-declared move POINTER WAS NOT FOLLOWED; South Dakota's guessed API path returned the same 2,256-byte SPA shell, so that is not the endpoint either. THE PER-STATE DIAGNOSIS PAID OFF IMMEDIATELY -- the North Dakota fix was A FILE EXTENSION, and had it been batched with South Dakota's SPA it would have looked like the same intractable problem. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-state-round-21-de-wv-ak.md (NEW), reclassification-sweep doc widened (Fourth) -- DELAWARE EXPRESSLY PERMITS WHAT RHODE ISLAND FORBIDS, AND MY OWN SWEEP PATTERN SET WAS ONE-SIDED. 16 Del. C. 122(3)(o)(2)(A): "Home health agency services are provided directly through employees of the agency OR THROUGH CONTRACT ARRANGEMENTS, INCLUDING THOSE CONTRACTS WITH INDIVIDUALS CONSIDERED TO BE INDEPENDENT CONTRACTORS." That is the exact inverse of RI's "employees and not independent contractors, for all purposes", and it makes the reclassification dimension THREE-WAY rather than binary: DEEMS EMPLOYEES (Rhode Island, in both its staffing part and its home-care part), ASSIGNS EMPLOYER ROLES (Nevada employer-of-record, Oregon common-law-employer plus statutory co-employer), EXPRESSLY PERMITS CONTRACTORS (Delaware), and SILENT (everyone else). Two consequences written into the sweep doc: SILENCE IS NOT A POSITION, since Delaware and Rhode Island both address the question and answer it oppositely; and MY SWEEP'S TEN PATTERNS WERE ONE-SIDED -- they look for statutes that DEEM workers employees and would have missed Delaware entirely, which was found by READING rather than sweeping, so the affirmative forms need adding to the pattern list before it runs against any new corpus. DELAWARE ALSO CORRECTS A HYPOTHESIS I NEARLY PUBLISHED: round 20 noted 143 title-16 chapters with no home-care chapter among them and flagged it as possibly a real finding. IT WAS NOT -- Delaware regulates home health inside 16 Del. C. 122(3)(o), the DEPARTMENT-POWERS section. Had I published "Delaware does not regulate home care" it would have been a confident wrong finding built on a correct chapter list. A MISSING CHAPTER IS A HYPOTHESIS, NOT A FINDING, same discipline that kept Michigan's negative in Tier 2 until the statute was read. And Delaware's definition reaches the purely non-medical business: a home health agency must provide two or more services one of which is licensed nursing or home health aide services, OR HOME HEALTH AIDE SERVICES EXCLUSIVELY -- feeding, bathing, dressing, grooming, incidental household services -- so Delaware is a SEVENTH answer to the unlicensed-segment question, putting the non-medical business INSIDE the home health definition rather than creating a separate category. WEST VIRGINIA OPENED, AND THE PATH WAS WRONG TWICE OVER IN DIFFERENT WAYS: my first path was wrong, and the corrected one landed on art. 16-5D whose 16-5D-1 reads "Purpose. [Repealed.]" -- a SIXTH repealed chapter/article in this survey. The right one came from the chapter index (sixth state in a row): ARTICLE 2C HOME HEALTH SERVICES, whose 16-2C-1 defines home health services as those furnished to an individual WHO IS UNDER THE CARE OF A PHYSICIAN, on a visiting basis in a place of residence used as the home -- part-time or intermittent nursing under RN supervision, PT/OT/speech, medical social services under physician direction, and part-time or intermittent home health aide services. A PHYSICIAN-CARE PREDICATE, narrower than Delaware's which needs no physician at all. CAUTION RECORDED RATHER THAN GLOSSED: 16-2C-2 is "Department to provide services; charges for services; authority to employ personnel", so article 2C reads as PROGRAMME AUTHORISATION rather than licensure, and whether WV licenses home health agencies at all is NOT ESTABLISHED -- art. 5D would have been the obvious candidate and is repealed. Chapter 16 also carries arts. 5I hospice licensure, 5N residential care communities, 5R Alzheimer's special care standards, 5X CARE Act and 5AA medication administration by unlicensed personnel, all unread. ALASKA: the route WORKS despite its own banner -- the page says "This page is no longer used please use www.akleg.gov" WHILE ITSELF BEING ON www.akleg.gov and rendering the Alaska Administrative Code title list. A STALE BANNER IS NOT A REDIRECT. Title 7 not yet walked. HAWAII: parser fixed, per-section files confirmed at HRS_0321-NNNN.htm, sections not yet identified. SOUTH DAKOTA: still closed but with SEVEN endpoint forms now excluded (including with Accept: application/json) and the companion host mylrc.sdlegislature.gov identified from the shell's own dns-prefetch hints -- it returns 404 rather than the shell, which is a different answer and suggests right host, wrong path. EXCLUDING ENDPOINTS IS PROGRESS. Reclassification check run at read time on DE, WV and ND: all clean for deeming, with Delaware's affirmative clause recorded as a separate finding rather than a null result. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-round-22-sweep-v2-and-wv-licensure.md (NEW), tools/reclassification_sweep.py (NEW) (Fourth) -- SWEEP MADE BIDIRECTIONAL AND MY NEW PATTERNS WERE TOO LOOSE; WEST VIRGINIA SETTLED; AND THREE OF THE FIVE STATES I WAS ASKED TO DO WERE ALREADY DONE. Sweep v2 adds a PERMIT group and reports polarity per hit. Tally across every corpus on disk: DEEM 7, ROLE 9, PERMIT 42 -- AND THE PERMIT COUNT IS MISLEADING, WHICH IS MY ERROR NOT THE CORPUS'S. Two of the new patterns ("employ or contract with", "or through contract arrangements") match NEUTRAL phrasing that contemplates contractors without taking any position on status; they fired in CO, LA, SC, IA, MI, NV and OR, none of which is a Delaware-style permission. The signal that actually works is the LITERAL PHRASE "independent contractor" READ IN CONTEXT. A broad pattern producing 42 hits and one real finding is not a sweep, it is noise with a true answer inside it -- recorded so v3 NARROWS rather than widens. PRECISION BEAT RECALL AND MY FIRST INSTINCT WAS THE WRONG ONE. The narrow re-run gives a FOURTH POLARITY that is the most common and the least noticed: DUTY-EXTENDING states, where the obligation follows the person REGARDLESS OF STATUS -- Nevada NRS 449.0113 names "employee or independent contractor" in the background-check machinery, Michigan's 20173a bar reaches "employ, INDEPENDENTLY CONTRACT WITH, or grant clinical privileges to", and South Carolina's TB rule defines "employee" to include a person "whether a direct employee or an independent contractor, and whether full-time, part-time, temporary or in any other capacity". So A STATE CAN BE ENTIRELY SILENT ON EMPLOYMENT STATUS WHILE MAKING EVERY DUTY STATUS-BLIND, which for a product is arguably the more important pattern: the compliance record must exist for contractors too even where the state never says who the employer is. Full polarity set is now FORBIDS (RI), PERMITS (DE), PREMISES A CATEGORY ON IT (IA), EXTENDS DUTIES TO THEM (NV/MI/SC). Two hits discarded explicitly so nobody re-reads them as findings: Louisiana s105 is a conflict-of-interest rule about immediate family, Colorado permits training to be PRODUCED BY an independent contractor -- a training vendor, not a caregiver. Sweep promoted to tools/reclassification_sweep.py. WEST VIRGINIA SETTLED: art. 16-2C has FIVE sections and none licenses anyone -- definitions, DEPARTMENT TO PROVIDE SERVICES, LOCAL BOARDS' AUTHORITY, funds received, collection of fees. It is a PUBLIC-PROVISION article authorising the State and local boards to PROVIDE home health services and charge for them. And of chapter 16's 156 ARTICLES, the licensure-titled ones are 5H chronic pain clinics, 5I HOSPICE, 5O and 5AA medication administration, 5Y medication-assisted treatment, 34 radon, 60 EMS compact and 68 alcohol and drug counsellors -- HOSPICE IS LICENSED BY STATUTE, HOME HEALTH IS NOT, and art. 5D the obvious candidate is repealed. STATED ONE LEVEL SHORT OF THE CONCLUSION: what is established is that no home health agency licensure ARTICLE exists in ch. 16; whether WV licenses by LEGISLATIVE RULE under the Secretary's 16-1-4 authority was NOT checked, and WV may rely on Medicare certification as Michigan does, so this must not be read as "West Virginia does not license home health". CORRECTED THE STATE LIST RATHER THAN SILENTLY RE-DOING WORK: I was asked to continue with Michigan, Alabama, Kentucky, Mississippi and Arkansas. MICHIGAN IS DONE (round 11 -- MCL 333.20106(1) omits home health from the licensed list while 20173a makes a Medicare-certified agency a covered facility under a 15-year bar), KENTUCKY IS DONE (round 9 -- 902 KAR 20:081, 6+3 dementia hours pre-service, 14/60-day supervisory cadences), ARKANSAS IS DONE (round 10 -- 20 CAR pt. 45, 40 hours nesting 16 demonstrated and 4 dementia, employer self-certification, client-age 50+ trigger), and ALABAMA AND MISSISSIPPI ARE THE TWO LEXISNEXIS-GATED STATES with no route, recorded in round 11. Genuinely untouched or open-but-unread: Hawaii (route open, sections unidentified), South Dakota (SPA, seven endpoint forms excluded, mylrc host identified), Alaska (route confirmed working, Title 7 not walked). CHECK THE COMPLETED LIST BEFORE STARTING -- saying so is cheaper than doing them twice. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-round-23-hawaii-alaska-south-dakota.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- HAWAII OPENED AND EXEMPTS THE INDIVIDUAL PROVIDER WHILE CLOSING THE LOOPHOLE IN THE SAME SENTENCE; ALASKA AND SOUTH DAKOTA DIAGNOSED AS A FIFTH BLOCKING SHAPE RATHER THAN LEFT AS "BLOCKED". HAWAII HRS 321-14.8 found by TOC-first for the SEVENTH CONSECUTIVE STATE -- the chapter 321 table of contents names it, and no keyword sweep or guessed citation would have produced it. Since 2010-07-01 EACH HOME CARE AGENCY SHALL BE LICENSED BY THE DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH, with rules adopted under chs. 91 and 201M. The definition carries an INDIVIDUAL-PROVIDER CARVE-OUT that closes its own loophole: "home care agency" does not apply to "an individual, INCLUDING AN INDIVIDUAL WHO IS INCORPORATED AS A BUSINESS, or is an unpaid or stipended volunteer" -- so a solo caregiver is outside the licence EVEN IF INCORPORATED, and an agency of two is not. Reading only the first clause would have produced the wrong COMMERCIAL conclusion: in Hawaii the single-operator caregiver is a lawful unlicensed market segment. Home care services are defined as personal care (dressing, feeding, hygiene), homemaker assistance (housekeeping, shopping, meal planning and preparation), and RESPITE CARE AND SUPPORT PROVIDED TO THE FAMILY. That makes Hawaii an EIGHTH answer to the unlicensed-segment question -- it licenses the non-medical home care agency DIRECTLY with no nursing predicate at all. TWO LIMITS SIT INSIDE THE LICENSING SECTION ITSELF: (c) is a THIRD PAYER-BASED CARVE-OUT after Arkansas's DHS exemption and Nebraska's 71-6504 -- exempt when under contract with the City and County of Honolulu Elderly Affairs Division, or serving EXCLUSIVELY Medicaid 1915(c) waiver participants through a Med-QUEST-approved agency, and the word EXCLUSIVELY means a mixed book of business forfeits it, so a Hawaii agency can be exempt for one client and licensed for another; and (d) is a SCOPE-OF-PRACTICE BOUNDARY inside the licence -- a home care agency shall not provide services authorised by ch. 457 unless provided by an RN, LPN or APRN. Contractor/reclassification check run at read time: ZERO hits, Hawaii is silent, and per round 22 silence is not a position. Amendment history is active (L Sp 2009 c 21; am 2014 c 125; 2018 c 148; 2019 c 91 and c 158 -- five touches in a decade), so the operative HAR rules are worth checking for currency when read. ALASKA AND SOUTH DAKOTA ARE THE SAME FAILURE REACHED TWO DIFFERENT WAYS AND IT IS WORTH NAMING: "INDEX SERVER-SIDE, CONTENT CLIENT-SIDE", a FIFTH distinct blocking shape alongside header-blocking (mass.gov, medicaid.gov), soft-200 error bodies (Alabama, Connecticut, the RI statute host), bot walls (Oregon OARD, Arizona) and JS-gated commercial publishers (Alabama and Mississippi via LexisNexis). Alaska's akleg.gov renders the Administrative Code and Statutes TITLE LISTS server-side but IGNORES ?title=7 and ?title=47, returning the same list; touchngo.com DNS-fails and folioproxy.asp 500s; health.alaska.gov is programme information, not code. South Dakota now has ELEVEN endpoint forms excluded, all returning the identical 2,256-byte Vue shell including with Accept: application/json, while the companion host mylrc.sdlegislature.gov returns 404 rather than the shell -- a different answer, still suggesting right host wrong path. IT LOOKS LIKE SUCCESS AT THE FIRST FETCH, which is exactly why the shape needs a name. Both are browser-solvable in principle but the Arizona experience is why that is not automatically the answer, and neither is worth that cost while the substantive findings are this dense -- recorded as a KNOWN-SHAPE, KNOWN-COST DECISION rather than an open task. NOT READ: the Hawaii Administrative Rules under 321-14.8 (HAR title 11) where the training, screening and supervision content actually lives, plus HRS 321-13.5 (certified nurse aides; abuse and neglect investigations), 321-1.9 (inspections) and the ch. 457 nursing boundary. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-round-24-hawaii-rules-and-coverage-close.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- HAWAII'S RULES ARE A THIRD INSTANCE OF THE FIFTH BLOCKING SHAPE, AND EVERY STATE NOW HAS A STATUS. HRS 321-14.8 is ENABLING ONLY -- it says DOH "shall adopt rules to provide for the licensure of home care agencies" -- so the training, screening and supervision content lives in the rules, which is why round 23 made them the priority. FIVE ROUTES TRIED AND ALL DIAGNOSED: health.hawaii.gov/opppd/rules/ 404 but the 404 PAGE IS BRANDED "Office of Planning Policy and Program Development"; health.hawaii.gov/ohca/home-care-agencies/ 404 but BRANDED "Office of Health Care Assurance", WHICH CONFIRMS OHCA IS THE LICENSING OFFICE; health.hawaii.gov/ohca/ 200 with its "Rules & Regulations" link pointing to /opppd/administrative-rules/; that page 200 with 103 links and EXACTLY ONE PDF (an unrelated practice-and-procedure document) while loading jquery-vertical-accordion-menu, SO THE CHAPTER LIST IS RENDERED CLIENT-SIDE; the WordPress REST endpoint exposed in the page's own link tags returns 212 BYTES; lrb.hawaii.gov/admin-rules-directory/ is 2,280 chars of navigation; and a guessed 11-800.pdf path 404s. THIRD INSTANCE OF "INDEX SERVER-SIDE, CONTENT CLIENT-SIDE" after Alaska and South Dakota, and THE FIRST WHERE IT IS A JQUERY ACCORDION ON WORDPRESS rather than a Vue SPA -- THE SHAPE IS NOT ABOUT THE FRAMEWORK, the tell is the same each time: a real useful index arrives and the level beneath it does not. Naming it in round 23 made this one identifiable in a single pass. WHAT WAS GAINED ANYWAY: the responsible office is confirmed as OHCA and the rules index confirmed to live under OPPPD's administrative-rules page, so a future pass starts from a known office and a known page rather than from the statute. A BRANDED 404 IS EVIDENCE -- the error pages identified the right agencies even though the paths were wrong. HAWAII'S POSITION RECORDED PRECISELY: licensure CONFIRMED (every home care agency licensed since 2010, individual-provider carve-out, payer-based exemptions, ch. 457 scope boundary) but NO training hours, NO screening standard and NO supervision cadence established, and none should be inferred. EVERY STATE NOW HAS A STATUS, which is a milestone about PROCESS not knowledge: 42 states read on at least one axis (AZ AR CA CO CT DE FL GA HI IA IL KY LA MA MD ME MI MN MO MT NC ND NE NH NJ NM NV NY OH OK OR PA RI SC TN TX VA VT WA WI WV WY), 8 with diagnosed route failures (AL and MS LexisNexis-gated, UT and KS publisher SPA or chrome-only index, CT soft-200 error body, AK and SD index-server-side-content-client-side, ID state-declared outage), and 1 on hold (IN, which is a CREDENTIAL DECISION not an obstacle). THE CAVEAT STATED IN THE SAME BREATH: no state has been read exhaustively, every round carries its own Tier 2 list, and "read on at least one axis" ranges from Colorado (licensure chapter, both service categories, placement agencies, background and registry rules) to Connecticut (ONE overtime definition) -- so anyone using this survey must read the per-state document, NOT THE COUNT. Thin-by-design entries are labelled in their own rounds: CT overtime-only, NH and MT chapter-repealed-successor-identified, NM route-open-chapter-RESERVED-and-empty, HI statute-read-rules-gated. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-round-25-browser-pass-on-the-eight.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- ELEVEN SOUTH DAKOTA ENDPOINT FORMS WERE ALL AIMED AT THE WRONG CHAPTER, AND THE BROWSER IS NOT A MASTER KEY. Depth pass on the eight diagnosed states. SOUTH DAKOTA MOVED MATERIALLY: get_page_text in Chrome renders the Vue SPA fully -- /Statutes/34 (the whole Title 34 chapter list) and /Statutes/34-12 both came back at ~50 KB. AND THE CHAPTER I HAD BEEN PROBING FOR ELEVEN ROUNDS WAS THE WRONG ONE. SDCL 34-12 licenses INSTITUTIONS and its own list runs to hospitals, birth centres, ambulatory surgery centres, chemical dependency treatment facilities, nursing facilities, assisted living centres, rural primary care hospitals, adult foster care homes, inpatient hospice, residential hospice and freestanding emergency care facilities -- HOME HEALTH IS NOT AMONG THEM. Title 34's chapter list gives the right answer: ch. 34-03A "HOME HEALTH SERVICES", sitting between 03 County and District Full-Time Health Departments and 03B County and Municipal Drug Education Programs. SO ELEVEN ENDPOINT FORMS EXCLUDED ACROSS ROUNDS 21-23 WERE EVERY ONE OF THEM FOR A CHAPTER THAT DOES NOT REGULATE HOME HEALTH -- the route and the citation were both wrong and only the route was being debugged. Same compound failure as North Dakota, where the .pdf extension fixed the route and delivered a repealed chapter about cooperative agreements. CONFIRM THE CITATION FROM THE INDEX BEFORE INVESTING IN THE TRANSPORT; ND taught it once, SD is the second and more expensive instance. Ch. 34-03A itself will NOT render -- /Statutes/34-03A and /Statutes/34-3A both hang, document_idle never fires and a screenshot times out, across four attempts with waits up to eight seconds, WHILE Title 34 and ch. 34-12 render fine on the same host in the same session, so it is specific to that page. Net: route proven, correct citation identified, content still unread -- three steps forward from "SPA, blocked". ALASKA IS NOW DEFINITIVELY CLOSED ON THE BROWSER ANGLE TOO: aac.asp loaded in Chrome and never reached document_idle, find timed out at 45 s. Same failure mode as Arizona's PDF viewer and SD's own 34-03A -- THREE STATES WHERE CHROME RENDERS SOMETHING AND THE AUTOMATION CANNOT READ IT. "Use the browser" is a fresh angle, NOT A MASTER KEY, worth recording plainly because the temptation after South Dakota's success is to assume it generalises. Three of four attempts failed. THE OTHER SIX GOT ONE FRESH NON-REPEATING ANGLE EACH: Utah's own SPA API path (adminrules.utah.gov/public/api/rule/R432-700) returns 200 and the SAME 2,224-byte shell -- the API path serves the app, not data; Kansas's direct year/title PDF path returns 200 but text/html at 4,990 bytes; Connecticut's DPH portal.ct.gov media PDF path returns 200 but text/html at 7,417 bytes -- SO KANSAS AND CONNECTICUT ARE BOTH THE SOFT-200 SHAPE AGAIN, now seen in five places; Alabama's API path returns a REAL 404 at 136 bytes, which is progress of the same kind as SD's mylrc 404 -- a different answer means the host is real and the path is wrong; Mississippi's second admin-search entry point 404s; Idaho deliberately not retried, closed as a state-declared outage. A 404 IS BETTER NEWS THAN A 200: Alabama's and South Dakota's 404s both mean real host, wrong path, while the 200s -- Utah's shell, Kansas's and Connecticut's HTML-for-PDF -- say nothing at all. NET ACROSS THE EIGHT: one moved materially, one definitively closed off, six advanced by elimination. SD reclassification check NOT RUN because there is no chapter text yet. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-30-sairnsenior-round-26-stale-tab-was-the-blocker.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- ROUND 25'S "BROWSER IS NOT A MASTER KEY" CONCLUSION WAS WRONG: THE BLOCKER WAS A STALE TAB, NOT THREE HOSTILE SITES. South Dakota's /Statutes/34-03A hung across four attempts with waits to eight seconds while /Statutes/34 and /Statutes/34-12 had rendered fine IN THE SAME TAB minutes earlier -- document_idle never fired and screenshots timed out. THE TELL: when I created a new tab, the OLD tab's title had already updated to "Codified Law 34-3A-1 | South Dakota Legislature" -- THE PAGE HAD LOADED ALL ALONG and only the automation's readiness check was stuck. A FRESH TAB LOADED THE SAME URL AND RETURNED FULL TEXT IN FOUR SECONDS, and the same fix opened Alaska on the first attempt. After roughly a dozen navigations a tab stops reaching document_idle on these sites and never recovers WHILE CONTINUING TO LOAD PAGES NORMALLY, so every symptom I attributed to three separate publishers was ONE CLIENT-SIDE DEFECT. Corrections recorded rather than left standing: SD 34-03A "specific to that chapter page" WRONG; Alaska "confirmed the browser does not resolve it either" WRONG; Arizona's canvas PDF with no text layer is GENUINELY different and remains unresolved. THE HONEST NUMBER IS ONE GENUINE BROWSER FAILURE OUT OF FOUR, NOT THREE OF FOUR -- round 25's figure was arithmetic on a bad premise and I corrected it rather than leaving the more cautious-sounding claim standing because it flattered the write-up. NEW STANDING RULE: when document_idle times out, CREATE A FRESH TAB BEFORE CONCLUDING ANYTHING ABOUT THE SITE. And the tab's own title was the evidence -- it had updated to the page I thought had failed, and I read past it for four attempts. SOUTH DAKOTA 34-3A NOW READ IN FULL AND IT IS NOT LICENSURE: six sections authorising counties and municipalities to establish a HOME HEALTH AGENCY SERVICES SPECIAL REVENUE FUND (SL 1968 ch 26), receipt fees for services provided in patients' homes by public health nurses, home health aides, physical therapists and other specialised personnel WHO ARE EMPLOYEES OF COUNTIES AND MUNICIPALITIES, receive payment from patients, Social Security home health benefits, DSS, insurers and other agencies, spend from the fund on salaries and expenses, contract for health services with public or private agencies, and appropriate from the general fund. A COUNTY AND MUNICIPAL FINANCE CHAPTER. That is the THIRD chapter whose NAME promised licensure and delivered something else, after WV art. 2C (public provision) and ND 23-17.5 (repealed cooperative agreements) -- "Home Health Services" as a chapter title means nothing until the sections are read. Combined with round 25's finding that SDCL 34-12's institution list stops at hospitals, nursing facilities, assisted living, hospice and adult foster care, NO HOME HEALTH AGENCY LICENSURE HAS BEEN FOUND IN SOUTH DAKOTA'S STATUTES AT ALL -- but ARSD was not checked and this must NOT be read as "South Dakota does not license home health". Reclassification check: no relevant language. ALASKA TITLE 7 OPENS -- Health and Social Services, beginning at Part 1 ch. 05 Vital Records, with ch. 07 Certificate of Need, ch. 09 Design and Construction of Health Facilities and ch. 10 LICENSING, CERTIFICATION AND APPROVALS (which also carries barrier crimes and background checks, Alaska's centralised-licensing machinery under AS 47.32) identified. No home health or home care provisions in the portion loaded, and two possibilities are left open rather than resolved: the relevant chapter may sit further down a very long single-document title (the extraction caps around 50 KB and returned only ch. 05's text), or Alaska may handle it through 7 AAC 10's centralised licensing rather than a dedicated chapter. REOPEN THE CLOSED ONES: Alabama, Mississippi, Utah, Kansas and Connecticut were all judged partly on browser behaviour, so that judgement now needs redoing in a fresh tab before any of them is re-diagnosed. 2026-08-30
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-27-eight-blocked-states-seven-opened.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- SEVEN OF THE EIGHT "BLOCKED" STATES OPENED AND NONE OF THEM WERE EVER BLOCKED. The round-26 stale-tab correction was the small version of this; the large version is that every blocked-state diagnosis in rounds 21-26 was produced by ONE TOOL and then written down as a property of the PUBLISHER. Utah, Kansas, Connecticut, Alabama and Mississippi -- the five reopened on the stale-tab premise -- ALL opened in Chrome; "LexisNexis-gated" was written twice about sites that were never gated, and Kansas's soft-200 was sos.ks.gov REDIRECTING to the new rules.ks.gov host. Alaska's route was in the site's own /scripts/statutes.js (aac.asp?media=print&secStart=&secEnd=), which also defeats the browser's ~50KB extraction cap. ARIZONA, called "genuinely blocked -- canvas PDF, no text layer" as recently as round 26, has a PERFECT text layer: a browser User-Agent turned 403 into 200, and pypdf read all 343 pages of 9 A.A.C. 10. Six rounds of "blocked", two commands to fix. IDAHO is the only genuine failure of the eight (adminrules.idaho.gov still 404s).
