SUBSTANTIVE PAYOFF -- FOUR ARCHETYPES ON THE AIDE-QUALIFICATION AXIS THAT ARE FOUR DIFFERENT DATA MODELS, NOT FOUR CONFIGURATIONS OF ONE: (A) KANSAS state credential + state test + registry -- CNA on the registry PLUS a 20-hour course, 30-question test, 22 to pass, 3 attempts/year, fail within a year and retake the WHOLE course, 8th-grade reading prescreen, ONE 90-day trainee window ever, plus a lapsed-credential fast path (28-51-115) letting an INACTIVE LPN/RN licensed within 24 months skip straight to the test; (B) CONNECTICUT 75-hour state curriculum with SIX named deemed-equivalent routes including out-of-state reciprocity, plus a 24-consecutive-month inactivity rule that voids it; (C) ALASKA federal credential + criminal-history gate -- Board of Nursing CNA, clean registry, criminal check WITH A VARIANCE MECHANISM at 7 AAC 10.935 (the only second-chance door found in 27 rounds), agency competency across 13 areas; (D) ARIZONA and MISSISSIPPI fully delegated/outcome-based -- NO hours, NO test, NO registry; the agency defines qualifications from the job description AND PATIENT ACUITY and must verify and document them, with supervision carrying the load. A single "aide qualified: yes/no" field is correct in Kansas and MEANINGLESS in Arizona. ALABAMA SAYS IN ITS OWN RULE THAT IT DOES NOT LICENSE: Ala. Admin. Code 410-2-4-.07(1)(b) states "There is no licensure requirement for home health agencies in Alabama" -- confirmed structurally by reading the full Agency 420 chapter list (no home health chapter) and a full-code phrase search (18 hits, all CON/Medicaid/workers-comp). Entry is instead a NUMERIC MARKET GATE: county-level need, 75% weighted to age 65+, APNS>=100 means need for exactly ONE more provider, at most one approval per county per cycle, and an 18-month protection window for the new entrant. Most distinctive state structure in 27 rounds. SOUTH DAKOTA: the full ARSD article list has no home health agency article (44:04 Medical Facilities is a historical shell, everything Transferred/Repealed to 44:73 in 2015) -- combined with round 26's statutory finding this is STRONG EVIDENCE, NOT PROOF, that SD does not license home health; a full-text SDCL search has NOT been run and the doc says so. Also nearly logged a false "South Dakota is behind Microsoft auth" from one admin path that 302s -- the site is fully public, and it was the browser that showed it. Tier 2 lists exactly what is route-open-but-unread in each state (Kansas supportive-care/HCBS tier, Connecticut's skilled D66-D79 regime, Alaska 7 AAC 10.935, Arizona's other eight sections, Mississippi's licence chapters) so nothing here reads as more complete than it is.
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-28-idaho-was-not-blocked-either.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- IDAHO WAS NOT BLOCKED EITHER: EIGHT OF EIGHT, AND ZERO OF THE EIGHT WERE EVER BLOCKED. Round 27's "Idaho is the only genuine failure" is corrected in place at the top of that file. Idaho opened on the SAME TWO ANGLES as the other seven: adminrules.idaho.gov returns 200/271KB (rebuilt WordPress, 2025 assets), /rules/current/16/index.html 302s to /current-rules/ -- a MOVED HOST, same shape as Kansas -- and the rules list is CLIENT-RENDERED, so curl sees 57 WordPress links and no rules while Chrome sees all 44 agencies. The 404 on the old chapter PDF path was the ENTIRE basis for "state-declared outage", and it had been carried forward untested for twenty-plus rounds. IDAPA 16 has 21 current chapters and NO home health chapter (16.03.07 is gone) -- BUT Idaho Code 39-2403 still makes licensure MANDATORY and 39-2405 obliges the board to adopt standards that are NOT IN THE PUBLISHED CODE, with a deeming route letting the department license Medicare-certified or HCFA-accredited agencies WITHOUT A SURVEY. Same deemed-status pattern as Arizona 36-425.01 (round 6), except here there are no state standards for it to run alongside. TWO PUBLISHER INDEX DEFECTS, EACH ONE NEARLY A FALSE FINDING: (1) Chapter 24's own page renders an EMPTY section list and its PDF is 325 characters of letterhead, yet 39-2401 through 39-2411 all return full unrepealed text; (2) the Title 39 index labels CHAPTER 56 "[REPEALED]" and does not link it, yet 39-5601 through 39-5609 render live as PERSONAL ASSISTANCE SERVICES with history notes ending in ordinary 2010 amendments and no repeal. A NEGATIVE CONTROL WAS RUN FIRST -- SECT39-2499 and SECT39-5610 both 404 -- which is what makes the 200s mean anything; a soft-200 would have produced eleven confident fabrications. Rule: confirm a chapter is empty by ASKING FOR ITS SECTIONS, not by reading its table of contents. Round 25 paid for the inverse (eleven SD endpoint forms against a wrong citation); here the citation was right and the INDEX was wrong. IDAHO'S NON-MEDICAL TIER WAS HIDING BEHIND THAT "[REPEALED]" LABEL: 39-5604 makes screening COMPULSORY (criminal background AND nurse aide registry checks, "shall require") while 39-5605 makes training DISCRETIONARY ("may require"), with a statutory grandfather period and explicit authority to vary training BY SERVICE TYPE AND CLIENT ACUITY -- mandatory vetting, discretionary competence, a fifth shape. KANSAS CORRECTION THAT SHARPENS ROUND 27: the archetype attaches to the WORKER CLASS, NOT THE STATE. K.A.R. 28-51-117 (supportive care, eff. 2022-05-20) has NO credential, NO registry, NO test, NO hours -- licensee-defined competency verified before unsupervised work and annually across 10 areas, on-site supervision every 3 months. So Kansas is Archetype A for aides and Archetype D for supportive care workers IN THE SAME ARTICLE; a state-level qualification_model field is already wrong. And 28-51-117(g) is the most product-relevant rule found in 28 rounds: a SIXTEEN-CATEGORY TASK-PERMISSION MATRIX WITH PER-TASK CLIENT-CONDITION PRECONDITIONS -- medication assistance only from a day/time-marked reminder container and never administration; skin care only if skin unbroken with no active chronic problem; feeding only if the client chews, swallows and sits upright unaided; dressing includes OTC support stockings but NOT prescription antiembolic ones; respiratory care prohibited outright; and NAIL TRIMMING permitted only if the agency ticked home health services on its application or renewal -- same worker, same client, same clippers, permitted or not on an administrative election made months earlier. That is an AUTHORISATION rule keyed to the client's condition at the moment of the task, and "is this worker qualified?" cannot express it.
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-29-worker-class-splits.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- CONNECTICUT'S UNREAD HALF, AND THE WORKER-CLASS SPLIT IS NOT A KANSAS QUIRK -- IT RECURS, BUT WITH A DIFFERENT DISCRIMINATOR IN EVERY STATE THAT HAS ONE. CT D66-D79 read: on the AIDE axis the two regimes are the SAME RULE RESTATED (D69(d)(2) reproduces D83(b) nearly verbatim -- same 75 hours, same six deeming routes, same 24-month inactivity rule, same 10-hour orientation), with two real differences: the skilled regime bars an agency from training if out of compliance with OBRA "and/or one or more condition of participation of 42 CFR part 484" (D83 cites OBRA only), and supervision is heavier (RN reachable and available for a home visit AT ALL TIMES INCLUDING NIGHTS/WEEKENDS/HOLIDAYS, 60-day RN assessment IN THE HOME while the aide works, and any task delegated beyond the listed duties needs a clinical-record entry showing the nurse assessed the aide's competence). WHERE CT DOES SPLIT IS THE ADMINISTRATOR CLASS, BY AGENCY TYPE: D67 (skilled) wants a master's +1yr or baccalaureate +2yrs or a licensed physician +1yr; D81 (homemaker) wants a baccalaureate +2yrs or a related-field baccalaureate. AND D67(a)(7) IS A SUNSETTING GRANDFATHER -- the 1979 route was void after 1 Jan 1986 unless the person also met routes (1)-(5) -- while CT's aide grandfather is permanent, so "grandfathered" is not a boolean, it has an expiry date in some rules. CT also states staffing thresholds more densely than anyone: 1 FT supervisor of clinical services per <=15 FTE professional direct staff, who may ALSO be administrator at <=6 FTE; therapy supervisor required at 5 FTE with span capped at 15; 1 PT/OT per <=6 assistants with a 30-day conference; 1 FT RN per <=15 FTE aides on duty; dedicated FT aide-program supervisor at 25+ aides. Compare Mississippi keying the same idea to PATIENT CENSUS (25/50) -- same constraint, incompatible units. CORPUS-WIDE TEST OVER THE 34 WRITE-UPS: TWO ATTRIBUTION PASSES DISAGREED ON ROUGHLY A THIRD OF STATES (Florida 3 vs 0, Delaware 1 vs 0, Utah 0 vs 2) -- a single pass would have shipped as a matrix and been wrong; only the union is used, and only as a candidate generator, with the known false positives named (supportive care attaches to AK/ID/IN/MS/SD purely because rounds 27-28 discuss Kansas near those names). ABSENCE IS THE TRUSTWORTHY SIGNAL: 9 states name NO worker class anywhere (MD, MN, MT, NH, NJ, NM, ND, VT, WY) and 5 name only a skilled one (AL, KY, ME, NC, SC) = 14 STATES WHERE A SECOND WORKER CLASS HAS PLAUSIBLY NEVER BEEN LOOKED AT. TWO PULLED OFF THAT LIST BLIND, BOTH HAD SPLITS THE CORPUS MISSED. NORTH CAROLINA splits by WHETHER THE WORK IS HANDS-ON: 10A NCAC 13J has three tiers -- licensed caregivers (agency-policy supervision), unlicensed hands-on caregivers (90-day home visit plus annual with the aide present), and a whole separate SECTION .1500 for companion/sitter/respite defined as "non-hands-on care services ... which do not require training or verification of skills by a Registered Nurse", where supervision drops to 3-month contact / 6-month visit / annual-with-worker and the AGENCY DIRECTOR need only be a high-school graduate or GED holder with one year of experience. MARYLAND splits by THE CLIENT'S CONDITION WITH A CONSENT OVERRIDE: COMAR 10.07.05.10D bars referring an uncertified caregiver unless the client needs no ADL help, OR needs only ADL help and the supervising nurse judges no predictable adverse health consequences, OR SIGNS A WAIVER OF SKILLED SERVICES FORM; a certified caregiver (Board-certified CNA / medication technician / medicine aide) is mandatory for medication administration. Maryland's screening is the most detailed found anywhere -- criminal history, licence verification, TB, references, employment history, I-9, IN-PERSON INTERVIEW BEFORE REFERRAL, and a SKILLS ASSESSMENT AND DEMONSTRATION BEFORE CLIENT REFERRAL -- while training (.11) sets a seven-element floor including CPR with NO duration, outside providers allowed only with written OHCQ approval. SO THERE ARE NOW FOUR CONFIRMED DISCRIMINATORS, and a state may use more than one: worker class (KS), task (NC, and KS 28-51-117(g)), client condition (MD), agency type (CT). The lowest common denominator is not a worker attribute at all -- it is (worker, task, client, agency, date) -> permitted or not, with the state deciding which of the five matter. ALSO RECORDED: the raw state corpus from rounds 1-26 IS GONE (it lived in session scratch dirs), which is why this analysis had to run over write-ups instead of source text; future fetches worth keeping need a durable home.
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-30-sources-persisted-and-four-candidates.md (NEW) + tools/sairn_source_fetch.py + docs/sources/sairnsenior/ (Fourth) -- SOURCES ARE NOW KEPT, AND FOUR OF THE TWELVE CANDIDATES OPENED; ALL FOUR HAD SOMETHING THE CORPUS HAD MISSED. New tool stores EXTRACTED TEXT (not the 7-20MB originals) at docs/sources/sairnsenior/<STATE>/<slug>.txt with a MANIFEST.json carrying URL, fetch date, http status, original byte size and SHA256 OF THE ORIGINAL BYTES -- reproducibility lives in the hash, not the bytes, and a changed hash later is itself a signal. 12 sources, 5.2MB. Three design points came from live friction, not planning: (1) urllib gets 403 from Arizona and Mississippi where curl gets 200 on the IDENTICAL url and User-Agent, so the tool falls back to curl; (2) --from-file exists because Mississippi's host served the 7MB PDF once this morning and 403'd the same URL an hour later -- a source can vanish AFTER you read it, which is the argument for the directory arriving while the directory was being built; (3) Maryland's publisher prints "Please do not scrape. Instead, bulk download." -- that host is not swept and the constraint is recorded in a publisher_note field so the next session inherits it. TWO OF TWELVE MANIFEST ENTRIES WERE MISLABELLED WITHIN AN HOUR AND WERE FIXED BEFORE COMMIT (a hospice chapter filed as home health; a home health reg filed under the in-home-care name its FILENAME claimed) -- building an index that lies would have been the joke. MINNESOTA IS A FIFTH DISCRIMINATOR AND THE INVERSE OF KANSAS: it ABOLISHES THE WORKER CLASS. No HHA, no PCA, no companion, no registry -- just "unlicensed personnel", with requirements scaling by which LICENCE THE EMPLOYER HOLDS (basic vs comprehensive, 144A.471 subd 5-7) and which tasks are delegated. Basic-tier tasks are explicitly "provided by licensed or unlicensed personnel"; medication support is reminders only; and clause (6) is a DEPENDENCY RULE -- housekeeping/shopping/laundry permitted ONLY if the provider is also doing one of the five hands-on tasks, so a pure chores service falls outside the licence entirely. Basic-tier unlicensed personnel MAY NOT perform delegated nursing or therapy tasks; comprehensive-tier ones need a practical skills test on EVERY delegated task they will perform, OR satisfy Medicare 42 CFR 483/484.36, OR hold a pre-19-April-1993 approved course. 144A.4795 subd 5-6 CLOSE THE CONTRACTOR AND TEMP-AGENCY ROUTES EXPLICITLY (temp staff "shall be treated as if they are staff of the home care provider"), and subd 4 STATUTORILY REQUIRES A LIVE COMPETENCY SYSTEM feeding up-to-date staff competency to the RN so delegation decisions can be made -- Minnesota requires by statute the thing a product would build. NEW MEXICO: 7.28.2 NMAC repealed 1 JULY 2024 and Chapter 28 Home Health Services is [Repealed] ENTIRELY, alongside a date-stamped clearing-out of Chapter 1 including Caregivers Criminal History Screening, Employee Abuse Registry, and abuse/neglect reporting and training for community providers. Successor NOT located (Title 7 ch.1 and Title 8 both checked). The NM Health Care Authority stood up the same date -- recorded AS A HYPOTHESIS, not a finding. What is established regardless: ANY PRE-JULY-2024 NEW MEXICO MATERIAL IN THIS CORPUS IS STALE. NORTH DAKOTA: round 26 worked from 23-17.5 (repealed cooperative agreements); THE HOME HEALTH CHAPTER IS 23-17.3 and it is live -- third wrong-citation incident in the survey. One worker class in statute, defined only as rendering "personal related service under the supervision of a registered professional nurse", all standards delegated to rules (NDAC unread); CON repealed 1995, which is the mirror of Alabama where CON is the ENTIRE gate. SOUTH CAROLINA has a SEPARATE ACT for the non-medical tier -- ch. 44-70 Licensure of In-Home Care Providers Act (2011) -- keyed to "a personal rather than a medical need ... not mandating continuing attention or supervision from trained and licensed medical personnel", with exclusions that include DIRECT HIRE BY THE CLIENT OR FAMILY and churches providing for a nominal fee, and a definition that REACHES REFERRAL BUSINESSES where the referrer has a financial interest (a marketplace trigger for the decision gate). BUT ITS LICENSURE REQUIREMENT IS DORMANT: the Act says 44-70-30 "become[s] effective upon the effective date of regulations promulgated" under 44-70-40, and WHETHER THOSE REGULATIONS EXIST IS NOT ESTABLISHED -- the exact inverse of Idaho's live-mandate-with-missing-rules. "Effective upon promulgation of regulations" is a distinct legal state that needs its own value; neither "licensed" nor "unlicensed" describes SC's in-home tier today. CITATION WARNING FOR THE WHOLE CORPUS: SC RENUMBERED HEALTH REGS FROM CHAPTER 61 TO CHAPTER 60 (Chapter 61 is now Environmental Services, and scdhec.gov now fails TLS after the 2024 DPH split), so any earlier "S.C. Reg. 61-<n>" health citation should be re-checked against Chapter 60. Still unread: MT, NH, NJ, VT, WY (routes probed and open; NH's He-P 800 is one 22MB HTML), KY and ME (routes unresolved), and Maryland's .12 waiver form plus the delegation rules.
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-31-six-more-candidates.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- SIX MORE CANDIDATES READ; NINE OF NINE PULLED OFF THE ABSENCE LIST HAD SOMETHING THE CORPUS MISSED. VERMONT IS AN EXCLUSIVE TERRITORIAL FRANCHISE: 33 VSA 6304(a) gives each designated agency "a franchise" for four years and says NO home health agency may serve patients in a designated area without being designated, and 6304(b) HARD-CODES THE INCUMBENTS INTO THE STATUTE BY COMPANY NAME AND TOWN; 6307 leaves one door, Commissioner contracts for specialised services "not available from the designated" agencies. Alabama at least COMPUTES a need number; Vermont wrote the incumbents into the code. Subchapter 2 (6321 attendant care) is the OPPOSITE model in the same state -- participant-directed, group-directed and personal services, with recipients given the right to "hire, train, and terminate the employment of attendants ... establish work schedules, manage the services, and oversee payments" -- a new axis: WHO EMPLOYS AND DIRECTS THE WORKER. Two chapter-name traps found getting there: 18 VSA ch.50 "Home Health Services" is departmental contracting and ch.57 "Home Care Services" was RECODIFIED IN 1989 and is empty. NEW HAMPSHIRE has TWO SEPARATE LICENCE PARTS -- He-P 809 Home Health Care Providers and He-P 822 Home Care Service Provider Agencies -- and 822.17 is agency-defined on qualifications but hard-edged on disqualification: criminal record check plus BEAS elder-registry check for employees, VOLUNTEERS AND INDEPENDENT CONTRACTORS; categorical bars including an ADMINISTRATIVE FINDING in any state; a MANDATORY-IN-FORM WAIVER (the department "shall grant a waiver" if the person does not pose a threat), the second second-chance door after Alaska 7 AAC 10.935; a continuing duty to notify and stop or seek a waiver if the fact surfaces post-hire; and (h) DELEGATED VETTING, where the licensee may accept a contracted agency's SIGNED STATEMENT in lieu of doing the checks. Plus orientation before client contact incl. body mechanics, annual in-service, MINIMUM AGE 18 for direct care, 2-step Mantoux TB within 12 months with permission to work between steps. KENTUCKY TRAINS BY THE CLIENT'S DIAGNOSIS: 902 KAR 20:081 implementing KRS 216.9375 requires an aide serving a patient who EXHIBITS SYMPTOMS of Alzheimer's or other dementia to complete 6 HOURS INITIAL BEFORE PROVIDING SERVICES plus 3 hours annual, and a 24-MONTH LAPSE IN DEMENTIA CARE SPECIFICALLY re-triggers the 6 hours within 60 days of resuming. Connecticut's 24-month rule voids the whole credential; Kentucky's is scoped to ONE DIAGNOSIS, so an aide can be fully current generally and disqualified for dementia clients -- no single "trained yes/no" field and no single expiry date can hold that. Same amendment let APRNs and PAs supervise home health in lieu of physicians. MAINE: round 15's citation was wrong (title22ch1666 is Appointment of Receivers); the chapter is 22 MRS ch.419, with chs.1622/1623 carrying non-medical tiers and ch.1625 fully repealed -- pattern-log incident 2 now resolved. Section 2147 EXCLUSIONS are broad and land on the product: families, friends and neighbors; sole practitioners otherwise licensed; CHORE SERVICES; and persons or agencies contracting or arranging home health services. MINNESOTA AND MAINE TREAT CHORES IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS -- MN covers housekeeping/shopping but ONLY as an adjunct to hands-on care, ME excludes chore services from licensure entirely. Section 2149-A frames vetting around "DIRECT ACCESS PERSONNEL" -- the discriminator is ACCESS to the client, a wider cousin of NC's hands-on test -- and 2149-B is marked EFFECTIVE ON CONTINGENCY, the second not-yet-in-force instance after South Carolina. MONTANA: one in-home licensed category; "personal care" is defined only as a RESIDENTS' service; the non-medical tier is Medicaid (Title 53 ch.6 Part 4, located not read), same shape as Idaho ch.56. MCA 50-5 Part 7 "Lay Caregivers" is a CARE-Act regime -- an UNPAID designated family caregiver with a statutory right to instruction before discharge -- not a licensure class, and a category no other state has surfaced. WYOMING has only "home health agency", defined as an agency "primarily engaged in ARRANGING and directly providing" services -- arranging is inside the licensed activity WITH NO FINANCIAL-INTEREST QUALIFIER, broader on its face than SC's referral clause; homemaker/personal care/chore appear only in adult protective services. NEW JERSEY route NOT resolved (njleg SPA returns JSON 404s on statute paths; Justia has it and was not used, per this survey's official-source practice) -- recorded as UNRESOLVED, NOT BLOCKED. HELD FOR MICHAEL, NOT DECIDED (section 8): SC's financial-interest referral clause, Wyoming's unqualified "arranging", and Vermont's designated-area prohibition all bear on whether a matching-and-payments platform becomes the LICENSED ENTITY rather than an intermediary, and whether Vermont's skilled tier is closed to a new entrant at all. The three provisions are established from official sources; how each state APPLIES them to a platform is not, and no guidance, enforcement history or counsel opinion has been read. Recommended for Michael to accept or reject: route these to the marketplace-model decision gate as SCOPE questions before further state reading. Chapter-name-trap count is now SIX.
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-32-new-jersey-agency-locked-credential.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- NEW JERSEY RESOLVED, AND IT HOLDS THE MOST DECISIVE PROVISION IN 32 ROUNDS. Round 31 recorded NJ as "unresolved, not blocked"; the statutes are behind a Folio NXT search app at lis.njleg.state.nj.us that curl cannot drive (38KB shell, JSON 404s on statute paths) and the browser can -- found in four calls. TWELVE OF TWELVE CANDIDATES NOW READ, TWELVE OF TWELVE HAD SOMETHING THE CORPUS MISSED. NJ CERTIFIES THE WORKER THROUGH THE BOARD OF NURSING and then AGENCY-LOCKS THE CREDENTIAL IN BOTH DIRECTIONS: 45:11-24.7 requires the certificate itself to carry the legend "Valid only if certified homemaker-home health aide is employed by a home health agency or health care service firm and is performing delegated nursing regimen or nursing tasks delegated through the authority of a duly licensed registered professional nurse"; and 45:11-24.6 lets the Board issue BIENNIAL RECERTIFICATION "only upon receiving documented proof from a home health agency or health care service firm that the aide is CURRENTLY EMPLOYED and REGULARLY SUPERVISED by a registered professional nurse." Every other state regulates the AGENCY and lets the credential travel with the worker; NJ makes the credential a function of WHO EMPLOYS AND SUPERVISES -- a directly-hired aide or a platform contractor is not on a valid certificate on the face of 24.7, and in two years has no certificate at all. NJ ALSO ANSWERS THE ORAL-TESTING QUESTION THE OPPOSITE WAY FROM KANSAS: 45:11-24.2 says a person may satisfy the examination requirement "by passing an oral competency evaluation in English or Spanish", while K.A.R. 28-51-116(e)(2) says "No test shall be given orally or by a sign language interpreter since reading and writing ... is an essential job task of a home health aide" -- two states, same accommodation question, opposite answers, each giving its reason in the text. TWO MORE NJ PATHWAYS: 30:4D-7qq creates a PAID FAMILY-MEMBER route (family member of a Medicaid/NJ FamilyCare enrollee may get Board of Nursing HHHA certification and serve that enrollee THROUGH AN AGENCY under RN direction, enrollee must be UNDER 21) -- compare Vermont 6321 where the recipient hires and directs outright, and Maine 2147 which just excludes families/friends/neighbors; three states, three answers to "can a relative be paid for this". And 26:2H-12.96 lets an LTC facility employ a certified HHHA AS a certified nurse aide provided they are enrolled in a CNA programme and working toward certification -- a cross-credential work-while-training bridge. GAP RECORDED NOT FIXED: these citations CANNOT be stored by tools/sairn_source_fetch.py -- Folio NXT serves from a stateful session behind gateway.dll with no stable per-section URL to fetch or hash, so NJ rests on a write-up like every pre-round-30 state, and the manifest's lack of an NJ entry must NOT be read as NJ being unread. Adding browser-capture raises a real design question (what gets hashed when there are no canonical bytes?) and was logged as an option rather than invented at the end of a session. HELD FOR MICHAEL, SHARPENED: round 31 held three marketplace-scope provisions (SC referral-with-financial-interest, WY unqualified "arranging", VT designated-area prohibition); NJ adds a fourth that is DIFFERENT IN KIND -- the first three ask whether the PLATFORM becomes the licensed entity, which a platform can restructure around; NJ asks whether the WORKERS hold a valid credential at all when nobody employs them, which it cannot. NOT DECIDED. What is NOT established and should be read FIRST: NJ's definition of "health care service firm", which sits elsewhere in NJ law and was not read -- if a platform IS a health care service firm the problem collapses in one step. Maryland is now the only partly-read state (COMAR 10.07.05 .12 waiver form and 10.27.11 delegation of nursing functions), and its publisher asks not to be swept, so those are two individual page reads.
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-32-new-jersey-agency-locked-credential.md (ADDENDUM 5a, same session) (Fourth) -- READ THE "HEALTH CARE SERVICE FIRM" DEFINITION THAT SECTION 5 SAID TO READ FIRST. IT IS NOT IN THE HEALTH TITLE -- it is N.J.S.A. 34:8-45.1 in TITLE 34 LABOR, attached to the Private Employment Agency Act, and IT NAMES THE BUSINESS MODEL: any firm "that is not a home health care agency ... or a hospice, which employs, places, arranges for the placement of, OR IN ANY WAY REFERS, an individual to provide companion services, health care services, or personal care services in the personal residence of a person with a disability or who is AGE 60 OR OLDER, regardless of the title by which the provider of the services is known, shall be registered as a Health Care Service Firm", enforceable by Consumer Affairs "whether the operations include the direct employment of individuals, THE USE OF AN INTERNET WEBSITE OR APPLICATION, OR ANY OTHER PROCESS OR BUSINESS MODEL." Where SC reaches referrers only with a financial interest and WY folds "arranging" in without saying why, NEW JERSEY WROTE THE PLATFORM INTO THE STATUTE as an anti-avoidance clause. Its three definitions cut the medical/non-medical line where nobody else does: "companion services" are non-medical supervision and socialization that EXCLUDE ADL assistance but MAY INCLUDE HOUSEHOLD CHORES; "health care services" are those "for which a license or certification is required as a pre-condition"; and "personal care services" are ADL assistance "performed BY LICENSED OR CERTIFIED PERSONNEL" -- so in NJ ADL assistance is BY DEFINITION credentialed work. Compare Maine (chore services excluded from licensure entirely) and Minnesota (unlicensed personnel may do ADLs under a basic licence): the same three activities are sorted into different boxes by three states. EFFECT ON THE HELD QUESTION -- HALF RESOLVED, HALF SHARPENED: a platform CAN be a health care service firm, indeed MUST register as one, so the certificate legend's "home health agency or health care service firm" is reachable. But 45:11-24.7 (validity) and 45:11-24.6 (renewal, on documented proof the aide is "currently employed and regularly supervised by a registered professional nurse") both say EMPLOYED BY, and a registered HCSF that PLACES INDEPENDENT CONTRACTORS is not obviously EMPLOYING them. Whether NJ treats HCSF placement as employment for those two sections is NOT ESTABLISHED -- it turns on the Consumer Affairs rules at N.J.A.C. 13:45B, NOT READ, and possibly on litigation history, NOT SEARCHED. The New Jersey question is now narrow enough for counsel in one sentence: does placement by a registered Health Care Service Firm satisfy "employed by" in N.J.S.A. 45:11-24.6 and 24.7? Also of note: the stale-tab rule paid off again -- a screenshot timed out on the LIS tab, creating a fresh tab unfroze it, and the horizontally clipped text was recovered by scrolling the document pane right rather than by guessing the cut-off words.
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-33-maryland-closed-and-status.md (NEW) (Fourth) -- MARYLAND CLOSED; ALL TWELVE CANDIDATES COMPLETE, TWELVE OF TWELVE HAD SOMETHING THE WRITE-UPS MISSED. MD's waiver is NARROWER than round 29 recorded: COMAR 10.07.05.12D(2) lets "a cognitively capable adult client, BUT NOT A CLIENT REPRESENTATIVE WITH LEGAL AUTHORITY to make health care decisions" waive the recommendation of CERTIFIED CARE for routine treatments or self-administration of medications, and D(3) lets that client waive recommended SKILLED CARE -- so the override that permits an uncertified worker is PERSONAL TO THE CLIENT and a guardian/agent/proxy cannot exercise it, with CAPACITY part of the test. D(4) requires a risks-and-benefits discussion on a signed dated informed consent form, and a separate signed waiver-of-skilled-services form in the record. Supervision cadence is keyed to the MEDICATION RELATIONSHIP (.12E(2)): RN on-site at least every 45 DAYS if staff administer medications, every 3 MONTHS if staff assist with self-administration, every 4 MONTHS if neither. COMAR 10.27.11 (Board of Nursing delegation) is the clearest statement in the survey of the authorisation model: .03A lets a nurse delegate to AN UNLICENSED INDIVIDUAL, a CNA or a medication technician and says "the delegating nurse RETAINS THE ACCOUNTABILITY"; .03C bars delegating anything requiring nursing judgment except in an emergency; .03D requires the nurse to assess the patient, EITHER INSTRUCT OR VERIFY COMPETENCY, supervise, stay accountable, evaluate and document; .03E makes the nurse "the primary decision maker" within an employer model of practice that must itself have mechanisms for identifying delegatees and RE-EVALUATING their competency; .04A sets the DEGREE of supervision from six factors including orientation to the SPECIFIC PATIENT ENVIRONMENT; .04C-D require on-site visits every 45 days for clients meeting .03F criteria and EVERY 2 WEEKS for those who do not. Nothing in it asks what the worker IS -- unlicensed individual and CNA appear in the same sentence throughout. DOC ALSO CARRIES A CONSOLIDATED STATUS for whoever picks this up: TEN discriminators now confirmed with citations (worker class, task, hands-on, direct access, client condition, client diagnosis, agency type, licence tier, territorial designation, EMPLOYMENT RELATIONSHIP) and a schema keyed to a worker credential cannot express seven of them; three states where the entry gate is not a qualification at all (AL numeric CON, VT territorial franchise, NM repealed); three legal states that are neither licensed nor unlicensed (SC contingent, ME effective-on-contingency, ID mandate-without-standards); and the open list in priority order, headed by the HELD marketplace question and N.J.A.C. 13:45B which would answer it. METHOD: the stale-tab rule paid off twice more, and the clause that mattered most -- "the use of an Internet website or application, or any other process or business model" -- was in a HORIZONTALLY CLIPPED region recovered by scrolling rather than guessed. Maryland's decisive rule was THREE HOPS from its licensure chapter (10.07.05.10D -> .12D -> 10.27.11 in a different title).
- docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-31-sairnsenior-round-33-maryland-closed-and-status.md (ADDENDUM 3a, same session) (Fourth) -- N.J.A.C. 13:45B ATTEMPTED AND STOPPED ONE CLICK SHORT, DELIBERATELY. Route for whoever picks it up: njconsumeraffairs.gov sits behind IMPERVA/INCAPSULA bot protection (curl gets a 212-byte JS challenge stub regardless of User-Agent; a real browser passes it normally) and its Law-and-Regulations page carries statutes only, no NJAC chapters; the NJ OFFICE OF ADMINISTRATIVE LAW'S OWN SITE LINKS OUT TO LEXISNEXIS for the code text, at advance.lexis.com in a container titled "New Jersey Register - Free Public Access"; and that container presents a NEW JERSEY TERMS-OF-USE DIALOG WITH AN "I AGREE" BUTTON before any search runs. I DID NOT CLICK IT -- accepting terms or agreements on Michael's behalf is not something to do unasked, and a research errand is not a reason to make an exception. Everything up to the dialog is done; the next person needs one decision and one click. WORTH RECORDING SEPARATELY: THIS IS THE GENUINE ARTICLE. Rounds 21-26 labelled ALABAMA and MISSISSIPPI "LexisNexis-gated" and round 27 showed both were wrong -- those states publish their codes themselves, openly. NEW JERSEY ACTUALLY IS: its administrative code reaches the public through a commercial publisher's portal linked from the state's own OAL page. The label was applied twice where it was false before being met once where it is true.
- CLAIMING: SAIRNfreedom Phase 3 -- gaming compliance and the money model (ORC 2915) -- starting now (fourth, 2026-09-01). Picked over SAIRNcode and SAIRNcare because it is by far the largest genuine gap: 1 of 6 phases built (54KB) vs SAIRNcare's phases 0-3 built (234KB) and SAIRNcode's 4 of 6 scoped items built (751KB). Depends only on Phase 1, which Cody built and live-verified 2026-08-31. Building the session-constraint calendar FIRST is the safe direction per the spec's own interlock warning -- Phase 2's booking surface must sit on top of Phase 3's refusals, not alongside them. Will log the result here.
- sairnfreedom.html (Fourth) -- SAIRNFREEDOM PHASE 3 BUILT, PUSHED (`7eafa71`) AND LIVE-VERIFIED ON THE DEPLOYED APP, not just locally: the same 25-check browser suite was re-run against https://sairn.vercel.app/sairnfreedom and passed 25/25 with zero page errors, after three polls (first two returned 200 with the OLD build -- a clean push is not proof the deploy landed, which is why the marker was `panel-sessions` rather than the HTTP code). Items 3.1, 3.2, 3.3, 3.4 and 3.6. ITEM 3.5 HELD BY MICHAEL'S DECISION, not oversight -- ORC 2915.10(A)(3) requires winner SSNs and that becomes its own researched task with the same rigour as the IOLTA work; the app SAYS SO ON SCREEN and explicitly disclaims producing a compliant 2915.10 record set, and the held items are enumerated (prize list with winner name/address, participant count (A)(5), food and beverage sales (A)(6) and expenses (A)(7), 3-year retention, the 1 November supply inventory (D), and notifying the AG where records are kept (B)). BUILT PHASE 3 BEFORE PHASE 2 ON PURPOSE -- the spec states the events calendar and the session constraints are ONE calendar, so building the booking surface first yields something that cheerfully books a fourth session in a seven-day window and reports it afterwards; `sfSessionRefusals()` is the function Phase 2 must call and it returns REFUSALS, not warnings. THE SHARPEST POINT, straight from the research doc's Correction 1: the 5+2+2 window is a DEFINITION, not a conduct rule -- a six-hour period is not a non-compliant session, it is NOT A SESSION under 2915.01(S)(1) and every per-session figure keyed to it is keyed to nothing; the refusal is worded exactly that way and browser-verified. ENFORCED, each pinned to its own division because sections of Ch. 2915 carry DIFFERENT effective dates (2915.01 is 2025-09-30, 2915.09 is 2021-09-30): (S)(1) five continuous hours; (S)(2) two-hour instant wings; 2915.09(C)(4) three sessions per seven days, checked over every window ANCHORED AT A SESSION DATE rather than sampled -- a maximal violating window can always be slid to start on a session, so that is exhaustive; (C)(5) the $6,000 cap at booking, and FLAGGED rather than refused when a recorded payout breaches it, because refusing to record a payout that already happened hides a violation instead of preventing one; (C)(6) the 2 a.m.-10 a.m. blackout INCLUDING the narrow 9 a.m. carve-out that applies only to a session starting at exactly 10:00 -- verified both ways, 60m before a 10:00 session passes and 120m before an 11:00 session (also 9 a.m.) is refused; (C)(7)/(C)(8)/(D)(1) operator eligibility; 2915.10(C) exclusive account with gross PROFIT deposited and never gross receipts, payment by check or EFT only and no cash path anywhere; OAC 109:1-4-08 licence gating, where holding only Type III now correctly FAILS because everything scheduled here is at a session. TWO HONEST LIMITS, BOTH ON SCREEN. Only 2915.01(GG)(11) has been read against primary source, so there is exactly ONE validated expense category and one catch-all the app explicitly does NOT validate -- inventing the other ten limbs of (GG) would be the fabrication class this platform exists to prevent. And a net-profit DISTRIBUTION must not be recorded as an expense: 2915.10(C) makes it look like one (it is drawn on the same account) but it is WHERE NET PROFIT WENT, not a subtraction in arriving at it, and mis-recording it would feed a wrong figure to the 2915.101 tiered calculation; there is deliberately no category for it and a block box says why. SPEC 0e IS WIRED, not just noted: a 2915 failure raises on the DASHBOARD as a liquor-permit exposure under OAC 4301:1-1-53(D)-(E), naming the post's own permit class -- there is no liquor dashboard until Phase 2, so it surfaces where a user will actually see it rather than waiting for a page that does not exist. ONE PHASE 1 CHANGE, required not cosmetic: Post Profile's single bingo-licence select became checkboxes, because Type II REQUIRES Type I and a single value cannot express a post holding both; Phase 1's value migrates non-destructively (p.bingo is left in place) and selecting Type II without Type I is REFUSED rather than silently corrected -- quietly fixing a licence record is how a wrong one survives. ALSO RECORDED RATHER THAN MISSED: 2915.09(C)(4)'s volunteer-firefighter/rescue exceptions are deliberately NOT implemented, since none of the five target orders qualifies and an exemption path nobody needs is a route to switching the limit off. CHECKS: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance 312/312, 0 duplicate globals, 9 panels / 9 nav items / 0 trapped panels, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 duplicate ids, 0 storage-key collisions, no alert(), no console.log(), no toISOString() outside the comment warning against it, escHtml on every rendered field, 0 dead functions. 42 statutory unit tests over the refusal engine plus 25 real-browser checks driving the actual UI, run locally AND against production. STORAGE IS STILL LOCAL and the dashboard still says so -- no sf_* table has been provisioned, unchanged from Phase 1 and disclosed rather than implied. 2026-09-01
- sairnfreedom.html (Fourth) -- SAIRNFREEDOM PHASE 2 BUILT, PUSHED (`e9cfe31`) AND LIVE-VERIFIED ON PRODUCTION: both browser suites re-run against https://sairn.vercel.app/sairnfreedom, 35/35 (Phase 2) and 25/25 (Phase 3 regression), zero page errors. First poll after the push served the OLD build again, same as Phase 3 -- HTTP 200 is not evidence the deploy landed, the marker was `panel-hall`. Items 2.1-2.7 complete. THE INTERLOCK IS REAL, not described: the events calendar calls `sfSessionRefusals()` ITSELF and a gaming event writes into the SAME `sf_sessions` store the Sessions panel reads -- browser-verified that booking a FOURTH session FROM THE CALENDAR is refused under 2915.09(C)(4) and a six-hour one is refused as NOT A SESSION under 2915.01(S)(1). That is the entire payoff of having built the constraint engine before the booking surface. 2.1 POS: members-only per ORC 4303.17(A)(1) gated on dues-currency DERIVED from the Phase 1 ledger rather than a new flag; permit hours from OAC 4301:1-1-49(B) where (B)(3) bars CONSUMPTION as well as sale, so the refusal says the room must be clear of drinks rather than implying last call is the gate; Sunday closed all day absent a D-6 (ORC 4303.182, election-gated) so D-6 is a recorded fact not an assumption; ORC 4301.22 age gates are conditions on the person SELLING -- under 18 no handling at all, under 19 no beer across a bar, under 21 no wine or spirits -- all six boundaries unit-tested. Stripe/Square are recorded as payment RAILS and nothing is charged, said on screen because a payment field that looks live and is not is the silent-failure class. GUESTS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT MODELLED AND THAT IS A FINDING: the liquor research found no statute, no rule and no definition of "guest" anywhere in Ch. 4301:1-1 -- nothing permitting accompanied guests and nothing prohibiting them; every Ohio lodge serves them in practice with no verified basis in either direction, so the POS sells to a member or refuses rather than encoding an open question as settled law. Goes to counsel. 2.2 close-out COMPUTES expected cash from the cash-rail sales rung that day instead of accepting a typed figure, and records the variance instead of absorbing it. 2.3 bottle fill is AUTOMATIC ONLY per the binding patent-avoidance decision -- photograph to the vision endpoint, and on ANY failure NOTHING is recorded; there is no slider, no tap-to-set and no typed fallback, because a typed fallback IS the adjustable guess the decision bars, and the browser test asserts no range input exists anywhere. 2.5 hall rental models three permit cases and REFUSES the third rather than pricing it -- a private/commercial renter wanting alcohol has no verified path since the D-4 reaches members only and the joint F-2 (OAC 4301:1-1-36(B)) is open only to a not-for-profit organised for a charitable/cultural/educational/fraternal/political purpose; verified live that the booking shows NO CHARGE AT ALL and is not stored. F-2 conditions enforced: 4 consecutive days max, one per 30 days per renter, $150+$10, peace-officer notification, proceeds destination. 2.7 IS THREE THINGS, NOT ONE: (a) comparing vendors already on file is grounded, deterministic, zero fabrication surface, needs no model; (b) market search exists ONLY for food, gaming supplies and entertainment, requires a real web_search CITATION and DISCARDS any answer without one -- the rule is enforced in CODE, not asked for in a prompt, which is the difference between a rule and a hope; (c) alcohol is REFUSED outright rather than warned about, because OAC 4301:1-1-43(J)(2) gives the lodge exactly one lawful vendor per brand per territory, spirits are a state monopoly at uniform price, discounts are banned both directions, and buying outside it is a first-degree misdemeanour (ORC 4301.99(C)) reaching the permit through 4301.25(A) -- THE EXPOSURE IS THE LODGE'S, which is why the flow is removed rather than captioned. A CORRECTION TO THE SPEC'S OWN FRAMING, stated because applying it would have been WRONG: neither markup floor binds this menu. Beer's 25% minimum retail markup (OAC 4301:1-1-72(B)(3)) is OFF-PREMISES only and a D-4 sells for consumption on the premises; wine's 33 1/3% (4301:1-1-03(C)) is a WHOLESALE floor governing what the lodge is CHARGED. So the app does not check menu prices against either, and does pour-cost analysis plus the posted-price audit those floors actually make possible. PHASE 2 CLOSED A PHASE 3 LOOP: ORC 2915.09(D)(1) bars compensating a bingo operator; Phase 3 shipped a MANUAL checkbox because no canteen payroll existed and inferring it would have been a fabricated check -- the roster exists now, so an operator matching PAID canteen staff BY NAME is caught automatically, and the manual flag stays because a person can be paid without being on that roster. ONE SHARED-HELPER FIX: `money()` rendered negatives as "$-4.00"; Phase 2's cash variance and Phase 3's negative gross profit are the first negative values this app has ever produced, so the sign now sits outside the symbol. ONE UX FIX with a real failure mode behind it: the event form now resets the "includes a bingo session" checkbox after a successful booking -- leaving it ticked silently created a second session on the next event, a side effect with no error. CHECKS: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance 537/537, 0 duplicate globals (151), 14 panels / 14 nav items / 0 trapped, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 duplicate ids, 0 storage-key collisions (12), 0 dead functions, no alert(), no console.log(). 42 Phase 3 + 67 Phase 2 statutory unit tests + 35 Phase 2 and 25 Phase 3 browser checks, all green locally AND against production. NOTE FOR THE NEXT SESSION: the browser harnesses originally used unscoped `text=` selectors and broke the moment Phase 2 added buttons with overlapping labels -- they are now scoped to `#panel-x >> button:has-text(...)`, and option pickers match by SUBSTRING because live labels carry suffixes like "(not dues-current)". STILL LOCAL STORAGE, still disclosed on the dashboard. STILL OPEN: Phase 4 (2915.101 tiered distribution + real net-profit distribution tracking, claimed), then Phases 5 and 6. 2026-09-01

Two polls served the OLD build first again -- third time this session that HTTP 200 was not evidence the deploy landed. Items 4.1-4.7. EVERY STATUTORY FIGURE RE-READ AGAINST PRIMARY SOURCE 2026-09-01 rather than carried from the research doc: codes.ohio.gov for ORC 2915.101, 2915.01(V) and 5739.02(B)(12); law.cornell.edu for 26 U.S.C. 170(c)(3) and (c)(4); IRS Rev. Proc. 2025-32 for the TY2026 figures. THE LOAD-BEARING FACT AND IT IS EASY TO GET BACKWARDS: TWO REGIMES SPLIT BY GAME TYPE, RUNNING AT ONCE ON DIFFERENT MONEY. 2915.01(V) opens "the net profit of bingo, OTHER THAN instant bingo or electronic instant bingo" -- the purpose-tagged regime governs TRADITIONAL bingo only, and 2915.101's percentages govern instant/e-instant with NO purpose list at all. So the app demands a (V) tag for traditional-bingo money and REFUSES to demand one for instant money; the purpose select disables itself and says why. Applying either rule to all gaming net profit would be wrong for the half it was not written for -- that is the single easiest way to build this feature incorrectly and have it look right. 4.2 TIERS WITH THE ASYMMETRY PRESERVED: first $250,000 at least 25% distributed / up to 75% retained; above it at least 50% distributed, 5% own charitable purposes, 45% retained. THERE IS NO 5% ALLOWANCE IN THE FIRST TIER and adding one "by symmetry" would overstate what may be kept -- unit-tested in both directions. Threshold is CONFIGURATION because the section says "or the adjusted amount" and the AG may move it for CPI. NO DISTRIBUTION DEADLINE IS INVENTED: the section sets none, so the app reports a running position rather than a missed date. EXPENSES ARE ALLOCATED, NOT MEASURED, AND THE SCREEN SAYS SO -- payments are per ACCOUNT and the session account lawfully holds both traditional and instant bingo; the statute prescribes no allocation method so gross profit within each account is the basis, disclosed as an estimate honest about being one. 4.1 TWO INDEPENDENT TAXONOMIES: (V)(2) is the 11-item 5739.02(B)(12) list PLUS scholarships / governmental agency / nonprofit youth activities / flags / patriotism / disaster relief = 17; (V)(3) is SIX, contains none of those words, and carries the fifteen-years-in-Ohio precondition which the app refuses against. National categories start EMPTY on purpose -- the five orders' reporting forms differ and none was read, so an invented list would look authoritative and be wrong. 4.3 cost-to-post is REFUSED as a (V)(2) destination (every limb of (V)(2) is money LEAVING the post) and correctly NOT refused for a fraternal lodge, since (V)(2) is the veterans limb -- both directions tested. 4.4/4.5 A THIRD LEGAL REGIME SPLITTING ON THE SAME VETERANS/FRATERNAL AXIS as the officer titles and the (V) lists: 170(c)(3) deducts a war-veterans post's gifts with NO donor-type and NO use condition; 170(c)(4) deducts a fraternal lodge's ONLY "In the case of a contribution or gift by an individual" AND "only if ... used exclusively for religious, charitable, scientific, literary, or educational purposes, or for the prevention of cruelty to children or animals" -- so the receipt is a GATE, not a sentence, and an entity's gift to a lodge produces a receipt that says NOT DEDUCTIBLE. Quid-pro-quo runs BOTH safe harbours: the emblem route ($69.50 minimum payment, $13.90 low-cost article) and the 2% route capped at $139, with 26 U.S.C. 6115's value statement emitted above $75 ($75 is STATUTORY and not inflation-indexed, unlike the other three). THE FIGURES LIVE IN A PER-YEAR TABLE CITING THE REVENUE PROCEDURE and an unloaded year yields NO CONCLUSION rather than a stale one -- Publication 1771 still prints 2023 numbers and is not the source. NOTHING IS EMAILED AND THAT IS STATED AS A GAP: the spec calls for automatic emailed receipts, there is no email endpoint for this app and no server table to queue against, so receipts are GENERATED for the post to send. Claiming otherwise is exactly the failure that left api/sairndental/send-reminder.js firing a 500 every hour for months. 4.6 outreach suggestions are checked IN CODE against this org's own enumerated purposes and DISCARDED if they name none -- same enforcement-in-code discipline as the Phase 2 market-search citation rule. 4.7 hours are DECIMAL (five minutes = 0.08), person-miles are round trip x occupants (easy to under-report by the whole carpool), the reporting period is configuration because the orders run four unshared fiscal years, and the panel states these hours are NOT Chapter 2915 records -- the two never reconcile and are not meant to. ONE FIX: the first Advance on a recognition award re-asserted "earned", which looked like the button did nothing; "earned" is DERIVED from the lifetime total so the first action is now "ordered". Signing is ECDSA P-256 via Web Crypto over a CANONICAL serialisation with recursively sorted keys -- JSON.stringify preserves insertion order, so structurally identical objects would otherwise sign differently. Round-tripped live: genuine file verifies, one altered figure is REJECTED not caveated, a swapped public key fails, and a lie about the stated fingerprint fails even though the signature itself was valid. Trust-on-first-use key distribution, verified by re-keying and re-importing: the change is flagged loudly because only the district can tell a rotated key from a different signer. COMPLIANCE ROW INCLUDED per Michael, BY CITATION AND COUNT NEVER BY NAME -- and the Phase 2 payroll cross-check reaches it, so an operator who is also paid canteen staff surfaces under 2915.09(D)(1). PHASE 5's two sharp edges were built first and most carefully. 5.6 SCOPED CONSENT AS AN ENUM, NEVER A BOOLEAN: ORC 2741.01(B)(4) makes FUNDRAISING a commercial purpose, so the same photograph of a child is lawful in a results story and prohibited in a donation appeal, and the standard Elks release is scoped to programme promotion only -- a boolean cannot express that, so it is not one. Programmes are NAMED PER ORDER and STRUCTURALLY DIFFERENT: Elks competitions with age divisions, VFW award-plus-curriculum with no bracket, Mooseheart an ongoing residential commitment that is not a contest at all -- a contest/round/winner schema fits one and breaks the others. LEGION AND EAGLES ARE EMPTY ON PURPOSE and say why; Eagles specifically could not be researched because the Grand Aerie Constitution is sold as a physical product. AN ARCHITECTURAL INVARIANT WITH AN OWNER: no child ever types into this app -- COPPA defines collection as gathering information FROM a child, so it does not reach an officer entering a reference, and it applies the moment a child enters anything; there is deliberately no participant-facing input anywhere in the product. 5.7 THE ABSENCE IS THE DESIGN: there is no field for what a claim is about, because 38 U.S.C. 5901 bars preparation/presentation/prosecution without recognition -- the app logs that a referral and appointment happened and holds none of the substance; available to EVERY order since Elks and Moose lodges run veteran services too; no fee may attach anywhere. SPEC 9d IS NOW ENFORCED RATHER THAN RECORDED -- Phase 1 surfaced the claim-strategy refusal as a NOTICE because no AI existed that could be asked; three now do, so it is in the SYSTEM PROMPT of all three (market search, outreach, newsletter) as a refusal not a disclaimer, asserted by a test that counts occurrences. 5.1 ONE WRITE PATH: onboarding fills the Members panel's own inputs and calls its handler, so admission cannot drift between two places; and spec 1d is finally IMPLEMENTED rather than described -- the banner goes STATIC on onboarding and animated everywhere else, browser-verified both ways. 5.2 a signature binds to the document VERSION and a HASH OF ITS TEXT so editing a signed document produces a new version instead of silently changing what somebody agreed to, and a typed name not matching the signer is refused; NO TEMPLATE SHIPS because a plausible-looking liability waiver written by software is worse than a blank box -- it looks checked. 5.3 casings tracked issued/recovered/presented with an impossible count REFUSED and a shortfall surfaced; assisting posts recorded as entity IDs, matching the district roll-up. 5.4 an odometer that goes backwards is refused. 5.5 THE NEWSLETTER IS CHECKED, NOT TRUSTED -- given an explicit fact table, every number in the draft matched back in code. THE WEBHOOK IS AN ADVISORY MIRROR, NOT A GRANT, and that is the load-bearing decision: verify.js still re-checks the real subscription every app load and remains the authority, nothing reads the mirror to decide entitlement, so a wrong RTDB rule (those live in the Firebase console, outside this repo and outside review here) costs a stale banner rather than free access. It is NOT the agent webhook renamed -- that writes Supabase sairn_agents and links by metadata.agent_id; SAIRNcash has no subscription table and its data lives in Firebase RTDB. What it adds over polling, since polling already covers revocation: promptness, an audit trail, and DUNNING, which polling cannot see at all -- invoice.payment_failed is invisible as "inactive" until Stripe gives up, so an expired card looks fully active right until cut-off. TWO TESTED CORRECTNESS GUARDS: audit entries keyed by Stripe's own event id so a retry overwrites rather than duplicates, and an ordering guard on Stripe's event timestamp because delivery order is not guaranteed and a late retry of an OLD event would otherwise resurrect a cancelled customer -- the stale event is still recorded, because "arrived late" is worth knowing. - sairnlegacy.html (Fourth) -- KEY-COLLISION INVESTIGATION: THE ONE THAT WAS REAL WAS NOT THE ONE REPORTED. Pushed `e5306ac`, live-verified 8/8 race checks + 20/20 predictive checks against https://sairn.vercel.app/sairnlegacy, zero page errors (two polls served the old build first, again). key_collision_check flags a key written from two differently-named VARIABLES; it cannot tell a lost update from a correct re-read, so both findings were checked against the DATA by driving the real functions rather than by reading them. leg_vehicles (saveVehicle/saveMaintenance) is NOT A BUG -- both read, mutate and write SYNCHRONOUSLY, finishing before either touches an await, so neither can observe the other mid-flight; proved by running them genuinely overlapped with sdnData slowed so saveMaintenance starts inside saveVehicle's await, and the new vehicle, the maintenance mileage update and the service record all survive. leg_preneed (savePreneed/confirmPayment) has the identical shape and is benign for the identical reason -- st('leg_preneed',plans) runs before the first await. THE REAL ONE WAS confirmReserve, found by scanning for the SHAPE rather than the symptom: read a collection, await, then write that same snapshot back. It is the only place in the file that does it. The 409 rollback wrote back the array captured BEFORE the network round-trip, so a rejected reservation silently reverted EVERY other change made to leg_merch_units while the request was in flight -- a release, a mark-sold, another unit's reservation -- despite only ever meaning to undo its own unit. Now re-reads and patches the single record it owns, and only if that record is still ITS reservation: a newer state written during the await is more correct than the one being rolled back to, and clobbering it would be the same bug pointing the other way. PROVED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS, which is the part that matters -- the same suite scores 6/8 against the pre-fix code (a unit sold during the in-flight request comes back Available, and a newer state for the reserving unit is overwritten) and 8/8 with the fix. THE COLLISION COUNT WENT 2 -> 3 AND THAT IS THE FIX, NOT A REGRESSION: re-reading before a post-await write necessarily introduces a second backing variable for the key, which is exactly what the checker counts -- the heuristic flags the CORRECT pattern, so that number must not be treated as a score to drive to zero. A PROCESS NOTE FOR THE NEXT SESSION: `git stash -q && cd <elsewhere> && ... ; git stash pop` runs the pop in the WRONG DIRECTORY and reports "No stash entries found" while the work sits safely in the repo's stash -- looked like data loss for a moment, was not. Run git from the repo or use absolute -C. CHECKS: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance DIFF:0, 268 globals 0 duplicates, 0 missing DOM targets, CRLF preserved. 2026-09-02
- sairncare.html (Fourth) -- SCOPED AGAINST THE LIVE APP FIRST, THEN FIXED THE ONE REAL DEFECT CLASS. Pushed `16da93c`, live-verified 14/14 browser checks against https://sairn.vercel.app/sairncare with the production before/after probe confirming clean, zero page errors (two polls served the old build first, again). THE REAL ONE: 17 calendar dates derived with `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10)`, which reads the UTC calendar date -- a day ahead of the facility's own from roughly 8pm Eastern onward, EVERY EVENING. Not cosmetic here because these land on records a regulator reads: a MEDICATION ADMINISTRATION on the evening med pass stamped tomorrow IN THE eMAR (and the controlled-substance count, the med reconciliation and the refusal alongside it); an INCIDENT REPORT -- a fall on the evening shift dated the day after it happened, in a record state licensing requires; `currentMonth()`, which rolls to the next month on the last evening of every month and which BILLING keys off; and `certDaysUntil()`, off by one for part of every day so a staff credential reads as expiring a day late. PROVED BEFORE AND AFTER with the browser clock pinned to 2026-09-30 21:30 America/New_York -- an evening where UTC has already rolled AND it is the last day of the month, the worst case for billing -- using a probe that references NOTHING existing only after the fix, so the same script runs against both versions: BEFORE eMAR 2026-10-01 / incident 2026-10-01 / billing month 2026-10; AFTER 2026-09-30 / 2026-09-30 / 2026-09. Fix is `alfLocalToday()`, same helper and reasoning as sairnvet.html's `localToday()`. SCOPE STATED IN THE CODE: calendar-date derivations only -- the one bare `toISOString()` storing an INSTANT (superseded_at) is correct and untouched because a timestamp is a moment not a calendar day, and `Date.UTC(y,m,0).getUTCDate()` for days-in-month is correct UTC arithmetic. TWO FALSE ALARMS CHECKED RATHER THAN FIXED, per tonight's sairnlegacy lesson: (1) duplicate ids `sc-census`/`sc-staff` live in MUTUALLY EXCLUSIVE branches of a state-specific template (OH/IN/MI/else, each ASSIGNING html= rather than appending) with each branch's reader in the matching branch, so only one is ever in the DOM; (2) seven key collisions are all the `['list','local']` hydrate-from-server merge, and each reads local INSIDE the `.then` -- after the network completes -- then appends only ids not already present, which is the correct post-await re-read, the same pattern confirmReserve was fixed to use an hour earlier, and the checker counts it as a collision only because it necessarily uses a second variable name. ALSO VERIFIED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: all seven v1 panels from `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-20-sairncare-v1-scope.md` exist (12 panels total); the alf_* resources are provisioned and session-gated live, CONTRAST-TESTED against a bogus resource name exactly as that doc's own verification plan requires (bogus returns the allowlist, real returns NO_SESSION); the 15 static KPI values in markup are all 0/$0 placeholders overwritten by JS, NOT fabricated numbers -- the opposite of what sairnvet's dashboard had. STILL OPEN AND NOT MINE TO DECIDE: the scope doc's own "Open question for Michael" -- the role vocabulary and panel list came from external research rather than a real facility SOP, and it flags that as needing a real operator's review before launch. That remains true and unaddressed. Of that doc's six items, five are built and the sixth (CPT BYO) is blocked on an AMA developer licence, which is external. THE FILE IS UNUSUALLY CLEAN on tonight's defect classes: 0 UTC calendar-date derivations (a localToday helper already exists, unlike sairncare which had 17), 0 missing DOM targets, 0 duplicate ids, 0 storage-key collisions, 0 undefined handlers, 0 inert buttons, 0 alert(), 0 console.log(), and the 7 static kpi-values in markup are all placeholders with no non-zero digit. ONE REAL DEFECT, SAME SHAPE AS sairnlegacy's confirmReserve EARLIER TONIGHT -- read a record, await, write the pre-await snapshot back. `scSaveSettings()` did `var prior = scSettingsRecord()`, optimistic write, `await scData('write','sc_settings',rec)`, then on refusal `localStorage.setItem('sc_settings', JSON.stringify([prior]))`. The merge-on-write was always right; the ROLLBACK was not -- it restored the WHOLE pre-await record, so anything another scSaveSettings() call changed during the round trip was silently discarded. THE FILE ITSELF DOCUMENTS THAT MORE THAN ONE PANEL WRITES THIS SAME 'default' ROW: retention lives in Privacy & Data, practice name and payer schedules live in Settings, and all four callers funnel through here -- so save a payer schedule successfully, have a practice-name save refused a moment later, and the payer schedule vanishes with nothing said about it. Now re-reads and reverts ONLY the keys this call set, back to their prior values, leaving every other field as it currently stands; a key that did not exist before is REMOVED rather than left behind; and the returned record is what was actually stored rather than the stale snapshot. PROVED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS with a probe touching only public behaviour so it runs unchanged against either version: 8/10 against HEAD (the concurrent payer schedule reads back as 0 AND the returned record disagrees with storage) and 10/10 with the fix. THIS IS NOW THE THIRD INSTANCE OF THIS EXACT SHAPE FOUND TONIGHT (sairnlegacy confirmReserve, sairncode scSaveSettings, and the sairncare hydrate-merge which was checked and found CORRECT) -- the read-await-write scan is worth keeping as a standing check, because none of the existing tools look for it and key_collision_check actively mislabels the correct version of the pattern as a collision. - sairncare.html (Fourth) -- eMAR RACE FIXED, the worst instance of tonight's recurring shape. Pushed `ebaa952`, live-verified 6/6 race + 14/14 date checks against production, zero page errors. FOUND BY A CROSS-APP SCAN FOR THE SHAPE rather than any reported symptom: read a collection, await, write the pre-await snapshot back. `reviewPharmacyOrder()` did `var list=marEntries()`, awaited the server write, then patched `list[idx]` and `st('alf_mar',list)` -- writing the WHOLE stale array. alf_mar IS THE eMAR: saveMarAdmin, saveCsCount, saveRecon and saveAssessRefusal all write it, and an evening med pass runs concurrently with a nurse working the pharmacy queue, so accepting one order could drop a MEDICATION ADMINISTRATION that had just been recorded, silently. The function's existing "server-first, no optimistic write" comment was right about the DIRECTION of the write and said nothing about the array being stale -- two independent things, only the second wrong. Now re-reads with marEntries() after the await and patches the single entry; if that entry is gone by then it reports the server accepted the change and the row is no longer in this device's list, rather than resurrecting a record the client no longer knows about. saveResident() in the SAME FILE already did it correctly, so this is an in-file precedent applied to the one place still writing a stale array. CHECKED NOT ASSUMED: the other six `st('alf_mar',list)` writes all complete BEFORE any await in their function, so a fresh read is guaranteed and none needs this; and saveResident, which the same scan flagged, is a FALSE POSITIVE because it reassigns from residents() after the await before writing -- the heuristic cannot see that. PROVED BOTH DIRECTIONS: 3/6 against HEAD (concurrent administration gone, entry count wrong, an order deleted mid-flight silently resurrected) and 6/6 with the fix. THE CROSS-APP SURVEY IS THE REUSABLE PART and is recorded here because it is cheap and nobody has it as a tool: scanning all 21 app files for (a) `new Date().toISOString().slice(0,10|7)` UTC calendar derivations and (b) the read-await-write shape produced EVERY real defect found in this stretch. Each needs the same individual judgement -- roughly half the hits tonight have been false positives, and the scan still flags sairncode's scSaveSettings AFTER its fix because the pre-await read remains, exactly like key_collision_check counting the corrected pattern as a new collision. NOT MINE TO DECIDE: I offered to add this as a tools/ check and Michael has not taken it up; it stays a recorded technique rather than a committed script. 2026-09-02
- api/_lib/ai-rate-limit.js + sql/ + tools/ (Fourth) -- THREE COMMITS. (1) `326444f` THE AI RATE LIMITER'S LIMIT WAS NEVER REAL. It did SELECT count then INSERT as TWO SEPARATE HTTP CALLS with nothing between them; Vercel runs these concurrently, so N simultaneous requests all read the SAME count, all decide they are under the limit, and all insert -- 50 requests arriving at count 199 against a limit of 200 were ALL permitted. Approximate, not enforced, and it would have STAYED approximate the moment SAIRN_AI_RATE_LIMIT_MODE=enforce was set, which is WORSE than observe mode because it would then look like a real cap. FIX: one RPC to `public.sairn_ai_rate_limit_consume()` taking a `pg_advisory_xact_lock` keyed on app_id, doing the count and the insert in one transaction -- one round trip instead of two, so faster as well as correct. WHY AN ADVISORY LOCK: there is no row to lock (append-only log, protected quantity is an aggregate, and SELECT..FOR UPDATE cannot lock rows a concurrent INSERT is about to create). A counter row with UPDATE..RETURNING would also be atomic and was REJECTED because it needs an UPDATE grant on a table that has only SELECT and INSERT, and this platform just went through a deliberate grant-narrowing sweep -- GRANT EXECUTE on one function is the narrower privilege. SECURITY INVOKER not DEFINER. A SECOND SEPARATE BUG FIXED ON THE WAY PAST: the old code set `Prefer: count=exact` and then IGNORED it, using rows.length -- the number of rows PostgREST chose to RETURN, bounded by its max-rows setting, so above that ceiling the count silently stops growing and the limit can never fire. Now reads the exact total from Content-Range and requests zero rows, which also stops shipping the whole day's log on every AI call. THE OLD PATH IS KEPT AS A FALLBACK until the migration runs and REPORTS ITSELF as `observe-racy`/`enforce-racy` with `atomic:false`, so the race is a visible property of the response rather than a silent property of the deployment. TESTED UNDER REAL CONCURRENCY with a delay between count and insert so callers genuinely interleave: ten concurrent racy calls ALL read the same count and none is flagged against a limit of 5 (the defect, reproduced); the same ten through the RPC see prior counts 0..9 all distinct with exactly the 6th-10th flagged; enforce mode permits exactly 5 of 8. LIVE: 25 CONCURRENT real requests through the deployed proxy, 25/25 HTTP 200, 25 real model responses, 0 errors, 0 demo_limit, 6.5s wall clock -- healthy under genuine concurrent load. WHAT I COULD NOT VERIFY LIVE, said plainly: the advisory lock lives in Postgres and the migration has not been run, so atomicity is proved by construction and by the SQL file's own runnable concurrency check, NOT by a live test. ALSO FOUND NOT FIXED: `api/_lib/courtlistener.js` has the IDENTICAL race, and its limits are CourtListener's documented per-minute/hour/day caps on a token shared by every SAIRNlaw firm, so exceeding them is a THIRD-PARTY problem; it uses rows.length with no count header at all. SAIRNlaw is under hank's claim -- flagged, not touched. THE PUSH GATE CAUGHT ME: I tried to commit-and-push SQL in one command and it denied it, correctly, because the hook runs before the commit exists so the outgoing-file list would have been empty. Split into two commands, no override used. (2) `fc7d102` + (3) `f73a349` THE SCAN IS NOW A REAL TOOL, `tools/sairn_stale_snapshot_scan.py`, covering both shapes that produced every real find tonight. False positives are SEPARATED into a LIKELY-OK bucket rather than auto-reported, and the header says plainly that the raw count is NOT a score and must not be driven to zero. TASK 3 -- "pick up the remaining unclaimed hits" -- RESOLVED INTO FIXING THE SCANNER, because both remaining unclaimed hits were the scanner's own defects: sairnbuild's "UTC derivation" was the TEXT OF A COMMENT describing the helper that had already replaced those 26 call sites (a fix reported as the bug it fixed -- comments are now stripped, as blank lines so line numbers stay true), and sairncash's initFirebase paired an unrelated pre-await getSub() with an unrelated post-await device-id write (a hit now requires the WRITTEN variable to be the SNAPSHOTTED one). SENSITIVITY RE-CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: run against sairncare at 16da93c~1, before tonight's fixes, the sharpened scan still finds all 17 UTC derivations and still puts reviewPharmacyOrder in the open bucket -- the narrowing removed noise, not reach. The header's original "a fixed site usually still triggers" was overstated after the sharpening and was CORRECTED rather than left standing. PLATFORM STATE: 5 UTC derivations (sairnroofing 4 with NO local-date helper, stonedesk-hr 1 with none) and 1 stale-snapshot site (stonedesk legacyRows) -- EVERY remaining hit is in a file under cc's active stonedesk-and-roofing claim, so none is mine to act on. 2026-09-02
- api/provisioner-health.js live probe + sairndental.html (Fourth) -- TWO THINGS, ONE OF THEM A CORRECTION TO MY OWN PRIOR ENTRY. (1) THE AI RATE LIMITER IS NOW GENUINELY ATOMIC IN PRODUCTION, and the entry above this one says the opposite -- it recorded "the migration has not been run, so atomicity is proved by construction and by the SQL file's own runnable concurrency check, NOT by a live test." That was true when written and is not true now. The `rate_limit_health` action added in `b91c826` was called live against https://sairn.vercel.app/api/provisioner-health and returned `{"ok":true,"atomic":true,"state":"ATOMIC","probe_status":200}` -- `public.sairn_ai_rate_limit_consume` exists and is callable by service_role, so the count and the insert happen in one transaction under an advisory lock. THE POINT OF THE PROBE IS EXACTLY THIS: the migration reached the environment at some point between the two entries and NOTHING would have reported it, which is the same trapdoor-transition blindness the file's own header was written about. It is read-only by construction -- it probes with an EMPTY app_id, which the function rejects before the lock and before the insert, so the health check cannot consume budget or add a row to the thing it measures. Concurrency re-run on the atomic path: 25 concurrent real requests through the deployed proxy, 25/25 HTTP 200, 25 real model responses, 0 errors, 0 demo_limit, 4.6s wall clock; and `api/_lib/ai-rate-limit.test.js` all-green, with ten concurrent RPC calls seeing prior counts 0..9 ALL DISTINCT and exactly the 6th-10th flagged against a limit of 5, versus the racy path where all ten read the same count. STILL OBSERVE MODE -- atomic:true is the precondition for setting SAIRN_AI_RATE_LIMIT_MODE=enforce, not a decision to set it, and that remains Michael's call on a threshold nobody has measured against real traffic. NOT FIXED AND STILL FLAGGED: `api/_lib/courtlistener.js` has the identical race against a THIRD PARTY's caps on a token shared by every SAIRNlaw firm. (2) SAIRNDENTAL CDT CODE VERSIONING BUILT (competitive-gap audit A4). Recovered a half-applied edit from an interrupted session: the markup had landed, the ~120 lines of JS behind it had not, and a failed edit had left an ORPHAN `</table>` (tag balance 16 open / 17 close) plus a duplicate `procedures-tbody` id -- an 8-column header over a 5-column render, with the whole feature inert. THE MECHANISM ONLY, NO CDT CONTENT: edition, effective window, supersession. CDT is ADA-copyright with no self-serve API, and SAIRNcode reached the same conclusion independently, so the practice's own licensed data is the only honest source; nothing is bundled. THE CHECK IS AGAINST THE DATE OF SERVICE, NOT TODAY, and that is the entire feature -- a retired code is CORRECT for a service performed while it was live and wrong only for one performed after, so banning retired codes would break legitimate back-dated claims and checking against today would flag them for the wrong reason. WIRED WHERE A REAL SERVICE DATE EXISTS rather than left as a table decoration: `openCompleteVisitModal()` checks the appointment's own `start_time`, which is the one date in this app that can differ from today. PROVED BOTH DIRECTIONS ON THE SAME CODE in a real browser -- D0999 (window 2024-01-01 -> 2025-12-31) reports "In effect" for a visit dated 2025-06-15 and "Retired ... replaced by D0121" for one dated 2026-06-15. It REPORTS, it does not block, for the reason above. "NOT VERIFIABLE" IS A REAL STATE, not a pass: every row that existed before this shipped has no window, cannot be checked, and says so -- calling it fine would be a false assurance about precisely the thing the feature exists to check. `dntServiceDate()` derives the practice's calendar date from the stored instant via local parts, NOT toISOString().slice(0,10), which is UTC's date and would push an evening appointment's date of service onto the following day -- the same defect class fixed in sairncare, sairnvet, sairnbuild and stonedesk-hr. ONE REFUSAL ADDED: an effective window whose retirement date precedes its effective date is refused at entry rather than stored, because it would make every date-of-service check on that code meaningless in both directions.
