Browser-verified: 8 header columns, 8 cells in every body row, 0 mismatch, all four CDT states render, maintenance panel populates from real counts, all eight add-form inputs read and cleared, inverted window refused and NOT stored, no horizontal overflow, 0 page errors and 0 console errors. SAIRNFREEDOM SWEEPS ALSO RUN CLEAN this session and are recorded here because the prior session left no evidence either way: 0 UTC derivations, 0 stale-snapshot sites, 25 key writes / 25 distinct keys / 0 collisions, 0 dead buttons in every actionable section. 2026-09-02
- sairngrounds.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/sairngrounds.js + sql/sairngrounds_caddie_schema.sql (Fourth) -- THE GOLF COURSE MODULE'S ON-COURSE LAYER BUILT. SAIRNGROUNDS-SCOPE.md section 5a base layer, items 1, 2, 3 and 5, plus the grounded half of item 4. THE GAP IN ONE LINE: item 10 shipped the course-mapping foundation on 2026-08-06 -- staff walk each hole and capture tee/pin/hazard points with a real GPS fix each -- and NOTHING EVER READ THOSE POINTS BACK. They were stored, counted in a badge on the Golf Course Module table, and used for nothing. This is the thing they were a foundation for. NO PAID COURSE-DATA VENDOR, unchanged: section 5a named 'licensed course mapping data (Golf Intelligence API or equivalent)' and the course's OWN mapped points are the 'or equivalent', which is exactly why the iGolf Connect path could be dropped without losing the feature. THE WHOLE RISK OF THIS FEATURE IS A PRECISION LIE, so that is what the design is built around: a laser rangefinder reads to about a yard and consumer phone GPS does not -- getCurrentPosition reports 5-15m routinely, which is 5-16 YARDS -- so a bare '147 yd' on screen would claim precision the hardware cannot deliver and a player would pick a club on it. FIVE RULES, EACH BROWSER-PROVED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS: (1) every distance carries the error band its own inputs support, and gcdFmtDistance() is the ONLY renderer so a bare number is not something a future caller can produce by forgetting; (2) the band is the REAL reported accuracy_m of the fixes involved -- the mapped pin's stored accuracy plus the live fix's -- ADDED, not averaged, because averaging would understate the one number that must not flatter; (3) a combined band over 25m yields NO yardage at all, since +/-27 yards is wider than the gap between clubs and is noise wearing a distance's clothes; (4) NO PIN MAPPED means no distance and an instruction to go map it -- the zone centroid is NOT substituted, and averaging a tee and a hazard into a 'pin' would be fabricated geometry; (5) the panel says in words that it is not a rangefinder. cmCaptureGps() already stored accuracy_m per point precisely so it could be honoured here rather than discarded -- that discipline paying off two features later. PACE OF PLAY IS THE LOOP BACK INTO OPERATIONS (item 5) and only complete pairs count: a hole arrived at and never left has no elapsed time and is EXCLUDED, not estimated from the round's end or the next hole's start, both of which would attribute walking and waiting to the wrong hole. MEDIAN NOT MEAN, and the test proves which -- samples 12/20/60 minutes give a median of 20 against a mean of 31, so one group that stopped for lunch on the 9th cannot drag a fine hole into looking slow. NO TARGET PACE IS INVENTED: a course's target is a local decision and building one in would grade every hole against a figure nobody set. ITEM 4 BUILT ONLY WHERE IT RESTS ON REAL DATA: a cart order hooks the EXISTING Merchandise/Bar catalogue and carries the player's hole and GPS position so the shop can find them -- and it is a REQUEST, not a sale, because the till that takes money and moves stock already exists in the Pro Shop and Bar panels and a second one would give two places to ring the same sale. An order with no GPS fix is REFUSED rather than sent, since a delivery with no position is worse than none. THE AI READ IS GROUNDED OR ABSENT: given only the measured distance with its band, the hole's own condition note and days since serviced, and the Weather Command Engine's current reading; where all three are missing NO CALL IS MADE AT ALL, because asking anyway is how a plausible invention reaches a screen. Its system prompt bars inventing a layout, hazard, slope, green speed or pin position, and bars recommending a club, since club distance is personal and the app does not know the player's. ONE REAL TRAP CAUGHT AND CLOSED IN THE UI: the hole PICKER and the hole ARRIVED AT are different things -- a player can legitimately range the next green -- so where they differ the panel says so, and the shots table names the hole its rows attach to; otherwise a shot would file quietly onto the wrong hole. ONE REAL DEFECT CAUGHT BEFORE PUSH BY VERIFYING RATHER THAN ASSUMING: adding the handler branches to api/sd-data.js is NOT enough -- the resource allowlist lives in api/_resources/sairngrounds.js, a separate file, and without that line both routes would have 400'd on the allowlist before ever reaching the branch. Found by probing the live endpoint for the resource name rather than by reading the diff, and it is the same two-places-one-change shape that file's own header was written about. WEATHER IS READ, NOT REFETCHED -- gcdWeatherSignal() reads the already-running Weather Command Engine's #grd-wx-status the same way schedWeatherCheck() and irrWeatherCheck() do, so there is no second geolocation prompt and no second Open-Meteo call. NOT BUILT, AND SAID PLAINLY RATHER THAN FAKED: tee-time booking mid-round (section 5a item 4) -- there is no tee sheet anywhere in this app, and a booking system is its own module, not a caddie feature; and AI purchase-history upsells, because msb_sales rows are not linked to a player, so any 'personalised' upsell would be invented. STILL NEEDS MICHAEL: sql/sairngrounds_caddie_schema.sql has NOT been run, so grd_rounds and grd_cart_orders are localStorage-only today and the app says so on every failed sync ('Order saved on this device only -- server sync failed, so the shop may not see it. Radio it through.'). VERIFIED: 3/3 script blocks node --check clean, div balance 894/894, 23 panels / 23 nav / 0 trapped, 0 duplicate ids, 0 duplicate globals, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 dead functions (277), 0 dead buttons in every actionable section, reachability clean, strict-args clean, 0 console.log, 0 UTC calendar derivations, 0 stale-snapshot sites, CRLF preserved. The 4 key collisions (grd_invoices, grd_schedule, msb_products, msb_sale_hours) are PRE-EXISTING -- re-run against HEAD to confirm, not assumed. 28 real-browser checks with geolocation and per-fix accuracy emulated, all green, zero page errors: haversine checked against an independently-known figure (0.001 deg latitude = 111.195m), the metre-to-yard constant, a 150m pin reading 164 yd +/-10 yd from a 5m fix and a 4m mapped pin, a pin-less hole showing NO yardage, a 40m-accuracy pin refused with both real accuracies named, a 100m shot reading 109 yd +/-12 yd from 5m and 6m fixes, a 40m-band shot still RECORDED but its distance withheld, the picker/arrived-hole warning in both directions, the cart order carrying hole and position, and the pace panel excluding the incomplete hole and reporting median over mean. Two api/sd-data-*.test.js suites fail at HEAD too (appointments-photos, complaints-readonly) -- checked against a stash rather than assumed mine; likewise the one failing assertion in api/_resources/extra-actions.test.js. 2026-09-02
- sairndental.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/sairndental.js + sql/sairndental_gfe_schema.sql (Fourth) -- GOOD FAITH ESTIMATES / NO SURPRISES ACT BUILT. Competitive-gap audit item A7, which recorded "Zero occurrences" in this app for a FEDERAL OBLIGATION with a hard deadline. THE AUDIT FLAGGED ITS OWN SOURCE as trade press covering ADA statements and said to verify against CMS primary -- so every rule here was read against the regulatory text itself (45 CFR 149.610 and 149.620, govinfo.gov CFR XML), and the PARAGRAPH NUMBERS ARE ON SCREEN so a practice can check the app rather than trust it. THE SHARPEST THING FOUND IN THE READ, and it is not in any summary: 149.610(b)(1)(vi)(A) and (B) OVERLAP AND CONFLICT. (A) says a service scheduled at least 3 business days out is due in 1 business day; (B) says one scheduled at least 10 business days out is due in 3. A service 12 business days out satisfies BOTH on their face and they give different answers. CMS's own decision tree reads them as tiers (3-9 -> 1 day, 10+ -> 3 days) and that is what the app shows as the deadline -- but where the two readings differ IT SAYS SO AND SHOWS THE EARLIER DATE BESIDE IT, because silently taking the later one is how a practice misses a federal deadline by two days on an app's say-so. Browser-proved in both directions: at a 5-day lead there is no alternate reading, at a 10-day lead both are shown. SECOND FINDING: THERE IS NO LIMB BELOW 3 BUSINESS DAYS. A service scheduled 2 business days out triggers no deadline from scheduling at all, so the app reports NO DEADLINE and says explicitly that this is NOT overdue -- reporting it as overdue would invent an obligation the rule does not create. A request still starts (C)'s 3-business-day clock regardless, and the app says which limb is doing the work. THIRD: "BUSINESS DAY" IS NOT DEFINED IN THE RULE, so the app counts weekdays and does NOT silently drop federal holidays -- it computes the weekday answer and then NAMES any holiday in the window with the earlier date, leaving the call to the practice. Quietly excluding them would be a reading of an undefined term presented as arithmetic. The eleven holidays of 5 U.S.C. 6103 are COMPUTED with the Saturday->Friday / Sunday->Monday observance shift, never a hardcoded table that goes stale next year -- verified against four independently-known 2026 dates including Independence Day falling on a Saturday and observed 2026-07-03. ELIGIBILITY IS A QUESTION, NOT AN INFERENCE: 149.610(a)(2)(xiii) covers an insured patient who does not seek to have a claim submitted, and (b)(1)(ii) requires the provider to ASK -- so a patient with a payer on file is NOT auto-excluded; the app says the question must be put. Also surfaced: (b)(1)(iv) makes ANY discussion or inquiry about potential cost a request, so "what will this run me?" starts the clock whether or not anyone called it one. THE COMPLETENESS GATE REFUSES RATHER THAN WARNS. Every required element of (c)(1) that the record cannot supply is named BY PARAGRAPH -- patient DOB, a service code and expected charge on every line, and the (c)(1)(v) identifiers -- and "Mark Issued" is BLOCKED while any is missing. An estimate handed to a patient missing a required element is a non-compliant document that looks compliant, which is worse than none: the practice believes the obligation is met. (c)(1)(v) NEEDED NEW FIELDS -- practice legal name, NPI, TIN and State did not exist anywhere in this app, so the panel captures them. THEY LIVE ON THE dnt_settings ROW, NOT ON EVERY ESTIMATE, because they are properties of the practice and copying them per row creates a second source that drifts. TWO REAL LOST-UPDATE BUGS CLOSED IN THE PROCESS, both introduced by that decision and both caught before push: (1) saveBookingSettings() rebuilt the settings record from only its own form fields, so saving booking settings would have WIPED the NPI/TIN -- it now merges onto the existing record; (2) dntSyncFromServer() REPLACES dnt_settings_obj wholesale with the server's row, so an identity saved only locally would have been silently erased by the next sync and the practice would have found out when a supposedly-compliant estimate reported a missing NPI -- saveGfeIdentity() now writes through to the server itself and says plainly when it could not. IT COMPOSES WITH THIS MORNING'S CDT WORK, which is why it was the right next pick: a GFE lists service codes, and each line is checked against the CDT effective window for the DATE OF SERVICE -- browser-verified that a code retired 2025-12-31 shows "Retired" on an estimate for a 2026-09-16 service, on the printed document, not just in a table. THE DISPUTE DISCLOSURE USES REAL FIGURES: $400 from 149.620(a)(2)(ii) ("at least $400 more") and 120 calendar days from 149.620(c)(1), with the threshold computed off this estimate's own total. NOT BUILT AND SAID ON SCREEN RATHER THAN IMPLIED: co-provider estimates. 149.610(c)(1)(iii) requires the itemised list grouped by provider and facility and (b)(1)(v) requires contacting co-providers within 1 business day; SAIRNdental gathers none of that and the panel says so. AND THE HONEST GAP IN MY OWN RESEARCH: whether HHS's 2022 enforcement discretion on co-provider charges still stands could NOT be confirmed against a primary source -- searches surfaced only the original 2022 window -- so nothing in the app assumes it does. ACCESS GATES CHOSEN DELIBERATELY, not inherited: dnt_gfe is registered in api/sd-data.js as BOTH financial (owner + front desk, the roles that actually issue estimates) AND patient-scoped on patient_id (it names one patient and carries their date of birth, so practice-wide visibility would undo the scoping dnt_patients enforces). The 1 key collision (dnt_settings_obj) is PRE-EXISTING and was re-checked against HEAD. 43 real-browser checks, all green, zero page errors -- business-day arithmetic against independently-known dates, all four federal-holiday observance shapes, all three (b)(1)(vi) limbs plus the sub-3-day no-deadline case, both eligibility directions, the refusal-to-issue, and every one of the ten (c)(1) content elements present on the rendered document. - sairnsenior.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/sairnsenior.js + sql/sairnsenior_referrals_schema.sql (Fourth) -- REFERRAL-SOURCE CRM BUILT. Competitive-gap audit item A7, verified still at zero before starting ("referral" and "referrer" both 0 occurrences at HEAD). PICKED BECAUSE THE AUDIT CALLS IT A DIFFERENTIATOR, NOT CATCH-UP: Mosai is built entirely around it and claims 10/10 of the top US home-health providers, while AxisCare, Aaniie, Alora and KanTime do not publish it at all -- so it is Tier A whitespace rather than a feature everyone else already has. It also needs no external vendor, unlike A1 (EVV transmission) and A4 (837), which is why it was buildable honestly tonight. THE DESIGN RULE THAT MAKES IT WORTH HAVING: a referral CRM is trivially easy to fill with numbers nobody can check, so EVERY figure is computed at render time from records the app already holds -- an admitted referral links to a real sen_clients row and the hours and revenue for a source are read back through that link from sen_visits and sen_claims. NOTHING IS STORED PRE-AGGREGATED, because a stored total drifts from the claims it came from and then has to be believed rather than checked. FOUR REFUSALS, each browser-proved: (1) CONVERSION COUNTS DECIDED REFERRALS ONLY -- admitted plus declined plus lost; a pending referral is in neither half and a source with nothing decided reads "--", never 0% or 100%, the same discipline SAIRNgrounds' quote win-rate uses; (2) AN ADMITTED REFERRAL WITH NO LINKED CLIENT CONTRIBUTES ZERO hours and zero revenue and is reported as unlinked, because attributing a guess would make the strongest-looking source the one with the sloppiest data entry; (3) SAMPLE SIZE IS PRINTED BESIDE EVERY RATE -- one-for-one is not a better source than twelve-for-twenty and a bare percentage says it is; (4) THERE IS NO SCORE AND NO RANKING, which would be an invented weighting presented as a measurement. THE SHARPEST THING IN THIS BUILD IS A PERMISSION-SHAPED FABRICATION AND IT IS SILENT: sen_claims is management-only, so a coordinator opening this panel has an EMPTY claims list and a revenue column would render $0 -- a fabricated figure produced by a permission check, exactly Guardian Check 0b's class. Revenue therefore reports "not visible to your role" for anyone who is not management, with an on-screen note naming the sen_claims gate. Verified in BOTH role directions in a real browser: owner sees $850, coordinator sees the refusal and NO $0 anywhere, and the coordinator still sees every non-financial figure. DELIVERED HOURS COUNT CLOCKED TIME ONLY, reusing the authorisation burn-down's own rule -- a scheduled visit nobody clocked is not delivered time; proved with a seeded 8-hour scheduled-but-never-clocked visit that correctly contributes nothing (4h + 2.5h = 6.5h, not 14.5h). THREE ENTRY REFUSALS rather than silent back-fills: a decided referral with no decision date is REFUSED and explicitly not assumed to be today (it would otherwise sit in the conversion denominator with an unknowable duration); a decision date before the referral date is refused; a referral with no source is refused because it cannot be measured against one. Each proved to leave the record COUNT UNCHANGED, not just to show a toast. THE OUTREACH LOG RECORDS WHAT HAPPENED AND NOTHING ELSE -- no automated next-best-action, which would be invented advice on a relationship the app cannot see. ACCESS GATE CHOSEN, NOT INHERITED: sen_referral_sources and sen_referrals are gated on SEN_CLIENT_BROAD_READ_ROLES (owner, billing, coordinator, scheduler) for BOTH read and write. Not management-only like sen_claims -- coordinators and schedulers take the referral call and putting the pipeline out of their reach is how a pipeline goes stale. Not every employee like the sen_caregivers read gate -- a referral row names a PROSPECTIVE client and the reason they were referred, which is PHI about someone who is not yet a client. Read and write are the same set deliberately: whoever can see a referral is whoever records its outcome. HYDRATION USES THE POST-AWAIT RE-READ -- local is read INSIDE the .then, after the network completes, the pattern standardised after confirmReserve. ONE TEST BUG FOUND AND IT WAS MINE, reported because it cuts the right way: I asserted the aggregate KPI at 67%, and the app said 75%. The app was right -- 67% is Riverside alone (2 of 3), 75% is all sources (3 admitted of 4 decided); the KPI is the aggregate and the table is per source and they are correctly different numbers. The assertion was corrected, not the code. The 4 key collisions (sen_clients, sen_caregivers, sen_visits, sen_claims) are PRE-EXISTING hydrate-merge patterns, re-checked against HEAD. 26 real-browser checks green across two roles, zero page errors. STILL NEEDS MICHAEL: sql/sairnsenior_referrals_schema.sql has not been run, so referrals are localStorage-only and every failed sync says so. 2026-09-02
- sairnsenior.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/sairnsenior.js + sql/sairnsenior_training_schema.sql (Fourth) -- CAREGIVER TRAINING-HOUR TRACKING BUILT. Competitive-gap audit item A6, verified still at zero before starting ("training" 0 occurrences at HEAD). State licensure and the federal conditions of participation both set in-service hour rules, and CareAcademy exists as a whole add-on category because agencies buy a second product for this. THE CLAIM WAS WRITTEN BY HAND AND WHY IS ON THE RECORD: sairn_claim.py BLOCKED it on a purely lexical overlap -- the only shared word with both blocking claims was "gap" (cody on sairnroofing warranty registration gap A1, hank on sairndental payer enrolment gap B1). Different apps, different files, different subjects. Per CLAUDE.md I said so out loud and wrote the claim directly rather than rewording the task string to slip past the matcher, which is how a gate gets hollowed out. That is defect 1 in CLAUDE.md's own list for that tool, now seen in the wild a second time. THE ONE THING THAT MAKES THIS DANGEROUS TO BUILD CARELESSLY, and it is the same shape as SAIRNfreedom's two-regimes finding: THE HOUR FIGURES ARE NOT UNIVERSAL AND THE WRONG ONE LOOKS RIGHT. Three rules, each read against PRIMARY SOURCE during the build, each scoped differently: 42 CFR 484.80(d) -- home health aide at a Medicare-certified agency, "at least 12 hours of in-service training during each 12-month period", RN-supervised; ORC 5164.913(A)(1) -- personal care aide under the Ohio integrated care delivery system, 30 hours pre-service and SIX hours in-service every 12 months, with (A)(2) CAPPING both so the department may not require more; OAC 5160-46-12(D)(3) -- non-licensed direct care staff at an Ohio home care waiver agency provider, 12 hours every 12 months, effective 2025-09-22. SIX VERSUS TWELVE IS A 2x ERROR on a survey-citable requirement. So a rule is DATA carrying its own citation and its own scope, a caregiver now carries the aide type and programme that select it (two new fields), and matching is EXACT on both -- a near-miss is not a match, because the near-misses in this domain are precisely the 6-versus-12 pairs. Browser-proved: the PCA gets the 6-hour rule and the HHA the 12-hour one, and the PCA's 6 hours read MET while the same 6 hours under the federal rule would read short. TWO STATES THAT ARE DELIBERATELY NOT VERDICTS. A caregiver with no aide type or programme recorded reads NO RULE ON FILE and the panel says nothing is asserted either way; a caregiver whose recorded scope matches no rule reads the same and says explicitly that this is not a pass and not a failure. CLEARING SOMEONE AGAINST A REQUIREMENT THAT DOES NOT APPLY TO THEM IS AS WRONG AS INVENTING ONE THAT DOES, and a compliance panel that defaults to the nearest figure does exactly that. Proved both ways, including the case that matters most: emptying the rules table makes ALL FOUR caregivers read no-rule and NONE read compliant. EVERY OTHER JURISDICTION SHIPS EMPTY ON PURPOSE -- a plausible invented figure for a state nobody read would look authoritative and be wrong, same reasoning as SAIRNfreedom's national categories. A RULE WITH NO CITATION IS REFUSED at entry: an uncheckable requirement inside a compliance tool is indistinguishable from an invented one, and the refusal says so. A completion date in the future is refused rather than clamped, because clamping silently changes what the agency recorded. Both proved to leave the record count unchanged, not merely to show a toast. HOURS COUNT INSIDE THE RULE'S OWN ROLLING WINDOW, computed from local calendar parts and not from a UTC date extraction which would move the boundary by a day every evening -- proved with a training 400 days old that correctly does not count, and with pre-service hours that correctly do not count toward in-service. The seed guards on the key being ABSENT, not falsy, so an emptied table stays empty (the standing trap this platform documents). ACCESS GATE: both resources ride the same intake-role gate as the referral CRM (owner, billing, coordinator, scheduler), because schedulers decide who can be sent to a visit and need to see standing, while a caregiver has no business reading the roster's compliance position; the shared branch's messages were generalised so a training 404 names the training schema file rather than the referrals one. VERIFIED: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance DIFF:0, 12 panels / 12 nav, 0 duplicate ids, 0 duplicate globals, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 dead functions (166), 0 dead buttons, reachability clean, strict-args clean, 0 console.log, 0 UTC calendar derivations, 0 stale-snapshot sites, SQL preflight clean, CRLF preserved. The 4 key collisions are PRE-EXISTING hydrate-merge patterns. 30 real-browser checks green, zero page errors. ONE BUG CAUGHT IN MY OWN GENERATED CODE before any test ran: a string-concatenation trick emitted both Edit buttons' onclick handlers with the record id left as literal source text rather than interpolated, so both would have thrown on click. Found by reading the emitted line rather than trusting the generator. STILL NEEDS MICHAEL: sql/sairnsenior_training_schema.sql has not been run, so rules and records are localStorage-only and every failed sync says so. 2026-09-02
- sairndental.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/sairndental.js + sql/sairndental_recall_schema.sql (Fourth) -- RECALL & REACTIVATION BUILT. Competitive-gap audit item A8, verified still at zero before starting ("recall" 0 occurrences at HEAD; the three "reactivat" hits were provider reactivation, unrelated). The audit called it table stakes in every product surveyed and made the sharper point that its absence from native PM tooling is WHY AN ENTIRE BOLT-ON CATEGORY EXISTS -- Weave, NexHealth, RevenueWell, Solutionreach -- which practices buy as a second product. THE THING THIS PANEL REFUSES TO DO IS ASSUME SIX MONTHS. A six-month recall is a convention, not a rule, and the profession's own guidance is that intervals should be risk-based and set per patient; shipping a default would put a number this software invented underneath a worklist a practice then acts on. So the interval is set PER PROCEDURE TYPE by the practice and blank by default (the correct answer for most codes), a PER-PATIENT override exists because the guidance is per patient, and a practice that has set none anywhere gets a PRECONDITION saying there is nothing to compute and why -- not an empty worklist. "Nobody is due" and "you have not told us what due means" are different statements, and rendering the second as the first is a clean-looking list built on nothing. Browser-proved in that state: all four KPIs read 0 and the panel explains itself. "NEVER HAD IT HERE" IS NOT "OVERDUE". Due dates count from the last COMPLETED appointment for that patient and that procedure; no such appointment means no baseline, reported as such, because counting from the day the record was created would make every new patient instantly overdue for everything. Proved twice: a CANCELLED appointment for the recall procedure is not a baseline, and a completed appointment for a DIFFERENT procedure is not one either. IT DOES NOT SEND ANYTHING and both the panel and the modal say so. Outreach is RECORDED, not performed, and logging "booked" does not create an appointment. A screen implying a reminder went out when nothing was sent is the exact silent failure that left api/sairndental/send-reminder.js returning a 500 every hour for months while everyone believed reminders were going out. MONTH ARITHMETIC IS CLAMPED, NOT OVERFLOWED: setMonth turns 31 Jan + 1 month into 3 March, which would silently skip a month on a due date -- clamped to the last day of the intended month and proved on five cases including 31 Aug + 6 months (2027-02-28, not 3 March) and 29 Feb + 12 months. All dates are local calendar parts, never a UTC extraction. ONE REAL DEFECT CAUGHT WHILE WIRING IT: checking only the patient's own phone and email would have reported EVERY PAEDIATRIC PATIENT as unreachable. This form already requires a guardian phone or email before a minor can be saved, so the contact details exist -- they are just not on the patient row. Reachability now includes guardian contact, and a minor with only guardian details is proved reachable. IT COMPOSES WITH TODAY'S CDT WORK: a recall row whose code is retired as of today is flagged, with the note separating the two -- the recall is fine, reporting that code on a service performed now is not. A future contact date is refused rather than clamped, proved to leave the record count unchanged. ONE TEST BUG WAS MINE AND IT CUT THE RIGHT WAY: I asserted a "current" patient is hidden from the worklist by matching her name and it failed -- she was on the list via a SECOND recall procedure she has never had here, which is a no-baseline row rather than a current one. The code was right; the assertion could not tell the two rows apart. Corrected the assertion, not the code. ACCESS: dnt_recall_outreach is registered patient-scoped (it names a patient and records what they said) and deliberately NOT financial -- it carries no charge, and the hygiene side works this list as much as the front desk, so the owner/front-desk money gate would hide the worklist from the people who work it. VERIFIED: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance DIFF:0, 19 panels / 19 nav, 0 duplicate ids, 0 duplicate globals, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 dead functions (198), 0 actionable dead buttons (one D2 cross-scope name, informational), reachability clean, strict-args clean, 0 console.log, 0 UTC calendar derivations, 0 stale-snapshot sites, SQL preflight clean, CRLF preserved. 35 real-browser checks green, zero page errors. STILL NEEDS MICHAEL: sql/sairndental_recall_schema.sql has not been run, so outreach is localStorage-only and every failed sync says so. 2026-09-02
- sairndental.html + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/sairndental.js + sql/sairndental_treatment_plans_schema.sql (Fourth) -- TREATMENT PLANNING BUILT. Competitive-gap audit item A9, verified still at zero before starting ("treatment plan" 0 occurrences at HEAD). The audit called it table stakes and drew the distinction that matters: it is DISTINCT FROM CHARGING A COMPLETED PROCEDURE -- this app could bill work it had already done and had no way to propose work it had not. THE LOAD-BEARING PART IS A CONFUSION THE FEATURE MUST NOT CREATE: a treatment-plan estimate and a GOOD FAITH ESTIMATE look identical on paper -- CDT codes with expected charges -- and are legally different objects. A GFE under 45 CFR 149.610 carries required content and a deadline; a plan estimate carries neither and satisfies none of it. A practice handing this to an uninsured patient believing the No Surprises Act was covered would be wrong in a way that is expensive and invisible. The panel says so, THE PRINTED PLAN REPEATS IT, and both point at the panel that does it properly. Building A9 straight after A7 is what made this visible -- the two were built by the same session a few hours apart, which is the only reason the collision was obvious rather than latent. WHAT IS COMPUTED AND WHAT IS REFUSED: fees default from the procedure's own fee and are editable; the insurance estimate comes from this practice's real dnt_coverage_rules; and where NO RULE EXISTS the app reports "no coverage rule" rather than $0, because a zero produced by an absent rule is a fabricated figure the patient-portion column would silently inherit. Proved three ways -- no rule for the procedure, no rule for the payer, no payer at all. NO TOTALS ARE STORED (a stored total drifts from the coverage rules it came from the moment one is edited, and then has to be believed rather than checked), and NOR IS "STARTED": whether an accepted plan has begun is DERIVED from real appointments for its planned procedures, proved in both directions -- booking flips it to started, cancelling flips it back, so a plan cannot report progress the schedule does not show. CASE ACCEPTANCE COUNTS DECIDED PLANS ONLY (accepted + declined); a proposed or presented plan is in neither half and a practice with nothing decided reads "--" rather than 0%, with the still-proposed plan proved excluded from the denominator rather than counted as a loss. THE CODE IS CHECKED AGAINST THE DAY THE WORK WOULD BE DONE, not today -- a plan is written before the service, so items carry an optional planned date and cdtStatusFor() is asked about THAT date. Proved with a code retiring 2026-12-31: an item planned for November reads In effect and one planned for the following February reads Retired. Planning ahead onto a code that retires before the appointment is exactly what this morning's CDT effective-window work was built for, and this is the first feature that actually exercises it. THREE ENTRY REFUSALS, each proved to leave state unchanged rather than merely to toast: an untitled plan (a patient shown two untitled plans cannot tell them apart), a plan with no items, and a ZERO-FEE ITEM, which would understate what the patient is being asked to accept. A decided plan with no decision date is refused and explicitly not back-filled with today; a future decision date is refused. TWO TEST ASSERTIONS WERE MINE AND WRONG, corrected rather than the code: I counted "no coverage rule" across the whole item box, which includes the explanatory note under the table, so it read 3 and said nothing about how many ROWS carried it (now counted in the estimate cells); and I asserted SAIRNgrounds' money format on a SAIRNdental total, where fmt() renders two decimal places. ACCESS: dnt_txplans is registered as BOTH financial (it prices what the patient is being asked to accept, same footing as dnt_charges and dnt_gfe) and patient-scoped on patient_id -- both chosen, neither inherited. VERIFIED: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance DIFF:0, 20 panels / 20 nav, 0 duplicate ids, 0 duplicate globals, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 dead functions (217), 0 actionable dead buttons (one D2 cross-scope name, informational), reachability clean, strict-args clean, 0 console.log, 0 UTC calendar derivations, 0 stale-snapshot sites, SQL preflight clean, CRLF preserved. 32 real-browser checks green, zero page errors. STILL NEEDS MICHAEL: sql/sairndental_treatment_plans_schema.sql has not been run, so plans are localStorage-only and every failed sync says so. 2026-09-02
- sairndental.html + api/sd-data.js (Fourth) -- DENIALS, APPEALS AND RECEIVABLE AGEING BUILT. Competitive-gap audit item B3, whose finding is unusual and worth restating: every enterprise vendor claims "RCM" and "claim scrubbing" and NONE published a concrete denials-and-appeals workflow, so this is a MARKET-WIDE gap rather than one vendor's weakness. VERIFIED BEFORE STARTING, AND THE STATE WAS WORSE THAN THE AUDIT SUGGESTED: "appeal" was 0 occurrences, and dnt_denial / dnt_ar / dnt_revenue -- registered server resources with real tables since the original schema -- had NO client accessor and no render function anywhere in the file. The audit's "dnt_denial + dnt_ar exist" was true of the tables and of nothing else; dntSyncFromServer even carries a comment saying so. TWO FIGURES THIS PANEL WILL NOT INVENT. (1) THE APPEAL DEADLINE comes from the payer contract and differs between payers -- there is no universal ninety days -- so the window is entered per payer WITH THE CONTRACT CLAUSE IT CAME FROM, and the source is REQUIRED and refused without it, for the same reason a training rule's citation is: an untraceable deadline is indistinguishable from a guess, and this one decides whether an appeal is worth writing. A denial from a payer with no window reads "no deadline on file" -- not timely, not late, because calling something timely against an invented deadline is worse than saying nothing, the practice then stops checking. (2) THE A/R BENCHMARK: the audit quotes ~30% of DSO claims sitting in 90+ day receivables and labels it trade-press, so it appears NOWHERE on the panel and a test asserts it does not -- a real ageing figure shown beside an unverified benchmark looks good or bad for no reason. ABANDONED APPEALS ARE EXCLUDED FROM BOTH HALVES of the success rate: walking away is not the payer deciding against you, and counting it as a loss would understate how the appeals actually argued went. Success counts DECIDED appeals only and reads "--" at a zero denominator. AGEING IS DERIVED, NEVER STORED -- computed at render time from dnt_charges and dnt_payments, payments applied oldest-charge-first because that is what an ageing report MEANS, said on screen rather than presented as this practice's allocation policy which the app has not been told. There is deliberately NO write to dnt_ar (a stored ageing row is stale the next morning), so that resource stays registered and unwritten as a decision rather than an oversight. AN UNDATED CHARGE IS EXCLUDED FROM EVERY BUCKET and counted separately -- dropping it into 0-30 would make the newest bucket absorb the oldest debt and flatter the report exactly where it should not. THE DENIAL REASON IS RECORDED, NEVER INTERPRETED: the payer's own code and wording are stored verbatim and at least one is required, because this app does not turn a denial into a root cause it guessed -- a wrong cause sends the appeal at the wrong argument and the window does not come round twice. NO NEW MIGRATION NEEDED, which is the useful part for Michael: dnt_denial's table already exists in sql/sairndental_data_schema.sql, and the appeal windows live on the dnt_settings row rather than in a table of their own -- same home and same write-through merge as the good-faith-estimate practice identity. The write-through matters: dntSyncFromServer REPLACES dnt_settings_obj wholesale, so a window saved only locally would be silently erased by the next sync, the same defect found and fixed on the GFE identity this morning. dnt_denial was already financial and was never patient-scoped because nothing read it; it joins DNT_PATIENT_SCOPED_RESOURCES now that it has a reader, since the row names a patient and carries what a payer refused to pay for their care. ONE TEST ASSERTION WAS MINE AND WRONG: I asserted the 0-30 bucket held one charge; it holds two, because the payment cleared the older charge first and the newer one survives -- which the very next assertion depends on. Corrected the assertion, not the code. VERIFIED: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance DIFF:0, 21 panels / 21 nav, 0 duplicate ids, 0 duplicate globals, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 dead functions (241), 0 actionable dead buttons, reachability clean, strict-args clean, 0 console.log, 0 UTC calendar derivations, 0 stale-snapshot sites, CRLF preserved. The one key collision (dnt_settings_obj) is PRE-EXISTING. Both dental API suites still 26/26 and 14/14. 35 real-browser checks green, zero page errors, covering all four ageing buckets, oldest-first payment application, the undated-charge exclusion, the fully-insured charge, all six appeal-window states and five entry refusals each proved to leave the record count unchanged. 2026-09-02
- sairndental.html (Fourth) -- THE CSV EXPORT EXPORTED THE SCREEN, NOT THE DATA. Found by re-checking the competitive audit's B5 line, which reads "Absent. CSV export only" -- a sentence that ASSUMES the CSV export works. It did not. Same stale-premise shape as B3's "dnt_denial + dnt_ar exist", which was true of the tables and of nothing else, and the second time today that re-verifying the audit's own premise rather than trusting it found the real problem. exportPanelCSV() scraped the rendered DOM of a panel, with four consequences each verified in a browser BEFORE the rewrite: (1) IT EXPORTED THE VIEW, NOT THE DATA -- whatever the table happened to be showing is what landed in the file, so the Recall worklist, which hides current patients unless a box is ticked, would have produced a file named for the whole dataset that silently omitted them; a plausible artefact that is quietly partial, the same failure class as a fabricated KPI and the same one StoneDesk's nesting export was caught with earlier today. (2) IT CONCATENATED EVERY TABLE IN THE PANEL with no separator and nothing saying which table a row came from -- the Denials panel has two, denials and receivable ageing, with different columns and different meanings. (3) IT EXPORTED UI TEXT: textContent of an action cell yields "Remove", a status cell yields "overdue by 317 d" -- labels drawn for a human, not values anything downstream can read. (4) IT EXISTED ON FOUR PANELS OF TWENTY-ONE, none of them clinical or financial. WHAT REPLACES IT: each export names its dataset, declares its columns, and reads them from the SAME accessor the panel reads, so an export cannot drift from what the app believes and cannot be narrowed by whatever filter is on screen; derived datasets (recall worklist, ageing buckets) call the very same functions the panel renders from. Nine datasets declared, every Export CSV button names one, and a test asserts that. AND IT SAYS WHAT IT CONTAINS -- the toast reports the row count and states the file is the full dataset rather than the filtered view, and every button carries the same in its title; the whole defect was a file that looked complete and was not, so the fix has to say so out loud. TWO SMALLER HONESTY POINTS: a heterogeneous credential (DEA registration, CE cycle and payer enrolment carry different fields on one record shape) has its type-specific half flattened into a `details` column rather than dropped, because picking one type's columns and losing the rest is the same partial-file defect in miniature; and the ageing export carries the excluded undated charges as their own labelled row so a reader can see the count that is deliberately in no bucket. Exports added for the five panels built today that had none: good faith estimates, recall worklist, treatment plans, denials, receivable ageing. ONE TEST ASSERTION WAS MINE AND WRONG: I asserted no export cell contains a rendered badge phrase and swept the header line in with the rows -- one column is legitimately NAMED "Items with no coverage rule". Corrected the assertion to scan rows only. VERIFIED: 1/1 script block node --check clean, div balance DIFF:0, 21 panels / 21 nav, 0 duplicate ids, 0 duplicate globals, 0 missing DOM targets, 0 dead functions (243 -- the old scraper is gone rather than orphaned), 0 actionable dead buttons, reachability clean, strict-args clean, 0 console.log, 0 UTC calendar derivations, 0 stale-snapshot sites, CRLF preserved. Both dental API suites still 26/26 and 14/14. 21 real-browser checks green, zero page errors, including the before-and-after that matters: the panel shows 2 rows while the dataset holds 3, and the export carries all 3. PLATFORM NOTE FOR WHOEVER PICKS UP THE AUDITS NEXT: with this closed, EVERY remaining item on both competitive-gap audits is blocked. Vendor or certification: SAIRNdental A1/A2/A3/A5/A6, SAIRNsenior A1/A4, StoneDesk GAP 4. Explicitly decision-gated in writing: SAIRNdental B2 and StoneDesk GAP 7 ("build this the moment a second location is confirmed for a real practice. Not before"), StoneDesk GAP 1 and GAP 8 (a public surface on a per-employee-session platform -- an architect question), StoneDesk GAP 2's machine half ("needs a scope call before any code"), StoneDesk GAP 6 (asked and answered: hold), and SAIRNdental B5's live-connector half (a real auth-surface decision). Held by another session: StoneDesk GAP 3 and GAP 5 and audit finding 4.2 (cc), SAIRNsenior A2 and A5 (hank), SAIRNroofing (cody). 2026-09-02
- stonedesk.html + stonedesk-catalog.html (NEW) + api/stonedesk-public.js (NEW) + api/stonedesk-track.js (NEW) + api/_lib/stonedesk-public.js (NEW) + api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/stonedesk.js + sql/stonedesk_public_surface_schema.sql (NEW) + vercel.json (Fourth) -- STONEDESK PUBLIC SURFACE BUILT: competitive-gap audit GAP 1 (public catalog + quote request) and the narrow order-tracking slice of GAP 8, both scoped and approved by Michael before any code. TWO COMMITS: 987b679 and fb94a0e. THE DECISION THAT SHAPES EVERYTHING: THIS IS NOT A MODE OF stonedesk.html. That file carries SAIRN's own chart of accounts, its price book and a patent deadline as LITERAL STRINGS in its HTML -- the residual the audit explicitly left open at 4.1, where gating the Executive Suite panel stopped UI access and did nothing about View Source. Serving it to an anonymous visitor would publish all of it. So the public surface is its own file with its own tables, and a test asserts the DEPLOYED public file contains no license key, no Supabase key, no price-book figure, no chart of accounts and no patent reference -- re-run against the deployed bytes, not just the repo copy. GAP 1: a visitor browses published slabs and asks for a quote with no account and no login. THREE REFUSALS, each tested: (1) a slab is public only on an explicit per-slab flag and ABSENT MEANS NO -- published:'yes' and published:1 do not publish a slab, only true does; (2) THE PUBLIC SHAPE IS BUILT BY NAMING ITS FIELDS, not by deleting private ones, so a field added to a slab blob later cannot leak by default -- a test feeds it a slab carrying cost, vendor, supplier, margin, internal grade and an invented future field and asserts the output keys are exactly the seven public ones; (3) a quote request lands in its OWN table as pending and never in sd_crm, because an anonymous submitter must not write into the record the business runs on -- same rule as public-book.js's never-auto-confirmed. COST IS ABSENT ON PURPOSE: what a shop paid is not what a customer pays. AN UNKNOWN SLUG AND AN UNPUBLISHED SHOP ANSWER IDENTICALLY, so the endpoint cannot be used to enumerate which shops exist, and unpublishing goes dark immediately including for anyone who already had the link. Rate limited per salted IP hash in two buckets (browsing generous, submitting five an hour) so reading cannot exhaust the ability to send one request; the read-then-write increment carries dental-public.js's own honest limitation, quoted rather than reinvented. GAP 8: order tracking by REVOCABLE LINK, NOT A CUSTOMER PASSWORD -- a customer is a different actor class from an employee, every credential here lives in a *_employee_auth table with a PIN and a lockout, and a customer has none of those. Followed sairnsenior_portal_links exactly. THE CUSTOMER ID IS NEVER SUPPLIED BY THE CALLER: there is no customer_id parameter on the public view action at all, and a test asserts the page sends exactly two keys. A revoked link and a link that never existed answer identically. The token is returned ONCE at creation and list does not even SELECT the column, so a secret never fetched cannot leak through a later change to that response. A holder sees name, project, stage and the shop's phone -- no quote, no notes, no slab, no rating, and a test asserts no money appears anywhere. AN UNRECOGNISED STAGE IS SHOWN EXACTLY AS THE SHOP RECORDED IT with a note saying so, and no step number is invented for it. sd_customers NOW HAS A SERVER, and that is not scope creep -- tracking cannot be honest without it. The shop's ENTIRE customer list was localStorage-only: every name, project and stage lived in one browser and died with its cache, the same state sd_crm was in before it got a sync. The alternative was a status snapshot on the link, which drifts the moment anyone updates the job and would show a customer in fabrication after their kitchen shipped. Read by any authenticated employee, written by management only; saveSD3Data() writes through fire-and-forget because it is called from render paths that must not block, and sdHydrateCustomers() re-reads local AFTER the await and never deletes, so a stale tab cannot overwrite a colleague's newer edit on startup. STAFF SIDE is management-only with the gate in showPanel(), not on the nav button, for the same reason 4.1's gate is there: a hidden button is not a gate. Slabs publish ONE AT A TIME -- no bulk button, because the default for a yard full of slabs should be off. Staff set a request's STATUS ONLY and the server enforces it; what the customer typed is evidence of what was asked for and a record editable after the fact stops being that. Every failure is reported rather than assumed: a failed shop save says nothing changed on the public page, a slab publish that syncs locally but not remotely says the catalog on the web has NOT changed, promoting a request says plainly it is NOT in the CRM yet, and a tracking link nobody has opened says never opened rather than showing a blank cell. THREE REAL BUGS IN MY OWN CODE, all caught before push. (1) The catalog's Ask-about-this-slab button built its onclick with JSON.stringify, which emits DOUBLE quotes and closed the HTML attribute -- the button did nothing, and the browser test caught it only because it CLICKED the button rather than reading the source. (2) The staff panel's three inline handlers had the same broken string concatenation that bit the SAIRNsenior training panel earlier today. (3) The first draft of the panel used .fr2, .sb-badge and .page-hdr -- none of which has any CSS in stonedesk.html, which uses inline styles -- so it was rewritten to the house idiom rather than shipping three classes with no rules. FOUR TEST ASSERTIONS WERE MINE AND WRONG and were corrected rather than the code: two scanned the file for the phrases chart-of-accounts and patent and matched MY OWN COMMENT explaining why those must never appear (HTML comments are now stripped first), and two expected whitespace between table cells that innerText does not insert. VERIFIED: 129/129 script blocks node --check clean, div balance DIFF:0, 63 panels / 63 nav ids PASS, 0 dead functions in the public file, 0 actionable dead buttons, SQL preflight clean on all five tables, CRLF preserved on every file. The one strict-args site (stonedesk.html:2986) and the one stale-snapshot site (:3348) BOTH PRE-EXIST AT HEAD -- confirmed against a stash, not assumed. 18 server-side unit tests that need no database because they are pure functions (api/stonedesk-public.test.js), 23 browser checks on the public catalog, 18 on the staff panel and 14 on the tracking page, all green, zero page errors. LIVE-VERIFIED against production: /stonedesk-catalog serves, both endpoints answer, and every gate returns the right refusal -- a malformed token refused before the database, NO_LICENSE without a key, NO_SESSION with a key but no session, and the correct NOT_PROVISIONED while the tables do not exist. WORTH RECORDING FOR THE NEXT SESSION: sairn.vercel.app served a VERCEL SECURITY CHECKPOINT (403, X-Vercel-Mitigated: challenge) across every static route AND the API for roughly ten minutes during this verification, then cleared on its own. It hit routes I never touched (/stonedesk, /sairndental), so it is not a property of this change -- but I have been polling that host hard all night with curl and Playwright, and bot mitigation is the obvious candidate. If a live check suddenly returns 403 with an Astro-looking HTML body, that is what it is: wait, do not start debugging the app. STILL NEEDS MICHAEL: sql/stonedesk_public_surface_schema.sql, five tables (sd_public_shop, sd_quote_requests, sd_order_links, sd_public_rate_limits, sd_customers). Until it runs, the catalog answers no-published-catalog, tracking answers NOT_PROVISIONED, and the customer write-through is a no-op that leaves the local copy exactly as it was. 2026-09-02

THE SCOPE CALL, MADE AND NOT DEFERRED: DXF yes, G-CODE NEVER. A DXF is geometry; G-code is a machine program needing feeds, speeds, tool numbers, blade thickness and a machine origin this app has never seen -- emitting "generic G-code" means inventing every one of those for an unknown machine, which is the fabrication class this codebase refuses, with a saw on the other end. The panel and the toast both say what it is NOT. THE Y AXIS IS THE WHOLE RISK: the canvas measures Y downward from top-left, DXF upward from bottom-left, so writing canvas Y straight in produces a VERTICALLY MIRRORED layout that opens cleanly, looks plausible, and cuts every piece in the wrong place. Kerf is deliberately NOT baked into the rectangles -- it is a CAM toolpath offset; baking it in would silently change every finished part size. RECOVERED FROM A DEAD SESSION: the code was uncommitted in the working tree when this session started, and its own comment claimed "there is a test that fails if it is ever emitted unflipped" -- NO SUCH TEST EXISTED. tests/nesting_dxf.js is now that test (26 assertions), and the claim was verified by MUTATING the flip line and watching five go red, not by observing green. Comment corrected to name the file and to record that it was mutation-verified.

Name resolution is deliberately NOT filtered, so a departed caregiver stops being assignable without turning into a bare employee id on every visit note; both directions are asserted. (2) tools/sairn_seam_check.py BLOCKED THE FIRST PUSH: roster() read a `roleLabel` no call site forwards. The fix was deleting the fallback, not declaring it -- an accidental fallback in a refusal message tells a customer to ask the wrong person for help. `audit` is declared `// seam-check: server-supplied` with the reason, because its absence is load-bearing: it is what makes the response OMIT `audited` rather than report `audited:false`, which would read as an audit attempted and failed. LAST_ADMIN WAS CONFIRMED REACHABLE BY MUTATION (five assertions go red when the guard is removed). Closes the largest unassigned item in docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md ("Zero server-side backup for any real business data", size L, open since 2026-08-27). MEASURED, AND WORSE THAN THE ROW SAID: 37 localStorage collections written, server sync for exactly TWO. Jobs, job costs, change orders, draw schedule, LIEN WAIVERS, safety incidents, daily logs, RFIs, submittals, timesheets -- one browser, no copy anywhere. Lien waivers are legal instruments; daily logs are what construction disputes turn on. THE HOOK IS IN st(), NOT AT THE ~100+ SAVE SITES, AND THAT IS THE DESIGN DECISION. A sync line per call site is a change that WILL be half-applied and the missed half is INVISIBLE -- the app looks identical and those records quietly stop being backed up. Direct evidence from earlier the same night: the roster filter that had to be repeated per picker was applied once per app and MISSED FIVE. One hook at the one function every write already passes through cannot be half-applied. It DIFFS -- only records whose JSON changed are pushed, or every repaint in this app becomes 300 network writes. EIGHT COLLECTIONS DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED, EACH WITH ITS REASON IN THE REGISTRY so nobody has to re-derive the judgement: bld_bids/bld_tna already sync through BESPOKE branches carrying real privacy gates (folding them into the generic loop would route them PAST those gates), bld_company_profile is an object not a keyed collection, bld_role/settings/seeded are app state, bld_integrations is connection config needing its own conflict story, bld_ai_chat is an unbounded transcript. HONEST SCOPE, STATED NOT IMPLIED: a BACKUP with per-record last-write-wins, not two-way sync. DELETIONS DO NOT PROPAGATE and hydration brings a locally deleted row back -- not an oversight: api/sd-data.js has NO delete path for any resource outside sc_*, platform-wide and deliberately, so there is nothing to call. A row reappearing is the honest behaviour of a backup that cannot delete, and it beats losing everything. No conflict resolution; same disclosed limitation as bldHydrateBids and sairndesign's sdnHydrateAll. CORRECTED A COMMENT THAT WAS THE REASON NOBODY TRIED. sairnbuild.html asserted "no BLD- row is provisioned yet, so every /api/sd-data call returns 401 INVALID_LICENSE" and cited SAIRNBUILD-SCOPE.md SS4a. FALSE, and measured against the deployed endpoint rather than assumed: BLD-PINNACLE-2026 returns 401 NO_SESSION (past licence validation), a made-up key returns INVALID_LICENSE. TWO DIFFERENT REFUSALS and only the second means unknown. A comment asserting the app CANNOT sync is worse than no comment. NO SESSION GATE ON THE THIRTY, and that is a decision: bld_bids and bld_tna each have a real per-person visibility rule, but these are the SHARED job record -- every role in a building company reads the schedule, the punch list and the daily log. The boundary that matters is the LICENCE. A future per-role rule on one of these needs its own bespoke branch, not a gate bolted onto the loop. 25 assertions in tests/sairnbuild_server_backup.js, which LIFTS THE REAL FUNCTIONS OUT OF THE PAGE rather than reimplementing them, and COUNTS CALLS rather than checking for errors -- every failure mode here is silent (pushes nothing / pushes everything on every render / pushes during seed / pushes a record the browser itself failed to store). Registry and handler cross-checked BOTH directions: 30 declared, 30 handled, no orphans either way. 252 resources across all apps, ZERO cross-app name collisions. ⚠ NOT DONE: sql/sairnbuild_data_schema.sql NEEDS RUNNING IN SUPABASE -- 30 tables.
