- sairn{dental,senior,care,build,design,law,legacy,roofing,code,biz,grounds,scape,freedom}.html + tests/licence_rekey_isolation.js (NEW) (Fourth) -- LICENCE RE-KEY ISOLATION ACROSS THIRTEEN APPS. Closes docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md's "No app clears local data on a license/device re-key; storage keys are not license-scoped", open and unassigned since 2026-08-27, size L. THE HARM: every app keeps its data in localStorage under a fixed prefix with NO relationship to which licence it belongs to. Change the key on a device -- demo upgraded to paid, a contractor moving between two shops, a resold laptop, support entering a different customer's key -- and the previous licence's jobs/patients/matters/clients are still there and the NEW licence sees them as its own. CROSS-TENANT EXPOSURE INSIDE THE BROWSER, which no amount of server-side licence scoping prevents because the leak never reaches a server. DELIBERATELY NOT THE FIX THE ROW PROPOSED. It said "namespace keys by license hash" -- that rewrites every read and write in thirteen large files and needs a migration for existing installs, and getting it wrong ORPHANS a customer's records permanently. One hook per app closes the same exposure: remember which licence the local data belongs to, and on a change stop and ask. THREE DECISIONS WORTH ARGUING WITH, stated not buried: (1) IT DOES NOT WIPE SILENTLY -- a legitimate re-key (demo->paid, same customer) would destroy real work, so it asks, names the item count, and a refusal keeps the door SHUT rather than mixing two licences. (2) ON FIRST RUN IT ADOPTS -- an existing install has no fingerprint and there is no way to know whose data it is; wiping on that guess is far worse. (3) THE PROMPT SAYS SERVER RECORDS ARE UNAFFECTED, because they are -- server data is per-licence and stays; only this device's copy goes, and the prompt also says local-only records are gone for good. THE PART THAT CAN DESTROY DATA IS THE PART MOST TESTED. All apps share ONE origin and ONE localStorage -- a real browser in August held 201 keys across eight apps. A prefix of 'sd' rather than 'sd_' matches sdn_clients and deletes a design studio's client list from inside StoneDesk (stonedesk.html's Layer 27 comment records exactly that). So: the underscore is load-bearing, every app carries a LIVE SCOPE ASSERTION that DISABLES the wipe rather than running a widened one, and the suite has a CROSS-APP MATRIX asserting each of the 13 ownership tests rejects every other app's real keys -- one assertion catching a widened prefix in any file at once. Fail-closed verified: a broken scope check refuses BOTH the wipe and the entry. TWO NOT COVERED, in a failing-if-forgotten assertion not prose: stonedesk.html writes stonedesk_license_key and READS sd_license_key -- two names, so the fingerprint must first decide which identifies the licence -- plus it owns the widest key space on the platform (83 sd_ keys plus stonedesk_/stonedesk:/stondesk_/fab_ forms) and another session was in it tonight. stonedesk-hr.html NEVER SETS a key; it reads StoneDesk's and shares its namespace (verified by grep), so there is no re-key event to hook. An ACCOUNTING assertion fails if any app storing a licence key is in neither list. TWO HARNESS BUGS FOUND, BOTH MAKING THE CODE LOOK SAFER THAN IT WAS, and both recorded in the test itself rather than quietly fixed: (1) the first storage double used a Proxy whose ownKeys trap violated an invariant, so it THREW, the guard's try/catch swallowed it, EVERY WIPE BECAME A SILENT NO-OP -- and all eleven "nothing was deleted" assertions passed. (2) the ordering check used lastIndexOf to find the guard call and found the `function` keyword instead, reporting sairnscape as broken when it is not. Also caught: a store-site regex that knew only two of the four real shapes and said "could not find where the key is stored" -- easy to soften into a skip, and a skipped ordering check is how a guard that runs AFTER the write ships looking green. 32 assertions; 13/13 files parse, divs balanced, no new missing-DOM ids or duplicate globals (sairnbuild's rBids is pre-existing at HEAD). 2026-09-03
- sairnscape.html + api/sd-data.js + tests/sairnscape_memory.js (NEW) + api/sd-data-memory-app-scope.test.js (NEW) (Fourth) -- SAIRNSCAPE'S MEMORY: A FEATURE THAT WAS SOLD AND HAD NEVER WORKED, NOW WORKING AND CROSS-DEVICE. Found by a sweep for live apps calling API paths that do not exist: /api/memory, /api/memory-cloud and /api/greeting, none of which has a file in api/. WHAT WAS BEING SOLD: marketing feature card -- "Claude remembers every property, every client, every issue" -- and a PRICING TABLE LINE ITEM, "Memory across all properties and clients". WHAT SHIPPED: cloudSaveMemory() wrote to localStorage and POSTed to the missing endpoint with the failure swallowed by a catch reading "local backup is enough"; cloudLoadMemory() and loadUserMemory() were DEFINED AND NEVER CALLED (zero call sites); the system prompt was SYSTEM+orgContext with no memory in it. So the assistant had never seen a single remembered fact on any install. Nothing threw, nothing logged, the page kept selling it. sairn_token, gating two of the calls, is read in three places and SET NOWHERE on the live platform. STEP 1 (f7bc7cd): called the existing local fallback and put it in the prompt; softened the copy to per-device -- Michael's call was to make the claim true rather than delete it. STEP 2 (15c1af8): the real cloud leg through the licence + employee session this app already has, into ai_memories via the already-gated `memory` resource. NO new endpoint, table or identity. THE IDENTITY THAT WAS REJECTED and why, recorded in the code: SAIRN_USER_ID is a string the browser mints from Date.now()+Math.random() and keeps in the same localStorage as the memory -- building on it means any caller writes under any uid and reads anyone else's by supplying it, AND it can never do cross-device because a second device mints a different uid. ai_memories WAS NOT SAFE TO SHARE until this: the memory resource hardcoded app_id 'stonedesk' on write and filtered on license_hash ALONE on read. Now app-scoped, backward compatible because stonedesk.html sends no app_id and every existing row is 'stonedesk'. The IN-APP assistant now has memory at all -- it was the only chat that never touched it, so a paying customer's real conversations contributed nothing. Demo chat stays local-only by design. ** TWO REAL BUGS, BOTH FOUND BY THE LIVE TWO-DEVICE CHECK AND NEITHER BY 36 GREEN ASSERTIONS. ** (1) 40ff33e: the session gate called verifySessionToken(..., 'stonedesk') for EVERY gated resource, so a correctly-signed-in SAIRNscape session failed verification and every memory call from a second app returned 403 "sign in first". The feature was DEAD IN PRODUCTION with the whole suite green. (2) 22ede1b: MEMORY_APPS derived from REGISTRY_MODULES -- apps owning a _resources file -- and SAIRNBIZ OWNS NONE, so a legitimate call was refused `app_id "sairnbiz" is not a known app`. "Derived rather than hand-written" was true and still wrong: deriving only beats hand-listing when the source answers the question being asked. Now derived from ROLES_BY_APP, the set of apps that have SESSIONS -- which is exactly what this resource requires -- so the set the gate accepts and the set that can pass it are the same set by construction. stonedesk_sub excluded: subs are a separate actor class and do not get the shop's memory. THE LESSON, and it is the same one twice: both suites reason about api/sd-data.js as SOURCE TEXT and about the client as EXTRACTED FUNCTIONS. Neither executes the request path, so the one line deciding whether a request is allowed was never run. The unit tests were not wrong, they were measuring something else. LIVE PROOF, against the deployed endpoint: two independently-minted sessions on SB-TEST-2026 (different tokens, which is what the server sees as two devices -- there is NO device identity anywhere in the path). Device A writes -> DEVICE B READS BACK THE EXACT TEXT. Unknown app_id -> 400 UNKNOWN_APP. No session -> 403. Bogus licence -> 401. Reading ANOTHER app's memory on your own licence -> 403, because the gate now requires THAT app's session, which is stronger than merely filtering it out. ⚠ WHAT WAS NOT DONE: the browser click-through on SAIRNscape itself. SCP-DEMO-2026 has an owner bootstrapped from an earlier session and the PIN is not recorded anywhere in the repo (bootstrap returns 409 ALREADY_PROVISIONED), so I could not sign in. The SERVER mechanism is proven end-to-end for a NON-STONEDESK app, which is precisely the path SAIRNscape uses; the SAIRNscape client wiring is covered by 26 assertions and confirmed present in the live page. If Michael has that PIN the click-through is a five-minute job. LEFT BEHIND on SB-TEST-2026: two probe rows in ai_memories (`ZZ-TWO-DEVICE-PROBE...`). No delete path exists for that resource -- platform-wide, deliberately -- so they stay. A test licence, and named so they are recognisable. ⚠ STILL DEAD, deliberately kept one more commit: the api/memory-cloud leg in cloudSaveMemory, so the diff adding the real path and the diff deleting the dead one stay separately reviewable. 2026-09-03
- api/greeting.js (NEW) + api/greeting.test.js (NEW) + sairnscape.html + sql/demo_owner_credentials_2026-09-03.sql (NEW) + docs/2026-09-03-demo-credentials.md (NEW) + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- THE GREETING ENDPOINT, THE PLATFORM CREDENTIAL SET, AND A PUBLISHED PIN THAT STILL OPENS A CUSTOMER LICENCE. (1) api/greeting NOW EXISTS. sairnscape.html had called it on every app open since the feature shipped and it had never been built -- 404, swallowed, static welcome shown instead, nobody ever saw a personalised greeting. IT CALLS NO MODEL, deliberately: a greeting must render before the user has asked for anything, so an LLM call would add a paid round trip to every open AND could state something about the customer's business nobody checked. Composes from the hour and weekday THE CLIENT reports (the server has no idea what timezone anyone is in) plus the app's own vocabulary. NEVER ECHOES CALLER INPUT, which is a SECURITY property: sairnscape injects the result with innerHTML inside a template literal, so anything returned is parsed as HTML; app_id is matched against a fixed table and anything else falls through to neutral copy. The client escapes it too -- two guards, because a future client could be pointed at a future endpoint that forgets. Deterministic (no Math.random, no server clock), 19 assertions. Two source assertions STRIP COMMENTS first: the first version failed on the file's own header saying "No Math.random()", the naive extraction finding the thing it was written to forbid. (2) EVERY APP NOW HAS A DEMO OWNER LOGIN. Nobody had ever held credentials for any demo/test licence -- the work logs record it repeatedly and the effect is that live click-through verification has been skipped or worked around for weeks. That cost is measurable: tonight's two production bugs were found ONLY because SB-TEST-2026 happened to have a documented PIN. 17 licences, one transaction, idempotent, hashes produced by the platform's OWN hashPin() and ROUND-TRIPPED through its OWN verifyPin() before being written. Role read per app -- SAIRNcode is `admin` and has no `owner` role at all. Carries the recoverability guard even though it only ADDS active owners: a guard written only when failure is feared is not a guard. Deletes and deactivates nothing. TWO LICENCES DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED -- SD-PINNACLE-2026 (real named accounts, cmonsul is a person) and RF-PINNACLE-2026. THE PROBE FOUND THREE THINGS NOBODY TRACKED: three licences had NO credential rows at all (LAW-PINNACLE-2026, SDN-PINNACLE-2026, SEN-PINNACLE-2026 -- not lost PINs, logins that never existed); RF-AUDIT-2026 DOES NOT EXIST; and SAIRNvet has no auth subsystem at all. (3) ** A PIN PUBLISHED IN THIS REPO STILL OPENS THE REAL SAIRNROOFING CUSTOMER LICENCE. ** Proven with ONE login attempt against the deployed api/rf-auth: rf-verify-admin / 481502 on RF-PINNACLE-2026 returns 200 WITH A SESSION TOKEN. The PIN is plaintext at sql/sairnroofing_verify_admin_seed.sql:21 and the account holds a provisioning role. The session was used for nothing and discarded. BOTH HALVES OF THE FIX WERE WRITTEN 2026-09-02 AND NEITHER WAS RUN: sairnroofing_audit_license_seed.sql (mints RF-AUDIT-2026; probed live -- 401, does not exist) and sairnroofing_verify_admin_removal_2026-09-02.sql (deletes the exposed account, carries the guard, passes the checker). Licence seed FIRST or roofing has no verification account at all. THE REST OF THE SWEEP IS CLEAN, one attempt each so nothing could be locked out: sc-verify-admin, sd-verify-admin and rf-foreman-1 all 401 -- their cleanups were run. Roofing's is the only one outstanding, and it is WHY roofing verification has been happening on the customer licence: there was nowhere else to run it. (4) SAIRNVET FLAGGED AS A MISSING SUBSYSTEM, not a missing credential, in its own index row -- the distinction is the point, because it will otherwise be read as "we just need the PIN". There is no PIN and no login: no api/sv-*.js anywhere, no sv_employee_auth schema, no resource registry, and the app's OWN source says at :7957 "this app's login is entirely client-side today -- no bearer token, no session, nothing server-verifiable". 54 panels, ~684KB, no roles, no sessions, no deactivation lifecycle. The DECISION (demo-tier or real product) comes before the work. !! I CANNOT RUN EITHER ROOFING FILE. No API path writes license_keys -- checked, not assumed -- so both need Michael in the Supabase SQL editor. SIX SQL FILES NOW PENDING FROM THIS SESSION: sairnroofing_audit_license_seed.sql and sairnroofing_verify_admin_removal_2026-09-02.sql (the security pair, in that order), demo_owner_credentials_2026-09-03.sql, sairnbuild_data_schema.sql, sairndental_bi_tokens_schema.sql, stonedesk_public_surface_schema.sql. 2026-09-03
- api/_lib/stonedesk-public.js + api/stonedesk-public.js + api/stonedesk-track.js + api/_lib/stonedesk-public.test.js (NEW) + api/stonedesk-track.test.js (NEW) + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- EVERY STONEDESK PUBLIC SURFACE ANSWERED A DEAD DATABASE BY BLAMING THE CUSTOMER, AND FIXING IT IMMEDIATELY EXPOSED THAT THE WHOLE STOREFRONT HAS NO TABLES. Sent to apply CC's SAIRNdental fix to StoneDesk's copy of the same helpers. Found the two I was sent for and FOUR MORE by asking the same question of the rest of the anonymous surface. (1) resolveShopSlug() did `if (!r.ok) return null` and the caller renders null as 404 "No published catalog at this address", so a revoked key or a missing GRANT told a customer the shop whose advertised link they had just followed does not exist -- no error, no 502, no log. It now THROWS; the sole caller already answers a logged 502 from its own catch. THE LIB'S HEADER WAS RIGHT AND STILL DID NOT COVER THIS: it explains that an unknown slug and an unpublished shop deliberately give the SAME answer so nobody can enumerate shops -- two states a visitor may not tell apart. A third state where the server COULD NOT ASK AT ALL was folded in with them, and the correct reasoning about the first two is exactly what made the third invisible. (2) checkAndIncrementRateLimit() failed OPEN twice: `existing.ok ? await existing.json() : []` read an unreachable store as count 0, and the increment was a bare `await fetch(...)` with NO .ok check at all, so a failed write meant the counter never rose and the limit could never re-engage for that window. Three states now, and the caller answers 503 UNAVAILABLE rather than 429 -- "too many requests" for an unreachable database is a wrong reason given confidently, and on a storefront it reads as the shop's own fault. (3) The catalog's remnant fetch was `if (rr.ok)` with no else. Written to tolerate ONE failure (a shop that has not run the sd_remnants migration) it tolerated every other one too, so a 401 rendered the storefront with a published for-sale inventory simply absent. Narrowed to 404, which is what PostgREST answers for a missing relation. (4)(5)(6) api/stonedesk-track.js had THREE, and it had no tests at all. sd_order_links was read with only 404/400 special-cased, so every other status fell through to `await lr.json()`, parsed the error body into a non-array, became link = null, and answered "This tracking link is not valid. Ask the shop for a new one." -- the shop would then have issued a new link that failed identically. sd_customers' `cr.ok ? ... : []` told a customer holding a valid active link that their job was "no longer on file", a sentence with a real-world meaning said to somebody whose countertop is in fabrication. The sd_public_shop read is DELIBERATELY STILL NON-FATAL and is now merely logged -- the stage is what the customer came for, the shop's name and phone are a courtesy beside it, and refusing to show an order status because a decorative field could not be read is the opposite mistake. THE MUTATION TEST IS REAL, NOT AN indexOf() ON THE SOURCE. The two refusal branches are order-dependent because an unreachable counter also reports allowed:false, so reading !rl.allowed first turns an outage into a 429 and makes the 503 unreachable code. An indexOf assertion would pass on a file whose branches were ordered right and whose states were wrong, and fail on a correct rewrite using a switch. The test COMPILES A DELIBERATELY-SWAPPED MUTANT of the handler in memory, asserts the mutant really does answer 429, then asserts the shipped file answers 503 -- and fails loudly if the two branches ever stop being adjacent, saying it must be REWRITTEN rather than deleted. 57 assertions across the two new files where there had been zero for either surface. THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST WAS NOT IN THE BRIEF AND WAS ONLY VISIBLE ONCE THE FIX SHIPPED. Live-verifying 420e529, the catalog answered 503 UNAVAILABLE where it had answered 404 an hour earlier. The Vercel runtime log for that exact request reads `stonedesk rate limit: counter read failed, HTTP 404` -- sd_public_rate_limits DOES NOT EXIST. api/stonedesk-track then answered 503 NOT_PROVISIONED for a well-formed token, which fires only on a 404/400 from sd_order_links. Two tables from the same file, both absent: sql/stonedesk_public_surface_schema.sql HAS NEVER BEEN RUN. So StoneDesk's public storefront shipped on 2026-09-02 with no backing tables, and every customer since has been told there is no catalog at that address -- which reads as "nobody has published a shop yet" and was really "the tables do not exist". The rate limiter guarding an unauthenticated write into a shop's lead table never existed either. THIS IS THE CLASS THE FIX EXISTS TO CATCH, CAUGHT ON ITS FIRST DEPLOY, AND IT CAUGHT ITSELF: an unprovisioned system wearing the costume of an empty one. NOTE FOR WHOEVER READS tools/sairn_sql_preflight.py NEXT: it reports that schema file CLEAN, and says so with its own warning attached -- it compares against the repo's CREATE TABLE statements, so A FILE WRITTEN AND NEVER RUN LOOKS PRESENT TO IT. That warning is not boilerplate; this is the case it describes. ALSO CLOSED AND RE-VERIFIED RATHER THAN TAKEN ON REPORT: the SAIRNroofing published-PIN row. Michael ran both SQL files; I probed production once each. rf-verify-admin / 481502 on RF-PINNACLE-2026 now answers 401 INVALID_CREDENTIALS where it returned 200 WITH A SESSION TOKEN on 2026-09-03, and RF-AUDIT-2026 answers 401 INVALID_CREDENTIALS rather than 401 INVALID_LICENSE -- past licence validation into credential checking, so the audit licence exists. CLAIM WRITTEN BY HAND: sairn_claim.py blocked stonedesk on a lexical overlap with cc's active sairndental claim, shared tokens 'public', 'fail', 'open'. Different app, different file, and the words overlap BECAUSE THE BUG DOES -- I was sent to apply the same fix to a second app. Recorded in the claim's note rather than reworded to slip past the matcher. OPEN, FOR MICHAEL, ONE FILE: run sql/stonedesk_public_surface_schema.sql. Additive, idempotent, five tables, RLS service-role-only, and its grants already include the `update` the counter's merge-duplicates upsert needs at plan time -- the exact verb whose absence took sairncash_waitlist down for hours on 2026-08-26. Confirm by re-probing the two endpoints afterwards, NOT by the editor reporting success.
- stonedesk.html + tests/public_catalog_no_false_empty.js (NEW) + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- THE PUBLIC CATALOG PANEL TURNED EVERY FAILED READ INTO A CONFIDENT EMPTY ANSWER, AND ONE OF THEM COULD TAKE A LIVE CATALOG OFFLINE. Picked as the authenticated half of the surface I had just fixed the public half of. All three reads in the panel had the same shape. THE DANGEROUS ONE: pcRead('sd_public_shop') returning null became {shop_slug:'',published:false}, and pcRenderShop() WROTE THAT INTO THE FORM -- every field cleared, the Publish box unticked -- above the sentence "No public address set yet, so nothing is reachable". A shop with a LIVE PUBLISHED CATALOG was told it had never set one up. Press Save on that screen and pcSaveShop() sends the form as it stands: blanks over the real record, published:false over a live page. THE CATALOG GOES DARK AND THE CONTACT DETAILS ARE GONE, caused by a screen that looked like a first-run setup form. That is why the fix DISABLES the form rather than only rewording it -- the danger is a Save pressed against a form the panel filled in itself. THE OTHER TWO ARE MISLEADING RATHER THAN DESTRUCTIVE AND STILL MATTER: quote requests rendered as "No quote requests yet", which is a claim about inbound business made from a request that never reached a table; tracking links rendered as "No tracking links issued yet", so a shop could reissue a link that was already live and could not revoke one it wanted dead. NONE OF IT WAS HYPOTHETICAL -- sd_public_shop and sd_quote_requests DO NOT EXIST in production (proven earlier tonight), so all three reads have been 404ing since the panel shipped on 2026-09-02. THE ROOT ENABLER WAS ONE LAYER DOWN AND REACHES EVERY PANEL ON THIS PLATFORM. sdData() recorded 401/403 in _sdAuthRefused -- added 2026-09-02 for exactly this class, and it named ONLY ONE CAUSE -- and recorded nothing for a 404, a 500, a 200 carrying ok:false, or a network throw. All four returned the same null an empty resource returns, and the first three actively SET _sdAuthRefused to false, so the earlier fix reported them as clean. There is now a second map _sdReadFailed and an sdReadFailedNote() beside sdAuthRefusedNote(). TWO MAPS RATHER THAN ONE, deliberately: "sign in" and "we could not reach the server" are different sentences and collapsing them is the same mistake as answering 429 for an outage. Return contract unchanged (data|null) because ~30 call sites do `if (data)`. THE MUTATION TEST IS THE PART I WOULD KEEP. The 22 behavioural assertions show the fixed panel behaving, and on their own they would pass just as happily against a panel that got the right answer by accident. Two more restore the two pre-fix lines IN MEMORY and assert the defect reappears exactly as reported -- the form blanked, the Publish box unticked, and the destructive write actually fired with published:false and shop_slug:''. The mutation asserts it changed the source, so a rename cannot turn it into a no-op that silently proves nothing. 24 assertions, 0 failures. TWO STALE INDEX ROWS CORRECTED WHILE IN THE FILE, both re-measured rather than reasoned about. (1) `onclick="saveShopSettings()" resolves to nothing` -- grep returns ZERO occurrences; the file records the removal at the site, dated 2026-08-23, FIVE DAYS BEFORE the row's own "found 2026-08-28", so it was written from an older scan and never re-checked. (2) `div balance is -4` -- div_balance_check now reports 4854/4854 DIFF:0 PASS. Somebody closed it and did not update the row. Recorded rather than deleted, because that row's own argument for fixing it was that a permanently-failing check trains people to ignore it, and a permanently-stale row does the same to this index. GUARDIAN ON stonedesk.html AFTER THE CHANGE: 129/129 script blocks parse, div balance PASS, duplicate-global clean, nav_panel PASS, zero duplicate ids, no new missing DOM targets (my eight ids all resolve). The 168-line missing-DOM-target output and the strict-args hit at :2986 are both pre-existing and both already have rows.
- stonedesk.html + api/_lib/exec-context.js + tests/pricing_single_source.js (NEW) + docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-02-stonedesk-worldwide-competitive-gap-audit.md + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- TWO PRICE LISTS THAT DISAGREED, IN TWO FILES, AND THE ONE A CUSTOMER SIGNS WAS THE WRONG ONE. BOTH WERE WRONG, NOT JUST ONE. Michael confirmed the real tiers: BUSINESS $299/mo, PROFESSIONAL $599/mo, ENTERPRISE $799/mo, plus custom pricing for larger shops, and NO ENTRY-LEVEL TIER. Before this the service-agreement generator offered Founding $199 / Solo $299 / Pro $499 / Shop $799 while the CEO and CFO advisors' price book in api/_lib/exec-context.js said Starter $199 / Professional $299 / Enterprise $599. Four tiers against three, two shared names with different money attached to one of them, and NOTHING ANYWHERE FORCED THEM TO MATCH. CC recorded the disagreement on 2026-09-03 (worklog line 511) and did not fix it, which was the right call at the time -- it needed a pricing decision, not a code change. A CUSTOM TIER WAS ADDED rather than leaving 'custom pricing for larger shops' unrepresentable. Without it the generator cannot produce an agreement for exactly the deal shape that tier exists for. It REFUSES to save without an agreed rate rather than defaulting to zero -- a Custom agreement saved blank would have printed "$0.00 / month" as a real contract term. THE FOUR RETIRED TIERS ARE DELIBERATELY NOT DELETED FROM THE PRINTER, and this is the part I would have got wrong if I had only done what was asked. Agreements signed under them are already in sd_agreements, and every downstream reader (the list, the CSV export, the e-sig modal, the printed agreement) reads ag.planLabel and ag.price OFF THE STORED RECORD rather than re-deriving from the plan key -- which is what makes retiring a tier safe at all. But the Founding branches are different: they add a RATE-LOCK SECTION and a "LOCKED FOR LIFE" rate box that are gated on ag.plan === 'founding'. Deleting those would have silently reprinted an already-signed contract WITHOUT THE CLAUSE THE CUSTOMER AGREED TO. They stay, marked AG_RETIRED_PLANS and unreachable for new agreements, with the reason written at each branch. THE TEST IS THE PART THAT LASTS. Correcting both lists is a one-off; the two lists are still in two files and the next edit to either can drift again exactly as this one did. tests/pricing_single_source.js reads BOTH and asserts they agree -- and WRITES THE CONFIRMED TIERS DOWN A THIRD TIME ON PURPOSE, so it is an independent opinion rather than a diff of the two files agreeing with each other. If both were edited wrongly in the same way, comparing them to each other would still pass. It also asserts the select the user actually sees offers the same four (the map being right does not help if the dropdown still lists Solo and Pro), that nothing under $299 is offered, that no live advisor line still says "Starter $", and that the Founding rate-lock clause is still present for reprints. 33 assertions, 0 failures. ONE DOC ANNOTATED RATHER THAN REWRITTEN. The 2026-09-02 competitive gap audit argued StoneDesk was "priced at or below the market's floor" off the $199/$299/$599 list and asked for a deliberate decision rather than a default. THIS WAS THAT DECISION, and it went the other way, so the conclusion is now false -- at a $299 floor StoneDesk starts INSIDE Moraware's $200-400 band rather than under it, and its $799 top tier is above iBlocky's EUR299-399. Annotated in place with the correction; the old numbers stay as the INPUT to the decision. The two other mentions in that doc are historical findings about what the file contained at the time and were left verbatim. PRE-EXISTING TEST FAILURE FOUND AND NOT FIXED, BECAUSE IT IS NOT DRIFT: api/sd-data-exec-context.test.js fails its app-count assertion in THIS CLONE ONLY. It counts root .html files with fs.readdirSync, which reads the WORKING DIRECTORY, and piac.html -- untracked, dated 2026-08-28, not mine and not in git -- makes the count 19 instead of 18. The tracked count is exactly 18 and the advisor line is correct. A test that fails on a developer's untracked scratch file trains people to ignore it; deriving the list from git ls-files would fix it, but that is a different change and piac.html is Michael's to keep or delete. Reported, not touched. GUARDIAN AFTER THE CHANGE: 129/129 script blocks parse, div balance 4854/4854 PASS, nav_panel PASS, dead-button audit A=0 B=0 C2=0 D1=0 (agPlanChanged is defined and reachable), exec-context.js node --check clean.
- stonedesk.html + tests/crm_seed_not_a_pipeline.js (NEW) + docs/2026-09-04-stonedesk-seed-fallback-audit.md (NEW) + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- THE SEED-FALLBACK AUDIT GUARDIAN HAS CARRIED SINCE 2026-08-22, RUN. THERE ARE 31 SITES, NOT ~28, AND EXACTLY ONE WAS THE DEFECT. GUARDIAN'S OWN TWO-PART TEST, APPLIED LITERALLY, FLAGS 18 OF THE 31 AND THAT ANSWER IS USELESS. Every one of the 18 computes a number from its seed, because that is what a demo seed is for; taken at face value the test says the app's entire demo experience is a defect, which is a product decision nobody asked for and is not what the Slabs finding was about. THE QUESTION THAT ACTUALLY SEPARATES THEM IS WHETHER THE SEED CAN MASK SOMETHING REAL. A seed can only hide data if data exists somewhere else to be hidden -- which needs a SERVER resource behind the key. Mechanically: for each of the 31 keys, does sdData('read','<key>' appear anywhere. ONE HIT: sd_crm. sd_crm IS THE SLABS DEFECT EXACTLY. load() is a documented local cache of the last real server read. With NO cache and a FAILED read it returned six invented leads and render() did real arithmetic on them: 6 leads, a $35,900 PIPELINE, 1 hot, 0% conversion, $5,983 average. Nothing said the read had failed. A shop with a broken session, an expired key or an unreachable database saw a populated CRM and a five-figure pipeline presented as its own. WHAT WAS ALREADY RIGHT, AND IT BOUNDS THE EXPOSURE: refresh() already left an EXISTING local cache untouched on a failed read rather than wiping it -- that half was honest. The gap was the case where there is NO cache to leave untouched, where the seed stepped in and answered for the server. And a SUCCESSFUL read of [] is saved as [], which is truthy, so the seed can never return after one good sync. It was only ever reachable before the first sync or after a failed one, and it is the second of those that was lying. THE SEED IS NOT DELETED, DELIBERATELY. Deleting it outright was the Slabs fix; here it would also delete the demo, which is Michael's decision and not one to take silently while fixing a bug. It is skipped for the case it never covered, via sdReadFailed('sd_crm') -- the mechanism I added earlier tonight for the Public Catalog panel, reused rather than reinvented. When the read failed and there is no cache the five KPIs render '--' and the table says the leads could not be loaded. THAT ASSERTION FAILED ON ITS FIRST RUN FOR THE RIGHT REASON AND I NEARLY SHIPPED IT PASSING FOR THE WRONG ONE: my fix moved the fallback out of load() into crmSeed(), so the key no longer appeared on a 'sdDemoCleared()' line and the key list came back EMPTY -- an assertion that would have passed forever while checking nothing. It now asserts sd_crm is in its own input list first. WHAT IS STILL OPEN IS A DECISION, NOT A DEFECT, AND I HAVE NOT TAKEN IT. The other 30 sites are purely local and hide nothing, but a first-run shop that never clears demo data still sees invented figures presented as its own business. The sharpest is sd_ap: FOUR dollar KPIs -- open payables, due-this-week, OVERDUE, month-to-date paid -- plus a full aging table, computed off six invented vendor bills DATED 2024, so the aging buckets read '60+ days' on bills that never existed. Full table with per-site figures in the new doc, with the method's limits stated: the money column is a regex sum over amt/val/cost/price/total/amount and is INDICATIVE, not exact. AND THE HALF I DID NOT DO IS NAMED RATHER THAN LEFT AMBIGUOUS: Guardian's follow-on also asks whether the other fifteen apps carry the same fallback shape. That is a separate pass and was not run. This audit is stonedesk.html only. Guardian after the change: 129/129 blocks parse, div balance 4854/4854 PASS, and the two suites written earlier tonight (pricing 33, public catalog 24) still pass.

NEITHER TOOL IS WRONG -- THE DEFECT LIVES IN THE GAP BETWEEN THEM, AND `if(el)` IS WHAT TURNS THE CRASH THAT WOULD HAVE EXPOSED IT INTO SILENCE. That idiom is defence against a TRANSIENT null and a silencer against a PERMANENT one, and nothing in the codebase distinguishes the two. A CONFIDENT SUCCESS MESSAGE FOR A THING THAT DID NOT HAPPEN, same class as the intake panel's 'Customer + Job created from intake!' in c2ac74c. IT ALSO NOW OPENS THE FORM. inv2-form is display:none until New Invoice is clicked, so pre-filling it while hidden would have been the same no-op wearing a different disguise -- invFromQuote() already sets that display and this follows it. Navigation moved from showPanel('invoices') to sbNav('invoices'); showPanel is a different, later mechanism and 'invoices' was never one of its ids. Intent PRESERVED rather than reinterpreted: it raises an invoice FOR THE DEPOSIT, which is what the old amount and description both said, with deposit left blank so invSave() computes balance = deposit owed, status Unpaid. TWO MORE FELL OUT OF THE SAME READ, BOTH WORSE. (1) invUpdateKPIs() computed five real figures and wrote every one into a retired id -- inv2-total, inv2-unpaid, inv2-partial, inv2-paid, inv2-ar. THE FOUR TILES A SHOP ACTUALLY SEES (Total Outstanding, Overdue, Open Invoices, Collected MTD) HAVE SHOWN THE HARDCODED $0/$0/0/$0 FROM THEIR OWN MARKUP SINCE THE PANEL SHIPPED. A shop with real unpaid invoices was told it was owed nothing. Guardian Check 0b in its purest form, kept silent by that function's own local `if (el)`. NOT A RENAME: the old ids counted invoices BY STATUS and the new tiles are labelled with money and with 'open', so mapping inv2-total onto inv2-kpi1 would have put a COUNT under a heading reading 'Total Outstanding'. Each tile is now computed from what its own label claims -- and `today`, which the function was already computing and never using, is what Overdue needed. (2) invMarkPaid() set inv.balance = 0 and THEN pushed {amount: inv.balance}, so EVERY 'Marked paid' payment record said the customer paid ZERO. Wrong on its own, and it would also have held Collected MTD at zero forever, because that figure sums payment records. The two defects were connected and the test asserts the connection: mark an invoice paid, then check the tile. COLLECTED MTD IS REAL, NOT INFERRED FROM A STATUS. Every payment already carries {amount, date, method} -- written by invSave()'s deposit branch and by invMarkPaid() -- so it sums payments dated inside the current month. An invoice's own `date` is when it was RAISED and cannot answer that question; asserted with a fixture whose last-month payment must not count. 16 assertions in tests/invoice_panel_kpis.js, including a mutation that puts the five retired ids back and asserts all four tiles return to their markup defaults -- 'a shop owed $2,500 was shown $0, which is what shipped' -- plus a standing check that no live line references any of the five retired ids. The suite reads the tile LABELS out of the markup rather than hardcoding them, so a relabelled tile fails loudly instead of being silently tested against the wrong claim. MISSING_TARGETS 160 -> 152. Next on the priority list is renderInventory() (wired, 10 callers, inv-low/inv-ok/inv-warn stock counters), and the Invoices result is the reason to expect it is real: same shape, same cause, same panel-rebuild history. - tools/sairn_claim.py + tests/claims/run_push_verify_probe.py (NEW) + CLAUDE.md (Fourth) -- THE COLLISION-PREVENTION TOOL WAS FAILING SILENTLY IN THREE PLACES, AND I FOUND THE SECOND AND THIRD ONLY BECAUSE THE PROBE FOR THE FIRST KEPT FAILING. (1) A CLAIM WHOSE PUSH FAILED WAS STILL REPORTED AS CLAIMED. save_mine() printed a failure line and returned None; both callers printed 'CLAIMED.' regardless. So a non-fast-forward -- THE ORDINARY CASE when two of four clones claim within seconds -- left the claim committed locally and INVISIBLE to every other clone while the tool said it was claimed. The collision this tool exists to prevent, happening inside the tool. REPRODUCED LIVE while claiming this very work: 'error: failed to push some refs' immediately followed by 'CLAIMED.'. Now retries fetch->rebase->push (a race is normal, not exceptional -- one file per clone means nothing can conflict), ABORTS a failed rebase instead of leaving the tree mid-rebase, and VERIFIES the commit is an ancestor of origin/main before reporting success. claim and release exit NON-ZERO and print NOT CLAIMED / NOT RELEASED otherwise. (2) FOUND BY THE PROBE, NOT BY ME: after a release whose push failed, the LOCAL file already said 'released', so re-running release matched no active claim, printed nothing wrong, EXITED 0, and never pushed the commit still sitting there. A second false success one layer behind the first. That path now publishes pending commits. I would not have looked for this -- the probe's 'origin shows it released' assertion failed and made me. (3) FOUND BY WATCHING THE TOOL WORK ON THE REAL REPO: CLAUDE.md documents that `list`/`check` DESTROY an uncommitted claim via `git checkout origin/main -- .claude/claims`. THE HALF IT MISSED IS WORSE -- that command also STAGES what it writes. Running `list` here left `M .claude/claims/fourth.json` staged holding ORIGIN'S OLDER COPY, i.e. a staged revert of a claim this clone had already committed; any later git commit sweeping the index would have deleted the record of a claim. Both commands now read the same bytes with `git show origin/main:<path>`, which cannot write anything, and load_all() warns loudly if it has to fall back to the working-tree copy rather than quietly narrowing its own scope. 22 assertions in tests/claims/run_push_verify_probe.py, against a REAL bare remote and real clones on disk, no network. A stubbed subprocess would assert that the code calls the commands it calls, which is not the question -- the question is whether a rebase actually resolves a non-fast-forward and whether the commit is genuinely an ancestor of origin/main afterwards. Covers: the happy path is verified not merely pushed; a claim RACED by another session's push still lands and does not lose that session's commit; an unpushable claim exits non-zero, never prints CLAIMED, says the work still reads as unclaimed to everyone else, and leaves no mid-rebase state; release reports honestly and recovers when the remote returns; and `list`/`check` leave an uncommitted hand-written claim on disk and stage nothing. CLAUDE.md's known-defects list updated: defect 2 marked FIXED with the staging half it never knew about, and the false-success one added as defect 3 with the standing lesson stated in general terms -- THE EXPENSIVE PART OF A FALSE SUCCESS IS NEVER THE ERROR, IT IS THE CONFIDENT LINE PRINTED AFTER IT. Same shape as the post-push watcher that swallowed a 403 and said nothing. NOT CHANGED, DELIBERATELY: the matcher's remaining false positives (a SUBJECT-level block still fires on a shared namespace like 'platform'). That is defect 1's documented residue and a different problem; run_matcher_probe.py still passes 0 failures.
- stonedesk.html + tests/ai_shortcuts_reach_the_chat.js (NEW) + docs/2026-09-04-stonedesk-missing-dom-targets-triage.md (Fourth) -- THREE WIRED AI BUTTONS OPENED THE PANEL AND SENT NOTHING, AND TWO OF THEM GENERATE LEGALLY REQUIRED COMPLIANCE DOCUMENTS. The triage line said 'sendMessage()/analyzeDocument()' as if it were one item; it was three live buttons -- safetyAIRootCause (5-Why root cause on a logged incident), ecpGenerate (OSHA Written Exposure Control Plan) and safetyGenerateAttestation (California STOP Act annual attestation). Each did sbNav('ai') then if(input){input.value=...;sendMessage();} against #userInput, WHICH NO LONGER EXISTS. THE GUARD IS WHY NOBODY NOTICED. if(input) meant no throw and no send: the panel opened and the chat was empty, which reads to a user as 'the AI had nothing to say' rather than 'this button is not connected'. Retargeted to sdAIQuick(), matching four earlier migrations (custFollowUpAI, vendorSpendAI, getAIQuoteAdvice, warrAskClaude) that were done before the job was left unfinished. sendMessage() ITSELF FAILED THE OPPOSITE WAY: const text = input.value.trim() unguarded, so any caller that DID reach it threw a TypeError into a console nobody has open. Silent in both directions, which is why the guard added now REPORTS -- console.error plus a toast naming sdAIQuick as the replacement -- instead of doing either. WORTH RECORDING ABOUT THE GREP: #userInput's CSS rule survived the element's removal, so grepping userInput returns hits and looks reassuring. The test asserts both facts -- that no element carries the id, and that the CSS rule is still there -- so the next person greping it knows why. I BROKE THE FILE ONCE DOING THIS AND CAUGHT IT IMMEDIATELY. My first attempt was a scripted rewrite of the three blocks; it mangled one and checkblocks caught it on the next run. Reverted with git checkout and redid all three as targeted Edits. THAT IS CLAUDE.md'S NEVER-BULK-REPLACE RULE EARNING ITS PLACE ON A 2MB FILE, live, in the middle of a change that was only twelve lines. 17 assertions, including: the prompts survived the retarget intact (a retarget that quietly dropped half a compliance prompt would be WORSE than the bug -- the button would work and produce a thinner document; asserted by checking 29 CFR 1910.1053 and Labor Code Section 6717 are still present); a derived check that NO remaining sendMessage() caller is reachable; sendMessage() returning cleanly and reporting when the element is absent, and still sending normally if restored; and a mutation rebuilding the old shape to prove it sends nothing and throws nothing. THE TEST HAD TO STRIP FULL-LINE COMMENTS BEFORE MATCHING, and failed once because of it: my own fix comments NAME the dead functions they retargeted away from, so a naive search found sendMessage() inside a comment and reported the fix as the bug. Guardian's dead-button audit learned exactly this and strips comments first -- the same trap, in a different tool, hit again. analyzeDocument() left for the deletion pass: it targets #messages, part of the same removed chat, and its fix is deletion rather than retargeting. MISSING_TARGETS unchanged at 149 (userInput is still referenced by four DEAD functions). Guardian: 129/129 blocks parse, div balance PASS.
- stonedesk.html + tests/crm_seed_not_a_pipeline.js + docs/2026-09-04-stonedesk-seed-fallback-audit.md + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- I WENT TO FIX A STALE ROW AND FOUND MY OWN AUDIT FROM EARLIER TONIGHT WAS WRONG. Used sairn-code-scrubber. THE ROW WAS STALE. 'panel-remnant double-render: sdRemnantRender() and remRender() both live, two containers', carrying a warning triangle. remRender() DOES NOT EXIST -- the duplicate rem* module and its separate sd_remnants (PLURAL) key were deleted on 2026-07-30, and stonedesk.html documents the deletion in TWO places, at the panel (:28700) and in the orphaned-module register (:37338), which also records it was verified dead before removal rather than assumed. The row survived five weeks after the thing it described was gone and would have sent a session hunting a live double-render that had not existed since July. Corrected by reading the file rather than the row. BUT READING IT FOUND SOMETHING REAL, AND IT IS A CORRECTION TO MY OWN WORK. My seed-fallback audit earlier tonight reported 'exactly ONE of the 31 seed sites has a server resource behind it: sd_crm'. THAT IS WRONG. THREE reach the server. The check searched for the literal sdData('read','<storage key>' -- which silently assumes the RESOURCE is named the same as the KEY. sd_remnant reads 'remnants', so the search could never have found it. AND sd_remnant IS THE SHARPER CASE OF THE TWO REAL ONES, BECAUSE IT SYNCS OUTWARD. sd_crm's seed only misleads the shop. sdRemnantHydrate() is a MERGE that keeps local-only rows and then save()s the result -- so on a device with an empty cache the six seed remnants were not merely rendered, they were WRITTEN INTO localStorage alongside the shop's real ones and became indistinguishable from them. From there one click of pcToggleRemnant calls sdRemnantSyncOne(), which writes to Supabase, and the public catalog publishes anything published+Available. THREE OF THE SIX SEED ROWS ARE Available, with invented stone, invented dimensions and an invented price. That is fabricated inventory on a real shop's customer-facing storefront. FIXED: seed rows are tagged _demo at load (a copy, so the tag rides through save() and survives the merge -- it has to, to still be protecting anything), and sdRemnantSyncOne refuses a tagged row unless sdIsDemoLicense() -- the same gate the seeds themselves now use, so the demo licence can still demo a populated storefront. The refusal returns {refused:'demo'} rather than null so pcToggleRemnant says 'that is demo inventory, so it will not publish' instead of 'try again with a connection', which would have been a wrong reason given confidently and would have sent a shop round the loop indefinitely. THE TEST I WROTE TONIGHT WAS CERTIFYING THE WRONG CLAIM AND PASSING. crm_seed_not_a_pipeline.js asserted backed == ['sd_crm'] by searching for that literal -- SCRUBBER ITEM 16 SHAPE B, asserting EXISTENCE where the requirement is USE, in a suite I had written hours earlier specifically to hold this class. It now asks whether the seed's own script block talks to the server AT ALL. That surfaced a THIRD: sd_employees, which reaches the server only for a different resource (employee_profile, {all:true}, to enrich the roster) and writes no roster row back, so a seeded employee cannot leave the device. I LISTED IT ANYWAY WITH ITS EXPOSURE WRITTEN OUT rather than filtering it away -- narrowing the check is exactly how it would go back to passing for the wrong reason, and the same over-flag reasoning the claim matcher uses applies: a five-second read costs less than a missed one. ALSO RECORDED IN THE TEST: the two seed suites now report DIFFERENT COUNTS (31 here, 32 in demo_seed_licence_scope.js) because their filters differ -- one matches `?[]:` and the other also catches `(d.length||sdDemoCleared())?d:SEED`. Both are correct. Said out loud in the file, because a bare mismatched number between two suites is exactly what sends someone hunting a defect that is not there. All suites green: crm_seed 13, demo_seed_licence_scope 15, invoice_panel_kpis 16, pricing_single_source 33, public_catalog 24, ai_shortcuts 17, claims push-verify probe 22. Guardian: 129/129 blocks parse, div balance PASS.
- stonedesk.html + tests/template_migration_orphaning.js (NEW) + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- THE SECOND STALE DUPLICATE-MODULE ROW IN A ROW, AND A REAL DEFECT HIDING INSIDE THE MIGRATION THAT RESOLVED IT. Used sairn-adversarial-reviewer. THE ROW WAS STALE, LIKE THE REMNANT ONE. 'Two parallel template modules with different storage keys', marked Open. The tm* module on sd_templates has been canonical since 2026-07-30, tmMigrateLegacyRecords() moves anything left in sd_template_records across on load, and stonedesk.html documents the merge in TWO places (:32967 and :37916). The row's own instruction -- check which key panel-templates renders from BEFORE merging -- had already been answered, correctly, five weeks earlier. THAT IS TWO OF TWO duplicate-module rows I have checked tonight that were resolved long ago and still carried as open; the orphaned-module register in the file records NINE completed removals and the index was never reconciled against it. BUT THE FAILURE THE ROW WARNED ABOUT WAS REAL, ONE LINE FURTHER DOWN THAN IT LOOKED. The migration ran `if(added)tmSave(); stRaw(FLAG,'1');` -- SAVE RESULT IGNORED, DONE-FLAG SET REGARDLESS. st() returns false on a localStorage quota failure, and that is not theoretical here: the mapping a few lines up embeds each legacy PHOTO as a data URL into the migrated record, which makes this the largest write the module ever performs. THE CONSEQUENCE WAS PERMANENT. A failed save set the flag anyway, so the migration NEVER RAN AGAIN, and the legacy rows sat in sd_template_records -- which the function deliberately never deletes, for reversibility -- with nothing left in the app that would ever move them. The panel showed an empty template list and said nothing. That is precisely the 'silently orphans real user data' outcome the row named, reached from a direction the row did not consider: it was watching the merge direction, and the merge direction was right. FIXED: the flag is set only if the save landed. Failing WITHOUT the flag is the safe direction -- the next load retries, and the existing already-present-id guard makes the retry harmless. The user is told local storage is full AND that nothing was lost, because the legacy key is still there. The surrounding bare catch(e){} now logs: a migration of real customer records that fails must not do so without a trace. 14 assertions in tests/template_migration_orphaning.js. The happy path is asserted first and in detail -- record count, the documented status mapping including running->reviewed, the photo and DXF name surviving, idempotency, and the legacy key never being deleted -- SO THE FIX CANNOT PASS BY BREAKING MIGRATION, which is the failure mode of a test that only checks the error path. Then: a quota failure leaves the flag unset, says 'Nothing was lost', logs, and a later retry succeeds; corrupt legacy JSON is logged rather than swallowed and does NOT mark the migration done; and a mutation restores the two original lines and asserts the flag gets set on a failed save. ALSO ASSERTED SO THE QUESTION IS NOT RE-OPENED: TM_KEY is sd_templates, and NOTHING in the file writes sd_template_records -- it is read-only legacy. That is the answer to the row's question, held mechanically instead of in a row nobody re-checked for five weeks. Guardian: 129/129 blocks parse, div balance PASS.
- tools/orphan_register_check.py (NEW) + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- RECONCILED THE OPEN-WORK INDEX AGAINST STONEDESK'S ORPHANED-MODULE REGISTER, AND THE HONEST RESULT IS SMALLER THAN MY OWN HYPOTHESIS. Used sairn-memory-curator. I said last commit that the index had 'drifted enough that trusting it wastes time'. THE RECONCILE DOES NOT SUPPORT THAT AS STATED. The register documents TEN removed duplicate modules; the index only ever carried rows for TWO of them, and I had already closed both. There is no backlog of stale register rows. The drift was that NOTHING LINKED THE TWO DOCUMENTS -- not that many rows had rotted. Recorded that way rather than letting the stronger claim stand. THE REGISTER ITSELF WAS VERIFIED CURRENT, NOT ASSUMED -- a register entry is a fact with a tense. Every function it names as deleted has no definition anywhere in the file, and every orphan key it names (sd_ts_entries, sd_ts_active, sd_remnants, sd_rec_log, sd_manifest_stops, sd_cg_history) has ZERO readers and ZERO writers. sd_template_records has exactly one read, which is the migration, which is correct and documented. THE TOOL DOES NOT PARSE THE REGISTER, AND THAT DECISION COST THREE FAILED ATTEMPTS TO REACH. (1) Narrow extraction captured 2 of the 9 names in entry 4 and MISSED remRender -- the exact identifier that prompted the whole tool. It came back CLEAN against the historical index and I nearly shipped it carrying a self-test claim in its own docstring that was FALSE. Found only by actually running the historical case instead of trusting the design. (2) Widening to 'every identifier minus the canonical ones' pulled the PROSE WORDS finish/load/desc/ship/unit/result/notes/customer/date and matched 'date' against a SAIRNlaw row about a holiday statute -- eleven false findings, which is how a checker gets ignored. (3) camelCase-only with a canonical-subtraction window then swallowed most of the real names. THAT CHURN IS THE FINDING: a checker built on parsing English commentary is the same class as an assertion that matches prose about the code -- Scrubber item 16 Shape A -- and I hit it three times while building a tool to catch documentation drift. It now ASKS THE FILE instead: for every OPEN StoneDesk index row, does each identifier the row writes as code (`name()` or `sd_key` in backticks) still exist. No prose interpreted on either side. IT HAS BEEN WATCHED GOING RED. Against the index as it stood before today's corrections it names row 177, remRender(), no definition. ONE OF THE TWO STALE ROWS, NOT BOTH -- the template row said 'different storage keys' and named neither in code form, so it is invisible to this check. That is the first limitation in its own docstring, DEMONSTRATED rather than asserted, and it is why the docstring says a clean run is not a claim that the index is current. TWO SMALLER TRAPS ON THE WAY: a cp1252 stdout crashed the tool INSIDE its own reporting path, because findings quote index rows containing em dashes and warning triangles -- a checker that dies while telling you what it found. And three `sd_*` citations in one row turned out to be SUPABASE TABLE names, not localStorage keys; the two namespaces share the prefix, so the tool now asks sql/ which is which. RESIDUAL, STATED RATHER THAN CLOSED: it sees only rows citing something in code form, only StoneDesk rows, and cannot detect a row stale for any other reason. Making it stronger needs the register to carry a structured line per entry instead of prose -- a change to ten documentation blocks that other sessions may be relying on the shape of, so it is a product call, not a fix I should make unilaterally.
- stonedesk.html + tools/orphan_register_check.py (Fourth) -- THE ORPHANED-MODULE REGISTER NOW CARRIES A MACHINE-READABLE LINE PER ENTRY, AND THE FIRST RUN FOUND TWO THINGS THE PROSE HAD BEEN WRONG ABOUT FOR WEEKS. Used sairn-memory-curator. ELEVEN @REGISTER LINES, NOT TEN. Michael approved restructuring the ten numbered entries; there is an ELEVENTH -- the Template/DXF manager -- which is UNNUMBERED and says 'RETIRED' rather than 'orphaned duplicate removed', so every phrasing-based scan had walked past it, INCLUDING ALL THREE of my own earlier parser attempts. That entry is the one the stale template index row was about. It could not be seen until it was given a structured line, which is the clearest possible argument for the format. THE INSERTION WAS PROVEN INSERT-ONLY, not assumed. The script verified line-by-line that every original line survives unchanged and in order before writing, and the diff is 35 insertions / 0 deletions across 11 entries plus two correction notes. Prose untouched -- it is where a human reads WHY a module was removed, and it is good writing. FIRST RUN OF THE STRUCTURED CHECK FOUND A REAL DOCUMENTATION ERROR: entry 5 names 'sdPOCreate/sdPORender' as the canonical survivors and sdPORender() DOES NOT EXIST -- there is no sign it ever did. The PO module exposes sdPOCreate/sdPOAI/sdPOPrint/sdPOExport and its renderer is a plain local function render() inside the IIFE, unnameable from outside. Corrected in the @REGISTER line with a dated note left in place. NOTED, NOT CHANGED, because it is a different question: that local render() runs at IIFE load and after sdPOCreate but is NOT called on nav, unlike every other panel -- harmless today because the data is local and the only mutation path re-renders, but it means the panel shows page-load state if anything else ever writes sd_pos. retired_keys IS A SEPARATE FIELD FROM orphan_keys, deliberately. sd_template_records is still READ, by tmMigrateLegacyRecords(), and never written. 'Dead' and 'read-only legacy' are different states; collapsing them would either raise a false alarm on the template manager or hide a real one where a key genuinely came back. The checker asserts exactly the right thing for each: an orphan key must be untouched, a retired key must have no writer AND at least one reader. THE CHECK IS NOW STRICTLY STRONGER AND THE OLD LIMITATIONS ARE GONE. Per entry it asserts five things against the FILE rather than the text: every deleted= name has no definition, every canonical= name has one, every orphan_keys= key is untouched, every canonical_key= is present, every retired_keys= key is read-only. Across the index it now checks EVERY open row (not just StoneDesk) and matches on bare word boundaries (not just identifiers written as code) -- both safe now, because the names come from a curated list somebody wrote down rather than a scrape of commentary. AND IT HAS BEEN WATCHED CATCHING BOTH ROWS. Against the pre-fix index: row 177 on remRender (a deleted= name) and row 178 on sd_template_records (a retired_keys= key). The prose-parsing version reached only the first, and only after three rewrites. Guardian: 129/129 blocks parse, div balance PASS, current index CLEAN.
- stonedesk.html + tests/quote_history_duplication.js (NEW) (Fourth) -- THE QUOTE HISTORY PANEL'S TOTAL VALUE CLIMBED ON ITS OWN EVERY TIME ANYONE CHANGED A STATUS. Used sairn-code-scrubber (item 5, collided storage keys). load() MERGES two stores -- sd_quote_history and sd_aiquotes -- so the panel can show both. save() wrote the WHOLE MERGED ARRAY back into sd_quote_history alone. There is exactly one mutator, sdHistoryUpdateStatus(), and it does `var d=load(); ...; save(d)`. So every status change copied every AI quote into the history store, and because load() concatenates sd_aiquotes again next time, the copies ACCUMULATE: history [H] + ai [A] -> save([H,A]) -> next load [H,A,A] -> next [H,A,A,A]. One duplicate per AI quote per status change, permanently. ALL FOUR KPIs ARE COMPUTED FROM THAT ARRAY -- Total Quotes, Total Value, Win Rate and Average -- so they inflate together. Derived by hand rather than read off a run: from a real $7,000 of quotes, two status changes produce $13,000. A shop would watch its own quoted pipeline grow without quoting anything. FOUND BY tools/key_collision_check.py, which flags sd_quote_history as written by two distinct backing variables -- the same tool and the same signal that found the sd_referrals duplicate on 2026-08-07, recorded in register entry 16. THE TOOL STILL FLAGS IT AFTER THE FIX, WITH THE NEW VARIABLE NAMES, AND THAT IS CORRECT -- two writers is exactly what the fix introduces on purpose. Written into the test's header so the next person does not 'fix' the fix: the tool is a POINTER, not a verdict; entry 16 says the same about its own finding ('traced by hand'). The suite is the verdict. ROUTING, NOT STRIPPING. save() now partitions the array by where each row came from and writes each half back to its own store. Stripping the AI rows out would have been simpler and would have traded a duplication bug for a DATA-LOSS one: a status change on an AI quote is a real edit, and today it is the only way to make one. The _src tag is added at load and removed on write, so it never reaches storage, and sd_aiquotes is only written when the array actually carries rows from it -- writing [] would have erased that store on any save made from a state where it happened to be empty. 10 assertions. The headline one is behavioural and is what Michael asked for: three quotes stay three across TEN status changes, and Total Value / Win Rate / Average are asserted stable as a set, not just the count. Plus: an AI-quote edit lands in sd_aiquotes and not in history; the tag never persists; an empty sd_aiquotes is not erased; an absent one is not invented; corrupt JSON in the second store does not take the first one's rows down; and a mutation restoring the one-line save() proves the counts climb 3 -> 5 -> 7 and the money reaches $13,000. Guardian: 129/129 blocks parse, div balance PASS. Four other suites re-run green, register check CLEAN.
- stonedesk.html + tools/key_collision_check.py (Fourth) -- FINISHED THE COLLISION SWEEP: TWO OF THE FOUR WERE BENIGN, ONE WAS A REAL SILENT FAILURE, AND THE TOOL NOW SAYS WHICH. Used sairn-silent-failure-sweep. THE TWO REMAINING COLLISIONS ARE BENIGN BY DESIGN, traced by hand rather than assumed: stonedesk:ai_memories and stonedesk:business_profile are each written by a sync function caching the SERVER copy and by a writer saving the LOCAL one. Same owner, same shape, server-wins-on-sync. Not the sd_referrals shape (two modules, two record shapes) that this tool was built for. BUT READING THEM FOUND A REAL DEFECT IN writeSDMemory(). Both its writes were fire-and-forget: st() returns false on a quota failure, sdData() returns null on any failure, and NEITHER result was read, inside a bare catch(e){}. The server one is what matters -- syncSDMemoriesFromSupabase() replaces the local cache WHOLESALE with whatever the server returns, so a memory whose server write failed sat in localStorage looking remembered until the next sync silently erased it. 'The AI stops forgetting what it told you two questions ago' is a SOLD CLAIM; it failing quietly is the same shape as the six window.fetch patches that had been doing nothing since they shipped. DELIBERATELY LOGGED AND NOT TOASTED, and that is a judgement rather than laziness: this fires unprompted every fifth assistant reply. The user did not ask for it and is mid-conversation. A toast would interrupt them to report a background housekeeping failure they cannot act on. The console lines are for whoever is looking, and the bare catch now logs too. saveSDProfile() HAS THE IDENTICAL DEFECT AND ZERO CALLERS. Quarantined by name per Check 0d rather than deleted -- it is the only write path that profile has and something will want it back -- with the defect written out at the site and LEFT UNFIXED ON PURPOSE, so the shape stays visible next to the warning instead of being half-fixed in dead code nobody reviews. THE TOOL NOW DISTINGUISHES A POINTER FROM A VERDICT. key_collision_check.py reported four collisions and three were correct by design; without a record, every session that runs it re-traces the same ones by hand, which is exactly what I just did. It now carries an ACKNOWLEDGED map: key -> the exact variable pair it is acknowledged FOR, plus a written reason. NAMING THE VARIABLES IS THE POINT. An allowlist that silences a key forever would be worse than none -- that is how sd_referrals hid until 2026-08-07. PROVEN WITH TEETH, not asserted: renaming one writer in a throwaway copy of the file re-opens the collision, exits 1, and prints 'this key IS acknowledged, but for [h, hist] -- the writers have changed'. Real file exits 0. Guardian: 129/129 blocks parse, div balance PASS, quote-history suite still 10/10.
- tools/key_collision_check.py + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- CLOSED THE '~40 UNTRACED STORAGE KEYS' ROW WITH A NUMBER, AND FOUND A DEFECT IN THE TOOL THAT WAS DOING THE COUNTING. Used sairn-code-scrubber. THE ROW ASKED FOR SOMETHING THAT IS NOW MECHANICALLY ANSWERABLE: 93 writes, 79 distinct keys, 4 collisions, 0 unacknowledged. Every collision traced by hand and recorded against the exact variable pair it is acknowledged for -- one was a real duplication bug fixed earlier tonight, two are sync-caches-the-server-copy pairs that are correct by design. A TOOL DEFECT, FOUND BY READING ITS OUTPUT INSTEAD OF ITS SUMMARY LINE. The wrapper alternation `(?:localStorage\.setItem|st|stRaw)\s*\(` had NO LEFT WORD BOUNDARY, so the helper `st` matched any identifier ending in those letters. showToast('Brand colors saved for this shop') was being counted as a storage write WITH THAT SENTENCE AS THE KEY -- four toast messages sat in the key list and the totals were inflated by three writes and three keys. It could not HIDE a real collision, only add noise, but noise in a collision detector is exactly what gets a collision detector ignored, and a toast string that happened to equal a real key would have manufactured a false one. Fixed with two different boundaries, not one: localStorage may legitimately be preceded by a dot (window.localStorage.setItem) while a bare helper never should be, because x.st(...) is somebody's method rather than this file's wrapper. TWO KEYS ARE INVISIBLE TO COLLISION DETECTION BY DESIGN and are now NAMED rather than left as an unknown quantity: sd_demo_cleared and sd_slab_legacy_dismissed are written as literals, not from a variable, so there is no backing variable to collide. Both are single-value flags. That is the honest residue of this check and it is two, not forty. THREE KEYS CARRY NO sd_ PREFIX AND ALL THREE WERE CHECKED RATHER THAN FLAGGED: quoteCounter is a deliberate exception already listed in SA_APP_EXACT; sairn_privacy_accepted is platform-wide on purpose; and stondesk_biz IS A TYPO -- missing the 'e' -- that is consistently misspelled on BOTH its writer and its reader, so it works. RECORDED, NOT RENAMED: a rename orphans every existing user's data, and a migration for a cosmetic typo is the exact hazard the tmMigrateLegacyRecords fix dealt with hours ago. CROSS-APP FINDING HANDED OFF RATHER THAN TAKEN: sairndental.html reports 1 UNACKNOWLEDGED collision -- dnt_settings_obj, with saveBookingSettings/dnPersistWindows/saveGfeIdentity all writing `rec` and dntSyncFromServer writing `serverSettings`. CC held sairndental at the time so I did not investigate it. Its own index row says what to look at first: three writers sharing one variable name across three save paths is the sd_invoices schema-collision shape, and on StoneDesk this exact sync-vs-local pattern was benign twice and a real bug once -- the difference was only visible by reading what each writer puts in the array. Regression-checked the boundary fix against three other apps: sairnbiz 32 writes / 0 collisions, sairnroofing 2 / 0, sairndental 45 / 1.
- sairnvet.html + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- AUDITED THE APP NOBODY HAD AUDITED, AND THE RESULT IS MOSTLY A CLEAN BILL PLUS TWO CAPABILITY CLAIMS THE CODE DOES NOT IMPLEMENT. Used sairn-guardian-v2. SAIRNVET IS MECHANICALLY CLEANER THAN STONEDESK ON EVERY CHECK: 1/1 script block parses, div balance 1213/1213, 367 globals with 0 duplicates, 45 storage keys with 0 collisions, nav PASS, dead-button audit 0 in EVERY section (A/B/C1/C2/D1/D2/E), and MISSING_TARGETS:0 across 775 getElementById calls. StoneDesk has 149 missing targets; this app has none. CHECK 0b's PRIMARY SHAPE CAME BACK EMPTY, AND THAT IS A REAL NEGATIVE RESULT WORTH RECORDING: all 173 KPI values are built from computed variables inside template strings and NOT ONE is a literal in the markup. My first scan looked for `<div class="kpi-val" id=...>` and found zero, which was MY lens being wrong rather than the app being clean -- SAIRNvet builds its KPI markup in JS instead of declaring it in static HTML. Corrected the scan before drawing a conclusion. It also does not use the SEED/demoCleared fallback pattern AT ALL -- zero occurrences -- so it does not carry the fabricated-demo-data class I have spent the night clearing out of StoneDesk. TWO CAPABILITY CLAIMS, BOTH FIXED, BOTH FOUND BY THE KEYWORD SWEEP 0b PRESCRIBES. (1) The Mobile/Ambulatory panel said 'Live tracking for farm-call and mobile-unit vets'. navigator.geolocation APPEARS NOWHERE IN THE FILE -- every row comes from a 'Log a Stop' form a vet fills in after the fact. That is the same claim StoneDesk had removed ('live-GPS/ETA claims with no tracking system') and 0b names it explicitly. (2) 'Client Portal -- client-facing appointment requests, records access, and messaging' is THREE claims and none is true: there is no client-facing page, no client login, no route but /sairnvet, and addPortalRequest() reads two text inputs a STAFF MEMBER types. Renamed to 'Client Requests' and both descriptions rewritten to what the panels actually do. THE KPIs UNDER BOTH PANELS ARE HONESTLY COMPUTED -- Distinct Clients, Requests This Week, Avg Response Time, Response Rate all derive from the log. THE DEFECT WAS THE WORDING ABOVE THEM, NOT THE ARITHMETIC, and the fix is wording only: no panel was deleted, no id changed, so nav, CSV export and print targets are untouched. CHECKED MY OWN FIX THE WAY THE AI-SHORTCUTS TEST TAUGHT ME TO: re-running the capability sweep still reported both claims, because MY OWN COMMENTS QUOTE THE OLD WORDING. Stripped HTML comments and // lines before re-matching -- 0 for 'Client Portal', 0 for 'live tracking', 0 for 'records access'. Same trap, third time tonight, caught immediately this time. COVERAGE DISCLOSED PER CHECK 0b-coverage RATHER THAN IMPLIED: this was a full mechanical pass, a file-wide capability keyword sweep across eight categories (SMS, e-signature, GPS, cloud storage, portals, notifications, email, payments), and a full scan of all 173 KPI value-pairs. THAT IS COVERAGE OF PATTERNS. NO PANEL WAS INDIVIDUALLY READ for whether its computed numbers mean what its labels say -- which is what the original 'twenty panels never audited' row actually asked for, and it stays open, sized L, with the mechanical half marked done. NOTED, NOT MINE: the index now has 8 malformed table rows (unescaped pipes). None is one I wrote tonight -- mine validate at 7 cells -- and cody holds that fix.
- tools/jscomments.py (NEW) + tools/sairn_seam_check.py + tools/sairn_stale_snapshot_scan.py + stonedesk.html + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- SEVEN COMMENT STRIPPERS IN tools/, THREE OF THEM DESTROYING MOST OF THEIR INPUT, AND THE FIX FOR IT WAS ALREADY WRITTEN DOWN IN ONE TOOL AND NEVER PROPAGATED. Used self-improving-agent (turn a lesson repeated three times into a mechanism). MICHAEL NAMED THE PATTERN AND THAT IS WHAT SENT ME LOOKING: three times tonight a check re-run after a fix still reported the defect because THE FIX'S OWN COMMENT QUOTED THE OLD WORDING. Each was caught by habit rather than mechanism. Per memory-curator section 6, a rule that fails twice needs a mechanism -- this had failed three times. WHAT I EXPECTED TO FIND was a missing stripper. WHAT I FOUND was seven of them, and a measured table that is much worse than the trap I went looking for. Against sairnvet.html (362 function declarations): dead_button_audit 94.6% survives / 0 lost, dead_function_sweep 95.0% / 0 lost, and discarded_verdict_check 11.1% / 350 LOST, seam_check 10.0% / 350 LOST, stale_snapshot_scan 11.1% / 350 LOST. Same three lost 200 of 212 on sairncare.html and 405 of 1003 on stonedesk.html. A SCANNER READING A TENTH OF A FILE AND REPORTING CLEAN IS NOT A WEAK CHECK, IT IS A FALSE ONE. I NEARLY GOT THIS WRONG THE SAME WAY I GOT SAIRNVET'S KPI SCAN WRONG. My first probe fed BARE JS to strippers designed for whole HTML documents, and reported dead_function_sweep as stripping nothing. Its docstring explains that it deliberately scopes JS-comment rules to inside <script> -- MY PROBE WAS UNFAIR, not the tool. Re-ran with full HTML before drawing any conclusion, and the picture inverted: dead_function_sweep is one of the two GOOD ones. THE CAUSE WAS DOCUMENTED IN ONE TOOL AND NOWHERE ELSE. sairn_dead_function_sweep.py's own docstring records that the /* inside <input accept="image/*"> opens a block comment which runs to the next */ anywhere in the file, and that it once blanked 80.5% of sairncare.html and reported init/saveFacility and seven others as DEAD. It was fixed THERE, in isolation. The three tools carrying the identical naive regex were never checked. SAME SHAPE AS THE ORPHANED NPS MODULE WHOSE DAMAGE TWIN NOBODY LOOKED FOR -- a documented fix for one instance did not prompt anyone to look for the others. tools/jscomments.py is now the one implementation, extracted from the state machine that measured best, with a 10-case probe covering every input that separated the seven: a // inside a string, a /* inside a string, a template literal, a regex containing //, a URL with //, length-and-newline preservation, and the accept="image/*" landmine by name. Measured across five app files afterwards: 0 function declarations lost in every one. Its measure() also CLASSIFIES rather than counts -- stonedesk's saUnlock disappears correctly because it is declared only inside a comment quoting removed code, and reporting that as 'lost' would have made this tool's own output wrong in exactly the way the tools it replaces are. IT IMMEDIATELY FOUND A REAL BUG THAT THE BLIND SCAN HAD BEEN REPORTING CLEAN. stonedesk's remake month-to-date KPI: (r.createdAt||'').slice(0,7) === new Date().toISOString().slice(0,7). BOTH SIDES UTC, so internally consistent and consistently wrong for a US shop -- createdAt is an ISO instant, so on the last evening of every month UTC has already rolled over and the KPI drops to the few records stamped in the new UTC month, reading ~0 for the final four to eight hours of every month. TWO LINES ABOVE IT, the 'completed today' KPI does the right thing with new Date(r.completedAt).toDateString(). The two disagreed about what month it was, two lines apart. Fixed to compare the record's LOCAL month against sdLocalToday(), which is consistent with the line above rather than merely different from what was there. PLATFORM-WIDE RE-SWEEP with the working scanner: 0 UTC calendar derivations across all 23 app html files. seam_check migrated too and still reports 75 clean / 0 findings -- same answer, now reached on the whole file instead of a partly-blanked one. Its exposure was narrower (2 of 80 api/*.js files) but included api/law-auth.js, a live auth endpoint, at 62% visible against 71%. HANDED OFF, NOT TAKEN: discarded_verdict_check.py is the third broken one and Cody holds it tonight. Its index row says exactly what to do and warns that its previous clean results were reached against ~11% of each file.
- tools/sairn_strict_args_check.py + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- RE-SWEEP AFTER THE STRIPPER FIX, AND IT CORRECTED TWO OF MY OWN CLAIMS FROM AN HOUR AGO. MICHAEL ASKED ME TO CONFIRM stale_snapshot_scan's STATUS AND NOT ASSUME. Confirmed mechanically (grep for the delegate, not memory): seam_check and stale_snapshot_scan BOTH migrated in fc07ed07 -- the UTC bug I found IS the evidence stale_snapshot_scan was migrated, since the blind version could not see that line. BUT A FOURTH TOOL WAS BLIND AND I HAD MISSED IT. sairn_strict_args_check.py -- GUARDIAN CHECK 31's SCANNER, WHICH RUNS BEFORE EVERY PUSH -- surviving 10.0% on sairnvet, 9.6% on sairncare, 51.7% on stonedesk. It failed to import in my first probe because it wraps sys.stdout at module load, and I recorded it as UNMEASURED rather than clean. THAT DISTINCTION IS THE ONLY REASON IT DID NOT STAY MISSED, and it is the same distinction seam_check's own header makes about 'the checker said nothing' versus 'the checker could not look'. Migrated. AND I WAS TOO GENEROUS ABOUT sairn_dead_function_sweep. I called it 'one of the two good ones': 0 declarations lost on sairnvet and sairncare, but 218 OF 1003 ON STONEDESK.HTML. Good on two apps, materially blind on the largest one. Corrected in the index; not migrated yet, so it stays a known gap rather than a silent one. THE RE-SWEEP ITSELF CAME BACK CLEAN, AND I RAN IT RATHER THAN ASSUMING IT. Check 31 across eight apps: IDENTICAL before and after -- 1 site on stonedesk, clean elsewhere. The reason matters and is worth stating rather than claiming a save: that tool strips comments only inside a 3000-character window after each matched function, so whole-file blindness never translated into missed findings there. stale_snapshot_scan across all 23 app html files: 0 UTC derivations, 1 stale-snapshot site (stonedesk, pre-existing, already listed). seam_check: 75 clean / 0 findings, unchanged. discarded_verdict_check RE-VERIFIED WITHOUT EDITING IT, because Cody holds it: loaded the module and swapped its strip_comments for the shared one AT RUNTIME, then ran it. 0 discarded verdicts, the same as shipped. It still needs the real migration, and its index row says so. NET RESULT OF THE WHOLE EXERCISE: ONE real defect (StoneDesk's remake MTD KPI computed in UTC on both sides, reading ~0 for the last hours of every month), found the moment the scanner could see the file. Every other previously-'clean' result re-ran clean for a reason I can name. That is a better answer than 'probably fine' and it took the re-run to get it.
- tools/sairn_dead_function_sweep.py + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- THE LAST BLIND TOOL, MIGRATED, AND IT IS THE ONE MIGRATION THAT CHANGED A RESULT. sairn_dead_function_sweep's in-script /*...*/ regex was losing 218 of stonedesk.html's 1003 function declarations while losing 0 on sairnvet and sairncare. Its own docstring already said the in-script hazard was 'smaller but not zero'; measured, 'not zero' was 21.7% OF THE LARGEST APP ON THE PLATFORM. Good on two apps and blind on the one that matters most is exactly the shape a per-app spot check misses. WHAT I DID NOT DO: replace its script scoping. That scoping is the fix for the accept="image/*" incident this tool documents, and jscomments knows JS but not HTML -- pointed at raw markup it would strip a bare // outside quotes. So the hard-won structure stays and only the blunt instrument inside each <script> body is swapped. Minimal change to a tool another session may run tomorrow. THE RESULT MOVED, AND IN THE DIRECTION THAT MATTERS. stonedesk.html: TOTAL_FUNCTIONS 1148 -> 1364 and DEAD 59 -> 93. The 216 extra definitions are ones the sweep HAD NEVER COLLECTED -- they sat inside spans the blind stripper blanked, so they were never reported live and never reported dead. They were invisible, which is worse than either. HAND-VERIFIED BEFORE WRITING IT DOWN, because this tool's own header insists on it and because I have over-claimed twice tonight. Five of the newly-visible dead names, checked by raw grep against the unmodified file: agMarkSigned, cartClear, commsSetTab, dbSaveSettings, dlCSV -- EACH OCCURS EXACTLY ONCE, as its own definition. dbSaveSettings is an async settings-save function nothing calls. CROSS-CONFIRMED BY A DIFFERENT TOOL: the new DEAD list overlaps heavily with the zero-caller readers my missing-DOM-target triage found independently earlier tonight (commsSend, commsTpl, commsAIDraft, custSave, bltnSave, bltnPost, aiQuickPrompt). Two tools, different methods, same names. NOT DELETED. The sweep is a report, not a gate, and removal belongs with the 98-id deletion pass already flagged as Michael's call. sairnvet, sairncare, sairnbiz and sairnroofing were unchanged by the migration, consistent with 0 declarations lost there -- so this is a stonedesk-sized finding, not a platform-wide one. That closes the stripper thread: five tools measured blind or partly blind, four migrated, one (discarded_verdict_check) re-verified read-only and left for Cody who holds it.
- docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth) -- RE-VERIFIED MY OWN PENDING-SQL ROW AGAINST THE LIVE DEPLOYMENT INSTEAD OF LETTING IT STAND. Used sairn-silent-failure-sweep (a migration is not done when a command exits clean, it is done when the destination is queried back). SAIRNBUILD'S SCHEMA IS STILL NOT RUN, AND THE ROW IS STILL ACCURATE -- which is the answer I wanted and not the one I assumed. Probed the deployed api/sd-data with BLD-PINNACLE-2026: read of bld_jobs returns {"ok":true,"data":[],"provisioned":false}; write returns 503 NOT_PROVISIONED naming sql/sairnbuild_data_schema.sql.
