THE HONEST-DEGRADE DESIGN WORKS END TO END AGAINST THE REAL DEPLOYMENT -- that is the part worth confirming, because it is what stops the gap reading as an EMPTY backup rather than an ABSENT one. I RE-DERIVED THE 'CROSS-CHECKED BOTH DIRECTIONS' CLAIM RATHER THAN TRUSTING MY OWN EARLIER WORD FOR IT: 30 registered BLD_RESOURCES against 30 create-table statements, zero on either side without a counterpart, and all 39 of the app's bld_* localStorage keys accounted for as registered, documented-exclusion, or identity. It holds. TWO FALSE ALARMS I RAISED AND KILLED BEFORE REPORTING, both mine. (1) A probe of 'bld_estimates' came back 400 'resource must be one of' and looked like a missing registration -- bld_estimates DOES NOT EXIST anywhere: not a table, not a key, not a resource. I invented it in the probe. (2) `grep -c 'grant.*delete'` returned 1 on the schema file, which read as a forbidden delete grant. IT IS THE COMMENT FORBIDDING DELETE GRANTS. FOURTH TIME TONIGHT that a grep matched prose about the code rather than the code, and the fourth time it was caught by reading the hit. That is now less a trap than a standing tax on grep-shaped checks. THE FILE IS READY WHENEVER MICHAEL RUNS IT: preflight clean (with its own caveat that a file written and never run looks PRESENT to it), 30 tables, 30 RLS enables, and NO POLICY BY DESIGN -- RLS on with no policy denies every role lacking BYPASSRLS, so service_role via api/sd-data.js is the only door, exactly as the header states. No delete grant anywhere. NOTHING TO FIX HERE. The value is a dated verification replacing an assumption, and a file confirmed runnable rather than assumed runnable.

- tools/sairn_claim_hook.py + tests/claims/run_push_verify_probe.py + .claude/claims/README.md + CLAUDE.md (Fourth, 2026-09-04, `39e5fee`) -- THE SAME FIX, IN THE COPY THAT ACTUALLY RUNS UNATTENDED. Earlier the same day the destructive `git checkout origin/main -- .claude/claims` was removed from `tools/sairn_claim.py` and CLAUDE.md recorded the defect as FIXED. It was not. `tools/sairn_claim_hook.py` carried its own copy of the identical line, and that is the file registered as a SessionStart hook -- so the defect was still firing automatically in every clone at every session start, while both the probe and the standing doc read green. THE FIX WAS APPLIED TO THE COPY A HUMAN INVOKES AND MISSED THE ONE THAT RUNS BY ITSELF. Found by a plain `git status` at session start showing `M .claude/claims/hank.json` STAGED, which nobody had staged: `git checkout <ref> -- path` both overwrites the working tree and stages what it wrote, so a later `git commit` sweeping the index would have committed origin's copy over a claim this clone had made -- the tool that exists to stop sessions colliding, deleting the record of one. The hook now fetches (which writes only a remote-tracking ref) and reads origin/main's claim files with `git show`; on an unreadable origin it falls back to local copies and SAYS the list may be incomplete rather than presenting stale data as current. PROBE SECTION 8 RUNS THE HOOK BINARY, NOT THE LIBRARY, and asserts six things: it stages nothing; it leaves an uncommitted hand-written claim byte-identical; IT LEAVES AN UNCOMMITTED EDIT TO A *TRACKED* CLAIM FILE INTACT (the real damage -- `git checkout <ref> -- path` only rewrites paths present in the ref, so a brand-new local file survives it while an edit to a tracked one is silently replaced, which is exactly what happened to hank.json and is why the pre-existing new-file assertion would NOT have caught this); it still reports a claim that exists only on origin; it never executes `git checkout`; and it does read with `git show`. ALL SIX FAIL AGAINST THE OLD HOOK -- verified by restoring it and re-running the probe, not assumed. Also corrected: `.claude/claims/README.md` claimed the hook "resolved from `C:/SAIRN/tools/`". It does not. Every hook command in `.claude/settings.json` is a RELATIVE `tools/...` path resolving inside the clone, and no settings file anywhere points at `C:/SAIRN/tools/` -- which holds byte-identical stale mirrors of several hooks, so editing one there changes nothing. THE STANDING LESSON, recorded in CLAUDE.md: a fix verified on the copy a human invokes is not verified if a second copy runs unattended -- grep the whole repo for the defective line before calling a defect closed.

- sairnscape.html + tests/ld_reports_unreadable.js + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth, 2026-09-04) -- SAIRNSCAPE'S `scpLd()` COULD NOT TELL A CORRUPT RECORD FROM AN ABSENT ONE, the 7th of the original 14 to be fixed and the 6th driven by the shared suite. Was `function scpLd(k,d){try{var r=localStorage.getItem(k);return r?JSON.parse(r):d;}catch(e){return d;}}` -- a record that will not parse returned exactly what a key that was never written returns, so "your data is corrupt" and "you have none yet" rendered identically and nothing was logged. Fixed to the shape SAIRNcare and SAIRNfreedom already carry: return `d` unchanged on every failure so all 44 call sites behave exactly as before, log on the catch, and report an UNAVAILABLE STORE differently from an UNPARSEABLE VALUE because the two need different fixes. THIS COPY WAS THE TRUTHINESS SPELLING, `r ? JSON.parse(r) : d`, the one SAIRNbiz had rather than the `r === null` every other app uses. It matters for exactly one value: an empty string took the ABSENT branch, silently. `scpSv()` JSON.stringify()s and can never write `''`, so an empty string in that store did not come from this app; it is an unreadable record and now reads as one, with the same return either way. A THIRD VACUITY OF THE SUITE'S OWN RECURRING SHAPE WAS FOUND AND CLOSED, and this is the part worth keeping. The source assertion had already gone vacuous twice on the BODY (first the `r === null` literal only, which SAIRNbiz passed without being fixed; then a second literal, which SAIRNfreedom passed vacuously on whitespace). This time it was the FUNCTION NAME: the suite hardcoded `function ld(k,d){` in both the vm driver and the source regex, and SAIRNscape's loader is `scpLd`. Adding the app unchanged would have produced a sixth green section that drove nothing and a source check true of a file it had never opened -- READING AS PROGRESS. The name is now derived from the app row, `load()` asserts it actually resolved to a function, and each source pattern carries a NEGATIVE CONTROL asserting it still matches the defect it names, so a pattern that stops matching fails instead of passing. Verified: `node tests/ld_reports_unreadable.js` 56/56; `tools/checkblocks.py` 5 blocks 0 failed; div_balance 496/496; nav_panel 12/12; panel_nesting 0 trapped; duplicate_global 0; missing_dom_target 0; `fail_open_check.py` browser pass down from 8 loaders to 7, and the suite asserts that number so a silent drop reads as a failure. REMAINING SEVEN, deliberately not swept in one pass: sairnbuild, sairndental, sairndesign, sairngrounds, sairnsenior `ld()`, and stonedesk `sdLoad()`/`itaLoad()` -- three of those files were held by another session's active claim at the time, which is why SAIRNscape was the one picked.

- PROCESS HAZARD FOUND THE HARD WAY (Fourth, 2026-09-04) -- NEVER `cd` INSIDE A BASH CALL IN THIS REPO. The Bash tool's working directory persists between calls, and every hook in `.claude/settings.json` is a RELATIVE `python tools/...` path resolved against that directory. One `cd docs && ...` left the shell in `docs/`, after which every PreToolUse hook looked for `docs/tools/...`, did not find it, and BLOCKED Bash, Edit and Write alike -- all three verified blocked, with no in-session recovery available, because the only way to reset the directory is a Bash call that the hook now refuses. Michael had to run `!cd /c/Users/marsh/Documents/SAIRN-fourth` by hand (the backslash form is eaten by the shell; use forward slashes). Use absolute paths or a tool's own `-C`/`--cwd` flag instead. This is the same relative-path property recorded above as the reason the `C:/SAIRN/tools/` mirrors are inert -- written down as a harmless fact an hour before walking into its other edge.

- api/sd-data.js + api/_resources/extra-actions.test.js + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth, 2026-09-04/05, `dc1458f6` + follow-up) -- A JUNK BEARER TOKEN COULD ENUMERATE ALL 268 RESOURCE NAMES, and the index row that had sat on it for eleven days undercounted them by ninety-seven. `POST /api/sd-data` with `Authorization: Bearer not-a-real-key` and an unknown resource answered 400 naming every registered resource on the platform, because the envelope gate ran above licence validation. Names only, never data, and every path past the gate still 401'd -- but it enumerated the whole surface to a caller with no credential, and let them tell a real resource from an invented one by which refusal came back. Michael's call to fix it. MEASURED RATHER THAN INHERITED: the row said 171; driving the pre-fix handler (`git show HEAD:api/sd-data.js`) with a junk token returned **268 of 268**. Licence validation now runs immediately after the bearer check, so an invalid token learns exactly one thing -- that its token is invalid -- and cannot distinguish a real resource from an invented one, a permitted verb from a refused one, or an oversized payload from a legal one. THE HARD PART WAS THE TEST SEAM, NOT THE REORDER. `api/_resources/extra-actions.test.js` drove ~20 gate assertions THROUGH the handler with a junk token, which only worked because of the very ordering being removed; with validation first every one of those calls returns the same 500 and allow becomes indistinguishable from refuse. The gate is now exported as `checkEnvelope` and driven directly -- the same function the handler calls, not a reimplementation, which is the test's own stated bar ("re-deriving the condition it is checking would pass against a broken gate"). Six new assertions go through the REAL handler for the ordering itself, INCLUDING ONE ON THE PRODUCTION PATH with env set and the licence lookup stubbed to "no such key", because the other five pass on the missing-env 500 that only a developer machine ever sees. LIVE-VERIFIED on production: five probes with a junk token returned byte-identical `401 INVALID_LICENSE`, zero resource names, one distinct body. The VALID-token direction was NOT verifiable by me and I did not pretend otherwise -- it needs a real licence key, which must never be typed anywhere but its destination. What does cover it: `api/sd-data-approvals.test.js` and its siblings stub `validateLicenseKey` to return a valid active licence and drive the real handler through the gate to a real write, and 56 suites touching sd-data pass. KNOWN COST, stated rather than discovered: every junk-token request now costs a `license_keys` lookup it did not before, and the first-failure response changed for every app.

- INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF THE ABOVE, by a separate agent, under the aviation-grade standard Michael set (2026-09-05) -- verdict: fix correct, leak closed, live-confirmed. It did NOT take the commit message's word for anything: it re-ran the junk-token probes itself, confirmed 268 two ways, audited all ELEVEN error messages in the file that echo the caller's `resource` back and found every one below the licence gate, confirmed the `checkEnvelope` export is not routable (Vercel invokes the default export only; `vercel.json` carries no rewrite that could address a named property) and is pure, and -- the part that mattered most -- REVERTED ONLY THE ORDERING in a scratch copy and got 28 passed / 6 failed, proving all six new assertions bite and none is vacuous. It also found the one client in the repo that branches on this 400, `sairnlaw.html:1389` `lawWriteFailText()`, and established it does not regress because it only fires for a caller with a valid active licence. TWO OF ITS FINDINGS WERE FIXED IMMEDIATELY, both mine: (1) MY REORDER LEFT DEAD CODE -- `if (!SUPABASE_URL || !SERVICE_KEY)` became unreachable, because `api/_lib/license.js` now checks the same two variables first and throws CONFIG. Dormant code that looks like a safety net is worse than none, and I did not notice it; the reviewer did. (2) the source-ordering assertion was purely lexical and a comment quoting `await validateLicenseKey(licenseKey)` above the gate would have let it pass with the real call below -- it strips comment lines now, and I proved the hardening rather than asserting it by poisoning a copy and watching the anchor move from offset 44 to 471. FOUR MORE FINDINGS BECAME ROWS RATHER THAN EDITS: the disclosure is now AUTHENTICATED rather than gone (any one app's customer still enumerates all 268 across every other app -- a cross-tenant decision for Michael, not for the session that made the change); the endpoint now makes an unauthenticated DB round trip with no rate limit, which is a database-amplification path where a free local refusal used to be; the same pre-auth verb disclosure survives in ~20 `api/*-auth.js` endpoints, so the CLASS is not closed platform-wide; and `api/*.test.js` files are deployed as publicly invocable serverless functions. THE STANDING LESSON: every one of the two fixed findings was in code I wrote an hour earlier and had already checked. A reviewer who reverts the change and watches the tests is worth more than a reviewer who reads it.

- api/*.test.js ARE PUBLIC FUNCTIONS, found 2026-09-05 (Fourth) in the production runtime logs while live-verifying something else -- then reproduced, because a log line is a claim. `GET https://sairn.vercel.app/api/sd-data-approvals.test` returns 500 FUNCTION_INVOCATION_FAILED, and the 500 IS the proof: Vercel routed it, loaded it and ran it. The runtime log for that anonymous GET carries the entire suite's output, all 23 assertions. The file `require`s `api/sd-data.js` and executes at module load. The response body discloses nothing, so this is not a data leak -- it is twenty-plus anonymous code-execution and compute surfaces that exist purely by accident of naming. `api/_lib/` and `api/_resources/` are protected by the leading underscore; the files sitting directly in `api/` are not. Recorded as its own row, not fixed here -- it is unrelated to the change I was sent to make, and a `.vercelignore` needs checking against whatever resolves those paths today.

- INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF CC's `569b2689` (SAIRNdental write-failure voice), Fourth, 2026-09-05 -- ASSIGNED, and read against the code rather than the commit message. THE FIX IS REAL AND ITS PROBES GENUINELY BITE: I re-ran all five mutations myself rather than trusting "all biting" -- restoring the local-first charge fails 2 assertions, asking for the wrong resource fails the pairing walk, restoring the false sentence fails 2, clearing the payment box early fails 1, removing the visit guard fails 1 -- and `sairndental.html` was sha256-identical afterwards. The walker really does cover 14 write paths against its own floor of 12. FOUR FINDINGS, NOT FIXED because that file is CC's and CC is mid-session elsewhere. (1) THE ONE THAT MATTERS: `dntWriteFailText()` returns `dntLastErrText(resource) || fallback`, and `sdnData()`'s `.catch` stores `e.message` -- a raw browser exception. So an offline save shows a receptionist "Failed to fetch", and a Vercel error page shows "Unexpected token '<'... is not valid JSON" because `r.json()` rejects into the same catch. Either REPLACES the fallback, and for dnt_charges/dnt_payments the fallback carries the only sentence that matters: nothing was recorded on this device or the server. (2) The ledger now drops rows on an UNREACHABLE server, not only a refused one -- server-first is right for a 400, but the early return fires on ANY null including no-licence and network-down, so a practice with a flaky connection loses every charge and payment it enters. `dntLastErrCode()` already exists to tell NETWORK/HTTP_5xx from a 400 and is unused. (3) Pre-existing and MISSED rather than introduced: `submitCompleteVisit()` still announces success when the APPOINTMENT write fails -- `setAppointmentStatus()` toasts its honest failure and the unconditional `toast('Visit completed, charge added')` overwrites it, because `toast()` is one element setting textContent; that function is also still local-first, so the visit reads Completed here and Scheduled everywhere else, with a charge attached. The commit tightened the charge half of that exact function and left the appointment half. (4) Two holes in the suite, neither a live bug today: the pairing walk exempts any function containing `dntLastErrText(` without checking WHICH resource it asks for (hand-checked -- all four exempted paths do pass their own), and `sdnData()` returns null WITHOUT setting `dntLastErr` when there is no licence key, so a stale message from an earlier failure can be shown as the reason.

- .vercelignore + docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth, 2026-09-05) -- 36 TEST FILES WERE PUBLICLY INVOCABLE SERVERLESS FUNCTIONS, and the fix is one line. Vercel filesystem-routes `api/`, and a LEADING UNDERSCORE on a directory is the only thing that opts a file out -- which is exactly why `api/_lib/` (102 test files) and `api/_resources/` (1) were never exposed while every `*.test.js` sitting in `api/`, `api/sairncash/` and `api/sairndental/` was. FOUND BY ACCIDENT, IN THE PRODUCTION RUNTIME LOGS, while live-verifying the sd-data reorder: a 500 on a path I had never heard of, `GET /api/sd-data-approvals.test`, with the entire suite's output -- all 23 assertions -- printed in the log for an ANONYMOUS request. THE 500 IS THE PROOF, NOT THE PROBLEM: a 500 means Vercel routed it, loaded the module and ran it. The file `require`s api/sd-data.js and executes at module load. The response body disclosed nothing, so this was never a data leak; it was 36 anonymous code-execution and compute surfaces existing purely by accident of naming, one of them `api/sairncash/stripe-webhook.test.js`. MY OWN COUNT WAS WRONG FIRST TIME AND I CAUGHT IT BEFORE IT SHIPPED: I filed the row saying 25, having counted only the top level. `api/sairncash/` and `api/sairndental/` carry no underscore either. Probed rather than reasoned about -- `/api/sairndental/public-book.test` 500, `/api/sairncash/verify.test` 500, `/api/_lib/auth.test` 404 -- which is both the correction and the confirmation that the underscore rule is what governs. Fixed with a single `*.test.js` line, deliberately NOT scoped to `api/`, after verifying no non-test file anywhere in the repo requires one. Also annotated the rest of `.vercelignore`: everything from `*.DAT*` to `.lesshst` is residue from when the home directory was itself a checkout of this repo, names nothing that exists in a real clone, and is left in place rather than deleted -- a deployment-config deletion with nothing to verify it against is not worth the risk, but a reader should not take those lines for evidence that this repo has a `Documents/` directory.

- api/_resources/index.js + api/sd-data.js + api/_lib/anon-rate-limit.js (NEW) + two test suites (Fourth, 2026-09-05) -- THE TWO DECISIONS MICHAEL MADE ON THE sd-data REORDER'S RESIDUALS, BUILT. (1) PER-APP SCOPING. Closing the anonymous hole on 2026-09-04 left an AUTHENTICATED one: any single app's customer still received every other app's resource names. `checkEnvelope()` takes the caller's app as a third argument now and names only that app's resources plus `shared` -- a SAIRNdental caller sees 27 names where it saw the whole platform. THE APP COMES FROM `lic.app_id`, THE LICENCE ROW, NEVER FROM `body.app_id`; a scoping rule the caller chooses is not a scoping rule, and sd-data.js already carries what trusting that body field cost the `memory` branch. A source assertion fails if it is ever fed from the body. THE RESIDUAL IS NOT CLAIMED CLOSED: nothing read `lic.app_id` before this change, so there is no evidence every live licence has it set, and an unrecognised or absent app_id FALLS BACK TO THE FULL LIST rather than to shared-only -- the wrong failure direction would hand a real legacy customer a list missing their own resources on the one message they read when already confused. The handler logs a warning naming any licence that takes the fallback, so that set is discoverable rather than assumed empty. Also learned: the total is not a fixed number -- 268 when the anonymous hole closed, 283 hours later, because other sessions register resources continuously. Do not hardcode it. (2) THE RATE LIMITER, AND THE DEVIATION I RAISED BEFORE BUILDING IT. Michael's instruction was "same shape as api/_lib/ai-rate-limit.js". That module is Supabase-backed, one RPC per call, so putting it in front of this path would make every junk-token request cost TWO database round trips instead of one -- it would measurably worsen the exact problem it was brought in to solve. Flagged and approved, so `api/_lib/anon-rate-limit.js` is in-memory, per-instance, runs BEFORE `validateLicenseKey`, and costs nothing. THE LOAD-BEARING PROPERTY IS THAT IT CAN NEVER REFUSE A WORKING CUSTOMER -- only a FAILED validation is recorded, so a valid key accumulates no count at any volume, which is why this enforces by default where ai-rate-limit.js observes by default: a refusal here can only land on a request that was already going to be refused one status earlier. Asserted through the REAL handler, not argued in a comment. An inactive licence, an upstream 502 and a missing bearer are all uncounted. WHAT IT DOES NOT DO IS IN ITS OWN HEADER: per-instance, cold-start resets it, and the address is platform-set but not unspoofable -- every degradation ends in "allowed", so the worst case is yesterday's behaviour. ITS OWN PROBE CAUGHT A REAL BUG IN IT: the map settled at 5001 rather than 5000 because prune ran before the insert, which is only visible if you assert the ceiling instead of trusting the call order. Verified: 17/17 in the new suite, 40/40 in extra-actions, 192 JS suites across the whole repo pass with 0 failures.

- api/_lib/anon-rate-limit.js + api/sd-data.js + suite + index (Fourth, 2026-09-05) -- THE RATE LIMITER I ARGUED FOR AND BUILT DOES NOT WORK, MEASURED IN PRODUCTION, AND I FOUND IT ONLY BECAUSE I LIVE-VERIFIED INSTEAD OF TRUSTING 17 GREEN ASSERTIONS. 40 concurrent junk-token requests returned 40x 401 and NOT ONE 429, against a limit of 20 in 60 seconds. An earlier sequential run of 30 could not have fired and that was arithmetic rather than a fault -- ~3.3s per request against a 60-second fixed window tops out near 18 -- but the concurrent burst was the case the thing exists for. THE FIRST FIX WAS INSTRUMENTATION, NOT A REPAIR, because nothing logged could distinguish three completely different causes: one instance failing to count, many instances each counting once, or clientAddress() returning empty and the limiter being inert. A LIMITER YOU CANNOT OBSERVE IS INDISTINGUISHABLE FROM ONE THAT DOES NOT RUN -- this platform's most-repeated defect class, shipped by the session that keeps writing it down. The instrumented burst was decisive: every line read `addr_src=x-vercel-forwarded-for tracked=1`, so the address IS read and each instance was tracking exactly one address correctly, and the counts climbed in a dozen PARALLEL sequences with the busiest reaching 10 of 20. HORIZONTAL SCALE-OUT DEFEATS A PER-INSTANCE COUNTER IN PROPORTION TO THE ATTACK: concurrency is precisely what makes Vercel add instances, so a bigger flood spreads across more of them and every individual count gets LOWER. The design fails hardest against exactly the case it was written for. Its only realistic effect while enforcing was a rare FALSE refusal of a real customer behind a shared address who mistyped a key -- all cost, no benefit -- so the DEFAULT IS NOW OBSERVE and it can refuse nobody, held by two assertions including one that asserts the finding is recorded in the module itself rather than only in a row somebody has to find. MY ARGUMENT WAS HALF RIGHT AND MY CONCLUSION WAS WRONG. A Supabase-backed limiter really would cost two round trips instead of one, which is why I refused ai-rate-limit.js's shape and Michael approved that reasoning -- but the answer was never an in-process counter on a platform that runs many processes. The right layer is a Vercel Firewall rule: it sees every instance and costs no database round trip. Raised for Michael as a decision (platform rule, or accept the amplification and DELETE the limiter rather than leave dormant code that reads like a defence) rather than decided here. The scoping half of the same commit is unaffected and independently live-verified. Kept honest by the same standard applied to everyone else's work tonight: a row that said FIXED now says MEASURED INERT, and says it before anyone had to catch it.

- INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF 3e21bc5d AND c341631a, folded in (Fourth, 2026-09-05) -- verdict: deploy fix clean and live-verified; scoping correctly sourced, correctly scoped, no cross-app leak, every new assertion mutation-proved; limiter well built and well tested AGAINST THE WRONG PROBLEM, which it measured from the sequential side (30 requests, 121s, zero 429s) while I was measuring it from the concurrent side. FIVE FINDINGS FIXED IMMEDIATELY, all in my own code. (1) THE ONE COVERAGE GAP: converting the window from FIXED to SLIDING -- a materially stricter behaviour that would ban a steady prober indefinitely -- passed all seventeen assertions. Closed, and proved load-bearing by mutating to sliding and watching it fail. (2) `clientAddress()` did not cap length, so MAX_TRACKED bounded the number of KEYS and not their size: the "bounded at 5000" comment was true of the count and false of the memory. Capped at 64 chars with its own assertion. (3) The 429 hardcoded "Wait a minute" while the window is env-configurable, and carried no Retry-After -- a confidently wrong sentence of exactly the shape this repo keeps recording. Both fixed. (4) The header argued spoofing only as EVASION and never as FRAMING: if the platform header is ever absent, x-real-ip and the first XFF entry are attacker-controlled and can poison a victim's bucket to get a real customer refused. Recorded as a blocker on ever enforcing again. (5) The suite inherited SAIRN_ANON_INVALID_LIMIT and WINDOW_SECONDS from the environment, so it passed either way while meaning something other than what it said; pinned. THREE FINDINGS BECAME ROWS. The sharpest: SCOPING THE LIST DID NOT CLOSE THE ORACLE, and it scopes a MESSAGE rather than access. Against the real handler with an active stonedesk licence, `__nope__` returns 400 with the scoped list but `law_matters`, `sc_denial` and `leg_cases` all return 200 -- no cross-tenant DATA leak, every row is license_hash-scoped, but the CAPABILITY is not app-gated at all, and any authenticated caller can still classify every guessed name one request at a time. Pre-existing, and it matters because the row read like an access control. Also: the fallback is NOT hypothetical -- sairnvet.html and sairncash.html both POST to sd-data and NEITHER has a registry module, so a live class of licences takes the full-283 fallback today. And a correction to my own commit: sairnlegacy.html carries the same `resource must be one of` client branch as sairnlaw.html; neither breaks, but my survey named one file and there were two. THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE SAME ONE AS THE LAST REVIEW: both reviews found real defects in code I had written within the hour and already checked. The reviewer that reverts the change and watches the tests is worth more than the reviewer that reads it.

- api/_resources/{index,sairnvet,sairncash,app-boundary.test}.js + api/sd-data.js (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- THE APP BOUNDARY: a licence for one app could address every other app's resource types, and now cannot. Michael's decision on the independent review's finding that the 2026-09-05 scoping fix was a MESSAGE control and not an access control. Against the real handler with an active `stonedesk` licence, `law_matters`, `sc_denial` and `leg_cases` all returned 200 -- no cross-tenant DATA leak, every row is license_hash-scoped so it saw its own empty slice, but the CAPABILITY was ungated across fifteen apps. THE MEASUREMENT CAME BEFORE THE CODE, because a wrong answer here is a platform-wide outage rather than a bug: every app HTML at the repo root was scanned for what it actually asks sd-data for, and every one touches only its own module plus `shared`. The two apparent exceptions were the whole risk and both dissolved on inspection -- stonedesk.html's `jobs` (owned by sairngrounds) and `progress_photos` (owned by nobody) both POST to /api/sd-sub-data, a different endpoint with its own three-name registry, confirmed by READING THE FETCH CALL rather than inferring from the name. That measurement is now an assertion that re-derives itself every run, so an app that starts crossing fails in the suite instead of in production. ONE RESPONSE FOR TWO CONDITIONS, DELIBERATELY: a foreign resource is answered exactly as a nonexistent one -- same 400, same scoped list, byte-identical body -- which closes the ENUMERATION ORACLE in the same move, because before this an authenticated caller could classify any guessed name one request at a time. A 403 would be more semantically precise and would rebuild the oracle it was supposed to close; indistinguishability over precision is the deliberate trade, and it preserves the response shape sairnlaw.html and sairnlegacy.html both match on. Unattributable licences are still allowed through -- same conservative direction as the scoped list, same reason, every one logged. SAIRN_APP_BOUNDARY=off disables ONLY the boundary, never the unregistered check, shouts on every request it lets through, and must be said out loud. SAIRNVET AND SAIRNCASH REGISTERED with EMPTY lists, which is the measured answer: neither owns a resource, and inventing sv_* names would have been a claim nothing backs. AND A CORRECTION TO THE REVIEW THAT ASKED FOR THEM: sairncash.html does NOT call sd-data at all -- its only "sd-data" string is inside a comment and its only endpoint is /api/sairncash/checkout. Registered anyway, because license_keys.app_id is set by whoever issues a key rather than by the page, so a SAIRNcash licence could be presented here by anything holding it. Verified: 12 assertions, 5 of which fail when isVisibleTo is mutated to always return true; 195 JS suites pass, 0 failures.

- stonedesk.html + tests/stonedesk_shop_identity.js (NEW) + tests/nesting_saw_ticket.js (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- EVERY CUSTOMER'S EXPORTS, PRINTS, AI PROMPTS AND OUTBOUND MESSAGES NAMED ONE SHOP. The row estimated "roughly a dozen" sites from a grep and said to enumerate before editing. Enumerated: 132 lines. ~30 CSV filenames, so a fabricator in Ohio downloaded `customers-pinnacle-2026-09-08.csv`; ~70 print headers, titles and footers; TWELVE AI prompts, so the advisor believed it was advising another company; and the SMS and email templates a shop sends to ITS OWN customers, signed with another company's name and carrying its phone, its domain and its Cleveland address. The phone and domain had to move with the name -- fixing only the name ships a template signed correctly with the wrong phone number, which is worse than leaving it. THE STORAGE ALREADY EXISTED and was already written by the admin Company Profile form; what was missing was readers, and what existed instead was three different private fallbacks plus one site reading `sd_shop_name`, a key nothing writes. THE DRY RUN EARNED ITS KEEP TWICE, and both would have shipped silently. (1) The first transformer picked the NEAREST preceding quote as the string delimiter; on `document.write('<p style="color:#888">Pinnacle Stone</p>')` that is the double quote inside the style attribute, so it produced `"+escHtml(sdShopName())+"` INSIDE a single-quoted string -- where quotes are ordinary characters and the whole expression prints literally on a customer's report. (2) Rewritten to scan real string state, it then wanted to splice STATIC MARKUP, because the apostrophe in "Hello! I'm your StoneDesk AI assistant" opens a string as far as a single-line scanner can tell. BOTH ARE SYNTACTICALLY VALID JAVASCRIPT THAT node --check ACCEPTS. Neither was caught by a checker; both were caught by reading the dry run, which is the whole reason CLAUDE.md says never bulk find-replace. Final split: 125 machine-edited, 7 by hand, 9 deliberately left (comments, seeded demo reviews and the demo employee row, the demo-mode label, the form placeholder, and SD-PINNACLE-2026 which is a licence KEY not a name). Static markup carries `<span class="sd-shop-name">` filled at load with textContent, never innerHTML, because a shop name is user input. NOTED AND NOT FIXED: eleven exports never named any shop at all -- not this defect, and widening the rule to them would be a new requirement rather than this one. THE FULL SWEEP CAUGHT A REGRESSION I HAD NOT PREDICTED: tests/nesting_saw_ticket.js drives the saw-ticket function in a vm and its context had no sdShopName, so seven assertions broke the moment the header stopped naming a fixed shop. Fixed in the HARNESS, not the code, with a deliberately distinctive stub value so a header that silently stops using it fails an assertion rather than looking plausible. Verified: 16 new assertions, 9 of which fail against the pre-sweep file; 129 script blocks parse, divs balanced, 0 duplicate globals, key_collision clean, and the 150 missing-DOM findings proved pre-existing by running the checker against the pre-edit copy rather than assumed; 197 JS suites pass, 0 failures.

- INDEPENDENT REVIEW OF THE APP BOUNDARY, folded in (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- verdict: the access control is sound, safe in production, and live-confirmed for every licence that can be checked. IT ALSO FOUND TWO OVERCLAIMS IN MY COMMIT, and both were the headline sentences. (1) THE ENUMERATION ORACLE WAS NOT CLOSED. The ACTION gate ran above the existence check, and its message depends on facts about the resource, so an EXTRA VERB still classified any guessed name one request at a time: `bogus_verb` on a foreign sc_ resource answered "or 'delete'" while an invented sc_ name did not, and `delete` on a real foreign name gave the resource refusal while an invented one gave the action refusal. Confirmed live against production with a real stonedesk licence. That covers all 28 sc_* names plus every evaluate/route/derive_charges/reserve owner. My "same 400, byte-identical body" was true ONLY for read and write -- which is the only case my own test drove, which is exactly how the claim survived. Visibility is decided FIRST now, so the action gate only ever sees a resource the caller can already see and nothing it says can leak. (2) THE SELF-RE-DERIVING OUTAGE GUARD -- the assertion I described as making a future cross-app call fail in the suite instead of in production -- MISSED THREE OF THE FOUR REALISTIC SHAPES. It matched only a literal `xData('verb','name')`, so a call through `rfDataRaw(` or `hrCall(` (helpers whose names do not contain "Data") and a foreign name added to a SYNC ARRAY all passed while the suite printed 12 passed, 0 failed. The reviewer proved it by injecting all four. The sweep is helper-agnostic and reads array literals now, and I re-verified all four injections are caught rather than trusting the fix. ALSO FIXED FROM THE SAME REVIEW: app_id was trimmed but not case-folded, so a licence issued as 'StoneDesk' would have silently disabled its own boundary AND received the full 283-name list, with no signal but the unattributable warn; the refusal object was built on every request including successful ones; and the boundary's console.warn was caller-driven and UNBOUNDED, on the endpoint that had just gained a rate limiter for that precise amplification shape -- now once per (app, resource) per instance, with the set itself capped. THE REVIEWER MEASURED LIVE WHAT MY COMMIT SAID WAS IMPOSSIBLE, and this is the part worth keeping: the scoped list is itself a read-only oracle for lic.app_id, so one probe per key attributed ALL TWENTY repo licences -- every one correct and lowercase, zero unattributable -- and an own-resource read for each returned 200 or 401, never 400. My commit asserted "no session can read license_keys.app_id in the real database" and the same commit made that false. AND A MISTAKE OF MY OWN, CAUGHT BY COMPARING TEST NAMES RATHER THAN COUNTS: my first attempt to update the ordering assertion sliced from that test to a distant section marker and silently DELETED SIX real assertions on the way; the suite still printed green at a lower count. Reverted and redone surgically, verified by diffing the list of test names before and after. Verified: 42 in extra-actions, 12 in app-boundary, 198 JS suites pass, 0 failures.

- stonedesk.html + tests/stonedesk_followup_chip.js (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- THE ROW'S HEADLINE WAS WRONG AND A DIFFERENT, REAL CRASH WAS ONE INDIRECTION AWAY. The row said missing_dom_target_check found 41 unguarded reads, 40 in dead code, and that the exception -- setMode() doing getElementById('messages').innerHTML -- was "reachable from a real onclick", with the next step being a live-DOM reachability run. DISPROVEN FROM SOURCE, conclusively, so no snapshot was needed: ZERO elements carry the bare `mode-btn` class (the three grep hits are `dc-mode-btn`, a different class wired to setDCMode -- found by matching class-list TOKENS instead of substrings, which is what made the original count look alarming); ZERO string or dynamic references to setMode, so nothing dispatches it by name; and its only two callers are `if(typeof ... === 'undefined')` shims that are UNCONDITIONALLY OVERWRITTEN by the real selectDocPrompt and selectCamPrompt, both inside immediately-invoked IIFEs -- verified they close with `})();` and that neither real handler calls setMode at all. So all 41 are dead. BUT THE APP DID HAVE A LIVE CRASH IN THIS FAMILY, AND THE CHECKER COULD NOT HAVE FOUND IT. installHook() wraps the LIVE addMsg(), so generateFollowUps() renders three "Ask a follow-up" chips into #chatArea after every real AI answer over 150 characters. The chip handler drove the RETIRED chat: addMessage('user',q) into a #messages that exists nowhere in the file, chatHistory which is only ever declared inside setMode(), and sendToClaudeAndRender(). A customer clicked a suggested follow-up, got a TypeError, and NOTHING HAPPENED -- silently, with the chips still on screen inviting another try. Retargeted to sdAIQuick(), the same fix the file already documents for custFollowUpAI/vendorSpendAI/warrAskClaude, and it now TELLS the user when the live sender is unavailable rather than failing silently a second way. THE DURABLE LESSON IS ABOUT THE CHECKER: missing_dom_target_check finds unguarded getElementById(...).prop reads, and this crash was a plain FUNCTION CALL to something that does the unguarded read one level down. The 41-item list was the wrong place to hunt live crashes and the live one was not on it. LEFT AND RECORDED, NOT FIXED: the two shims at :17436-7 are dead normally and WORSE THAN NOTHING in a degraded load -- if either IIFE throws they survive, and their whole body calls a function guaranteed to throw. That belongs with the 18-function dead-code sweep. Verified: 8 assertions driving the real handler including a negative control that still matches the old one; 129 script blocks parse, divs balanced, 0 duplicate globals; the 150 missing-DOM findings unchanged before and after, which is correct since addMessage itself is still dead code; 199 JS suites pass, 0 failures.

- api/{alf,bld,dnt,grd,law,leg,mech,rf,sb,sc,scp,sd,sd-sub,sdn,sen}-auth.js + api/preauth-envelope-ordering.test.js + index (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- THE FIFTEEN DEFERRED AUTH HANDLERS, and the checker that can finally gate a push. Held half of the 2026-09-05 sweep: fourteen data endpoints were reordered then and these fifteen were left because two other sessions were live in auth files that day. Body parse and action enum now sit BELOW validateLicenseKey; the 405 and the bearer-presence check stay above deliberately, because neither discloses anything and a missing credential must still cost no lookup -- both asserted rather than assumed. THE DELIVERABLE BEYOND THE LEAK IS THE EXIT CODE. tools/preauth_oracle_check.py returns 1 while any DISCLOSURE stands, so it could never gate a push while these fifteen carried the shape. MEASURED BOTH WAYS IN ONE RUN rather than argued: restore the pre-reorder files and it exits 1; the reordered files exit 0 with PREAUTH_DISCLOSURES:0. The suite asserts that exit code. What was actually leaking was modest and the row was honest about it -- each file disclosed its own action vocabulary, and those verb names already ship inside the downloadable client JS. The material half was the ORACLE: malformed JSON, a valid envelope with a bad action, and a bad licence were three distinguishable answers to a caller holding no credential, and all three now answer 401 INVALID_LICENSE identically. THE ONE REMAINING ORACLE LINE IS A FALSE POSITIVE and is recorded so nobody chases it: api/sd-data.js's own 429 rate-limit refusal, deliberately above the licence call and disclosing nothing. The checker matches SHAPES and cannot tell a designed pre-auth refusal from an oracle; it does not affect the exit code, which keys on disclosures only. THE EDIT WAS SCRIPTED BUT NOT BLIND: the transformer refused to touch any file where `body` or `action` was referenced between the block's old and new positions, so a file needing a hand read would have been reported rather than mangled -- zero did, which is itself the evidence the fifteen really are one template. Extended the existing suite rather than adding a file: 15 per-file assertions with TWO anchors each so a partial move fails, plus validated-once, bearer-first, method-first, the checker's exit code, and ONE handler (sb-auth) driven END TO END -- junk token gets 401 naming none of its verbs, malformed and well-formed bodies answer identically, and a VALID licence still reaches the envelope gate, which is what proves the gate was moved rather than deleted. Restoring the pre-reorder files fails 16 assertions. Verified: 59 in that suite, 200 JS suites pass, 0 failures. The cost the row predicted -- suites asserting the old pre-auth 400s -- did not materialise; nothing anywhere asserted them.

- tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py + tests/push_gate/check7_probe.py (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- PREAUTH_ORACLE_CHECK IS NOW A BLOCKING PUSH GATE, check 7. Michael asked whether it was wired in after the fifteen handlers landed; it was report-only, and the standing rule is to promote a checker once its findings are clean. It had never been promotable: it exited 1 continuously from the 2026-09-05 sweep until 2026-09-08, and a gate switched on over standing findings is one somebody disables within the hour -- which is exactly why check 5 went in report-only. BLOCKS ON DISCLOSURES ONLY, matching the checker's own exit code. The weaker ORACLE tier is reported and never blocks, because one of those lines today is api/sd-data.js's 429 rate limiter sitting above the licence call BY DESIGN; the checker matches SHAPES and cannot tell a deliberate pre-auth refusal from a leak, so blocking that tier would deny a correct push. Reads the COMMIT BEING PUSHED rather than the working tree -- check 1's own recorded lesson, since `git push origin <sha>:main` ships something other than what is checked out -- and is scoped to the api/ endpoints the push touches, the same standard as checks 5 and 6. PROVEN BY DENIAL, WHICH IS THE POINT: a gate whose findings are clean has by construction never denied anything, so nobody knows whether it CAN. The probe puts the reorder BACK in api/sb-auth.js -- the exact pre-2026-09-08 shape, not an invented fixture -- asserts the push is refused and names the file, and separately asserts a CLEAN change to the SAME endpoint is allowed, so the passing arm cannot be explained by the scope filter alone. THE PROBE'S OWN FIRST RUN IS THE LESSON: it reported NOT BLOCKED against a push the gate had correctly refused, because the matcher looked for 'BEFORE the caller is authenticated' and the deny message line-wraps between 'the' and 'caller'. Exit code right, file named, and the one assertion that mattered red for the wrong reason -- which is why these arms assert on the refusal REASON rather than the exit code, and why a probe that has only ever been written and not RUN against the real defect proves nothing. Fails open and loudly on any internal error, same standard as every other check in the hook. Verified: 7/7 probe checks, whole-tree run still exits 0.

- tools/public_endpoint_declarations.json (NEW) + tools/preauth_oracle_check.py + tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py + tests/push_gate/check7_probe.py (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- THE 25 NO-BOUNDARY ENDPOINTS ARE DECLARED, AND AN UNDECLARED ONE CANNOT BE PUSHED. The checker had always printed them and its header had always said they must be READ rather than assumed public; nothing recorded the OUTCOME of reading them, so the list was re-triaged from scratch every time and a new one could be added with nothing to notice it. That is exactly how api/claude.js sat there as an open Anthropic proxy -- its own comment said "this endpoint has no other auth beyond a client-supplied app_id", so the design was known, WRITTEN DOWN IN THE FILE, and still unnoticed for months. Now one entry per endpoint with a class and an evidenced reason: public-by-design 11, bespoke-auth 7, webhook-signature 2, cron-secret 2, pilot-only 4. An undeclared no-boundary handler fails the checker (new UNDECLARED_PUBLIC_HANDLERS counter folded into its exit code) and is DENIED by push-gate check 7. THE READ FOUND TWO THINGS A BLANKET EXEMPTION WOULD HAVE BURIED. api/sd-agent.js is a FALSE POSITIVE -- it authenticates at :228 via store.validateAndGate(), a wrapper AUTH_RE does not know by name, exactly the class the checker's own header warns about. And api/agent/enqueue.js lets anyone knowing an agent_id queue an ARBITRARY operation to a customer's on-prem agent; its own header says that is "fine for a single-customer pilot" and "must NOT be treated as secure multi-tenant isolation... before more than one customer relies on this in parallel" -- a sound judgement with an expiry date nobody was watching, and whether it still holds is a question about the sairn_agents table that no session can read, so it went to Michael as its own row rather than being guessed. ALSO SURFACED AND DELIBERATELY NOT TOUCHED: now that api/claude.js HAS a boundary (Cody's fix), its body-parse refusals are visible as two ORACLE lines at :220 and :225 -- the same envelope-ordering shape as the twenty-nine already reordered. Low severity, not blocking since check 7 gates on DISCLOSURES only, and that file is under Cody's active claim, so it is recorded rather than edited under someone else's claim. PROVEN BY DENIAL: the probe gains a third arm planting a NEW endpoint that authenticates nothing and declares nothing -- the claude.js shape -- and asserts the push is refused; 10/10 arms with the repo restored and asserted restored. Verified: checker exits 0 with 25 declared and 0 undeclared; 201 JS suites pass, 0 failures.

- docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- THE sairn_agents TENANCY QUESTION IS ANSWERED: Michael ran sql/sairn_agents_tenancy_check_2026-09-08.sql and block 1 returned 0 customers, 0 agent rows enqueueable now. api/agent/enqueue.js's single-customer pilot precondition genuinely holds, nothing can currently be queued to any agent, and no fix is needed today. STATED PRECISELY RATHER THAN ROUNDED UP, because the difference will matter to whoever reads this next: what was confirmed is that zero rows are ENQUEUEABLE -- active, not canceled, not an expired trial. Blocks 3 and 4 were not reported back, so the table may still hold pending, revoked or trial-expired rows. That changes nothing about today's answer and everything about whether someone may later cite this row as "the table is empty". THE SAFETY IS A PROPERTY OF THE DATA, NOT OF THE CODE, which is the whole reason this closes as a STANDING RE-CHECK and not as done: enqueue.js is unchanged and still accepts an arbitrary operation with no credential, so the first customer to go active makes it reachable and the second makes it a cross-tenant hole. The re-check trigger is written into the row's Next action rather than left in a commit message -- the failure this whole thread was about was a sound judgement whose precondition nobody was watching, and a fix that ends by creating a second unwatched precondition would have repeated it.

- docs/SAIRN-OPEN-WORK-INDEX.md (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- TWO REPO-ROOT UNKNOWNS ANSWERED, and the second one was sitting in every session's `git status` all night. (1) The `.agents/` untracked pile does NOT exist -- checked in ALL FOUR clones rather than only this one, deliberately, because an untracked pile is per-clone by definition and "not in my clone" would have described one machine rather than closing the row. The only `agents` directory anywhere is `.claude/agents`, Claude Code's own subagent definitions, tracked, and not what the row meant. (2) `piac.html`, 138 KB, untracked at the repo root since 2026-08-28, is a SAVED COPY OF AN INDIANA DEPARTMENT OF HEALTH 404 PAGE -- `<title>Indiana Department of Health: Error - Page Not Found</title>`, with ZERO occurrences of SAIRN, supabase, sairn.vercel.app, api/ or localStorage. Not an app, not a draft, not a fixture, and present only in SAIRN-fourth. THE ONE QUESTION WORTH ANSWERING BEFORE LEAVING IT ALONE was whether it ships: it does not, because Vercel builds from the GitHub source and an untracked file is not in it, so `vercel.json`'s `cp *.html dist/` never sees it. NOT DELETED, on purpose -- it is somebody else's file and the `.agents` row's own instruction is to confirm what wrote something before removing it. Filed as its own row for whoever saved it. WORTH SAYING PLAINLY ALONGSIDE THIS: the unassigned backlog is now mostly DECISION-BLOCKED rather than work-blocked. Delete capability via sd-data (row 54) needs a soft-vs-hard-delete call; the load-state gates (68) need a decision on invocation, not more tooling; `license_keys` grants (98) and the two service_role grant rows (204, 205) need the schema owner; graphify (179) needs a scope decision; and the StoneDesk twelve local-only collections need Michael's call on scope. That is not a complaint about the backlog -- it is what a backlog looks like after the independently-actionable work has been cleared, and it is the more useful thing to report than picking a make-work row.

- api/_resources/stonedesk.js + api/sd-data.js + sql/stonedesk_data_schema.sql (NEW) + stonedesk.html + tests/stonedesk_server_backup.js (NEW) (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- STONEDESK'S LOCAL-ONLY RECORD NOW REACHES A SERVER. Michael's decision, and the first thing the work did was correct the number he authorised it against: the row said TWELVE, and re-running tools/local_only_collection_check.py after its own same-day repair -- commit message "the checker could not read half of StoneDesk" -- reports 26 of 37. Flagged BEFORE building rather than discovered mid-migration, and the corrected split approved. (CC had independently reached 26 in the row while I was measuring; the two agree.) 21 BACKED UP through one generic read/write pair, SD_LOCAL_RESOURCES, same shape as BLD_RESOURCES and SB_RESOURCES rather than 21 copy-pasted blocks. 7 EXCLUDED WITH A REASON EACH in the registry file, because "not in the list" and "decided against" look identical to the next reader: a regenerable counter, two device-config keys, an AI transcript, a log, derived market data, and sd_intake -- which is ALREADY server-backed through sb.from(INTAKE_TABLE), so adding it would have created a second, differently-tenanted copy of the same records. sd_templates and sd_email_threats were read individually and placed IN: the first is a 5-state workflow with a statusLog[] audit trail, the second a persisted array that is not recomputed. THE SCHEMA IS GENERATED FROM THE HANDLER'S OWN MAP rather than retyped, so a table name and its id column cannot drift, and the suite then re-checks registry/handler/schema agreement PAIRWISE -- two of three agreeing is exactly how drift hides. The id-column check is the sharp one: the handler upserts on_conflict=license_hash,<idCol>, and a schema whose unique constraint names a different column silently inserts a duplicate row per write instead of updating one. IT IS A BACKUP, NOT TWO-WAY SYNC, stated in three places rather than discovered: per-record last-write-wins, additive-only hydration, deletions do not propagate. HYDRATION IS WIRED INTO sdApplyLoggedInSession AND THE SUITE ASSERTS IT IS CALLED -- a backup nothing reads back is write-only, a second device would never see the first's records, and that is dormant code wearing a feature's name. TWO REGRESSIONS CAUGHT BY EXISTING SUITES, both real and neither predicted: st() referenced SD_SYNCED_ON unguarded, which broke it the moment tests/st_reports_failure.js lifts it out and drives it alone; and my backup hook's catch(e){} was a BARE catch in a wrapper that suite pins at ZERO for this app specifically -- the logger guard now lives in sdBackupHookFailed(), which also keeps "the save failed" and "only the backup failed" as two different sentences to the user. AND ONE FALSE POSITIVE IN MY OWN NEW SUITE: the no-delete-grant assertion matched the header comment EXPLAINING that there is no delete grant -- the same detector-matched-a-comment bug sairn-code-scrubber item 16 Shape A records and the pre-auth checker shipped with. Caught by running it, not by reading it. THE FLOOR IS NOT CLOSED AND THE ROW SAYS SO: ~45 unclassified keys and ~30 SAFE_DEMO_KEYS names are untouched by this and are their own pass. Verified: 16 new assertions, 205 JS suites pass, 130 script blocks parse, divs balanced, 0 duplicate globals, SQL preflight clean, md_table_check clean.

- api/_resources/stonedesk.js + api/sd-data.js + tests/stonedesk_server_backup.js (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- SOFT DELETE, Michael's decision on the platform's long-open "no delete capability" row. The record is marked and hidden; the row stays and stays recoverable. AND IT NEEDED NO NEW DATABASE PRIVILEGE, which is the point of the design: the marker lives inside the existing `data` jsonb and the write is an UPDATE, so sql/stonedesk_data_schema.sql still grants select/insert/update and no delete -- asserted rather than intended. THE VERB IS `soft_delete`, NOT `delete`, and that is deliberate: `delete` already exists on this platform and means a real DELETE -- the SAIRNcode Compliance-Admin branch issues the only method:'DELETE' in api/sd-data.js -- and two verbs that destroy different amounts of data must not share a name, because a caller copying a working call from one family to another would silently get the other behaviour. A resource may not hold both, asserted. READ-MODIFY-WRITE, NOT A BLIND UPSERT OF THE CALLER'S COPY: the write path takes the full record from the client, but a DELETE must not double as an opportunity to overwrite the stored record with a stale one, so this reads what is actually stored, adds the marker, and writes that back. A MISSING RECORD IS A 404 NAMING WHAT DID NOT HAPPEN, never a silent success -- the false-success shape this repo keeps recording. Reads filter `data->>_deleted_at=is.null`, and the null-safety is asserted specifically: `eq.` instead of `is.null` there would have hidden every row written before the marker existed. SCOPED TO ONE FAMILY ON PURPOSE -- BLD, SB, LEG, SDN, LAW and DNT share the table shape and could take the verb mechanically, but each has its own session or role gate and "who may delete" is not the same question as "who may write". ONE REAL BUG OF MY OWN, CAUGHT BY EIGHT EXISTING SUITES AT ONCE: the branch was first placed ABOVE the `const SD_LOCAL_RESOURCES` it reads, so every request to EVERY resource died on a TDZ ReferenceError -- the guard is evaluated on every call, not only for this family. node --check accepted it happily. Verified: 22 assertions in that suite, 205 JS suites pass.

- LIVE-VERIFIED THE STONEDESK BACKUP AFTER MICHAEL RAN THE MIGRATION (Fourth, 2026-09-08) -- all 21 resources return provisioned:true on a StoneDesk demo licence, where every one answered provisioned:false before the schema existed. The app boundary still holds on the new tables: a sairngrounds licence asking for sd_invoices and a sairnscape licence asking for sd_templates both get the scoped 400, not access. The write path refuses a payload with no id before touching the database. An unauthenticated caller still gets 401 and learns none of the new table names. DELIBERATELY DID NOT WRITE A TEST ROW: there is no hard-delete path, so a probe record would be permanent junk -- which is precisely the 15-hand-written-cleanup-files problem the index already carries a row for. A read returning provisioned:true is sufficient evidence the table exists and the handler reaches it; the write path shares the same table and its 503 was triggered by the PostgREST 404 that is now gone.

- docs/2026-09-09-load-state-gate-invocation.md (NEW) + docs/2026-09-09-license-keys-and-service-role-grant-state.md (NEW) + docs/2026-09-09-graphify-scope.md (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-09) -- THE THREE WRITEUPS, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM FOUND ITS ROW ALREADY STALE. (1) LOAD-STATE GATES: the row asks for an invocation decision and names `tools/deadline_load_state_check.py` as canonical -- THAT FILE DOES NOT EXIST in any clone, the tool is `sairn_load_state_check.py` and it serves all four apps. The real coverage, which the row does not state: its ONLY caller is push-gate check 1, which fires only when the outgoing commits touch a seed file, so A SEED CHANGE CANNOT SHIP UNLOADED AND A QUIET LICENCE IS NEVER CHECKED -- a half-completed loader, a licence provisioned later from a stale load, or a hand edit in the SQL editor are all invisible. Recommended a Vercel cron behind CRON_SECRET on one ground only: it is the sole option where the four customer licence keys DO NOT MOVE. GitHub Actions needs them as repo secrets, which is a new home for a secret, and this project's standing rule is that a secret only ever goes to its real destination. Two things left to Michael because they are judgment: where the alarm goes (Vercel runtime logs is moving the problem, not solving it) and whether it gates or only reports. (2) LICENCE_KEYS / SERVICE_ROLE: FOUR ROWS, THREE OF THEM DESCRIBING WORK ALREADY RUN, TWO FOR A FORTNIGHT. The TRUNCATE row's status says "draft fix written, not run" while the file's own line 3 says SECTION 2 WAS RUN 2026-08-25 CLEAN PASS and a RESOLVED row in the same index carries the verification. - tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py + tests/push_gate/{check8_probe.py (NEW),check4_probe.py,check7_probe.py} + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- PUSH-GATE CHECK 8: A PROBE FIXTURE COMMIT CANNOT REACH ORIGIN, and I built it a day late. Yesterday I reverted b909dbee, wrote the mechanism down, and FILED the gate as a row instead of building it. This morning fab44663 "PROBE seam violation" reached origin/main and deployed -- check4_probe's arm 2, which deletes `service_methods: body.service_methods` from api/legal-deadlines.js, THE FIELD WHOSE ABSENCE ALREADY MADE SAIRNLAW RUN FLORIDA FIVE DAYS LATE ONCE. AND MY OWN FIX YESTERDAY WIDENED THE EXPOSURE: repairing check4_probe's twelve-day silent SKIP is what made it actually run. The repair was right; shipping it without this gate was not. CHECK 8 denies in both hook modes and names the sha and subject. A DRY RUN IS EXEMPT AND THAT IS CORRECTNESS, NOT CONVENIENCE -- it publishes nothing, and check4/check7 each have an arm whose whole point is that a CLEAN change is ALLOWED, which would otherwise stop short of what it names. PreToolUse reads --dry-run/-n from the command text; GIT HANDS A PRE-PUSH HOOK NO DRY-RUN SIGNAL AT ALL, verified rather than assumed, so the two probes declare themselves with SAIRN_PROBE_PUSH=1, suppressing check 8 only and set nowhere else. ITS POSITION IS LOAD-BEARING: immediately before check 1, so checks 2-7 answer first and a planted seam violation is still denied BY CHECK 4 naming the field. My first version sat AFTER check 1 and never ran at all -- check 1 exits early on `if not apps` for any push touching no seed file, i.e. nearly every push -- and the probe caught that, not reading. 11 assertions, control first, all three exemptions proven against the same planted commit. STATED LIMIT: it catches the commit at the door and does not stop the probes creating it; a detached-HEAD probe would remove the hazard instead and that trade wants deciding rather than assuming. My own duplicate revert of fab44663 was DROPPED once I found Cody had already pushed one -- checking origin before pushing a revert is the same lesson as checking claims before starting.

- sairnfreedom.html (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE BACKUP HOOK ADDED A SECOND SILENT CATCH TO st(), AND THE SUITE WAS RIGHT TO REFUSE IT. tests/st_reports_failure.js went red platform-wide -- 145 of 146 -- with "expected exactly 1 bare catch(es) in the wrapper -- got 2", SAIRNfreedom only, from 55889165's server backup putting its console.error behind an inline logger guard inside st().
