RAISING SAIRNFREEDOM TO 2 WOULD HAVE BEEN THE WRONG FIX -- that hatch is for a guard that genuinely must live inline and a logger does not. Lifted into sfBackupHookFailed(), same shape and reason as StoneDesk's sdBackupHookFailed(): the outer catch means the record was NOT saved, this one means it WAS saved locally and only the backup threw, and collapsing them tells somebody their work was lost when it was not. Behaviour unchanged, location only. 58/58, both extracted blocks node --check clean, html_script_check clean. Not my code and unclaimed; claimed before touching it.

- tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py + tests/push_gate/check9_probe.py (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- PUSH-GATE CHECK 9: THE NAMED GUARD AND SEAM TESTS CAN NOW STOP A PUSH, Michael's decision after deadline-endpoint-inputs.test.js caught a live legal-deadline field being dropped and was powerless, the suite being REPORT-ONLY after a push. NOT THE FULL SUITE, deliberately: 146 files on every push is a real tax and most test one unit's behaviour, which the author's own run already catches. THE REGISTRY IS THE SEAM CLASS -- tests that check two independently-maintained sides still AGREE -- because those fail when somebody ELSE changed one side and therefore arrive silently, usually by rebase. Five entries, each a recorded defect, each carrying WHAT IT GUARDS and WHY IT EXISTS so the refusal explains itself. WHOLE TREE, NOT THE TOUCHED FILES, and that inverts checks 5/6/7 on purpose: fab44663 reached every clone by rebase, so scoping to touched files would have skipped the one test that names it -- the refusal says in those words that it may not be your change, and tells the reader not to raise a count to get past it. THE CLEAN-TREE FALSE-ALARM HAZARD WAS THE THING NOT TO REINTRODUCE and it is handled rather than hoped away: check 9 reads the working tree, several suite probes mutate tracked files mid-arm, so it checks run_all_tests.py's per-clone lock and says COULD NOT TELL if a run is in flight -- it never denies on a maybe -- and an ordinary dirty tree is PROVEN not to be a finding with a real modification rather than argued. ONE CONTEXT OBJECT PER RUN, because check 1 has its own emitter and two JSON objects on stdout is a payload nothing promises to read past the first of. TWO OF MY OWN MISTAKES, both caught by the probe and neither by reading: the first version sat after check 1 and NEVER RAN, because check 1 exits early on `if not apps` for any push touching no seed file; and GUARD_TESTS was a local variable that the probe parsed out of the source, splitting on the first `]`, which lands inside one entry's own prose ["mail","email"] -- it reported a ONE-ENTRY registry and then passed its existence check on that one entry, a check that looked green while inspecting a fifth of what it named. GUARD_TESTS is module-level now so the probe reads the real object. 21 assertions, driven entirely through the PreToolUse stdin path so it strands no PROBE commit of its own, planting THE REAL HISTORICAL BREAK rather than an invented one, with arm 4 holding that same break under the lock so the exemption is isolated to exactly one variable.

- tests/st_reports_failure.js + tests/stonedesk_server_backup.js (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- TWO ASSERTIONS THAT WENT RED ON CORRECT CHANGES, and neither was widened. (1) SAIRNfreedom's bare-catch count: another session read the same red suite an hour before me and named the count 2 with its reason, verified it still bit at 3, and was CORRECT ABOUT THE CODE AS IT STOOD; my commit had moved the second logger out of st() into sfBackupHookFailed(), so the code had 1 and the fixture said 2 and the suite went red in the other direction. Restored to the default and recorded as SUPERSEDED RATHER THAN WRONG, because naming a higher count is the escape hatch for a guard that must live INLINE and a logger does not. (2) My own soft_delete assertion counted the PLATFORM-WIDE registry aggregate and asserted 21, which is StoneDesk's number -- it read as a StoneDesk assertion and was not one, and the second half required every grant anywhere to appear in StoneDesk's own map. eb640ae8 gave dnt_supplies the verb, a deliberate reviewed decision in another app, and both halves failed on a correct change -- exactly the pressure that gets an assertion deleted. SPLIT RATHER THAN WIDENED: StoneDesk counted on its own, still 21, and the spread beyond it asserted BY NAME, so a new family taking the verb has to be written down. Both verified in BOTH directions -- restoring the old expectation makes each fail again, so neither is now a loosened count.

- sairnvet.html + tests/sairnvet_seed_never_syncs.js + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE LAST TWO OF THE FIVE INDEPENDENT-REVIEW FINDINGS. CHECKED WHAT WAS ALREADY DONE BEFORE STARTING, and three of the five were: a858cab7 took the demo-seed-reaching-the-live-server one, ad588e6c took the 64KB photo cap AND the additive-only hydration disclosure. Verified against origin/main's blob rather than my working tree, because a suite run was live and sairnvet.html is a probe target -- reading the working tree mid-run is the confusion this session already recorded. THE SUPPRESSION FLAG IS THE SERIOUS ONE: svHydrateAll() set svSyncSuppressed=true, called st(), set it false, WITH NO finally -- six lines below svSeedStore()'s own comment saying "a throw inside a saver must not leave the whole app's backup suppressed for the rest of the session". IT IS WORSE AT THAT SITE THAN ANYWHERE ELSE IN THE FILE and that is why it needed its own assertion rather than a widened one: the enclosing promise ends in .catch(function(){}), so a throw out of st() is not merely unhandled, it is INVISIBLE -- nothing logs, the hydrate resolves, and the flag stays true for the life of the page, after which svSyncCollection() returns on its first line for every collection and every later write is saved locally and silently never backed up while the app looks entirely normal. st() really can throw: svBlockForCorruptStore() on an unparseable key, plus the backup hook it carries -- and a hydrate is exactly when a corrupt key is most likely to be met, being the first thing that touches every collection after sign-in. THE SECOND: a 2026-08-09 audit had replaced a false delivery promise on the peer-consult toast with "no cross-device delivery yet", CORRECTLY -- and 55889165 put sv_peerconsults in SV_SYNCED, so the sentence has been false in the other direction since. A MESSAGE THAT UNDERSTATES IS NOT SAFE JUST BECAUSE IT UNDERSTATES: a vet reads it and walks a colleague to this machine for a consult that already reached theirs, and never thinks of the record as having left the device. The new wording says only what is true -- it arrives when they next SIGN IN, because svHydrateAll() runs there and nowhere else, and "sent" would rebuild the promise the audit removed -- and names the photo as staying only when there IS one. THE TEST FOR IT IS A SEAM RATHER THAN A WORDING PIN: no live string in the file may deny cross-device delivery for ANY collection, so the next one to join SV_SYNCED cannot leave a stale denial behind. 18/18 up from 14, NEGATIVE CONTROL RUN IN A THROWAWAY WORKTREE at origin/main (given an absolute path OUTSIDE the clone, per the lesson about worktrees invisible to git status) -- all four new arms red against the pre-fix file and green after. Both extracted script blocks node --check clean. LIVE-VERIFIED after waiting for the deployment to actually carry the commit rather than assuming from elapsed time; the only remaining "no cross-device delivery" strings on production are two of my own comments quoting the old wording, checked line by line rather than by a bare substring test that would have reported a false failure.

- tests/sairndental_outbound_queue_probe.py + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- A MUTATION ARM THAT HAD STOPPED PROVING ANYTHING, UNDER A HEADLINE THAT SENT THE READER THE WRONG WAY. Arm 15 had been failing as "PROBES THAT DID NOT BITE". THE ARM WAS NOT BROKEN AND THE CODE WAS NOT UNPROTECTED -- its ANCHOR had gone stale. The anchor was the Prefer header plus the first half of the body line; the DNT_VENDOR_OBJECTS write branch was later added with an identical header and an identical body prefix, so the anchor matched TWO places and the probe REFUSED TO MUTATE. That refusal is the probe working -- arm 14 already carries a comment on why it must not pick whichever came first -- but the arm proved nothing from the day the vendor branch landed until somebody read the output rather than the headline. [idCol] vs vIdCol is what tells the two branches apart, and the anchor now includes it so no amount of convergence in the surrounding lines can make it ambiguous again. THE HEADLINE WAS THE OTHER HALF AND IT IS THE PART THAT GENERALISES: "DID NOT BITE" covered two OPPOSITE states -- SILENT means the mutation landed and the suite stayed green, so THE CODE IS UNPROTECTED; ANCHOR-n means the mutation never landed, so THE PROBE IS STALE and the code may be perfectly well covered with nothing measured either way. They send a reader to opposite places. Two separate lines now, each with its consequence spelled out. AN ANCHOR IS A STRING MATCH AGAINST CODE SOMEBODY ELSE KEEPS EDITING, so going stale is its NORMAL AGEING rather than an accident, and worth looking for in every other mutation probe on the platform. DELIBERATELY DID NOT ADD A VENDOR-BRANCH ARM: it is not the outbound queue this probe is named for, and an arm that came back SILENT would be a second finding to chase inside the fix for the first. Flagged in the row instead. 15/15 bite, both target files restored byte-identical, exit 0 where it was 1. No live surface -- this is test tooling, and the commit says so rather than implying a verification that did not happen.

- tests/push_gate/{check4,check7,check9}_probe.py + tests/seam_check/{run_delegation,run_or_default,run_ref}_probe.py + tests/write_readback_shape_probe.py + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE ANCHOR-STALENESS SWEEP, on Michael's direction after arm 15. MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: every probe that mutates a REAL source file by literal anchor was read and sorted by what it can DETECT. Six already count and report ANCHOR-n (law_custody_attribution, license_trial_gate, sairndental_write_failure, sd_timesheet_pay_est, suite_control_backfill, sairndental_outbound_queue). SEVEN were presence-only -- they catch an anchor that has GONE and say nothing about one that has become AMBIGUOUS, and each then calls .replace(...,1) or re.sub(count=1) or .index(), all of which take whichever came first. check7's arm 1 asserted NOTHING AT ALL: a vanished anchor would have committed the file UNCHANGED and reported that a clean change was allowed, which is true of any file. NOTHING WAS AMBIGUOUS ON THE DAY -- all seven anchors count exactly one, before and after, so this closes a DETECTION gap and did not catch a live break, and the row says so rather than implying a save. THE CONTROL FOUND A VACUOUS ASSERTION INSIDE MY OWN FIX, and that is the part worth keeping: each new assertion was driven red in a throwaway worktree by DUPLICATING the anchor AND COMMITTING IT, so the clean-tree guards would not skip instead of failing. Six fired and named the count. check4's did not -- its anchor regex wraps the line in leading and trailing newline matches, and that CANNOT match two ADJACENT copies, because the trailing newline of the first is the leading newline of the second and re.findall does not return overlapping matches. Two identical lines in a row counted as ONE: the assertion was already the shape it exists to forbid. Reading it did not show that; only running the control did. Now anchored on line start and end under re.MULTILINE, verified 1 normally and 2 on a duplicate. ALSO: writing the index row, I put the regex literal INTO the markdown cell and its \n was written as a real newline, splitting one row across four physical lines -- md_table_check caught it, and the row was REBUILT WHOLE without the regex rather than patched cell by cell, which is the rule CLAUDE.md already carries for pipes in cells.

- tests/sairnvet_audit_and_controlled.js (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE HARNESS DIED BEFORE ASSERTING ANYTHING AND HAD BEEN RED AT ORIGIN. `ReferenceError: svSeedStore is not defined` -- all 34 checks dead. The seed-leak sweep wrapped getControlledLog() in svSeedStore(), and this file builds its sandbox by SLICING named functions out of sairnvet.html into a new Function(...), so a function that gains a call to a helper the slice list does not include stops being loadable. It surfaces as a stack trace rather than a named assertion, which is why it reads like a broken test rather than a harness one line short. THE HELPER IS SLICED, NOT STUBBED: a stub returning saver(rows) would go green and silently stop testing the thing worth testing, since the real svSeedStore sets svSyncSuppressed and clears it in a finally -- exactly what a seeding test should exercise. Confirmed the failure reproduces at origin/main in a throwaway worktree BEFORE touching it, so it was a real red on every clone and not something my tree caused. 34/34.

- api/sd-data.js + api/sd-data-employees-refusal.test.js (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE employees READ BLAMED THE ROLE FOR A REFUSAL WHOSE CAUSE WAS THE APP. One sentence covered three different facts: no session at all, a VALID session belonging to another app, and a StoneDesk session whose role really is denied. Only the third is about a role and the message asserted the third every time. Found while closing the SAIRNbiz syncEmps row -- holding a valid SAIRNbiz OWNER session and being sent to the role model of an app that was never the problem. NOT A SECURITY DEFECT AND NOT COSMETIC: it is the class this file already treats carefully elsewhere ("no patients" vs "you are not linked yet"), and THREE live clients read this resource -- stonedesk, sairnbuild, sairnsenior -- two of which are not StoneDesk, so the misleading answer is the one they get the day they are provisioned. THE SECOND VERIFY IS FOR THE MESSAGE AND NOTHING ELSE, and that distinction is load-bearing: naming the caller's app needs a verify WITHOUT expectedApp, and dropping expectedApp is exactly the looseness the existing comment warns about, since this branch's deny-list names only StoneDesk roles and a SAIRNbiz role would slip through it. The strict stonedesk-bound session stays the only thing authorization reads, and the unbound one is deliberately not assigned anywhere it could be mistaken for a session -- section 3 of the suite is that assertion, every role of every other app refused with no data on the response. THE CONTROL CAUGHT MY OWN HARNESS ASSERTING NOTHING, and that is the part worth keeping: the first version reported 7/7 AGAINST THE PRE-FIX HANDLER, where three assertions must fail, because test() was SYNCHRONOUS and called an async fn() inside try/catch without awaiting it, so every rejection landed after the pass counter had already incremented. Reading it did not show that; running it against the code it was written to reject did. Measured both ways now: 7/7 on the fix, 4/7 at origin/main with exactly the three message assertions red, and sections 2 and 3 green on BOTH -- which is what proves this changed the MESSAGE and not who gets in. An invented role was caught too (signSessionToken refuses `hr` for sairnbuild), so rosters are taken per app from api/_lib/auth.js rather than shared. LIVE-VERIFIED on production after waiting for the deploy to actually carry the commit: WRONG_APP_SESSION naming sairnbiz, FORBIDDEN/No valid session for both no-token and garbage-token, and no response carries a data key. FLAGGED NOT FIXED: sairnbuild.html and sairnsenior.html both tell the user this read fails because no licence is provisioned yet -- true today, wrong the moment one is, the same shape as the SAIRNvet consult toast. Left alone rather than rewritten for a state that does not exist. ALSO: writing the index row I put a `||` from the source condition into a table cell and md_table_check caught the 11-cells-vs-9 immediately -- escaped, which is the rule CLAUDE.md already carries.

- docs/CRITICALITY-TIERS.md (NEW) + tools/criticality_tier_check.py (NEW) + tests/run_criticality_tier_probe.py (NEW) + index + traceability matrix (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE THIRD STANDING DISCIPLINE, and the one nothing on this platform had: SOUP and the traceability matrix already existed, no file stated a criticality tier for a VERTICAL. ONE SCHEME, NOT TWO -- the A/B/C definitions are lifted verbatim from docs/SOUP-REGISTER.md, which already tiers COMPONENTS and says in its own words that tiering applies to features too; inventing a second vocabulary is how a platform ends up with HIGH and P1 and CRITICAL meaning three different things in three files. EVERY TIER CITES SOMETHING ALREADY RECORDED IN THIS REPO -- a commit, an index row, a schema file, a real incident -- so a reader checks the claim against the source rather than against my table. 8 Tier A, 8 Tier B, 6 UNTIERED, 22 rows against 23 files on disk with the 23rd (piac.html) declared not-a-vertical OUT LOUD rather than filtered silently. UNTIERED IS DELIBERATE AND IS THE HONEST PART: guessing a tier is worse than an open question because a guess is indistinguishable from a decision once written down. Five of the six are the SAME unanswered platform question -- does an unauthenticated public surface inherit the tier of the app it writes into -- which is ONE decision and Michael's, not five; the sixth is sairncash, which becomes Tier A the day a Stripe key is set, and its row says the tier must be revisited in that same change rather than afterwards. THE B TIER IS WEAKER THAN THE A TIER AND THE FILE SAYS SO: six verticals sit at B on identical evidence (employee auth present, nothing recorded tying them to money or a regulated record), and an absence of RECORDED evidence is not a measured absence. THE CHECKER REPORTS AND NEVER REWRITES -- the tier and its sentence are a judgement, and a regenerated table would look authoritative precisely because a machine made it, having deleted the part that matters; same reasoning as soup_register_check.py and sairn_app_map_check.py. Its header states what it CANNOT see (whether a tier is RIGHT) and it prints that caveat on every clean run. IT CAUGHT TWO GAPS IN MY OWN FIRST DRAFT: two UNTIERED rows said "same question as the row above" without stating what would settle them, which turns UNTIERED into a permanent shrug. 12 assertions, control first, every arm mutating a copy in a THROWAWAY WORKTREE and the last arm asserting this clone's register never moved. ONE ARM WAS WRONG FIRST AND THE FIX IS RECORDED: piac.html is UNTRACKED, so a fresh worktree does not have it and the exclusion arm was measuring the environment rather than the logic -- it now provokes the file into existence. AND THE STANDING DISCIPLINE CAUGHT ME WITHIN THE HOUR: adding three files without regenerating the matrix made tools/traceability_matrix.py --check go red through Cody's report-only runner, so my own new probe was untraced. Index row added naming both the tool and the probe, matrix regenerated, both green.

Four rows closed on it -- sairndental-book, sairndental-complaint, stonedesk-intake, stonedesk-catalog, all A. ONE OF THE FOUR CAME BACK THE OTHER WAY AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT FOR WRITING A SETTLING CONDITION AS SOMETHING CHECKABLE: stonedesk-catalog carried "settles by confirming it cannot write anything", and the first draft had assumed a catalogue is read-only. IT IS NOT -- it issues action:'quote_request' against /api/stonedesk-public alongside catalog and view. Had the condition been left as an assumption rather than a check, the assumption would have become the tier, silently, in the one file whose entire value is that a reader can go and verify each claim against its source. stonedesk-hr.html settled SEPARATELY and is NOT part of that decision because it is not public: it calls /api/sd-data with app_id 'stonedesk' and sd_session_token, so it shares StoneDesk's session and reaches employee records with it -- Tier A for the same reason stonedesk.html is. 13 A, 8 B, 1 UNTIERED, and the one left has a DATE rather than a question: sairncash becomes A the day a Stripe key is set. THE PROBE'S ARM 6 HAD TO MOVE AND IT ANNOUNCED ITSELF RATHER THAN ROTTING -- it anchored on stonedesk-catalog being UNTIERED and its fixture assertion failed loudly on the first run after the row changed, which is the probe working. Repointed at sairncash, the row that is UNTIERED by design, and it now COUNTS its anchor rather than merely finding it, per today's platform anchor sweep. Checker 0 problems, probe 12/12, matrix regenerated and matching its sources.

Every B row rested on one sentence -- "nothing recorded ties it to money or a regulated record" -- which was an absence of RECORDING because nobody had looked, and the file said so in its own gap section rather than pretending otherwise. MEASURED FROM api/_resources/<app>.js AND THE CLIENT WRITE SITES, not from a keyword scan: all eight have a money-bearing resource reaching a server. sairnroofing is the strongest -- rf_invoices carries the verbs ['issue','add_payment','reconcile_claim'] and sairnroofing.html:2912 calls add_payment live, which is the A definition verbatim. sairnfreedom has an actual sf_ledger. sairnlegacy's leg_invoices bills PRE-NEED accounts, money held against a future obligation, the least forgiving shape of a billing error on the list. sairnmechanical's row had HEDGED ("money-adjacent but the decision is open") where a measurement was available, which is its own small lesson. ON EXACTLY THE BASIS stonedesk.html IS A -- "a quote or invoice wrong" -- all eight are A; calling StoneDesk A for invoices while calling seven other apps B for holding invoices is an inconsistency, not a tier. THE CONSEQUENCE IS RAISED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED: 21 of 22 verticals are now Tier A, and a register where almost everything is A has lost the thing a tier is FOR. Two readings and neither is mine to pick -- either the A definition is too broad for a portfolio of trade businesses where EVERY app invoices, so invoicing cannot be the discriminator, or the portfolio genuinely is uniformly high-criticality and the tier belongs per FEATURE AREA within an app rather than per app. THE MEASUREMENT IS REPORTED AS IT CAME OUT RATHER THAN TUNED TO KEEP THE DISTRIBUTION LOOKING USEFUL: a register adjusted until its shape looks right is the fabrication class this platform keeps finding, one level up. THE PROBE'S ARM 5 MOVED FOR THE SECOND TIME IN ONE DAY AND ANNOUNCED ITSELF BOTH TIMES -- it anchored on sairnscape being B, the fixture assertion failed loudly on the next run, and it now points at sairnlaw, whose A is backed by a recorded incident rather than by a classification that could be revised. Michael's decision after the app-level version collapsed to 21 of 22 verticals at Tier A: the measurement was honest and the GRANULARITY was wrong, because a roofing app's invoicing panel and its colour-theme settings do not carry the same consequence and one label per app forces them into one answer. THE UNIT IS NOW api/_resources/<app>.js, the same unit SOUP and the traceability matrix already use -- reused rather than invented, and the A/B/C vocabulary is still the SOUP register's so the platform has one scheme. ALL 382 REGISTERED RESOURCES ACROSS 17 APPS: 78 A, 299 B, 5 C -- 20% Tier A, a distribution that discriminates where 21-of-22 did not. TIER A IS HAND-VERIFIED with one written evidence each naming a commit, an incident, a regulation or a registry line; B and C are rule-classified; and the CHECKER REFUSES A TIER A ROW WITH AN EMPTY EVIDENCE CELL so the difference is enforced rather than promised -- claiming 382 rows had each been individually read would not be true. THE KEYWORD PASS WAS WRONG IN BOTH DIRECTIONS AND THAT IS THE WHOLE ARGUMENT FOR HAND-VERIFYING A: it proposed sf_accounts and law_opaccounts as Tier C because "accounts" contains "counts" -- a member ledger and a law firm's OPERATING ACCOUNT, one character from being filed as preferences -- and it MISSED dnt_coverage_rules, dnt_txplans, leg_preneed and rf_contingency_rules, all money, none matching a money keyword. dnt_coverage_rules is the sharpest: computeEstimatedInsurance() LOCKS its result onto a charge and never recomputes, so one wrong rule is wrong on every charge written while it stood. Each correction is named in its own row rather than silently applied. THE A CRITERIA ARE BROAD ON PURPOSE -- money AND anything upstream of money, regulated data AND licensure, plus any resource with a documented incident -- because the dangerous direction is something high-stakes filed as lower-risk; that is why bld_price_points, sf_vendor_prices and dnt_vendor_pricing_rules are A despite none being a transaction. RECONCILED RATHER THAN TRUSTED: every hand-written A entry was checked against the registries and none was orphaned, and seven names in the stage-1 draft turned out not to be registered resources at all (including sd_slabs, a promoted table). THE 299-ROW B TIER IS THE HONEST WEAK POINT AND THE FILE SAYS SO. The checker was rewritten for the new unit and caught a real gap on its first run -- sairncash had a registry and no rollup line, so an app nobody had even said "not yet" about was invisible. The probe was rebuilt for the new structure after BOTH its previous anchors failed loudly during the day as the rows they named changed tier: 12 assertions, control first, every arm in a throwaway worktree, last arm asserting this clone's register never moved.

- docs/MASTER-PLAN.md (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- WHERE EVERY VERTICAL ACTUALLY STANDS, AND WHAT FINISHED MEANS. Michael's ask, and deliberately NOT a restatement of docs/2026-09-10-verification-programme-plan.md (Cody), which is the SCOPE and ORDER of the four-part programme -- this is the STATUS against it plus the definition of done, and it cites that document rather than repeating it. EVERY GATE IS CHECKABLE BY SOMETHING THAT ALREADY EXISTS, which is the whole point since a definition of "finished" nobody can verify is a wish: BUILT = the resource registry plus a migration actually RUN; TIERED = tools/criticality_tier_check.py; TRACEABLE = tools/traceability_matrix.py --check; FAULT-TESTED = a mutation probe that plants a real defect and proves a guard DENIES, because a guard that has never been red is not known to be a guard. THE HEADLINE: sairndesign, sairnfreedom and sairngrounds have NO DEDICATED TEST SUITE AT ALL -- 83 registered resources reaching a server with nothing named for them. Verified by LISTING the files rather than trusting the count that produced it. Fault testing is concentrated rather than spread: 14 probes plant into real source and eight of them are SAIRNdental's; sairnfreedom and sairnroofing have none. THE DERIVATION CAUGHT ITSELF BEING WRONG AND THAT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE TABLE: the first pass matched each app's NAME in a test filename and reported SEVEN apps with zero dedicated suites -- BLIND ZEROS, because SAIRNroofing's suites are roofing-*/rf-*, SAIRNcare's are alf-*, SAIRNcode's are sc-*. sairn-portfolio-triage has an entire section on exactly that failure and it applied to me; with a real prefix map SAIRNroofing has 26 suites, not 0. Three of the seven were real and they are the finding. NO DATES, AND THE DOCUMENT SAYS WHY: I do not control session scheduling and a date invented here is the confident wrong number this platform keeps finding, so it states the remaining WORK instead -- three first suites, two missing fault probes, 187 untraced tests, and one live read per app for gate 1, which needs somebody with the keys rather than more code.

- docs/MASTER-PLAN.md + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- GATE 1 IS PARTLY ANSWERED, AND IT IS THE ONE GATE THAT IS ATTESTED RATHER THAN DERIVED. Everything else in the master plan is read from the repo; whether a migration has actually run against a live licence cannot be, so the section names WHO confirmed it and WHEN, per the rule that a status fact with a tense is only true as of the read behind it. SEVEN CONFIRMED RUN by Michael directly on 2026-09-10 -- stonedesk_data_schema, sairnvet_data_schema, sairnfreedom_data_schema, sairnfreedom_license_seed, sairndental_vendor_schema, sairnmechanical_records_schema, license_keys_grant_review_2026-09-05 -- and every one was verified to EXIST ON DISK before being cited, because a document whose value is checkability cannot carry an uncheckable citation. TWO ARE CONFIRMED ONLY IN PART AND THE TABLE SAYS WHICH PART RATHER THAN ROUNDING UP: stonedesk_data_schema covers the 21 BACKUP tables and NOT StoneDesk's original schema, and sairndental_vendor_schema covers the VENDOR tables only. An app is not "built" because one of its schemas ran. STILL UNVERIFIED AND NAMED EXPLICITLY -- SAIRNlaw, SAIRNbiz, SAIRNbuild, StoneDesk's original schema, and every app in neither list -- because an app absent from BOTH lists reads as probably fine, which is the exact shape of a silent wrong status. SAIRNbiz carries the one genuine qualification and it is recorded as the NARROWER claim: a live authenticated write through syncEmps() returned 200 with written:1 earlier today, which proves the employees table exists and is writable, and is not the same statement as "its schemas are run". The section also states the rule that matters going forward: a cell in that table must never be filled from inference, because an app whose code looks complete tells you nothing about whether its migration ran.

It was the worst of the three: 35 registered resources reaching a server, no file named for it, no fault probe. THE SUITE GUARDS THE THREE SIDES THAT MUST AGREE AND DRIFT SILENTLY WHEN THEY DO NOT -- registry, client push list, schema -- in ALL FOUR directions rather than the convenient two: registered-with-no-table, table-nothing-can-write, registered-but-never-pushed, pushed-but-unregistered. AND THE ORC 2915 SIDE BY NAME, WHICH IS THE PART A PAIRWISE COUNT CANNOT DO: drop sf_disbursements from all three sides and every pairwise assertion still passes, because the three lists then agree perfectly with each other. Arm 3 of the probe does exactly that and the by-name section is the only thing that catches it -- the registry's own header names charitable disbursements as a reason the app got a server at all. It also PINS this morning's silent-catch fix: st() must delegate to sfBackupHookFailed() and the two failures must keep saying DIFFERENT things, because collapsing them tells somebody their work was lost when it was not. THE PROBE MUTATES A COPY IN A THROWAWAY WORKTREE and its last two arms assert this clone never moved -- not tidiness, but the lesson this repo established the hard way today when a stranded PROBE commit reached origin twice in two days. THE ANCHOR COUNTING CAUGHT ME THREE TIMES WHILE WRITING IT: 'sf_ledger', matches twice in the HTML, 'sf_disbursements', twice, and sf_disbursements NINE times in the schema. Each would have planted in whichever came first and asserted something about a line nobody chose. That is the morning's platform anchor sweep applied to my own NEW code rather than only to the old, and it is the first time that guard has fired on something being written rather than something inherited. Suite 14/14, probe 8 arms plus 2 restore assertions. sairndesign and sairngrounds still have none -- 48 registered resources between them, and the master plan's headline is updated to say two rather than three.

- tests/sairndesign_server_backup.js (NEW) + tests/sairngrounds_server_backup.js (NEW) + tests/sairndesign_sairngrounds_fault_probe.py (NEW) + MASTER-PLAN + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE LAST TWO FIRST-SUITES, closing all three verticals the master plan measured with no file named for them. THE PROBE FOUND A REAL DESIGN FLAW IN MY OWN SUITES ON ITS FIRST RUN AND THAT IS WORTH MORE THAN THE SUITES: the by-name section iterated REG.resources and filtered by tier, so REMOVING A RESOURCE FROM THE REGISTRY ALSO REMOVED ITS OWN ASSERTION. The arm that drops a Tier A money resource from registry, client and schema together reported NO failure -- not because the suite tolerated the defect but because the test for it had quietly stopped existing. A GUARD THAT CAN BE SWITCHED OFF BY THE CHANGE IT EXISTS TO CATCH IS WORSE THAN NO GUARD, BECAUSE IT REPORTS GREEN. Both suites now read Tier A from docs/CRITICALITY-TIERS.md, slicing the app's own section, so removing the resource FAILS. THE GENERALISABLE HALF: any other guard on this platform that derives its own SUBJECT from the thing it is guarding has the same hole, and that is worth a sweep by whoever picks it up. SAIRNGROUNDS NEEDED A REAL ALIAS MODEL RATHER THAN A LOOSER RULE: four resources are registered under BARE names -- jobs, quotes, properties, golf_zones -- while their tables carry grd_, and a naive equality check reports four missing tables AND four orphans, eight false findings, which is the blind-spot class sairn-portfolio-triage has a section about. api/sd-data.js:2288 maps them explicitly and :3007 says the bare names exist to avoid a collision, so the four are LISTED and a fifth added later fails the suite rather than being absorbed; two further arms hold the aliases honest, one pointing at a table that is gone and one kept alive by a resource nobody registers. THE ANCHOR COUNTING FIRED TWICE MORE ON NEW CODE: 'sdn_invoices' matches EIGHT times in sairndesign.html and public.sdn_invoices SIX times in its schema, so the by-name arms became consistent RENAMES with the count asserted first -- the rule is "know how many you are hitting", not "it must be one". And msb_licenses turned out to live in the PHASE 2 schema file, found because the count guard returned 0 against the first one rather than by assuming. sairndesign 8/8, sairngrounds 11/11, probe 8 arms plus 2 restore assertions, seven files restored byte-identical. GATE 1 IS STILL OPEN FOR ALL THREE: these are SOURCE-AGREEMENT suites and none of them makes a live write.

- tests/{sairnfreedom,sairndesign,sairngrounds}_server_backup.js + docs/2026-09-10-self-referential-guard-sweep.md (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE SELF-REFERENTIAL-GUARD SWEEP, AND THE ANSWER IS A NEGATIVE RESULT REPORTED AS ONE. Thirteen derived-subject suites examined: TWELVE ALREADY PIN A COUNT, one does not and is benign, and the hole was in the three files written this session. It would have been easy to write this up as a platform-wide problem -- the shape is real and genuinely dangerous, a guard disarmed by the very defect it exists to catch, reporting green while it happens -- but the measurement says the platform had already solved it everywhere except in today's code, and inflating that would be the fabrication class one level up. DERIVING THE SUBJECT IS USUALLY RIGHT AND THIS IS NOT AN ARGUMENT AGAINST IT: the two approaches are blind in OPPOSITE directions, a derived list catches an ADDITION and loses its assertion on a REMOVAL while a hardcoded list is the reverse -- tests/sairnvet_seed_never_syncs.js says so in its own header and is correct that a hardcoded list "would pass forever while the app grew a fortieth". THE FIX IS TO DERIVE AND PIN THE COUNT, which is what the twelve do; the three newest now pin 35, 18 and 30 with the number stated as needing a HAND edit, because that edit is the point -- it makes a removal visible in a diff instead of silently shrinking the test surface. ONE IS LEFT UNPINNED ON PURPOSE: api/_lib/anon-rate-limit.test.js iterates reg.RESOURCE_NAMES to assert the 429 names none of them, and an empty registry is not the defect it guards, so pinning there would be ceremony rather than coverage -- and that judgement is exactly why this sweep is a DOCUMENT rather than a tool. THE DETECTOR OVER-REPORTED AND THAT IS THE REUSABLE HALF: a first pass matching `.length >= N` named SIX unpinned suites and FOUR were false, because this repo also writes assert.strictEqual(x.length, 30) which that pattern misses, and one subject (st_reports_failure's APPS) is a literal in the test rather than a derived list at all. Every candidate was opened and read before being reported -- the same blind-zero class sairn-portfolio-triage documents, hit TWICE IN ONE DAY by this session, once on the master plan's test counts and once here. NOT COVERED AND NAMED RATHER THAN IMPLIED: the checkers in tools/ have the same shape with a DIFFERENT answer in each case -- deleting an api/ handler is not itself a defect, deleting a registry resource is -- and that is a second sweep with a judgement call per checker.

- tools/run_all_tests.py + tools/report_only_checks.py + tools/preauth_oracle_check.py + tools/write_without_readback_check.py + tests/run_all_tests_floor_probe.py (NEW) + tests/run_report_only_checks_probe.py + docs/2026-09-10-self-referential-guard-sweep.md + index (Fourth, 2026-09-10) -- THE SECOND HALF OF THE SWEEP, AND IT DID NOT COME OUT LIKE THE FIRST. Part one was an honest negative result: twelve of thirteen derived-subject SUITES already pinned a count. Part two found THREE REAL HOLES IN NINE CHECKERS, and the reason is structural rather than about who wrote what -- A SUITE'S SUBJECT IS A REGISTRY SOMEBODY CURATED AND HAS A NUMBER ATTACHED; A CHECKER'S SUBJECT IS USUALLY THE FILESYSTEM, and "whatever is there" cannot be zero-checked against itself. THE JUDGEMENT WAS MADE ONCE PER CHECKER, three different answers: the risk leaves with the subject (an api/ handler -- not a defect); the risk stays and the guard leaves (a test file -- always a defect); NOBODY CHOSE IT AT ALL (git failed, wrong directory, a glob stopped matching) -- and the third is the one this half is really about, because an empty subject list produces a report BYTE-IDENTICAL to a clean one. (1) THE TEST RUNNER DID NOT KNOW HOW MANY TESTS THERE SHOULD BE -- `ALL N TEST FILES PASS` for whatever N the walk returned, so twenty deleted files print a TRUE sentence about a suite that lost twenty guards. MIN_TEST_FILES is a FLOOR at runtime (no clone mid-addition false-alarms, so nobody routes around it) and an EQUALITY at commit time via the new probe (no dead zone). I INHERITED THAT PIN UNCOMMITTED FROM THE COMPACTED SESSION AND IT WAS WRONG IN BOTH WAYS THIS DOCUMENT IS ABOUT, neither visible in its diff: it said 163 while discover() returned 180 -- seventeen files deletable in silence, 85% of its own twenty-file example -- and it was added to _main_body() and NOT to _hook_body(), THE COPY THAT RUNS UNATTENDED AFTER EVERY PUSH IN EVERY CLONE. That is the half that mattered: your own deletion is in your own diff, a deletion arriving by REBASE from another clone was visible to nothing else. CLAUDE.md's sairn_claim_hook.py lesson, hit again without being recognised; the probe now asserts ONE definition and exactly TWO comparisons so a third copy cannot skip the check. (2) report_only_checks.py REPORTED A CLEAN PLATFORM AFTER SCANNING ZERO APPS -- six of 21 promoted entries run per-app over 22 apps, 132 checker runs, and an empty target list meant the loop never executed, findings and unrun both stayed [], and the hook printed EXACTLY what a clean sweep prints. PROVED BY FORCING app_files() TO [], not argued from reading. Two ways in, both closed: `git ls-files` failing was read as "no apps" (returncode never checked -- git off the hook's PATH, an index locked by one of four clones mid-rebase), and zero targets now lands in unrun. A DELETED APP IS DELIBERATELY NOT WHAT THIS GUARDS. ONE THING LEFT ALONE ON PURPOSE: the non-root exclusion notice prints on the hand run only, because the hook's stdout is a single JSON payload a stray line would corrupt -- the file CLAIMED it was "PRINTED rather than silent" and that claim is now scoped to where it is true. (3) preauth_oracle_check.py audited its accepted-exemptions file for entries matching nothing, on its own reasoning that a suppression outlives what it suppressed, and the DECLARATIONS file had no equivalent -- STALE DECLARATION added, mutation-proved by injecting a declaration for a file that does not exist; and HANDLERS_SCANNED, because four counts and no statement of how many handlers were LOOKED AT means a walk over zero files reads like a clean run over 63. THE CWD-RELATIVE CLASS WAS MEASURED, NOT REASONED ABOUT: four checkers were run from docs/ and their real output read; three disclose a zero, write_without_readback_check.py printed an empty table, `none`, `none` and exit 0 with NO COUNT ANYWHERE -- coverage disclosure added. THEIR ANCHORING IS STILL OPEN and is named as open. NOT DONE AND SAID PLAINLY: the other ~75 tools were not individually read -- candidates came from grepping for filesystem discovery, so a checker deriving its subject by parsing or importing would not have appeared, and part one records what a grep alone is worth (six reported, four false). TWO UNRELATED FINDINGS RECORDED AS ROWS RATHER THAN FOLDED IN: defect_register.py --check fails today on two commit SHAs absent from the repo AFTER A FETCH (it is the one red in the post-push report-only sweep, which is how a report-only checker gets switched off); and ONE `cd` INTO A SUBDIRECTORY BRICKS EVERY BASH CALL for the rest of a session -- hooks are relative paths resolved against the Bash tool's persisted cwd, so after `cd tools` the PreToolUse hooks fail and block the tool call BEFORE it runs, including the `cd ..` that would fix it, and Write/Edit are blocked the same way. It fails CLOSED, which is the safe direction. Hit live and escaped through a non-hook-gated tool.

- tools/run_all_tests.py + tests/run_all_tests_floor_probe.py + sweep doc + index (Fourth, 2026-09-11) -- THE FLOOR IS A FLOOR AT BOTH ENDS NOW, `count >= pin`, on Michael's decision. It shipped hours earlier with the probe asserting EQUALITY, on the reasoning that a pin below the real count is a dead zone nothing can see -- and IT FIRED ON THE VERY FIRST REBASE AFTER IT WAS WRITTEN, 181 becoming 184 because other clones had added tests, failing the suite for a session that had added nothing. With four clones pushing that is the normal night, not an edge case. THE REASONING FOR THE CHANGE, recorded because it is the interesting half: a floor keeps ALL of the real protection, since any silent DELETION still fails immediately, and drops the ONLY false-positive shape, which was a legitimate ADDITION arriving by rebase. A stale floor after growth is cosmetic -- it leaves a window in which that many deletions would be quiet -- and it is bumped as housekeeping. Friction on every rebase is what gets a gate switched off out of annoyance, and a switched-off gate protects nothing. GROWTH IS STILL PRINTED with the number to write, so the bump is offered rather than demanded. ARM 1'S MUTATION PROOF IS NAMED RATHER THAN FAKED: the comparison cannot be driven short without deleting real files, so arms 2-6 (which patch discover() and prove the same constant makes BOTH bodies fire) are the proof, and arm 1 adds only the case they cannot see -- a pin written ABOVE the real count, which would false-alarm forever. Verified by temporarily setting the pin to 9999 and watching arm 1 fail, then restoring. ONE SELF-INFLICTED SLIP WORTH RECORDING: the script that did that temporary edit read and wrote the file in TEXT mode, which converted 533 LF line endings to CRLF -- exactly the hazard CLAUDE.md's verification lesson 5 names. Caught by git's own warning, restored in binary mode, diff confirmed as 22/17 lines rather than a whole-file rewrite.

- tools/defect_register.py + docs/defect-density-register.json + tests/run_defect_register_probe.py + index (Fourth, 2026-09-11) -- THE REGISTER POINTED AT COMMITS THAT NEVER EXISTED ON MAIN, AND THE CAUSE WAS STRUCTURAL RATHER THAN ANYBODY'S MISTAKE. I reported it yesterday as 5 problems while verifying unrelated work; it was 12 by the time I picked it up twelve hours later, and that growth is what turned it from housekeeping into a class. `--add` derives the SHA from the commit in front of it, and with four clones pushing most commits are rebased onto somebody else's work before they reach origin -- so THE RECORDED SHA IS THE PRE-REBASE ONE AND THE COMMIT IT NAMES NEVER EXISTED ON MAIN. Every record written-then-rebased goes stale on its own, with nothing anybody did wrong. THE CONSEQUENCE WAS NOT COSMETIC: this is one of the promoted report-only checkers, so EVERY post-push sweep in EVERY clone carried a finding -- which is exactly how a report-only checker earns the reputation that gets it switched off before it is ever promoted to a gate, in this file's own words about nav_panel_check. THE FIX IS TO TREAT THE SHA AS WHAT IT ACTUALLY IS, a convenience pointer, and the record's identity as its SUBJECT, which git rebase preserves while rewriting the hash. `--check` re-resolves by subject and REPORTS the drift; it still FAILS when neither half resolves, which is the real "a register pointing at nothing, whose length reads as evidence" case the check was written for, and it FAILS on an AMBIGUOUS subject rather than picking one, because guessing which of two commits a record meant is the thing a register must never do. `--reseat` writes them back as a SEPARATE command -- a checker that edits what it checks is not a checker -- and DELIBERATELY DOES NOT RE-SORT: the sort key is (date, commit), so re-seating twelve SHAs reordered the file and produced a 157-LINE DIFF FOR A 12-LINE CHANGE. That is measured, not guessed; the first version sorted and that is what it produced, and a repair nobody can review is a repair nobody checks. A SECOND, QUIETER HOLE UNDERNEATH THE LOUD ONE, and it would have outlived the fix for the loud one: the vocabulary checks sat after a `continue`, so the moment a SHA went stale the detection_method / layer / severity of that record stopped being checked at all. ALL 12 RE-SEATED, --check clean, and run_report_only_checks_probe.py H6 -- "a clean sweep stays silent" -- PASSES FOR THE FIRST TIME. ONE CORRECTION TO THE EXISTING PROBE THAT MATTERED: arm E1 planted commit='000000000000' by copying records[0], which carried records[0]'s SUBJECT, so the planted record still named a real commit and only its hash was wrong -- that is the REBASE case, not the points-at-nothing case, and under the new behaviour it would have gone green while testing the wrong thing. Blanked the subject so it really resolves to nothing. AND THE AMBIGUITY ARM MANUFACTURES ITS FIXTURE rather than hoping one exists: two empty commits with the same subject, made in the detached throwaway worktree so nothing is committed on any branch of this clone. My first version skipped itself when the planted subject turned out to be unique -- A SKIPPED ARM IS AN UNTESTED BRANCH WEARING A GREEN TICK. 37 arms, was 24.

Part two measured these four by running them from docs/ and reading the real output; all four defaulted to a glob relative to the CURRENT DIRECTORY and scanned nothing, printing an empty table + `none` + `none` + exit 0 (write_without_readback), `0 clean, 0 not-forwarded, 0 could-not-tell` (seam), three zeros on one line (stale_snapshot) and `clean -- no ... sites / in 0 file(s)` (strict_args). THIS IS THE THIRD ROW OF PART TWO'S TABLE -- the subject list going empty for a reason NOBODY CHOSE -- and the trigger is not hypothetical: a single `cd tools` yesterday left this session's shell in a subdirectory for the rest of the turn, and because every hook command in .claude/settings.json is a RELATIVE path resolved against that same working directory, even `cd ..` was blocked. WHAT CHANGED AND WHAT DELIBERATELY DID NOT: the DEFAULT target list is anchored to a REPO derived from __file__ (the convention ~20 other tools here already follow), and an EXPLICIT path argument stays CWD-relative, because a path a human types means what they typed from where they typed it -- the probe asserts the same relative name FAILS from docs/ rather than silently resolving to the repo copy. report_only_checks.py was never exposed, because it passes cwd=REPO to every subprocess: the same fix one layer up, and why the promoted sweep was unaffected while hand runs were not.
