Two places, one assumption; fixing the loud one and stopping is the shape this repo keeps recording against itself. Caught only because the probe asserts BYTE-IDENTICAL output rather than "it looks anchored now." A limitation is NAMED RATHER THAN FIXED: under --ref that isfile() still asks the working tree, so a helper present in the ref but absent on disk is skipped -- pre-existing, and it makes the check miss a seam rather than invent one. THE PROBE'S OWN NON-TRIVIALITY ARM GOT IT WRONG FIRST: one loose regex hunting the word "file" passed three tools and FAILED sairn_seam_check.py for the wrong reason, because that tool discloses its coverage as `75 clean, ...` and names no files at all. Four exact patterns replaced the heuristic -- a checker's output format is not something to guess at from three siblings. All four seam_check probes, both write_readback probes and local_only_probe re-run green, including the --ref path the push gate uses.

- tests/push_gate/refspec_and_override_probe.py (Fourth, 2026-09-11) -- THE ONE RED IN THE FULL SUITE WAS THE PROBE, NOT THE GATE, AND IT FAILED FOR TWO DIFFERENT WRONG REASONS. Arm: "an UNRESOLVABLE base still widens -- [] there would fail open on a real push." It ran against REPO and asserted outgoing_files() returns something; the widening fallback goes `@{u}..tip` then `origin/main..tip`, so IT CAN ONLY RETURN SOMETHING WHEN THIS CLONE IS AHEAD OF ORIGIN. Observed failing minutes after a successful push, with HEAD an ANCESTOR of origin/main (another clone had pushed) and both fallback ranges empty. THE CODE UNDER TEST WAS NOT WRONG and that is why this matters: on a real push HEAD is ahead of origin by definition -- you are pushing something -- so the widening does fire where it counts, and the arm was reporting a fail-open that cannot occur in the scenario it names. IT IS THE MIRROR IMAGE OF THE FLAKINESS THE COMMENT TWENTY LINES ABOVE IT ALREADY DOCUMENTS for the A2 arm: same file, same root cause, same session's sync state standing in for a fact about code. The A2 comment was written when the clone was AHEAD; this arm breaks when it is BEHIND or in sync. So the precondition is now BUILT rather than hoped for: a detached throwaway worktree with one commit on top, so origin/main..HEAD is non-empty by construction, and nothing is committed on any branch of this clone. MY OWN FIRST VERSION OF THAT FIXTURE WAS ALSO WRONG, and it failed against correct code for a SECOND reason: I used `commit --allow-empty`, but outgoing_files() widens on `git log --name-only`, so a commit touching no files produces no filenames, the output is falsy, and it falls straight through to `return []`. An empty commit proves the range is non-empty and proves nothing about a function that reports PATHS. The fixture now writes and commits a real file, and the validity arm asserts the outgoing range NAMES A FILE rather than merely existing. MUTATION-PROVED rather than argued: the real call widens to 1 file, and the fail-open shape it guards (returning []) makes the assertion False. 0 failures, worktree removed, tree byte-identical after.

- SAIRN-PLATFORM-2026-09-11-pushgate-empty-range-handoff.md (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-11) -- HANDED THE PUSH-GATE FAIL-OPEN TO CODY AND STOOD DOWN, Michael's decision, because Cody was already mid-implementation on outgoing_files()/outgoing_subjects() and two stacking fixes to one function is the risk. I FIRST ARGUED THE TOOL WAS FINE AND ONLY THE PROBE WAS WRONG, AND I WAS HALF WRONG: the probe half was right and is fixed separately in ee60b7e2; the tool half was not, and I found the real shape only while checking Cody's framing rather than defending my own. WHAT THE HANDOFF CARRIES, all measured in this clone today rather than reasoned from the code: (1) `base` is only ever non-None in PREPUSH mode -- :582 sets it None and :597 is the only assignment, inside `if MODE == 'prepush'` -- so in the PreToolUse hook, the one that runs on every `git push` typed in a session, it is ALWAYS None and the fallback chain is the entire protection; (2) that chain is thinner than it reads, because `@{u}` IS `origin/main` when pushing main, so the three-level fallback is ONE LEVEL TRIED TWICE; (3) THE `HEAD~1` LAST RESORT THE DOCSTRING PROMISES DOES NOT EXIST -- one occurrence in the whole file, at :352, in that sentence. Measured with the clone behind origin: outgoing_files(repo, None, "HEAD") and outgoing_subjects(repo, None, "HEAD") both return [] with HEAD an ancestor of origin/main, which the gate reads as "no seed touched." REACHABLE BY ORDINARY PUSHES THAT SEND REAL CONTENT -- `git push origin HEAD:some-branch` while behind (the ordinary state with four clones pushing all night), an older-sha push, a force-push after origin moved backwards. TWO THINGS STATED AS OPEN RATHER THAN IMPLIED CLOSED: I did NOT demonstrate end to end that a denied seed change slips through on a live push, only that the range function returns [] under conditions a real push reaches; and outgoing_subjects() is UNVERIFIED beyond that single measurement even though it feeds check 8's PROBE-commit detection at :1288. AND A WARNING FOR THE FIX RATHER THAN JUST THE DEFECT: failing closed is not free here, because the gate BLOCKS and the reachable cases are LEGITIMATE pushes -- a naive inversion denies real work and teaches people to reach for SAIRN_SEED_GATE=off, which is the same gate hollowed out by a different route. The HEAD~1 fallback the docstring already promises looks like the cheaper half, and that is offered as a suggestion to the owner rather than a decision.

- .claude/settings.json + tools/session_lock_check.py + tests/hook_command_anchoring_probe.py (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-11) -- THE `cd` BRICK, CLOSED, AND THE FIX EXPOSED A WORSE DEFECT THAN THE ONE IT FIXED. Hook commands were `python tools/<name>.py`, a path relative to the working directory, and Claude Code runs hooks in the BASH TOOL'S PERSISTED working directory -- so one `cd tools` yesterday resolved every PreToolUse hook to `tools/tools/<name>.py`, failed to open, and FAILED THE TOOL CALL BEFORE THE COMMAND RAN, including the `cd ..` that would have fixed it; Write and Edit were blocked the same way by redaction_check.py, so writing a shim was blocked too. All 9 commands now use `python "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR:-.}/tools/<name>.py"`. THE `:-.` FALLBACK IS THE POINT AND NOT DECORATION: without it an unset variable resolves to `/tools/x.py` and fails from the repo root as well, which is WORSE than the bug being fixed -- with it the command degrades to exactly what it was before, so the change cannot be worse than what it replaces. CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR IS SET BY THIS HARNESS, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED: I wired a temporary PostToolUse hook that wrote its cwd and that variable to a marker file, got `PROJDIR=C:/Users/marsh/Documents/SAIRN-fourth`, and that same probe proved hook commands go through a SHELL (the `$(...)` expanded), which the `${VAR:-default}` form depends on. Settings restored from a byte backup immediately after. THE SECOND HALF IS THE ONE THAT COULD HAVE DONE REAL DAMAGE. Making the scripts REACHABLE from a subdirectory means they now RUN there, where before they did not run at all -- so all eight were checked for working-directory dependence and ONE HAD IT: session_lock_check.clone_name() read os.path.basename(os.getcwd()), returning 'tools' from tools/ and 'docs' from docs/, measured before and after. EVERY CLONE HAS A tools/ AND A docs/, so a drifted cwd would not merely mislabel a session -- ALL FOUR CLONES WOULD CLAIM THE SAME LOCK AND REPORT EACH OTHER AS DUPLICATE SESSIONS, in the one directory where they are guaranteed to collide. Converting a brick into a wrong answer would have been a worse trade than leaving it alone, which is the whole reason that check was run rather than a note saying the scripts should be fine. It derives from __file__ now. The other seven need no change AND THAT WAS CHECKED: sairn_push_gate_hook.py and sairn_claim_hook.py both walk up from os.getcwd() to the repo root, which still lands correctly from a subdirectory, and the rest use __file__ or take paths from the hook payload. MUTATION-PROVED by reverting one command to the bare form and watching the probe fail and name it, with settings.json restored byte-identical by sha256. ONE DEFECT IN MY OWN PROBE, caught by its own timeout: arm 4 first ran `report_only_checks.py --help`, which has no such flag and falls through to the FULL report -- it now executes each module's BODY via importlib without running __main__, which is exactly what the brick prevented and is all this arm needs to prove.

- tests/push_gate/check4_probe.py + check7_probe.py + check8_probe.py + docs/2026-09-10-run-all-tests-hook-PAUSED.md + index (Fourth, 2026-09-11) -- THE PROBES NO LONGER COMMIT TO THE WORKING BRANCH, which is re-enable condition 1 of the paused full-suite hook and the open half of the stranded-PROBE-commit row that was left as MINE. They planted fixtures with `git commit` on `main` and `git reset --mixed` back -- safe for one runner on a quiet branch, and not safe when the hook fired on every Bash call: copies raced, each reset to its own start, and FIVE stranded PROBE commits reached origin/main in a day, one of them deleting `service_methods: body.service_methods` from api/legal-deadlines.js, the exact line whose absence had already made SAIRNlaw run Florida five days late. A DETACHED WORKTREE, NOT A DETACHED HEAD, and the index row's own stated objection is what chose it: the recorded worry was that a probe which dies leaves the clone in a confusing state, and a worktree moves that residue OUT of the clone -- a hard kill leaves a directory in the temp folder rather than a HEAD anybody has to notice. A detached worktree has no branch tip, so there is no tip for a lost reset race to strand a commit on. atexit rather than try/finally, because wrapping 120 lines of heavily commented arms in a try block would reindent all of it and make the diff unreadable; atexit covers normal exit, sys.exit() and an unhandled exception, and SIGKILL skips it exactly as the old reset did -- stated rather than glossed. THE PRE-PUSH HOOK STILL FIRES FROM A WORKTREE (core.hooksPath is .githooks and worktrees share the common git dir) and that was MEASURED BEFORE THE CHANGE WAS WRITTEN, by planting the seam violation in a prototype worktree and watching the real refusal text come back. THE PAUSE DOC NAMED TWO PROBES AND THERE WERE THREE. check8_probe.py plants `PROBE check8 planted fixture` on the same branch in the same way -- the probe for the gate that exists BECAUSE a stranded PROBE commit shipped to production, carrying the defect that gate is about. Found by grepping every tests/**/*.py that runs `git commit` rather than working from the document's list, which is CLAUDE.md's standing lesson that a fix verified on the copies somebody wrote down is not verified on the ones they did not. The only other two hits build their own throwaway repos and were already safe; check9 commits nothing. THE MOVE EXPOSED AN ARM PASSING FOR THE WRONG REASON: check8's two PreToolUse arms drove the hook with `git push origin main`, and in a detached worktree `main` is the CLONE's branch, not the checkout's HEAD where the fixture lives -- the planted arm went from DENIED to not-denied, and THE MORE DANGEROUS HALF IS THAT THE CONTROL ARM HAD BEEN REPORTING ALLOWED FOR A REASON WITH NOTHING TO DO WITH CHECK 8. Both use HEAD:main now, which pushed_tip() resolves to HEAD. EACH PROBE NOW ASSERTS THE CLONE WAS UNTOUCHED -- git status --porcelain and HEAD compared before and after -- because the existing `restored`/`head_restored` assertions were about the tree the probe had just been mutating and NONE OF THEM WAS THE CLAIM THAT WAS ACTUALLY VIOLATED. THE CLEAN-TREE PRECONDITION WAS REMOVED AND THAT IS A COVERAGE GAIN: it existed because `git add` in the clone could sweep uncommitted work into a probe commit, which a worktree built from HEAD cannot contain, and it had been SKIPPING the only proof these blocking gates have on every run in any clone with a modified tracked file. All three re-run green against a deliberately dirty tree. Also fixed check7's summary line, which printed a hardcoded `10 checks` regardless of how many ran -- the same derived-subject blindness the 2026-09-10 sweep went looking for, in the one line a reader uses to judge coverage. NO THIRD STRUCTURAL GUARD WAS ADDED, deliberately and said out loud: the behavioural assertion lives inside each probe, which is the self-referential shape, so the independent backstop is check 8 in a different file; a grep-based guard was considered and rejected as too weak to be worth the rot. BOTH RE-ENABLE CONDITIONS ARE NOW MET and written up in the pause doc, with condition 2 re-measured rather than assumed -- RE-ENABLING THE HOOK REMAINS MICHAEL'S CALL, because it is a shared hook firing in four live sessions and pausing it was his.

- SAIRN-PLATFORM-2026-09-11-gate-column-check-regex-handoff.md (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-11) -- HANDED CC A FOUR-PERCENT CHECKER, found while triaging two new suite reds rather than by looking for it. tools/gate_column_check.py:60's REST_RE second alternative BEGINS WITH A RAW 0x08 BACKSPACE BYTE where `\b` was meant -- in a raw string `\b` is the two characters backslash-b; what is on disk is the control character itself, so the alternative requires a literal backspace in the source being scanned and CAN NEVER MATCH. Proven against the pattern as it sits on disk rather than argued from reading: `rest('rpc` and `restUrl('foo` both NO MATCH, and with the escape restored `rest('rpc` matches while `xrest('rpc` correctly does not, so the boundary is load-bearing rather than decorative. COUNTED, NOT DESCRIBED: across every tracked *.js/*.html outside archive/, as shipped it sees 26 CALL SITES AND 7 TABLES; fixed it sees 498 AND 162 -- 472 call sites and 155 tables invisible, including alf_clients, alf_mar, alf_staff_credentials, ai_memories and business_profiles, i.e. most of the platform including the clinical and credential tables. THE CONCLUSION IS AFFECTED AND NOT JUST THE TOOL: a "no gate reads a missing column" result from this run is a statement about 4% of the surface, and the known trial_ends_at case sits in api/sd-data.js which the SURVIVING first alternative happens to cover -- so a green run would look consistent with the one case everybody already knows about, which is the worst possible shape for a false negative. NOT FIXED, because the file is inside CC's active `gate-reads-nonexistent-column` claim and it is a one-character change they can make instantly; the handoff says so plainly and suggests re-running their conclusions afterwards, since 472 more call sites is likely to make the finding set DIFFERENT rather than merely larger. MY OWN FIRST MEASUREMENT WAS WRONG AND I CAUGHT IT BEFORE REPORTING: a `.replace('\x08', '\b')` comparison printed 26 vs 26 and "0 invisible", which contradicted the direct match test -- I stopped and drove the single question (does the shipped pattern match `rest('rpc`?) instead of trusting the comparison, and the counts above come from the corrected version. A measurement that disagrees with a direct observation is the measurement that is wrong. THE CONTROL-CHAR CHECKER DID EXACTLY WHAT IT WAS BUILT FOR: CLAUDE.md records two of the four literal control bytes found 2026-09-10 as regexes that could never match, one of them guarding a database privilege -- this is the third, in a checker written the same week, and it was caught by a report-only checker promoted days earlier rather than by anybody reading the file.

- tools/sairn_push_gate_hook.py (docstring) + index + handoff (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- I RETRACT THE PUSH-GATE FAIL-OPEN. Cody implemented the fail-closed widening on Michael's decision and then REVERTED IT in 014953aa, having built the scenario in a real clone level with its origin. I checked their reasoning against my own framing rather than accepting it, and it holds: THE RANGE IS EMPTY ONLY WHEN THE PUSHED TIP IS AN ANCESTOR OF WHAT ORIGIN ALREADY HAS, and such a push ships no new objects -- to `main` it is a non-fast-forward the remote rejects, and to a NEW branch it creates a ref over commits origin already holds, so no seed content is newly published. MY OWN EXAMPLE WAS THE COUNTER-EXAMPLE: I offered `git push origin HEAD:some-branch` while the clone is BEHIND as an ordinary push sending real content, and while behind those commits are already on origin. I did not check that before writing it down. THE WIDENING HAD A REAL COST and that is the part worth keeping: an accurate [] became a 1,671-file scan of the whole history handed to every SQL-consuming check, which then judged files no push was sending -- one whack-a-mole per check and no new branch ever pushable, which is how a gate gets switched off. WHAT KEPT IT FROM BEING A CONFIDENT WRONG CLAIM is that the handoff said in those words that the end-to-end case was NOT demonstrated and that outgoing_subjects() was unverified; that caveat is the only reason this is a retraction rather than a blocking gate rebuilt around a defect nobody had. THE THREE FACTS UNDER THE CLAIM STAND and one was a real defect: `base` is only ever set in PREPUSH mode; `@{u}` IS `origin/main` when pushing main, so the chain is ONE RUNG TRIED TWICE; and THE `HEAD~1` LAST RESORT THE DOCSTRING PROMISED WAS NEVER IMPLEMENTED. Fixed that one -- docstring only, zero behaviour change -- and recorded IN THE FUNCTION why an empty range is correct, with Cody's revert commit cited, so the next reader who notices it does not re-report it a third time. TWO SESSIONS REPORTED THIS INDEPENDENTLY IN ONE DAY, which is the argument for the note living in the code rather than only in an index row. One self-inflicted trap caught on the way: my correction sentence originally said grepping the file for HEAD~1 returns exactly one hit, which my own correction made false -- reworded so the durable claim is about `refs` rather than about a grep count.

- tools/literal_drift_check.py + tests/run_literal_drift_determinism_probe.py (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- A CHECKER THAT ANSWERED DIFFERENTLY ON IDENTICAL INPUT, and it was worse than the row reporting it said. `sorted(set(...), key=len, reverse=True)`: sorted is STABLE, so equal-length strings kept whatever order the SET iterated them in, and set iteration order for strings depends on PYTHONHASHSEED, which the interpreter varies between processes. CC's row recorded it as "same lines, same ratio, opposite A/B labels" and called it harmless; measured across seeds, THE PAIR NUMBERING MOVES TOO -- at one seed `pair 5` is 63-vs-52 chars at ratio 0.904, at another 63-vs-42 at 0.800, a DIFFERENT FINDING UNDER THE SAME LABEL. Anything citing a pair number was reading noise, and diffing two runs is exactly what comment_sensitivity_check.py does, which is how it surfaced at all. QUANTIFIED WITH THE OLD SORT RESTORED: stonedesk and sairnvet each give SIX distinct outputs across six seeds, sairnlaw three, sairncode and sairndental two, sairnsenior and sairnfreedom stable. Fixed by sorting on (-len, text), a total order over distinct strings; all 23 apps now byte-identical across seeds. TWO DEFECTS IN MY OWN PROBE, BOTH FOUND BY RUNNING IT, AND BOTH ARE THE CLASS THE PROBE GUARDS. (1) THE MUTATION ARM EXERCISED NOTHING: I wrote the reverted copy to tempfile.mkdtemp(), where it died on `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'comment_quote_check'` because the tool imports siblings relative to its own path -- it printed ZERO lines at every seed, so "identical across seeds" was true of nothing and the arm reported the defect as UNDETECTABLE. HAD I WRITTEN THE COMPARISON THE OTHER WAY ROUND IT WOULD HAVE REPORTED PASS. The copy lives in tools/ now, prefixed zz_tmp_ so a stranded one is obvious, removed in a finally, with an arm asserting it really produces pairs BEFORE any seed comparison is believed. (2) THE STRUCTURAL ARM FAILED A CORRECT FILE: it searched the RAW source for `key=len` and matched the fix's own comment explaining why that is wrong -- CLAUDE.md's comment-quote rule, added 2026-09-11 after two instances in one day, hit a THIRD time by somebody who had read the rule an hour earlier. It reads a comment-stripped copy now AND asserts the raw source still mentions it, so the arm cannot pass because the string vanished. THE FIRST DEFECT IS THE ONE WORTH REMEMBERING: a control that cannot run is indistinguishable from a control that finds nothing, and the difference is one direction of a comparison.

- docs/2026-09-12-checker-determinism-sweep.md (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- THE DETERMINISM SWEEP, AND IT IS AN HONEST NEGATIVE RESULT. Having found one checker whose output depended on PYTHONHASHSEED, I asked how many others do: 26 promoted checkers at three seeds, the 6 apps-mode ones re-run across SIX apps, plus three unpromoted tools carrying the same code shape. EXACTLY ONE was seed-dependent -- literal_drift_check.py, the one that started it, already fixed in ac8f8491. Everything else is byte-identical across seeds. WHY THE CLASS DESERVED A SWEEP RATHER THAN A FIX AND A SHRUG: it cannot be noticed by USING a checker, because every individual run looks authoritative and the disagreement exists only BETWEEN runs, which nothing compares unless it sets out to -- the original surfaced only because comment_sensitivity_check.py diffs two runs for an unrelated purpose. And 21 of the 26 are wired into report_only_checks.py, whose output is read after every push BY A MECHANISM rather than by a person. ONE APP IS NOT COVERAGE, AND THAT IS MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: with the defect present, literal_drift gave SIX distinct outputs on sairnvet and was PERFECTLY STABLE on sairnsenior and sairnfreedom -- a single-app sweep would have cleared it, which is why the apps-mode pass runs six apps. THE CONTROL IS WHAT MAKES "NONE" A RESULT RATHER THAN AN ABSENCE: I copied the fixed tool with the old sort restored and ran it through the identical path -- 3 distinct outputs, 234 lines each -- so the method demonstrably sees the defect it hunts. That control only works because the copy lives in tools/; a copy in a temp directory dies on ModuleNotFoundError, prints nothing at every seed, and READS AS STABLE, which is the trap I had already fallen into once today inside the probe for this same tool. traceability_matrix.py WAS MEASURED AS A GENERATOR and not only as --check, because the registry runs the one-line check which never exercises the code that writes the committed doc -- sha256 of the written file is identical at four seeds, and the doc was restored byte-identical afterwards. THE SHAPE, STATED SO IT IS USABLE: `sorted(set(...))` with NO key is a total order and is CORRECT, 19 times over in tools/; the hazard is `sorted(set(...), key=<lossy>)`, of which there are three sites. THE TWO THAT ARE NOT THE DEFECT ARE BENIGN BY ARGUMENT AND NOT MERELY BY MEASUREMENT -- traceability_matrix.py:82 feeds only a `len(top) == 1` test, and local_only_collection_check.py:555 builds a regex alternation that is never printed whose alternatives are distinct literals so at most one can match at a position. Named in the doc so the next reader does not re-derive them, and so the hazard is already written down if either ever starts printing its intermediate. NO PERMANENT CHECKER WAS ADDED AND THAT IS A DECISION, not an omission: a guard flagging `key=` on a `sorted(set(` is 3 sites and 2 false positives -- 67% on its first real run, which is the exact profile report_only_checks.py's own header says earns a checker the reputation that gets it switched off before promotion. The concrete guard is the per-tool probe; the sweep costs about six minutes and is a periodic exercise the document exists to make repeatable rather than re-invented.

- api/_lib/roofing-credentials-endpoint.test.js + sql/append_only_grant_audit.sql + index (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- MEASURED THE CROSS-FILE-NOTES ROW INSTEAD OF GUESSING AT IT, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT A CHECK IS NOT VIABLE. The row had sat open since 2026-08-25 saying "not worth a new check yet on ONE instance", which is the kind of maybe that rots. Scanned every comment in 828 tracked .py/.js/.sql/.html files for mentions of another tracked file: 2,746 mentions, 1,989 ONE-WAY -- 72%. A one-way mention is the NORM and not a signal; "see also X", "same pattern as Y" are the bulk of it. Narrowing to defect language left 115 pairs, still unreadable. Narrowing to the sharpest shape -- A asserts B has never been run / does not exist -- left 15, short enough to read, AND READING THEM IS WHAT MATTERED: 13 OF THE 15 WERE FALSE. THREE OF THOSE FALSE POSITIVES WERE THE SAME TRAP THIS REPO KEEPS RECORDING AND I NEARLY EDITED THREE CORRECT FILES BECAUSE OF IT: sairndental_license_seed.sql, sairndesign_license_seed.sql and sairnlegacy_license_seed.sql each carry a LEGEND for a diagnostic curl -- "200 with provisioned:false -> the license row is good, but X_data_schema.sql has NOT been run yet" -- which DESCRIBES WHAT A RESPONSE MEANS rather than asserting anything about the world. Caught by reading before editing, which is the only reason it is a near-miss rather than three wrong commits. TWO WERE REAL AND BOTH ARE CORRECTED. (1) roofing-credentials-endpoint.test.js gave "sql/sairnroofing_certifications_schema.sql has not been run in Supabase" as the WHOLE JUSTIFICATION for stubbing rather than round-tripping, and both its tables are live in the 2026-09-11 capture -- so the live round trip is now a gap BY CHOICE rather than by circumstance, which the header now says instead of quietly dropping the sentence. 24/24 still pass. (2) append_only_grant_audit.sql re-asserted a dated spec's claim in the PRESENT TENSE -- "written 2026-08-10 and still true today" -- and sairncash_waitlist is live; sairnscape_org_intel is still absent, so half that paragraph stands and is left standing. The dated spec itself is NOT wrong and was left alone: it was true when written. THE REUSABLE SHAPE IS NARROWER THAN THE ROW'S TITLE -- not "a note in the wrong file" but A CLAIM BORROWED FROM A DATED DOCUMENT AND RE-ASSERTED IN THE PRESENT TENSE, with nothing connecting the borrowing back to the source when the source's fact changes. AND ABSENCE FROM A SCHEMA CAPTURE IS EVIDENCE, NOT PROOF -- PRESENCE IS CONCLUSIVE, which is why both corrections rest on tables that ARE there rather than on tables that are missing. ONE CONTRADICTION RESOLVED BY LOOKING RATHER THAN ASSUMING: bld_bids is live while sairnbuild_data_schema.sql's 31 tables are all absent -- no contradiction, bld_bids is provisioned by a different file, and I checked that before writing anything down. Still absent from the capture and recorded: sairnbuild_data_schema.sql (31 tables), sairngrounds_caddie_schema.sql (2), and stonedesk_public_surface_schema.sql (5) which is CC'S ACTIVE CLAIM and untouched.

- docs/2026-09-12-tools-package-plan.md (NEW) (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- THE TOOLS PACKAGE: INVENTORY AND STRUCTURE DONE, GENERICISATION SCOPED SEPARATELY on Michael's instruction. Built on Cody's GENERATED docs/TOOLING-INVENTORY.md rather than re-deriving, plus a coupling measure taken per file against comment-stripped code: how many times the executable half names something only this platform has (api/_resources, license_hash, license_keys, supabase, an app name, a PINNACLE key, .claude/). tools/ holds 99 files and MOST OF THEM ARE NOT PRODUCT -- 11 gen_*_calendar/seed files are SAIRNlaw jurisdiction DATA, the claim system and push-gate hook and load-state gate are infrastructure for four clones against one repo (sellable as a PATTERN, not as code), the live probes need our keys and our deployment, and the rest are libraries. WHAT IS LEFT IS THE PACKAGE: 48 TOOLS, ~13,100 LOC, 404 COUPLING REFERENCES, and that last number is the genericisation job stated as a number rather than a feeling. STRUCTURED AS SEVEN SUITES AND NOT FIFTY SCRIPTS, because fifty loose checkers is not a product -- nobody knows what to run or in what order. Each suite is a DEFECT CLASS with its own runner, worked incident and statement of what it cannot see: silent failure (8 tools, 88 refs), claim honesty (8, 67), suite integrity (4, 10), schema drift (7, 94), source hygiene (10, 37), governance registers (7, 68), the harness (4, 40). SUITE 7 IS WHAT MAKES IT A PRODUCT RATHER THAN A TOOLBOX -- everything above it is a collection of scripts until something invokes it automatically and reports in one voice, and this repo's own inventory proves the point: 43 of 99 files are invoked by NOTHING, discovered only by generating the inventory. SUITE 3 IS THE DIFFERENTIATOR NOBODY ELSE SELLS -- checking that your TESTS are honest, that a control really breaks what it claims to break, that an assertion is not matching a comment about the code, that a checker answers the same twice -- and it is also the CHEAPEST to ship first at 10 refs across 1,092 lines. THE 404 REFS ARE FOUR DIFFERENT JOBS, not one: hardcoded repo paths (a config file, mechanical); APP-SHAPE ASSUMPTIONS (single-file HTML with inline script blocks, license_hash-scoped rows -- needs an ADAPTER INTERFACE and is where "runs on someone else's code" is won or lost, not doable by find-and-replace); platform assumptions (Supabase/PostgREST/Vercel, concentrated in suite 4 which has the highest coupling DENSITY in the package); and house document formats (ship the templates -- least work, arguably most valuable for a course). ESTIMATE 9-12 FOCUSED SESSIONS with the adapter design as a gate everything else depends on, and it should be REVIEWED BEFORE ANYTHING IS BUILT ON IT. TWO DECISIONS RAISED BEFORE BUILDING RATHER THAN DURING: whether the package must run on non-JavaScript codebases (materially bigger adapter, and suites 1/2/5 change shape), and whether it ships as one installable package or seven separately adoptable ones (changes the harness). ONE RISK NAMED UP FRONT: every tool here earned its keep on a real incident IN THIS CODEBASE, and genericising strips exactly the specificity that makes it accurate -- the false-positive rate on outside code is UNKNOWN, and this package's own standard says a checker that false-alarms early gets switched off before it is trusted. Mitigation is to run each ported suite against two or three real outside codebases and PUBLISH THE MEASURED false-positive rate per checker rather than a claim that it works.

- packages/testint/ (NEW: languages.py, comments.py, README, 29-arm control) (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- SUITE 3 MVP FOUNDATION BUILT, on Michael's two confirmed decisions: BROADER LANGUAGE SUPPORT (not JS/HTML-only) and SEVEN SEPARATELY ADOPTABLE SUITES with Suite 3 shipping first. Suite 3 was chosen as the MVP because it is both the strongest differentiator -- checking that your TESTS are honest is a thing nobody else sells -- and the cheapest, at 10 coupling refs across 1,092 lines. CONFIRMED BY MEASUREMENT RATHER THAN BY THE PLAN: its four tools' only SAIRN coupling is directory globs (tests/, tools/, api/*.test.js) plus one allowlist keyed on our file paths, which is DATA and becomes user config. No adapter interface needed, which is why this suite can ship before the adapter that suites 1/2/5 depend on. THE LOAD-BEARING PIECE IS THE COMMENT SCANNER, because every check here has to answer "is this text CODE or prose about code" first, and the multi-language requirement makes that the design problem. ONE scanner driven by a per-language PROFILE TABLE -- adding a language is a dictionary entry, not a code path, which is the only way a suite claiming to work on "your codebase" can be honest about what it supports. Eight profiles: javascript (also .html, which needs the JS profile rather than the html one because of accept="image/*"), python (docstrings are STRINGS not comments and are preserved, stated), c_family, rust (block comments NEST), ruby, sql (doubled '' quotes AND nesting blocks), html, shell. AN UNKNOWN EXTENSION RAISES RATHER THAN GUESSING, because a guessed profile is how a scanner reads a fraction of a file and reports clean. KNOWN LIMITS ARE RETURNED BY caveats_for() rather than hidden -- heredocs, r#"..."#, %w[], JSX -- so a clean result on such a file can be reported WITH its caveat instead of as a bare zero. VALIDATED DIFFERENTIALLY AGAINST THE BATTLE-TESTED SINGLE-LANGUAGE STRIPPER ON 23 REAL PRODUCTION FILES, not self-assessed: byte-identical on all 23. THAT TEST FOUND A REAL DEFECT IN MY OWN CODE THAT READING WOULD NOT HAVE: I had put `<` in the regex-context set, on the CORRECT reasoning that `a < /re/.source` is legal JavaScript -- but in HTML-embedded script `</div>` puts a `/` straight after a `<`, the scan runs forward for a closing `/` and finds it inside the NEXT HTML COMMENT, so the `<!--` never opens and 267 characters of comment survived as code in stonedesk.html. The battle-tested implementation excludes `<` and `>` for exactly that reason and I had generalised past it. Fixed, the trade stated in the code (`a < /re/.test(b)` now reads as division, which is rare and harmless to a comment stripper where swallowing an HTML comment is not), and pinned as its own arm. 29 control arms, and they are OF TWO KINDS DELIBERATELY -- a comment really is removed, AND CODE REALLY IS NOT REMOVED -- because the second kind catches the expensive failure and is the kind a smoke test skips. survival() returns the non-whitespace survival fraction for any file so a clean result can be doubted BEFORE it is trusted: measured across the 23 app files it runs 72.9% to 100%, and the historical naive-regex implementations sat at 11%.

- packages/testint/ config.py + assertions.py + check_comment_quote.py + examples (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- THE FIRST OF SUITE 3'S THREE CHECKS IS PORTED, GENERICISED AND VALIDATED AGAINST THE REAL REPO. Three pieces: a CONFIG (tests/sources/checkers/probes as globs, no conventions -- a tool that assumes tests/ and src/ works on the repo it was written in and nowhere else, which is the difference between a tool and a product), an ASSERTION PROFILE TABLE per language (how each language says "read this file" and "is this literal in it" -- javascript, python, go, ruby, and adding one is an entry not a code path), and the check itself. THE VARIABLE MATTERS AND NOT JUST THE CALL, carried over from the original: only a search against the variable BOUND TO THE RAW FILE can be testing comments, because the first version of the ancestor matched any x.indexOf('lit') and immediately false-alarmed on a probe searching a comment-STRIPPED copy it had made itself. VALIDATED AGAINST THIS REPO, 313 test files, 384 assertions inspected: 7 comment-only findings, and I READ ALL SEVEN -- every one is genuinely an assertion matching only a comment, so the measured FALSE-POSITIVE RATE OF THE SHAPE IS 0/7. Four are cases the incumbent SAIRN tool already declares deliberately; THREE ARE NOVEL AND THE INCUMBENT COULD NOT SEE THEM -- dnt_vendor_backup_probe.py's arm8_reason_is_recorded (a Python test; the incumbent reads only JS), sairnscape_memory.js asserting an explanatory comment exists, and stonedesk_damage_no_twin.js using a comment as a block LOCATOR. The incumbent inspects 5 assertions; this inspects 384. All seven are deliberate and belong in an allow file with reasons, which is the honest answer rather than seven fixes. THREE DEFECTS IN MY OWN CODE, ALL FOUND BY RUNNING IT AND NONE BY READING IT. (1) A RELATIVE `root` RESOLVED AGAINST THE CWD, so the same config from two directories resolved two different repos and one matched NOTHING -- which every check here would have reported as a clean run over zero files. Relative roots are now relative to the config file. Same cwd-anchoring class I fixed across the SAIRN tools this week, committed again in new code. (2) THE ALLOW FILE RESOLVED AGAINST THE REPO ROOT rather than beside the config, which made the SHIPPED EXAMPLE unloadable -- and the tool correctly answered COULD NOT RUN with exit 3 rather than proceeding with an empty allowlist, which is the designed behaviour working. (3) THE REPORT CRASHED ON ITS OWN FINDINGS with UnicodeEncodeError on a cp1252 console, after the summary had printed -- right exit code, detail gone. ONE-HOP CONSTANT RESOLUTION ADDED because the measurement demanded it: 178 of 384 assertions (46%) were skipped and the commonest cause by far was a bare identifier, `readFileSync(HTML, 'utf8')`, where the path lives in a constant defined earlier -- not a rare style, the dominant one. One level ONLY and stated as such: chasing a variable through reassignment is a dataflow problem, and a half-right analysis resolving the WRONG file is strictly worse than could-not-tell. Skips fell 178 to 139 (46% to 36%), resolved assertions 184 to 223, AND THE FINDING COUNT STAYED AT 7 -- coverage up, noise unchanged. The remaining 139 are genuinely variable-driven (loop variables, u.file) and are COUNTED AND PRINTED rather than silent, because a checker that quietly narrows its own subject is the exact defect this suite is sold to find.

- packages/testint/ check_mutation_anchor.py + check_determinism.py (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- SUITE 3'S OTHER TWO CHECKS PORTED; ALL THREE NOW EXIST. MUTATION ANCHOR: reproduces the incumbent EXACTLY on the real repo -- 70 anchors, 0 bad, 0 unread, matching tools/mutation_anchor_check.py run side by side -- while adding a language-agnostic path. THE CARRIED-OVER PROPERTY THAT MATTERS IS THAT IT NEVER IMPORTS A CONTROL: most have no `if __name__ == "__main__"` guard, so importing one RUNS it, mutating a live source file and shelling out, and the ancestor's first version did exactly that, hung, was killed mid-run and left api/_lib/dental-guardian.js modified on disk with an injected line. Python controls are read with ast; everything else declares in a `<control>.mutations.json` sidecar read as DATA. Neither path executes anything, which is the only acceptable design for a read-only checker -- it must not be able to change the thing it inspects. A control it cannot read is COULD NOT READ and sets exit 2, never counted clean, because "we could not check it" and "it is fine" are the two answers this suite exists to keep apart. DETERMINISM: the check I invented this session, now a real tool. Runs each configured checker at several PYTHONHASHSEED values and compares stdout byte for byte. TWO WAYS THE MEASUREMENT ITSELF LIES ARE GUARDED EXPLICITLY BECAUSE I HIT BOTH WHILE BUILDING IT. (1) A CHECKER THAT CANNOT RUN IS PERFECTLY STABLE -- a command dying on an import error prints nothing at every seed and "identical across seeds" is true of nothing, so no-output is reported as COULD NOT RUN and never as stable. That is the exact trap I fell into twice, first in the literal_drift probe's mutation arm and again in the sweep. (2) ONE INPUT IS NOT COVERAGE -- with the real defect present the original checker gave SIX distinct outputs on sairnvet and was perfectly stable on sairnsenior and sairnfreedom, so a single-target sweep would have cleared it; the config takes a target list and the report says so. AND IT SHIPS WITH `--self-test`, which plants a genuinely seed-dependent program, runs it through the identical code path, and FAILS if it comes back stable -- because a sweep returning NONE is indistinguishable from a sweep that is broken, and the shipped clean report says so in its own output and tells the reader the command to prove it. Self-test verified: 4 distinct outputs, DETECTED. Run against three real checkers x two targets: 6 runs, 0 varies, 0 could-not-run.

- packages/testint/ run.py + tests/test_checks.py + README (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- SUITE 3 IS A PRODUCT NOW, not three scripts. THE RUNNER EXISTS BECAUSE THREE SCRIPTS IN A DIRECTORY IS NOT A MECHANISM, and this package's own source repo is the evidence: the tooling inventory found 43 OF 99 TOOLS INVOKED BY NOTHING AT ALL, every one written for a real incident, committed, and never pointed at the codebase again. FOUR EXIT CODES AND THE DISTINCTIONS ARE THE WHOLE POINT: 0 ran and found nothing, 1 found something, 2 COULD NOT READ part of its subject, 3 COULD NOT RUN at all -- and 2 and 3 are NEVER folded into 0, because "we could not check it" and "it is fine" are the two answers this suite exists to keep apart, and a runner that collapses them undoes every check underneath it. IT DELIBERATELY DOES NOT AGGREGATE FINDINGS INTO A SCORE: a count across three different questions is a number nobody can act on, and the moment a number exists somebody drives it to zero by the cheapest available route, which for a checker is switching it off. THE CONTROLS ARE THE PART THAT MAKES THE PACKAGE CREDIBLE: tests/test_checks.py, 24 arms, one per check IN BOTH DIRECTIONS. Each builds a COMPLETE THROWAWAY PROJECT in a temp directory -- config, source, test -- plants the defect, asserts the check reports it, then plants the CLEAN version and asserts it stays quiet. Both halves are required: a check that always reports passes the first alone and a check that never reports passes the second alone, so only the pair says anything. The fixtures double as the clearest documentation of what each check catches, because each is the smallest project in which the defect is REAL -- a guard deleted with only the comment describing it left, an anchor refactored out from under a control, a sorted(set, key=len). ONE ARM ASSERTS SOMETHING NO OTHER TEST CAN: check_mutation_anchor PARSES controls and never imports them, and the fixture is a control that writes a file at import time -- the arm passes only if that file NEVER APPEARS. That is the hazard that hung the ancestor and left a live source file mutated on disk, now permanently held rather than remembered. Also pinned: an allow entry with a blank reason is a CONFIG ERROR exit 3 and not a pass, and a config with no checkers makes the runner exit 3 rather than reporting two clean checks as a clean run. 24/24 pass. Suite 3 now has three checks, a runner, 29 scanner arms, 24 control arms, a self-test, and a README that documents what it cannot see.

- packages/testint/validation/ (NEW) + assertions.py + check_comment_quote.py (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- RAN SUITE 3 AGAINST CODEBASES NOBODY HERE WROTE, which is the mitigation the package plan COMMITTED to rather than a claim, and it immediately paid for itself. Two corpora: CPython's standard library (2,246 .py files, 1,131 of them tests, written by hundreds of people over thirty years) and third-party npm packages (66 test files across 23 packages). THE CHECK COMMITTED TWO FALSE CLEANS ON FIRST CONTACT WITH FOREIGN CODE, BOTH OF WHICH WOULD HAVE SHIPPED, and both were invisible on the repo it was written for because there it works. (1) IT INSPECTED 0 ASSERTIONS ACROSS 1,131 REAL TEST FILES AND PRINTED "No undeclared comment-only assertion" WITH EXIT 0 -- my Python binding pattern expected the one-line `x = open(p).read()`, and measured across 400 CPython test files that form appears ZERO times while `with open(...) as f:` appears 750. The dominant idiom in the language was simply not matched. (2) FIXED, IT THEN INSPECTED 21 AND SKIPPED ALL 21 -- AND PRINTED A CLEAN LINE AGAIN. `inspected` counts ATTEMPTS; only `inspected - skipped` counts ANSWERS, and a guard on the wrong one of those two is no guard. It exits 3 COULD NOT RUN now and says in those words that nothing was classified. THE RULE THIS SUITE IS SOLD ON, BROKEN TWICE BY THE SUITE ITSELF, caught only by pointing it at code nobody here wrote -- no amount of reading would have surfaced either. WHY IT CANNOT RUN THERE, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED: the check's premise is SOURCE-INSPECTION TESTING, a test reading source as text and searching it for a literal. Share of test files that read a file as text -- this repo JS 67.3% (167/248), this repo Python 53.0% (35/66), CPython stdlib 17.3% (196/1131) -- and the CPython reads are of FIXTURE DATA rather than of source, which is why all 21 resolvable-looking targets resolved to nothing. The check is not broken there; it has nothing to say there. THE PRODUCT CONSEQUENCE IS REAL AND IS MICHAEL'S TO WEIGH: the three checks have MATERIALLY DIFFERENT REACH and selling them as one thing would be a claim the measurement does not support. check_determinism is the broadest (any codebase running checkers whose output a machine reads; no assumptions about test style; ships a --self-test proving the method sees the defect). check_mutation_anchor applies wherever mutation controls exist, which is a discipline a team has or does not. check_comment_quote applies where a codebase does source-inspection testing -- common here, 3-4x rarer in CPython -- and the honest pitch is "if your tests read your source as text, this finds the assertions that have stopped checking", not "this works on any test suite". Home repo unchanged after both fixes: 384 inspected, 223 resolved, 7 findings, 0 false positives; all 24 control arms and all 29 scanner arms still pass.

- packages/testint/ README positioning + pyproject.toml + cli entry point (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- THE POSITIONING DECISION APPLIED AND THE PACKAGE MADE INSTALLABLE. Michael approved honest per-check positioning over one uniform "works on any codebase" claim, so the README now carries a MEASURED applicability table rather than a pitch: check_determinism broadest (any codebase running checkers whose output something reads, no assumptions about test style, ships a --self-test proving the method sees the defect); check_mutation_anchor applies wherever mutation controls exist, a discipline a team has or does not; check_comment_quote narrowest AND HONESTLY SO, with the pitch Michael approved verbatim -- "if your tests read your source as text, this finds the assertions that have stopped checking" -- not "works on any test suite". The numbers behind it are in the table: 67.3% of this repo's JS tests and 53.0% of its Python tests read a file as text, against 17.3% of CPython's, and CPython's reads are of FIXTURE DATA rather than source, so the check reports COULD NOT RUN there, which is the correct answer and not a clean one. PACKAGING: pyproject.toml with a `testint` console entry point, so install means pip install rather than copying a directory. NO RUNTIME DEPENDENCIES, DELIBERATELY AND STATED IN THE FILE -- this is a tool people install to audit a codebase they already do not fully trust, and every dependency it drags in is another thing they have to trust in order to run it. Everything is stdlib. THE LICENCE IS DELIBERATELY UNSET AND THE REASON IS WRITTEN WHERE SOMEBODY WILL SEE IT: it is a commercial decision, not a default for whoever wrote the packaging file to pick, and a wrong one is expensive to walk back once the package is distributed. A build without it is fine for local install; it must be set before publishing. That is MICHAEL'S CALL and is flagged rather than guessed. cli() is a separate function from main() because [project.scripts] needs a no-argument callable while main() takes argv so the controls can drive it without touching sys.argv. All 24 control arms and 29 scanner arms still pass.

- packages/testint/LICENSE (NEW) + pyproject.toml + README (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- LICENCE SET TO PROPRIETARY on Michael's decision: "Proprietary -- All Rights Reserved, Copyright (c) 2026 SAIRN Tech LLC", declared in pyproject via `license = { file = "LICENSE" }` plus the `License :: Other/Proprietary License` classifier, with a LICENSE file and a README section. THE REASONING IS RECORDED IN ALL THREE PLACES RATHER THAN JUST DECIDED, because the choice is ONE-WAY: a proprietary work can later be released under an open licence, and source once released under an open licence CANNOT BE RECALLED from anyone who already holds a copy -- so starting proprietary keeps both options open while the revenue path is protected, and the reverse does not. The LICENSE text says plainly that possession conveys no right to use and that any use needs a separate written agreement, which is the distinction that actually matters for a package someone might be handed rather than buy. Verified by parsing pyproject with tomllib rather than by reading it. Controls still 24/24.

- tools/checker_control_check.py (NEW) + tests/run_checker_control_probe.py (NEW) + index (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- THE META-CHECKER: every promoted checker must have a control that PROVES it can fire, and FIVE HAVE NONE AT ALL. Michael's initiative, split with Cody on his decision -- Cody owns the gate-tool half under `blocking-checkers-can-actually-fail`, I own the registry-wide mechanism and will consume their per-tool fixtures as inputs. THE CASE THAT PROMPTED IT: checkblocks.py ALWAYS EXITED 0 and nothing noticed, because A CHECKER THAT ALWAYS PASSES LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A CODEBASE THAT IS ALWAYS CLEAN. It was found when somebody thought to test it by hand, and thinking to test it by hand is not a mechanism. FIRST RUN, 32 promoted checkers against 201 test files: 5 NO CONTROL (checkblocks.py, div_balance_check.py, orphan_register_check.py, sairn_dead_button_audit.py, vercel_config_check.py), 2 ONE DIRECTION (key_collision_check.py and traceability_matrix.py -- both seen to FIRE, neither seen to stay quiet, which is the half that catches a checker that reports on everything), 24 BOTH EVIDENCED, 1 EXEMPT. IT SAYS EVIDENCED AND NOT PROVEN AND THE DISTINCTION IS DELIBERATE: direction is inferred from assertions near the reference, and only reading the fixture can tell a control that plants a REAL defect from one that plants a shape the checker happens to match -- so the evidence is QUOTED rather than counted, and the word does the work. NO CONTROL is the verdict that is mechanical and certain: nothing anywhere in tests/ names that checker, so nothing has ever seen it fail. IT READS THE REGISTRY RATHER THAN A HAND LIST, so promoting a checker without a control is caught the same day instead of whenever somebody re-types the list -- the same derived-subject discipline as the self-referential sweep. AND IT READS TEST SOURCE WITH COMMENTS STRIPPED, which is the arm the tool exists to deserve: the cheapest way to fake this exact evidence is `# expect exit 1` beside no assertion at all, a class this platform recorded THREE TIMES IN TWO DAYS and twice inside its own probes, so a tool producing false reassurance about false reassurance would be the worst possible outcome. The probe pins it in BOTH languages because Python and JS are stripped by different code paths and testing one proves one. 14 arms, all planting a verdict and asserting the tool reaches it, plus the opposite. The single exemption carries its reason in the code: npm_audit_check.py's subject is the public advisory database, and a fixture pinning an advisory that will be fixed upstream is worse than none.

- tests/run_uncontrolled_checkers_probe.py (NEW) + tools/checker_control_check.py + probe (Fourth, 2026-09-12) -- CONTROL PAIRS FOR THE UNCONTROLLED CHECKERS, AND THE META-CHECKER CAUGHT ITSELF LYING WITHIN THE HOUR. Wrote plant-defect/plant-clean pairs for checkblocks.py, div_balance_check.py and vercel_config_check.py -- 13 arms, all passing. checkblocks.py was the point of the whole exercise: it ALWAYS EXITED 0 while printing FAILED_BLOCKS:1, it is Guardian Check 0a which CLAUDE.md calls non-negotiable and says hard-blocks everything else, it was fixed 2026-09-12, AND NOTHING PROVED THE FIX -- a repair nobody has watched fail is a repair nobody has tested. Now pinned in all three directions: a block that does not parse exits 1 and says FAILED_BLOCKS:1, a clean file exits 0, and A FILE WITH NO SCRIPT BLOCK AT ALL EXITS 2 AND SAYS COULD NOT RUN, because reporting 0 there would be the original defect wearing a different hat. Also pinned div_balance's 2026-08-07 fix that a <div> inside an HTML COMMENT must not count, which nothing pinned. THEN THE IMPORTANT PART. After adding three controls the meta-checker reported NO CONTROL 5 -> 0, which was FALSE FOR TWO OF THEM: my probe's DOCSTRING names all five checkers, attribution was at FILE level, so every fires/silent assertion anywhere in the file counted for every checker the file mentioned. THE TOOL PRODUCED FALSE REASSURANCE ABOUT FALSE REASSURANCE, which is the worst outcome available to it, and it did so on the first control file ever written against it. A PYTHON DOCSTRING IS A STRING AND NOT A COMMENT, so the comment-stripping I was so careful about neither did nor could remove it -- I had defended the wrong door. Fixed with PROXIMITY ATTRIBUTION: an assertion counts for a checker only within 15 lines of a line naming it, deliberately tight because a control's assertions sit beside the call that produced them, and a mention with nothing near it is a MENTION whose verdict is REFERENCED ONLY, which does not pass. THE CORRECTED PICTURE IS WORSE THAN THE FLATTERING ONE AND THAT IS THE POINT: 2 REFERENCED ONLY, 9 ONE DIRECTION, 20 BOTH EVIDENCED, 1 exempt -- the file-level version was flattering BY CONSTRUCTION. Pinned as arm 4b with a fixture that tests one checker and merely MENTIONS another 40 lines away, asserting exactly one comes back BOTH EVIDENCED and the other REFERENCED ONLY. 17 arms on the meta-checker now. TWO STILL UNCONTROLLED AND NAMED RATHER THAN QUIETLY DROPPED: orphan_register_check.py needs an index plus a stonedesk-shaped fixture, and sairn_dead_button_audit.py ALWAYS exits 0 by design so its control must assert on output SECTIONS rather than on exit code -- both are real work, not oversights.

- tools/checker_control_check.py + tests/run_checker_control_probe.py + seven control files declared (Fourth, 2026-09-13) -- THE META-CHECKER NOW DEMANDS A DECLARATION, AFTER THREE INFERENCE MODELS WERE EACH WRONG WITHIN AN HOUR OF BEING WRITTEN. FILE LEVEL over-credited: my own first control file named five checkers in its DOCSTRING while testing three and all five came back proven, which is the tool producing false reassurance ABOUT false reassurance -- and a Python docstring is a STRING not a comment, so the comment-stripping I had been careful about neither did nor could remove it; I had defended the wrong door. PROXIMITY (15 lines) then under-credited immediately: tests/run_defect_register_probe.py binds TOOL once at the top and asserts both directions 150 lines below, so a thorough control read as ONE DIRECTION. INVOCATION under-credited differently: real controls wrap subprocess in a run() helper, so the checker's name never appears inside the call span -- which marked the controls I had written THAT MORNING as REFERENCED ONLY. THREE MODELS, THREE WRONG ANSWERS, EACH PLAUSIBLE, and the lesson is not that a fourth heuristic would work: WHICH CHECKER A TEST IS A CONTROL FOR IS A FACT ITS AUTHOR KNOWS AND NOTHING ELSE RELIABLY DOES. So it is declared -- `CONTROLS_FOR = ['x.py']` -- one line, unambiguous, and philosophically right for a tool whose whole job is to DEMAND proof rather than infer it: an UNDECLARED control is exactly the "we think it is covered" state the tool exists to end. FOUR VERDICTS NOW: NO DECLARED CONTROL, DECLARED-NO-ASSERTIONS, ONE DIRECTION, BOTH EVIDENCED, plus EXEMPT. The report NAMES the files that mention an undeclared checker, so the burn-down is one line per control rather than an investigation. HONEST BASELINE AFTER DECLARING THE SEVEN I OWN: 7 BOTH EVIDENCED, 24 NO DECLARED CONTROL, 1 exempt -- worse-looking than every inference model produced, and the only one of the four that is actually true. TWO MORE VACUOUS ASSERTIONS FOUND INSIDE MY OWN PROBE while migrating it: `'DECLARED, NO ASSERTIONS' in out` matched the SUMMARY line "DECLARED, NO ASSERTIONS : 0", so two arms passed while the count was ZERO -- a substring search against a whole report, which is the same vacuous-check class the probe exists to police, committed inside it. Fixed with verdict_row(), which requires the verdict and the checker name ON THE SAME ROW. All five touched probes re-run green.

- fourteen control files declared + docs/TOOLING-INVENTORY.md regenerated (Fourth, 2026-09-13) -- THE DECLARATION BURN-DOWN, 24 UNDECLARED DOWN TO 10, AND THE RULE USED TO GET THERE IS THE POINT. A candidate scorer proposed a control file for all 24 and SEVERAL PROPOSALS WERE WRONG IN EXACTLY THE WAY THE TOOL HAD JUST BEEN FIXED TO STOP BEING WRONG: run_report_only_checks_probe.py came up for three checkers because it tests the RUNNER, and my own run_uncontrolled_checkers_probe.py came up for orphan_register_check and sairn_dead_button_audit because its DOCSTRING names five checkers while testing three. Declaring those would have re-introduced the over-credit BY HAND, one commit after removing it from the code. So the applied rule is a FACT RATHER THAN A GUESS: a file named run_<stem>_probe.py or <stem>.test.js IS that checker's control, because its author said so by naming it -- widened only to strip a trailing _check/_audit/_scan, since a checker named x_check.py is conventionally probed by run_x_probe.py. Everything the name does not decide is LEFT UNDECLARED and the tool keeps reporting it, which is the honest outcome rather than a tidier number. 14 files declared, every one re-run green. TEN REMAIN AND THEY ARE THE ONES A HUMAN HAS TO READ: duplicate_global, fail_open, key_collision, nav_panel, orphan_register, panel_nesting, sairn_dead_button_audit, sairn_strict_args, schema_snapshot_freshness, write_without_readback -- several are named only by files that test something else, which is precisely why the name rule refused to guess. ONE ONE-DIRECTION CASE LEFT: traceability_matrix has 5 fires and 0 silent, so it has been seen to FAIL and never to pass quietly. FOUND WHILE VERIFYING: tests/run_tooling_inventory_probe.py was ALREADY RED before I touched it -- the generated docs/TOOLING-INVENTORY.md no longer matched the repo, because my own commits added checker_control_check.py and several test files since it was last generated. Checked whether my edit caused it by stashing the edit and re-running rather than assuming; it failed identically. Regenerated, and the probe is green. That is the generated-document discipline working exactly as designed: a mismatch is a finding, not a document somebody forgot.

- THE BLOCKER, and it is the finding (Fourth, 2026-09-13) -- SAIRNvet has NO per-employee identity. Fifteen apps ship api/*-auth.js and `sv` is not one of them; grep -rln sv_employee_auth over sql/ and api/ returns nothing; it writes through sd-data.js keyed on license_hash and app_id only. So the app holding the DEA-relevant, append-only, Tier A, no-removal-path controlled-substance register is the ONE app that knows which practice is writing and never which person. A witnessing lock needs an identity to witness with, and one recording that 'the practice confirmed it' is theatre. Raised as its own index row, size M, not novel to fix.
- ONE DECISION LEFT TO MICHAEL, deliberately (Fourth, 2026-09-13) -- single-operator confirm versus a genuine second witness. The research finding is that independent double-review still fails at a measurable rate, which cuts both ways: a single-operator confirm is much weaker because the person who erred is the person confirming, and a true second witness is not a workflow a one-vet practice can produce at all. That is a staffing and product call, and the code differs substantially -- a second-person lock needs a second live session, a roster of who may witness, and a refusal when only one person is logged in.

- ITEM 78 TARGET 2 VERIFIED A SECOND TIME, AND THE SECOND PASS FOUND THE ASSUMPTION THE FIRST WAS RESTING ON (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- `enumerateLocked()` collapsed each request into ONE atomic step because "the advisory lock means no other request can observe or act on the count in between". True of the INSERT; true of the COUNT only because of the ISOLATION LEVEL, which nothing stated. `pg_advisory_xact_lock` serialises ACQUISITION and does not move the SNAPSHOT, so under repeatable read a caller that waited on the lock counts against a world where the holder had not committed -- and the cap over-runs WITH THE LOCK WORKING IN EVERY STEP. Third enumerator, 90 schedules, exhibits it. The function now RAISES `invalid_transaction_state` rather than relying on nobody running one `alter role ... set default_transaction_isolation`; raising rather than returning an error object because the client treats an RPC failure as fail-open-and-log while an error OBJECT would be read as a real answer. 21 arms to 34. AND A CORRECTION TO MY OWN CLAIM: the index row and the tool header both said the RPC was "verified NEVER RUN against db/schema_snapshot.json". That snapshot is TABLES ONLY -- the words function, routine, returns and plpgsql appear ZERO times in the whole file. A zero there is NOT CAPTURED, not NOT RUN. Michael says it was closed live 2026-09-02; this clone has no SUPABASE keys so the honest state is COULD NOT TELL, and the tool now declines to say.

The first judgment turned out to be about the checker: `public-complaint-submit.js` sat in UNGUARDED with one of the best guards on the platform (hashed submission_key, read back in a ten-minute window, returns the ORIGINAL answer on a duplicate, own migration) purely because `submission_key` was not one of the seven names KEY_NAMES knew. THE SHAPE MATTERS MORE THAN THE WORD: three of those seven match ZERO files and never have, so the vocabulary was written from what an idempotency key is usually CALLED rather than what this platform names them. The derived alternative was tried and REJECTED and is recorded so it is not rebuilt -- "non-scope column both filtered and written" matches 37 files, sixteen of them keyed on `active`, and would have flipped dozens to GUARDED, which is the fail-open direction. Real judgments: stripe-webhook SAFE (absolute assignment, not an increment) but carrying a DIFFERENT recorded defect -- Stripe does not guarantee ORDER, so a delayed invoice.paid after a cancellation flips the agent back to paid; trial-start FIX (the ROW cannot duplicate, email is UNIQUE, but the REQUEST is not idempotent -- a lost response means the retry gets ALREADY_EXISTS and the caller never receives the token that was minted); enqueue FIX (an arbitrary agent operation executed twice); _lib/audit.js ACCEPTED and deliberately NOT guarded, because an audit log records what was ATTEMPTED and a dedupe key there would HIDE a real double execution.
- ITEM 101 -- THE WITNESSING LOCK HAD SOFT CAPTURE AND HARD CAPTURE AND NOTHING BETWEEN THEM (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- `docs/2026-09-14-graduated-mechanical-commitment.md`. Everything `requireWitness()` re-checked was a fact about the TOKEN; not one was a fact about the WORLD, which had up to TOKEN_TTL_MS to move. The TTL is a deadline and a deadline re-verifies nothing. THE FILE NAMED ITS OWN HAZARD 300 LINES ABOVE THE PLACE IT DID NOT CHECK IT -- activeCaller() says "a witness signature from a revoked account is worse than no signature" and was called on policy/set_policy/request/countersign, never on the spend path. A vet could confirm a controlled-substance entry, be deactivated, and the write would still land attributed to them within ten minutes on a Tier A DEA-relevant register with no removal path. Fixed: the attester (and countersigner) re-read active BEFORE the spend, so a refusal they cannot act on does not also burn their confirmation. 33 arms to 38. FINDING 1 (HIGH) is the one that mattered: `sql/backup_reader_role.sql` created the role with the literal password `REPLACE_ME_BEFORE_RUNNING` inside `if not exists`, and THIS REPO IS PUBLIC, so one un-edited run mints a LOGIN role with BYPASSRLS and SELECT on every table in `public` with a password anybody can read -- and the idempotence guard meant re-running the corrected file changed NOTHING. The file now RAISES while the placeholder is present and sets the password UNCONDITIONALLY, so a re-run is also the rotation path for a role already created wrong. FINDING 4: the role-gate header claimed every invariant while implementing five; I1/I2 are never evaluated for ANY app and appear in NO count, because "not checkable" is about role sets that could not be READ. The run now prints them with where each IS proven.
- ITEM 6 -- THE TWO FIXES BUILT, AND ONE OF THEM IS NOT THE OBVIOUS FIX (Fourth, 2026-09-14). trial-start takes an OPTIONAL client idempotency_key stored as a sha256. It is deliberately NOT "return the existing token on 409", which is what public-complaint-submit.js does: that endpoint is public, `Access-Control-Allow-Origin *`, and its only input is an EMAIL ADDRESS, so returning the token would hand a stranger the trial for any address they can guess. The complaint handler can do it because its key hashes the patient name AND the message -- content only the submitter had. enqueue takes an OPTIONAL key with a PARTIAL UNIQUE index on (agent_id, key) and returns the ORIGINAL command_id; not hashed, and the migration says why rather than copying the shape -- that key retrieves a credential and this one retrieves nothing. BOTH send the new column ONLY when a key is present, because PostgREST 400s an unknown column and sending it unconditionally would break every caller until the migration ran.
- DERIVING THE IDEMPOTENCY VOCABULARY: THREE ATTEMPTS, ALL MEASURED, ALL WORSE (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- recorded in the tool so they are not rebuilt. A "non-scope column filtered AND written" = 37 files, sixteen keyed on `active`. B "a read that short-circuits a later write", the RIGHT semantic by regex = 24 files, admits `license_hash` and MISSES public-complaint-submit.js whose early return is nested two ifs deep. C "schema-declared UNIQUE" = 50 columns and also misses submission_key, which deliberately carries an index and not a constraint. B needs an AST and there is no JS parser in this repo. SO THE CANDIDATES ARE DERIVED AND THE CLASSIFICATION STAYS HAND-WRITTEN, which is the half that actually failed -- and the reporter caught MY OWN new `idempotency_key_hash` column as UNCLASSIFIED minutes after I wrote it.

The index is MEASURED now, which your file's own header already demanded for section A's call count. Nothing of yours was wrong; the number simply reflects a real change. - ITEM 6 TRIAGE 6 JUDGED TO 19 OF 29, AND THE TRIAGE IS WHAT FOUND THE REAL GAP (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- 8 untriaged and NAMED rather than counted. Reading `api/_lib/courtlistener.js` to judge it SAFE (it calls an RPC that does the count and the insert under `pg_advisory_xact_lock`) is what showed two of its siblings were never swept. THE SHARPEST SINGLE JUDGMENT: `api/sairncash/trial-renew.js` is a READ-MODIFY-WRITE on a counter -- it SELECTs renewal_count, computes +1 in JavaScript, and PATCHes it back with a fresh expires_at. Two failures, both real: a LOST UPDATE when two renewals race, and DOUBLE EXTENSION on retry, where a lost response means the retry reads N+1, writes N+2 and a NEW 30-day expiry -- the trial extended twice from ONE admin approval on an admin-gated endpoint. Recorded FIX with both halves of the design (a compare-and-set closes the lost update, an idempotency key closes the double extension); NOT built this session.
- FOUR RATE LIMITERS, TWO FIXED, TWO NEVER SWEPT (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- `api/_lib/wex.js` and `api/_lib/intl-caselaw.js` both carried the item 78 count-then-insert race: read the window in one HTTP call, insert in another. `sql/sairnlaw_rate_limit_consume_fn_2026-09-14.sql`. RE-QUALIFIED, NOT COPIED (disciplines item 7): it takes the TABLE as a parameter because intl-caselaw is generic over its ledgers, and that name is ALLOWLISTED rather than merely quoted -- `quote_ident` is safe against injection and would STILL let a caller consume against any table, the same call `api/_lib/audit.js` made for audit tables. A refused call does NOT write a row, which is the OPPOSITE of the AI limiter and deliberate: a recorded refusal would extend the very crawl-delay window it was refused by, so a caller polling every second would hold itself out forever. The READ COMMITTED precondition travelled with the pattern rather than waiting to be rediscovered. AND THE CONSEQUENCE IS NOT OURS TO ACCEPT -- these honour law.cornell.edu's published `Crawl-delay: 10`, so over-running risks the IP being blocked for every customer at once. The racy path is kept and LABELLED (`racy` on every result) because until the migration runs the RPC does not exist; 25 arms, section D drives every way the RPC can fail to answer and asserts each falls back to a path that still honours the window rather than returning "go ahead".
- THE ONLY TIER FINDING ON THE PLATFORM WAS A COMMENT (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- `criticality_tier_check.py` reported `sairnvet/sv_audit_backed is registered and has no row`, the single ⚠ in the master plan. It is NOT a registered resource: it sits in `notSynced`, the list of keys that deliberately never reach a server. `apps_with_registries()` matched any line shaped `'name',` anywhere in the file, AND THE ANSWER DEPENDED ON COMMENT PLACEMENT -- sairnvet declares three local-only keys together, two carry TRAILING comments so the line did not end in `',` and were invisible, while `sv_audit_backed` has its comment on the lines ABOVE and was scraped. Three siblings, one flagged, for no reason a reader could see. The reader now takes names from the `resources: [` array only and all THREE real shapes parse (multi-line, `resources: []`, `resources: RESOURCES`) -- the first narrowed version knew only the first and called the other two a changed file shape, which was loud and wrong. An unparseable file is an ERROR, not an app with no resources. RESOURCES_REGISTERED 385 -> 384, now equal to RESOURCE_ROWS; PROBLEMS 1 -> 0; every vertical is tier-clean. It also took `tests/run_criticality_tier_probe.py` from 2 failing arms back to green -- it had been red since the key landed this morning.
- TRIAL-RENEW FIXED, AND ONE FIX WOULD NOT HAVE DONE IT (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- the handler read renewal_count, added one in JavaScript and PATCHed it back with a fresh expires_at from NOW, on the only write path that can extend expires_at. A COMPARE-AND-SET closes the lost update (`renewal_count=eq.<N>`, or `is.null` for an untouched row -- a NULL count with `eq.0` would make the FIRST renewal of every trial match nothing). An OPTIONAL idempotency key closes the double extension. NEITHER COVERS THE OTHER: a key cannot close the race because two real approvals carry different keys, and a CAS cannot close the retry because a sequential retry reads the fresh value and its CAS succeeds honestly. THE ARM MOST LIKELY TO BE GOT WRONG: PostgREST answers the LOSER of a compare-and-set with 200 AND AN EMPTY ARRAY, so a handler checking only `ok` tells a stale writer it succeeded -- and `return=minimal` returns no body to count, so the PATCH asks for `return=representation`. A CAS nobody can read the result of is not a CAS. 11 arms, three sabotages.
- THE SUPABASE_ADMIN QUESTION IS ANSWERED, AND THE ANSWER IS NEITHER YES NOR NO (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- it WAS measured properly on 2026-08-26: ownership across `public` 100% `postgres`, 251 tables, ZERO supabase_admin-owned objects. So nothing on this project has ever created an object in `public` as supabase_admin and the default ACL has never fired. **THAT MEASUREMENT CANNOT CLOSE THE ACCEPTED RISK, because there are 380 tables now** -- 129 created since anybody looked, 51% growth, and the row's own re-check trigger has not been evaluated against one of them. The row names the monitor as one SELECT and nothing runs it. `tools/ownership_evidence_drift.py` measures the one thing a clone CAN measure and is RED; it says outright this is NOT a claim the ACL has fired, and that 129 is itself a LOWER BOUND because a table dropped and recreated by another role changes the count by nothing. Registered report-only -- it reports that nobody has LOOKED, which must never refuse a push. TO CLOSE IT: Michael runs Section 1 of `sql/supabase_admin_default_acl_check_2026-08-26.sql` and the numbers go in `tools/ownership_evidence_drift.json`.
- ITEM 6 TRIAGE 19 TO 24 OF 29, two untriaged and named (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- `api/sc-credentials.js` and `api/sd-webauthn.js`, both credential inserts, are UNREAD not cleared. TWO MORE READ-MODIFY-WRITES found: `dnt-bi.js` is the same shape as trial-renew and is ACCEPTED because THE AUTHOR ALREADY MADE AND WROTE DOWN THAT CALL in the function's own comment -- the entry RECORDS a judgment rather than re-deriving one, with the condition under which it stops holding. `audit-checkpoint.js` is SAFE because the schema carries `unique (audit_table, window_end)` and its verify step says why -- the checker cannot see a constraint that lives in SQL, which is its scope limit and not a gap in that file. `sen-portal.js` and `stonedesk-track.js` are ACCEPTED with the contrast named: a retry there leaves an ORPHANED working credential, the opposite of trial-start where the caller LOST the token that was minted.
- A CORRECT REFUSAL WORTH RECORDING (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- `tooling_inventory.py` REFUSED my PURPOSES entry for the new tool, because a report-only tool's description already lives in REGISTRY and a second copy is the claim-in-two-places failure that document exists to prevent. The PURPOSES line was DELETED rather than reworded to match, which is what its own message asks for.
- ITEM 6 TRIAGE CLOSES AT 26 OF 26, 0 UNTRIAGED (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- and the tool now REFUSES to let that read as more than it is: zero untriaged is every write THIS CHECKER CAN SEE, not every retryable write on the platform. The last two files were the most interesting. `sc-credentials.js` is the BEST-GUARDED write in the list and the checker could not see any of it: a conditional UPDATE filtered on `updated_at=eq.<the value it read>`, a PLAIN INSERT rather than an upsert so a concurrent first-write loses LOUDLY, and the only correct retry in the list -- bounded at ONE, re-reading and RE-APPLYING the same single-service change so a concurrent edit to a different service survives. THAT IS A THIRD GUARD CLASS THE CHECKER CANNOT RECOGNISE: it asks "is there an idempotency KEY" (identifying the REQUEST) and this is OPTIMISTIC CONCURRENCY (a compare-and-set on a VERSION column, identifying the STATE). Deliberately NOT folded into the vocabulary -- they answer different questions and conflating them is the fail-open direction. `sd-webauthn.js` is SAFE because `unique (credential_id)` is GLOBAL and the id comes from the authenticator, not the server, so a retry collides; its 409 is a generic upstream error, the same SHAPE as trial-start's lost token with a much milder consequence because nothing is lost. Three checker scope limits are now recorded in the register against the files that revealed each.

- TWO SUITES WERE RED ON ORIGIN/MAIN AND NOTHING REPORTED IT (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- found by surveying rather than by being told: I ran every `tests/*.js` looking for the next gap. 101 green, 3 red. WHY NOBODY KNEW: `run_all_tests.py --hook` has been PAUSED since 2026-09-10 (Michael's call, after five stranded PROBE commits reached origin in one day). That pause is correct and is NOT undone here; this is what its cost looks like. BOTH RED SUITES HAD THE SAME CAUSE AND NEEDED DIFFERENT FIXES. `sairnbiz_server_backup.js` asserted the population is exactly TEN and the three-way match added sb_po and sb_recv without updating it. I MEASURED ALL FOUR SIDES BEFORE MOVING THE NUMBER -- registry, sd-data.js SB_RESOURCES, sairnbiz.html SB_SYNCED, and the SQL table AND grant for every one -- because "update the count until it passes" is how a real disagreement gets buried. They agree at 12; nothing had drifted. The literal assertion STAYS (a resource in three of four files and not the fourth is what that section exists to catch) and the history is kept in the comment rather than overwritten: nine, then ten, then twelve. THREE OTHER ARMS IN THE SAME FILE GOT THE OPPOSITE FIX -- `r.failed` and `r.notProvisioned` are about ERROR ACCOUNTING, not the population, so a literal there made every resource addition break a test about something else. They derive from SB_SYNCED now and fail CLOSED: a broken anchor gives 0 and a module-level assertion stops the suite rather than letting every count arm pass vacuously. `sairnbiz_incidents_kpi.js` carried `resources.length === 10` inside a test named "sb_incidents IS in the backup registry" -- REMOVED rather than updated, because a test about injury logs should not fail when somebody adds purchase orders.

It is red in THIS clone only, because it does `fs.readdirSync(ROOT)` over every `.html` on the FILESYSTEM and an untracked `piac.html` (dated 2026-08-28, not mine, never committed) has been sitting in the root all session. A suite any scratch file in any clone can turn red is a suite people learn to ignore -- raised rather than changed, because it is somebody else's file and the fix is theirs to choose. SOMEBODY SHOULD ALSO SAY WHAT piac.html IS: it has survived every clean tree check tonight as `?? piac.html`.

- A STRAY FILE COULD TURN FOUR SUITES RED, NOT ONE (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- asked to harden `public_token_leaves_the_address_bar.js`, swept for the same shape FIRST and found it in three more suites at five more call sites. All four did `fs.readdirSync(ROOT).filter(f => f.endsWith('.html'))`, asserting over whatever `.html` happens to be in a clone's WORKING DIRECTORY. THE OTHER THREE SURVIVED `piac.html` BY LUCK, NOT DESIGN: `licence_rekey_isolation` filters on /^sairn|^stonedesk/ and `piac` matches neither; `idless_rows_are_reported` and `sairn_storage_wrapper_honesty` had assertions that tolerated the extra file. A differently-named stray takes any of them red. `tests/rootpages.js` is now the ONE definition of "a root app page this repository ships" -- a fact about the git index, not about somebody's working directory -- because four copies of a rule is four places for it to drift. IT FAILS CLOSED and that is the half worth testing: an empty or implausibly short list is a broken reader, and every caller's loop would pass VACUOUSLY against it, so it THROWS rather than returning []. Verified by breaking the pathspec: ALL FOUR go red, not just the one I was asked to fix. STRAYS ARE NAMED RATHER THAN SILENTLY SKIPPED -- that line is currently the only thing on the platform that says piac.html exists. PROVEN ON A CLEAN origin/main WORKTREE WITH A STRAY PLANTED: all four stay green, and removing the tracked-only scope brings the false red straight back.
- A FALSE RED IS NOT THE HARMLESS DIRECTION (Fourth, 2026-09-14) -- worth keeping as the reasoning rather than the fix: a suite that goes red for reasons unrelated to the code is one people learn to skip, and the next time it is red for a real reason nobody looks. This repo has the receipt for the other direction in the same week -- `run_all_tests.py --hook` is paused, a suite that goes red can stay red for days, and two did.

The fourth is the one nobody named and it is where this work came from. `docs/2026-09-02-competitive-gap-status-rederived.md` supersedes STATUS for the first two only; the 09-03 audit has never had a status re-derivation, so everything in it needs re-verifying against code before it is treated as open. MOST REMAINING ITEMS IN THE STATUS DOC ARE VENDOR- OR CERTIFICATION-BLOCKED (837/835, clearinghouse, EPCS/PDMP, EVV aggregator, telephony IVR) -- open, but not buildable in-house, which is worth knowing before anyone reaches for one.

Both fixed to the EMPLOYER half deliberately -- one is the cost of hiring, the other sums fully-loaded cost; neither is withholding, and the two constants are identical numbers and different money.
